# Usability review — October 2026

This is the record of the usability review behind [`USABILITY-PLAN.md`](../USABILITY-PLAN.md). Twelve reviewers each examined one user-facing area of gvm, and a completeness critic added three more. Every finding was then checked by two independent verifiers. One re-read the code and re-ran the commands to confirm the evidence (**fact**). The other judged the suggestion against the hard design rules (**fit**). Where the fit verifier proposed a safer variant, that variant is the suggestion shown here. All 210 findings survived verification: 61 high, 109 medium and 40 low.

The review ran against commit `b69119b` (after Phase 19) on a Windows development build, so line references point at that revision. **Status** is `open` until the phase named in *Plan* lands. Each phase then closes its ids here, with a link to the resolving commit.

Personas: **operator** (owns the VPS), **dashboard-user** (friend or moderator without SSH), **player**, **manifest-author** (adds games through YAML), **contributor**.

## Areas

- [Install and first server](#onboarding) — 15 findings
- [CLI ergonomics and consistency](#cli-ergonomics) — 14 findings
- [Day-2 operations and visibility](#day2-ops) — 15 findings
- [Mods and workshop content](#content-mods) — 15 findings
- [Choosing a game and configuring it](#game-catalog-config) — 13 findings
- [Web dashboard and API](#web-dashboard) — 13 findings
- [Users, tokens, secrets and audit](#admin-security) — 15 findings
- [Ports, firewall, edge and connectivity](#network-firewall) — 11 findings
- [Adopting an existing server](#migration-adopt) — 14 findings
- [When things go wrong](#failure-recovery) — 15 findings
- [Documentation for users](#documentation) — 15 findings
- [Hands-on CLI walkthrough](#cli-walkthrough) — 15 findings
- [Off-box backups and disaster recovery (losing the VPS, moving to a new one)](#offsite-backup-dr) — 15 findings
- [Will each shipped game actually boot under gvm's hardened unit? (Mono/Wine vs W^X, Proton, 32-bit, xvfb)](#game-runtime-compat) — 13 findings
- [Upgrading gvm itself, and removing it cleanly](#gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall) — 12 findings

<a id="onboarding"></a>
## Install and first server (`onboarding`)

On a fresh Debian/Ubuntu VPS, gvm cannot currently get an operator to a joinable managed game server. Nothing installs gvm itself: there is no install script, .deb, release binary, `gvm init`/`setup` command, or Install section in the README. The units, tmpfiles fragment, system users and groups, SteamCMD and Postgres all have to be set up by hand from the source tree and a 7,148-line ARCHITECTURE.md, and some errors point to a "gvm package's postinstall" that does not exist. Past that, the managed path dead-ends. `gvm install` always refuses because the SteamCMD and Minecraft providers are built but not wired. Secrets can't be set, because `gvm secret set` waits on a gvmd that is unimplemented and that the CLI is forbidden to dial, so 11 of 16 games run with empty passwords. `gvm firewall` is unbuilt, `gvm doctor` reports false problems on every host, and no command tells a player which address:port to join. The dashboard-user has no path at all: `gvm web` and `gvm gvmd` are unimplemented and `user create` needs gvmd. The parts that are built are good: a 16-game catalog with app ids built in, `create` with automatic conflict-aware ports and clear missing-var errors, an honest `apply --diff`, and refusals that never fake success. So the plan is mostly wiring, a host bootstrap command, a guided path and connect info, not a redesign.

**Strengths to preserve**

- `gvm game list` ships 16 games with Steam app ids, ports, readiness and shutdown ladders built in (e.g. valheim = app 896660). An operator never needs to look up an app id or a default port.
- `gvm create` allocates and writes down ports automatically, using every occupancy source (other instances, live listeners, the ephemeral range, the reserved list), and prints exactly which ports it chose.
- When a required var is missing, `gvm create` names each one with its help text ("--var public_name=…"). A typo'd or undeclared --var, or a duplicated one, is refused instead of silently ignored.
- `gvm create` says what it did NOT do ("nothing else on this host changed: no account, no directories, no unit.") and names the next command ("next: gvm apply <n> --diff"). `--dry-run` prints the YAML without writing it.
- `gvm apply --diff` is the same plan apply executes, with add/change/remove counts, explicit '!' lines for what was not checked, and "nothing has been applied.". A second apply on a converged host is a no-op.
- Unbuilt paths refuse honestly and exit non-zero (`gvm install` prints its full preflight and then names the missing provider wiring), so a script never mistakes a no-op for success.
- Running without root gets a refusal that names the fix ("Run it as root (sudo gvm create)"), and instance-name errors state the exact allowed grammar.
- Sensible defaults are written automatically: a nightly backup at 04:00 with keep 14 (Valheim), memory limits from the manifest's sizing, and the unit enabled at boot with no dependency on the control plane.
- The SteamCMD locator's error already contains the exact prerequisite command (`dpkg --add-architecture i386 && apt-get install lib32gcc-s1`), which can be reused in a doctor check.

### onboarding-1 — No way to install gvm itself: no installer, package, release binary or README install steps

**High** · operator · missing-feature · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 2, 7 · Plan: **23** → 52 · Status: open

**Evidence.** One narrowing: the half-written-YAML case needs /etc/gvm/instances to exist while group gvm does not. systemd-tmpfiles normally skips the `d /etc/gvm 0750 root gvm` line when the group is missing, and then requireParent (fs.go:676-680) refuses before any write. So the case only happens when the operator created the directories by hand. That is plausible but rarer than the finding implies. (orig: README.md:47-57 has only `make build`/`make check` and no install section. `packaging/` holds only systemd/ and tmpfiles/; the Phase 12 plan lists packaging/nfpm.yaml, postinstall.sh, gvmd.service and gvm-web.service (docs/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:608-610), and none exist. There is no `make deb` target (Makefile:27-91) and CI only cross-compiles to /dev/null (.github/workflows/ci.yml 'cross-compile the deploy target'), so no release binaries exist. packaging/packaging.go:24-27 says the mapping to /usr/lib is "the installer's business", but no installer exists and no command writes packaging.FS to disk. packaging/tmpfiles/gvm.conf:43,50-51,57-61 needs users and groups gvm, gvm-fetch, gvm-content and caddy, which nothing creates; the only recipe is ARCHITECTURE.md §14.2 (line 4597-4600) inside a 7,148-line spec. Units hard-code /usr/local/bin/gvm (packaging/systemd/gvm@.service ExecStart; internal/reconcile/probe.go:30). Errors assume a package exists: internal/hostops/fs.go:679 "is the gvm package installed and has systemd-tmpfiles run?" and fs.go:530 "the gvm package's postinstall creates it". The adopt runbook's step 1 is `sudo dpkg -i gvm_0.4.1_amd64.deb` (ARCHITECTURE.md:6846), a package that cannot be built. fs.go:605-610 writes the spec and then chowns it to group gvm, so a missing group leaves the YAML on disk, and the retry refuses with "already exists; `gvm create` never overwrites" (create.go:396-399). Contrast legacy/projectzomboid-bash/README.md, which has a five-line Install block with time and RAM expectations.)

**Impact.** An operator who is not a Go developer needs a Go 1.26 toolchain, has to copy files out of a source checkout, create four system accounts and groups, run systemd-tmpfiles, enable the guard and verify timers, and install SteamCMD with i386 multiarch, all reconstructed from a design spec. One wrong step surfaces later as an obscure chown or missing-parent error mid-command, sometimes leaving a half-written instance file.

**Suggestion.** 1. Implement the host-side install once, as an idempotent `sudo gvm host init [--diff]`. It copies itself to /usr/local/bin/gvm with temp+fsync+rename, and writes packaging.FS to /usr/lib/systemd/system and /usr/lib/tmpfiles.d with one mapping table in Go. It creates gvm, gvm-fetch, gvm-console and gvm-content (with the memberships from ARCHITECTURE §14.2) by exec of /usr/sbin/useradd and groupadd, runs systemd-tmpfiles --create, reloads systemd over D-Bus, and enables gvm-guard.timer and gvm-job@_verify.timer. It prints the apt lines and never runs them, and it does not touch ufw. 2. Make the planned nfpm .deb's postinstall a single `gvm host init` call, so the mapping lives in one place. 3. Move the two caddy-group lines (/run/gvm/web, /etc/gvm/caddy) into a separate gvm-web tmpfiles fragment installed only with the dashboard, so a games-only host converges cleanly. 4. Add a release workflow that publishes static amd64/arm64 binaries plus SHA256SUMS, and a README Install section: download, `sha256sum -c`, `sudo ./gvm host init`, the apt lines, then `sudo gvm doctor`. 5. Make WriteAtomic chown the temp file before the rename, so a failed chown never leaves a spec behind. Also have create pre-check `user.LookupGroup("gvm")` and name `gvm host init` in the error.

### onboarding-2 — `gvm install` can never install game files: the SteamCMD and Minecraft providers exist but are not wired

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 12 · Plan: **31** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/install.go:109-111 always ends with `ErrProviderUnbuilt` ("the provider seam is not built in this release"). internal/cli/jobengines.go:157-160 and 415-418 refuse update and repair for the same reason ("needs a provider context ... internal/update.Bind over steamcmd.New + jobs.NewSteamRunner"). `grep -rn "internal/provider" internal/cli cmd` finds no imports, although internal/provider/steamcmd and internal/provider/minecraft are implemented. Without an install, start refuses: internal/lifecycle/verbs.go:217-222 "the provider's install sentinel is not on disk" with the hint "gvm install <n>", which is a loop. The IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN Milestone A ("gvm create + gvm apply + gvm start brings up a fresh Valheim instance") cannot be reached on a fresh box.

**Impact.** The core journey (create, apply, install, start, players join) stops at step 3 for every one of the 16 shipped games. A new operator's only route to a running server under gvm is adopting an install they already have. Nightly `update` jobs written by create also fail.

**Suggestion.** Wire HostEnv to build the provider context: steamcmd.New(steamcmd.WithRunner(jobs.NewSteamRunner(...))) under update.Bind for install, update and repair, and the minecraft provider for the Paper/Forge/NeoForge manifests. Run post_install hooks and the D-32 post-verification, keeping Plan network-read-only and Apply digest-verified (rule 12). Until that ships, `gvm create` should print "managed installs are not available in this build; see `gvm adopt`" instead of implying `install` is the next step.

### onboarding-3 — Game passwords can't be set: `gvm secret set` waits on a gvmd the CLI may not dial, so 11/16 games run with empty secrets

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 3, 10 · Plan: **29** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Manifests referencing .Secret.* number 12 of 16, not 11; 7daystodie-telnet.yaml also does. Runner refusal: internal/cli/run.go:167-195 plus internal/runner/preflight.go:185 and :236-253 (all referenced secrets are required, `secret_set` guards notwithstanding). Manual path: internal/reconcile/units.go:523-546 and internal/secrets/refs.go:136-152 (layout instances/<name>/<id>, where the id comes from secrets.FromTemplate, e.g. rcon_password -> rcon). Even `gvm secret show-path` (secret.go:75-88) returns 'requires gvmd'. (orig: Ran: `gvm.exe secret set --instance x --name rcon_password --generate` -> "gvm secret: secret set: requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running (`systemctl status gvmd`)" exit 1 (internal/cli/secret.go:57, user.go:141-143). gvmd is `notImplemented` (root.go:101), no gvmd.service is shipped, and by design the CLI never dials admin.sock (transport.go:29-32), so this can never succeed. That breaks hard rule 1 (every CLI command works as root with gvmd dead). secrets.Store.Write and GeneratePassword exist (internal/secrets/filestore.go:122, generate.go:62) but are unused by the CLI. create.go:511-514 prints "secrets ... not generated yet; the unit starts without them and the game gets an empty value". 11 manifests reference .Secret.* (valheim, cs2 gslt/rcon, rust, vrising, palworld, PZ admin/join/rcon, 7dtd, ark, corekeeper, generic-steam, minecraft-paper). Valheim defaults `public: "true"` (games/valheim.yaml:115) and drops `-password` when join_password is unset (valheim.yaml:41-42), so the default first Valheim server is publicly listed with no password. games/minecraft-paper.yaml:19-21 promises "a random secret generated at create time", which create does not do.)

**Impact.** An operator cannot password-protect a server, set a CS2 GSLT, or give RCON-controlled games (PZ, Minecraft, Rust) a working RCON password. The error sends them hunting for a daemon that doesn't exist. The default Valheim server is open to anyone who finds it in the browser.

**Suggestion.** 1. Implement `sudo gvm secret set <instance> <name> (--generate | --stdin)` in-process as root through hostops and secrets.Store. It reads stdin without echo, rejects ' " \ $ ` plus ValidateValue's rules, and runs the manifest's spec.validators with the candidate in ValidatorInput.Secrets before writing. It is audited as root_cli with the name and fingerprint only. On success it prints `next: sudo gvm apply <n> && sudo gvm restart <n>`. 2. Implement `list` and `show-path` locally too: Store.List and Store.Path read no values. 3. In `gvm create`, after the YAML write succeeds, generate any missing machine-only secrets the manifest references (rcon/admin/telnet). Never overwrite an existing file, skip adopted instances, and have --dry-run list them as 'would generate'. 4. For join_password, generate one by default and print it once, labelled 'share with players'. `--join-password-stdin` sets your own value. `--no-join-password` opts out and prints a warning when the game's public var is true.

### onboarding-6 — No way to onboard a dashboard user: `gvm web`/`gvmd` unimplemented, no units, `user create` needs gvmd and lacks owner

**High** · dashboard-user · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 3, 8, 9 · Plan: **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Ran: `gvm.exe web` -> "gvm web: not implemented yet" exit 3; `gvm.exe gvmd` -> "gvm gvmd: not implemented yet" exit 3 (root.go:101-102), though internal/web/server.go exists. No gvmd.service or gvm-web.service in packaging/systemd. user.go:63 `user create` returns errRequiresDaemon, and `gvm user create --help` shows only "-role viewer|operator|admin". ARCHITECTURE.md §14.9 (5698-5721) specifies that root creates the first account in-process (`sudo gvm user create --email ... --role owner` with TTY password and TOTP enrolment), with no daemon involved. The non-root error also suggests `--remote`/GVM_TOKEN "(`gvm token create` mints one)" (transport.go:154-156), but token create needs gvmd (token.go:42) and the remote client is unbuilt (transport.go:174-175).

**Impact.** An operator cannot let a friend or moderator start, stop or restart a server or read its console without giving them root SSH. The suggested token route loops back to the same 'requires gvmd' error.

**Suggestion.** Phase it. (1) Root in-process, never admin.sock: `sudo gvm user create --email --role owner|admin|viewer` (TTY password, TOTP mandatory for owner), `gvm user invite` (ceiling viewer, as designed), `sudo gvm user grant <email> <instance> operator|viewer` for a per-instance friend or moderator, and `token create/list/revoke`. All are audited root_cli. When Postgres is unreachable they fail with 'needs Postgres (dashboard users live there)', not 'requires gvmd'. (2) `gvm host init --dashboard` creates master.key (0600 root) if absent. It installs the gvmd, gvm-web and gvm-db-migrate units but does not start the dashboard. It prints the apt lines (postgresql, Caddy from its official repo) and the pg_hba/pg_ident lines, with the 'insert above local all all peer' warning, then 'next: sudo gvm db migrate'. (3) Wire the gvmd and web roles with TOTP verified in gvmd. (4) Have doctor verify the pg_hba ordering. Never auto-migrate.

### onboarding-4 — No way to open game ports: apply stages no firewall rules, `gvm firewall` is unbuilt, and nothing lists which ports to allow

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: 33 → **40** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Valheim ports are at games/valheim.yaml:84-88 (game/query udp public, spare udp expose:none), not :97-99. `gvm ports` (internal/cli/ports.go:233-246) partly mitigates this by listing proto/port per owner. (orig: Ran: `gvm.exe firewall` -> "gvm firewall: not implemented yet" exit 3 (root.go:152). internal/reconcile/apply.go:105-214 BuildPlan has no firewall step, although IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:593 says "gvm apply on a host with ufw inactive stages rules and prints the `gvm firewall enable` instruction". `gvm create`/`apply`/`start` output never mentions which protocol/port pairs are expose:public (e.g. valheim udp/2456-2457, valheim.yaml:97-99). internal/firewall (guard, reconcile, ufw parser) is built but used only by doctor, and doctor's fixes point to the unbuilt command (checks_fw.go:56 "gvm firewall enable"; :82,147,176 "gvm firewall apply").)

**Impact.** On a box with ufw active (or a cloud security group) the server starts, `gvm status` says running, and nobody can join, with no hint why. On a box with ufw inactive, ports are open by accident and the derived-firewall feature silently does nothing.

**Suggestion.** 1. Add a firewall step to reconcile.BuildPlan, rendered in `apply --diff` as in the spec. For the instance's expose:public and admin ports it ADDS gvm:<instance>:<port>-tagged rules through the detected backend (ufw or nft). When ufw is inactive it stages them and says so. It deletes only gvm-tagged rules, and none when the parse is not trusted (D-42). It never touches SSH rules and never enables ufw (D-41). 2. Wire `sudo gvm firewall enable` as the only path that turns ufw on. It refuses when SSH detection is not confident, re-verifies the SSH rules after enabling, and prints the 'open a second SSH session' warning. 3. Wire `gvm firewall --diff` for a host-wide read-only view, and `adopt-rules` with confirmation. 4. End every apply and create with one line: 'open in your VPS provider's firewall too: udp/2456-2457'.

### onboarding-5 — `gvm doctor` can't confirm an install: it never checks instances, fakes firewall state, and fails every host

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 8 · Plan: **24** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Add: internal/doctor/checks_host_linux.go:15-21 reports a missing /run/gvm as CheckSkip ('tmpfiles not run?') rather than a problem, and HOST-009 checks only /run/gvm and /run/gvm/web. (orig: Ran: `gvm.exe doctor` -> "✗ HOST-001 Postgres socket not found ... ✗ FW-001 firewall backend ufw is not active  Fix: gvm firewall enable ... ✗ FW-003 ufw status numbered could not be fully parsed; deletions suppressed (D-42) ... 3 problem(s) found" exit 1. internal/cli/doctor.go:57-60 hard-codes `liveActive := false; liveTrusted := false` ("stub until ufw backend CLI wiring is in place"), so FW-001/FW-003 fail on Linux too. doctor.go:150-151 `doctorLoadInstances() []spec.Instance { return nil }`, so no INST-* check ever runs. checks_host_db.go:119-125 makes a missing Postgres a Problem, although its own comment (line 22) says DB checks "return Skip gracefully" and Postgres is optional for running games. HOST-010 (checks_host.go:39-65) reads the units EMBEDDED in the binary, not the installed ones. No check covers units installed in /usr/lib/systemd/system, /usr/local/bin/gvm presence or version, the gvm/gvm-fetch/gvm-content groups, the tmpfiles tree beyond /run/gvm, steamcmd/i386 or Java. IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:621 requires "a clean host reports 0 problems".)

**Impact.** After a manual install, the one command meant to answer "did I do it right?" always says 3 problems, two of them with fixes that print "not implemented yet". It never notices the things that actually break a first server: missing template unit, missing groups, no steamcmd, wrong binary path. Operators learn to ignore it.

**Suggestion.** 1. Load instances from lifecycle.List plus pinnedManifest, so INST-* checks run. 2. Feed the real backend state into the FW checks. An unparseable or absent backend becomes a Skip with the reason. An inactive ufw becomes a Warning listing the expose:public ports. 3. HOST-001: when Postgres is absent, emit a Note ('dashboard/audit unavailable; game servers unaffected') unless a gvm-web unit is installed. 4. Add HOST checks: packaging.FS byte-matches /usr/lib; the installed gvm's sha256 or buildinfo matches the running binary (no exec); the system users, groups and memberships exist; each gvm.conf path has its mode and owner; steamcmd is locatable via steamcmd.Locator; a suitable Java exists for minecraft-* instances; and the no-postgresql-dependency check also runs over installed units and drop-ins. 5. Add a unit test that fails when any Fix string names an unrouted or notImplemented verb (shared with onboarding-8).

### onboarding-7 — Players are never told how to connect: no join address in create, start or status

**Medium** · player · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 10 · Plan: **33** · Status: open

**Evidence.** port_stride is at games/valheim.yaml:82 (not :96). `gvm ports` (internal/cli/ports.go:233-246) also lists per-instance proto/port, so 'only create's output shows that, once' is not quite right. (orig: `grep -rniE 'connect (to|with)|steam://|public ip' internal/cli internal/lifecycle internal/web` finds nothing. lifecycle/status.go:180-233 Status.Report prints phase, unit, pid, ready time and players, but no ports or address. start.go:125-159 prints before/after phases and steps only. The manifest schema has no connect hint; Expose public/admin/none is the only reachability field (spec/manifest.go:104-112). port_stride moves a second instance off the default (valheim.yaml:96 `port_stride: {vh: 10}` puts valheim-2 on 2466), and only create's output (create.go:496-502) shows that, once. Valheim players join on the game port, but the Steam browser uses the query port, and nothing explains the difference.)

**Impact.** After a successful start, the operator has to work out the VPS's public IP, which of 2-3 ports players type, and whether a password is needed, then relay that by hand. With a second instance on a non-default port, players type the default and fail.

**Suggestion.** Add an optional, template-free manifest block, e.g. `connect: {port: game, browser_port: query, note: "..."}`. It is validated as port names that must exist, and defaults to the first expose:public port when absent. Add `spec.public_address` to gvm.yaml. host init or doctor suggests it from local interface addresses and flags NAT/private ranges, with no external lookup. Print a `join <addr>:<port>` line in create, after a successful start, and in status/list. `gvm status <n> --share` prints the player snippet: address:port, the browser port if different, 'password required: yes/no' derived from Store.Has (never the value), and the note.

### onboarding-8 — Help, errors and hints advertise commands that don't work and point to commands that don't exist

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1 · Plan: **20** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Also spec/validate.go:350 WithFix names `gvm manifest upgrade`. (orig: usage.go:16 prints "So every command below works over SSH as root with nothing else running." above a list that includes `firewall` (not implemented), `install` (always refuses), and `user`, `token`, `secret` and `audit`, whose every subcommand returns "requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running (`systemctl status gvmd`)" (user.go:141-143) for a daemon that is not implemented and that the CLI may not reach. Hints naming nonexistent verbs: lifecycle/derive.go:168 and verbs.go:206 "gvm manifest upgrade <n> --dry"; content/lock.go:245; doctor checks_edge.go:163,186,194 "gvm caddy render"; checks_edge.go:66 "apt install caddy (or use gvm bundled build)" (no bundled build exists, and the distro caddy is far below the enforced 2.11.1 minimum). README.md:28-33 lists Milestones A-D with no indication of which work.)

**Impact.** An operator follows a printed hint, hits "unknown command" or "not implemented", and can't tell a misconfiguration from a missing feature. 'Ensure the daemon is running' sends them debugging systemd for a service that was never shipped.

**Suggestion.** Keep the banner but scope it: 'Every instance and operations command works over SSH as root with gvmd, the dashboard and Postgres all down. Administration commands need Postgres.' Generate '(not yet available in this build)' in usage from the routing table (notImplemented, or a per-command 'unbuilt' flag), not by hand. Replace errRequiresDaemon with '`gvm X` is not available in this build' until each verb is implemented in-process. Drop the --remote/GVM_TOKEN suggestion while ErrRemoteUnbuilt holds. Add a test that extracts every `gvm <verb>` in Hint, Fix and error strings across internal/ and fails unless the verb is routed and built. Add a 'What works today' table to the README.

### onboarding-9 — Can't see a game's settings or required passwords before create; error points to a command that doesn't list them

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort S · fact: partial · fit: sound · Plan: **26** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Partial existing path: internal/cli/create.go:284-294 (missing required vars are listed with help) and :449-452 (--dry-run prints the YAML with all vars and ports), both after the root check at :368-378. (orig: create.go:261: "%s declares no variable %q; `gvm game validate %s` lists them". Ran: `gvm.exe game validate valheim` -> "✓ 4 vars, memory_model flat — MemoryHigh 2688M, MemoryMax 3G at the declared defaults": a count with no names. `gvm.exe game validate minecraft-paper` -> "✓ 14 vars ...". `gvm game` subcommands are only validate/list/which/hooks (`gvm.exe game -h`). The secrets a game needs (cs2: gslt, rcon_password) appear only after create writes the file (create.go:511-514). Optional settings such as valheim `public`/`crossplay`/`world_name` (immutable, apply: rebuild) are discoverable only by reading games/*.yaml from source.)

**Impact.** An operator can't plan a server (world name is immutable!), learn that CS2 needs a GSLT, or see the RAM floor without reading YAML in the repo. They discover options by trial and error, and some choices can't be changed later without a rebuild.

**Suggestion.** Add `gvm game show <id>` printing each var (type, default, required, min/max, apply: restart|rebuild|immutable, help), secrets used, ports with proto/expose, ram_floor and disk_floor, the recommended jobs, and a ready-to-edit `sudo gvm create <name> --game <id> --var ...` example line. Fix the create.go:261 message to name it.

### onboarding-10 — Per-command help is broken: `gvm help X` gives one line; `-h` exits 1 with an unrelated error (and keeps running as root)

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Ran: `gvm.exe help create` -> "gvm create — define a new instance" (root.go:254-265 prints only the summary, although usage ends with 'Run "gvm help <command>" for detail.'). Ran: `gvm.exe create -h` -> full usage, then "gvm create: gvm: no transport for this command: ..." exit=1; same for apply/start/install. Cause: flagErr turns flag.ErrHelp into nil (game.go:613-618), so parseCreateArgs returns empty options with no error and runCreate carries on (create.go:127-130, 368-384). As root it reaches createExec with an empty name and game. `gvm.exe secret -h` -> "secret: unknown subcommand \"-h\"" exit 1. `gvm.exe doctor -h` -> "gvm doctor: flag: help requested" exit 1. `gvm.exe user create --help` -> "gvm user: flag: help requested" exit 1.

**Impact.** The obvious way to learn a command either shows nothing useful or ends in a scary error and a failure exit code. As root, `-h` actually runs part of the command with empty arguments.

**Suggestion.** Make `gvm help <cmd>` dispatch `<cmd> -h`. Have flagErr return a sentinel (errHelpShown) that Main maps to exit 0 with nothing else printed. Accept -h/--help/help on every subcommand group (secret, user, token, db, job, content). Add 1-2 concrete examples to each usage block, e.g. `sudo gvm create valheim-1 --game valheim --var public_name="Friends"`.

### onboarding-11 — First server takes 5-6 separate commands with no single guided path, and apply doesn't say what comes next

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4, 5, 6 · Plan: 26 → **34** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The designed flow is create -> apply --diff -> apply -> install -> start (create.go:5-7, 517). `grep -n "next:" internal/cli/*.go` matches only create.go:517. apply.go:181-186 and diff.go:68-85 end with change counts and no next step. lifecycle/verbs.go:221-222 offers `--force` to start a server whose install sentinel is missing ("this refusal can be overridden: gvm start <n> --force", start.go:114-116), which invites a guess on a fresh instance.

**Impact.** Each step is well explained on its own, but a new operator has to remember the sequence. After apply they are left guessing between install and start, and may reach for the offered --force.

**Suggestion.** Now: end apply, install, start and update with a `next:` line from lifecycle.Derive. Only offer --force for a missing sentinel when the instance has run before; a never-installed instance gets 'next: sudo gvm install <n>'. After onboarding-2 lands, add `sudo gvm up <name> --game <id> [--var ...]` with these rules. It refuses if the instance already exists, and never re-creates or overwrites. It shows the create YAML and the `apply --diff` plan and asks once on a TTY; it requires --yes when not interactive. It runs create, apply, install and start as the same separately audited verbs. It never passes --force or --allow-unprobed. It stops at the first refusal and prints that refusal's hint. It finishes with the join line from onboarding-7. --dry-run stops after printing the plan.

### onboarding-12 — `gvm start` waits silently for up to 15-30 minutes and on failure prints only 'systemd job ... failed'

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: **34** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/lifecycle/manager.go:186-188 returns before After is derived, so printing a reason on failure needs a re-observe. hard_deadline is at valheim.yaml:57 and 7daystodie.yaml:58. (orig: hostops/systemd_linux.go:59-91 blocks until the Type=notify job finishes, so up to readiness.hard_deadline (valheim.yaml:58 900s; 7DTD 30 min per games/7daystodie.yaml:209 help text). start.go:87-97: when mgr.Do returns an error, the Result (with After.Reason/Hint) is discarded and only the error is printed, e.g. `systemd job for gvm@<n>.service ended "failed"` (systemd_linux.go:85). Manifest fail_marker hints such as valheim.yaml:65-67 ("Valheim refuses a join password under 5 chars...") are never shown by `start`. Nothing points to `gvm logs <n>` or `gvm status <n>`.)

**Impact.** An operator watching a blank terminal for 10+ minutes on first world generation will assume it's hung and Ctrl-C it. When boot fails, the useful diagnosis the manifest author wrote is hidden behind a generic systemd message.

**Suggestion.** While waiting, print progress from status.json and the manifest's readiness.progress markers ("Load world", "Zonesystem Awake"), with elapsed time against hard_deadline, and note that Ctrl-C does not stop the server. On failure, print res.After.Reason and Hint (including fail_marker hints), the last ~20 console lines, and `next: sudo gvm logs <n>`. Add `--no-wait` for scripts.

### onboarding-13 — No RAM/disk preflight: manifests declare floors but `gvm create` never checks them against the VPS

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **26** · Status: open

**Evidence.** RAMFloorMB is also consumed at internal/spec/sizing.go:125-126 (flat-model MemoryMax base), in addition to the comment at reconcile/units.go:635. (orig: spec/sizing.go:134-136: "DiskFloorBytes is the free space `gvm create` and doctor require before an install", but `grep -rn DiskFloorBytes internal` finds no caller. ram_floor_mb is used only to render a comment in the drop-in (reconcile/units.go:634-635). createExec (create.go:394-475) reads no MemTotal and calls no Statfs. The legacy README's Sizing table ("8GB is the floor" for PZ) has no equivalent in gvm's output.)

**Impact.** On a 4 GB VPS, `gvm create ark-1 --game ark-se` or a second PZ instance succeeds silently. The first symptom is an OOM-stopped server (OOMPolicy=stop) or a SteamCMD download that fills the disk mid-install.

**Suggestion.** In `gvm create` and `gvm doctor` (as an INST check), compare the manifest's ram_floor/MemoryMax plus the sum of other enabled instances' MemoryMax against /proc/meminfo MemTotal, and disk_floor_gb against free space at paths.install. Print a warning with the numbers ("valheim needs ~3G; 1.4G unclaimed on this host"), not a refusal, so the operator decides. Show the same numbers in `gvm game show`.

### onboarding-14 — Minecraft's EULA is accepted silently on the operator's behalf

**Low** · operator · safety-ux · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 10 · Plan: **30** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/hook/mc.go:30-36,50 mc.accept_eula writes "eula=true" to eula.txt as a post_install hook (games/minecraft-paper.yaml:159). No var, flag or prompt asks the operator, and create's output (create.go:483-518) never mentions the EULA.

**Impact.** The operator never sees the Mojang EULA they are agreeing to by running the server. It is a legal acceptance gvm makes for them without telling them.

**Suggestion.** Add an `eula_accepted: {type: bool, required: true, apply: restart, help: "Set true to accept the Minecraft EULA: https://aka.ms/MinecraftEULA"}` var to every minecraft-* manifest, with a revision bump. Add a closed-registry validator `mc.eula_accepted` that rejects false. Make mc.accept_eula refuse with a clear error unless the var is true. Keep its never-overwrite behaviour, so existing installs and operator-written eula.txt files are untouched. Instances pinned to the old revision are unaffected until they upgrade.

### onboarding-15 — Operator-facing errors are full of internal design ids and security rationale

**Low** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 3 · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Replace the 'section 6.7' example (comments only) with internal/cli/job.go:147, internal/cli/content.go:59, internal/archive/destroy.go:73. (orig: Ran: `gvm.exe create Valheim_1 --game valheim` -> "...names are lowercase [a-z0-9-] and may not start or end with '-' (D-45)". doctor: "deletions suppressed (D-42)". Every forgot-sudo error appends two lines of threat model: "gvm deliberately does not dial /run/gvm/admin.sock: that socket is 0660 root:gvm, so a CLI that used it would hand the whole root verb surface to every member of group gvm" (transport.go:157-159). create notes reference the "section 6.7" internals.)

**Impact.** Routine mistakes like forgetting sudo or using an uppercase name produce walls of text with references to a spec the operator has never read, burying the one-line fix.

**Suggestion.** Lead every error with one actionable line, e.g. 'run as root: sudo gvm create valheim-1 --game valheim'. Remove the admin.sock rationale sentence from Resolve's default message; it stays in the code comment and the test, plus a short 'why: docs/security.md#admin-socket' link if wanted. Strip '(D-nn)' and 'section x.y' suffixes from operator-facing strings, keeping the precise rule text. Add a test that fails on a D-nn reference in Hint, Fix or error strings outside comments. No global --verbose flag.


---

<a id="cli-ergonomics"></a>
## CLI ergonomics and consistency (`cli-ergonomics`)

For an operator, gvm's CLI is honest and safety-minded underneath: typed-name confirmation, plans you can preview, refusals that say nothing was touched, and create/validate output that explains itself. The command surface around that core misleads people, though. The top-level usage lists install, update, rebuild, restore, content sync, firewall, user/token/secret/audit and --remote as if they work. In this build every one of them ends in a refusal, and the wording and exit code differ from verb to verb. Many refusals and hints send the operator to commands that do not exist (gvm instance promote, gvm manifest upgrade, gvm game approve, gvm content resolve-blocked, gvm firewall enable) or to a loop that cannot be finished (GVM_TOKEN -> gvm token create -> "requires gvmd" -> gvmd "not implemented yet"). Help is inconsistent and sometimes broken: `--help` exits 1 for most verbs, `sudo gvm create --help` and `sudo gvm logs --help` panic, and `gvm help <cmd>` prints only one line. Output regularly exposes design-doc IDs (D-45, section 16.4), Go package paths and doubled sentinel prefixes. Flag spellings (--dry/--dry-run/--diff), the way an instance is named, --json coverage and exit codes vary by command. The biggest gains come from cheap consistency work (one help/exit/error convention plus a test that every suggested `gvm ...` command exists) and from three dead ends: secrets, the manifest override approval, and destroy archive selection. None of these changes needs a weaker safety rule.

**Strengths to preserve**

- Typed-name confirmation is a single shared implementation (TypedConfirm, internal/cli/apply.go:224-277). It is used by apply, destroy, rebuild and restore, compares the typed name exactly, reads one line and has no y/n shortcut. `--no-pre-archive` demands `--confirm <name>` even when --yes is given (restore.go:131-140).
- `gvm create` says what it wrote, what it did NOT do ("no account, no directories, no unit") and the exact next command (`next: gvm apply <name> --diff`). `--dry-run` prints the YAML (create.go:439-442, 483-518).
- Variable handling helps the user. An undeclared --var is refused with a pointer to `gvm game validate`, missing required vars are listed with the manifest's help text, and a duplicate --var is refused instead of last-wins (create.go:62-80, 258-297).
- `gvm apply --diff` prints the exact plan apply would execute. It counts adds, changes and removes, says what was NOT verified (write-probe, schedules) and ends with "nothing has been applied" (diff.go:27-66). A converged apply reports nothing to do.
- `gvm game validate` is a strong tool for manifest authors: a ✓/✗ checklist, then file:line:col diagnostics, each with a why/fix line. `gvm game list` and `gvm game which` show which layer (embedded / packaged / override) wins.
- Refusals often name the next step. Lifecycle refusals offer `--force` only when the refusal can actually be overridden (start.go:112-123). destroy says "Stop it first: gvm stop X". A job that leaves a server down says it IS STOPPED and that `gvm start X` is immediate (job.go:305-308). An empty `gvm list` says how to create an instance.
- Flags can go before or after positional arguments (parseInterspersed, control.go:59-71). backup verify/prune/list accept the instance either positionally or as --instance.
- `gvm console` makes detaching explicit (Ctrl-D or :detach, the server keeps running), has --no-color and --no-follow, and validates actor claims without echoing them back.
- status and list never present an unchecked source as fine ("not checked on this host", `?` gap lines). `list --problems` exists, and --json is available on status, list, start/stop/restart and the job verbs.
- `gvm job list` answers "what runs on this box at 05:00": schedule, kind, whether the job stops the server, and its failure policy, host-level jobs included.
- Instance-name errors explain why a name is reserved (internal/instance/name.go:50-67), so an operator does not cycle through other names that are reserved for the same reason.

### cli-ergonomics-1 — Top-level usage advertises verbs this build cannot run; each refusal uses different words and exit codes, and job list calls them runnable

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **20** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Only games/projectzomboid.yaml recommends an `update` job; valheim recommends only backup. The sentinels' intent is documented at install.go:27-33 and jobengines.go:47-52. (orig: `gvm` usage lists install, update, rebuild, restore, firewall, content (sync), adopt, user/token/secret/audit with no marking. Production wiring: install.go:109-111 always ends `%w: %s declares provider %q, whose driver lands with the blob store` (ErrProviderUnbuilt). jobengines.go:152-160 (update) fails with "needs a provider context to run SteamCMD through (internal/update.Bind over steamcmd.New + jobs.NewSteamRunner)". jobengines.go:162-169 (content sync) and 171-181 (rebuild) fail with ErrContentApplyUnbuilt. jobengines.go:411-419 (update --repair). internal/jobs/execute.go:278-280 marks restore as pending ("internal/backup's restore path (section 13.2)"), and restore.go:181-183 for `--to` says "extracting into %s is internal/archive.Restore, which this build has; what it is missing is the path that drives it". content.go:1278-1285: `gvm content sync` without --dry-run says "That job is not in this build". adopt.go:23-28: cutover/rollback "not yet implemented". Ran: `gvm firewall` -> "gvm firewall: not implemented yet" exit=3. `gvm gvmd` / `gvm web` -> exit=3. Every other refusal above exits 1, because root.go:230-242 maps only ErrNotImplemented to 3. job.go:541 marks a job runnable when jobs.Lookup succeeds, and execute.go:259-268 registers update, content_sync and rebuild runners. So `gvm job list` shows the nightly update as runnable, while the update refusal itself tells the user "`gvm job list` says which kinds this build can run". README promises unbuilt commands "exit non-zero with `not implemented`".)

**Impact.** An operator following create -> apply -> install -> start stops at install with a message about "the blob store". Nightly update timers that create.go writes from recommended_jobs fail every night while job list calls them runnable. Scripts cannot tell "not available in this build" (exit 1) from a real failure (exit 1).

**Suggestion.** Add an `Available func() (bool, why string)` (or a static flag) to Command and to each subcommand table entry. Have usage, `gvm help` and `gvm job list` read it, so unbuilt entries show "(not in this build)" or are hidden. Make every unbuilt refusal (ErrProviderUnbuilt, ErrContentApplyUnbuilt, ErrRemoteUnbuilt, errRequiresDaemon, adopt cutover/rollback, jobs pending) wrap ErrNotImplemented, so it exits 3 with one standard sentence: "gvm <verb>: not available in this build; nothing was changed." Drop the Go identifiers from that sentence. Have jobRows run the same engine-availability check as buildJobEnv, so job list and job run agree. Optionally make `gvm version --features` list what this build can do.

### cli-ergonomics-2 — The remote/admin/secret path is a closed loop: every way out points at something that does not work

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 3, 4, 10 · Plan: 20 → **29** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Manual workaround: write /etc/gvm/secrets/instances/<inst>/<secret-name> (0600 root:root, layout in ARCHITECTURE.md:76-77). It is read by internal/cli/instance.go:252-257 and internal/secrets/filestore.go:139-160. ARCHITECTURE.md:5704 specifies `sudo gvm user create` in-process. (orig: Non-root error (transport.go:154-159), quoted from the run: "gvm create: gvm: no transport for this command: `gvm create` is a host operation. Run it as root (sudo gvm create), or point it at a dashboard with --remote / GVM_URL plus GVM_TOKEN (`gvm token create` mints one)." Following that advice: `GVM_URL=https://gvm.example.com GVM_TOKEN=abc gvm status valheim-1` -> "gvm: the remote transport is not built in this release ... needs the API client, which lands with the web tier" (RequireLocal, transport.go:169-178). Yet `--remote` appears in the help of about 15 verbs. `gvm token create --name x` -> "gvm token: token create: requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running (`systemctl status gvmd`)" (user.go:141-143). `gvm gvmd` -> "not implemented yet" exit=3, and packaging/systemd ships no gvmd.service. `gvm secret set --name rcon --instance x` gives the same "requires gvmd" error (secret.go:43-58), as do user list/create, audit tail and secret list/rotate/show-path. Meanwhile `gvm create` says "secrets ... not generated yet; the unit starts without them and the game gets an empty value" (create.go:511-514) without naming any command. For Valheim, `-password` is only passed `when: {secret_set: join_password}` (games/valheim.yaml), so the server comes up without a password.)

**Impact.** An operator cannot set a join password, an RCON password or a CurseForge key from the CLI at all. A friends-only server quietly runs open. A non-root user, or anyone trying the dashboard route, follows three suggested fixes in a row and none of them works. The "requires gvmd" wording also implies the CLI talks to gvmd, which hard rule 3 forbids.

**Suggestion.** 1) Implement `gvm secret set|list|show-path|rotate <instance> <name>` in-process as root (RouteHostOp) through secrets.Store. Never touch admin.sock. Take the value from a no-echo TTY prompt (x/term), from stdin when stdin is not a TTY, or from --generate (secrets.GeneratePassword). Never accept it as an argv flag. Reject ' " \ $ ` and newlines (rule 10), and run the manifest's named validators when they apply, for example valheim.password_rules (minimum 5 characters, not a substring of the server name). Never print the value; print only its length and fingerprint. Treat the secret_refs fingerprint and audit row in Postgres as best-effort and spool them when Postgres is down (rule 1). After a set, print 'takes effect at next start: gvm restart <inst>'. Restart only with an explicit --restart, and only through the normal stop ladder (rule 4). 2) Have create's secrets line name the exact command: `gvm secret set <inst> join_password`. When the secret is guarded by `when: secret_set`, say plainly that the server will run WITHOUT a password until it is set. 3) Implement `gvm audit tail|search` over the on-disk spool, optionally merged with Postgres when it is reachable. 4) Until the web tier exists, have `gvm user` and `gvm token` refuse with the standard 'not available in this build' message (exit 3). Remove the 'requires gvmd / systemctl status gvmd' wording everywhere. When the web tier lands, build them in-process as root against Postgres, as ARCHITECTURE 14.9 describes. 5) Remove the --remote/GVM_URL/`gvm token create` clause and the admin.sock paragraph from the non-root error, which leaves just 'run it as root: sudo gvm <verb>'. Hide --remote from help until RequireLocal can return anything other than ErrRemoteUnbuilt.

### cli-ergonomics-3 — --help is broken or inconsistent: exits 1, runs the command, or panics as root; `gvm help <cmd>` shows only one line

**High** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** update, rebuild, restore and ports --help exit 0 correctly. The bug is in the verbs that use the parseInterspersed + flagErr pattern and then keep running. (orig: flagErr (game.go:613-618) turns flag.ErrHelp into nil. Parsers that return `xxxOptions{}, flagErr(err)` (create.go:127-130, logs.go:60-63, apply.go:52-55, start.go:47-50, status.go:41-44, list.go:44-47, destroy.go:66-69, install.go:53-56, console.go:94-97) therefore let the command carry on with an empty name. Ran `gvm <verb> --help` for every verb: usage, then "gvm start: gvm: no transport..." exit=1. The same happens for create, install, apply, stop, restart, status, list, logs and destroy. Running as root through a `go test -overlay` probe (Geteuid=0, empty HostEnv, repo untouched), `create --help` and `logs --help` PANIC: "instance: instance: invalid name: a name is required; call ValidateName before deriving names from operator input", because PathsFor calls MustValidName (internal/instance/paths.go:176-177, create.go:395, logs.go:99). `list --help` runs the whole listing. Group commands are inconsistent: `gvm user --help` -> "gvm user: user: unknown subcommand \"--help\"" exit=1, and the same for adopt/token/secret/audit/db, while content/job/control/backup/game accept -h/--help/help. `gvm doctor --help` -> "Usage of doctor:" ... "gvm doctor: flag: help requested" exit=1. `gvm help create` -> "gvm create — define a new instance" (root.go:254-265), although usage.go:39 says 'Run "gvm help <command>" for detail.' No help screen contains an Examples section.)

**Impact.** The first thing a new operator types, `sudo gvm create --help`, prints usage and then a Go panic with a stack trace. Everywhere else a help request looks like a failure (exit 1 plus an error line). The advertised `gvm help <cmd>` gives no detail at all.

**Suggestion.** Use the errHelpShown sentinel as proposed: usage goes to stdout, exit 0. Make it structurally impossible for a parse helper to return (zero options, nil): each parser returns errHelpShown or a fully validated name, and instance.ValidateName runs before anything reaches PathsFor. Route `gvm help <cmd> [sub]` and `<group> -h|--help|help` through the same per-command usage function, using a shared subcommand-table type for every group (user, token, secret, audit, db, adopt). Add Examples blocks. Put the recover() only around non-role verbs (routeOf(verb) != RouteRole): it prints one line plus a bug-report hint, and the full stack under GVM_DEBUG=1. Leave run, run-prepare, run-shutdown and probe-write to crash normally so the journal keeps the trace and their exit codes are untouched.

### cli-ergonomics-5 — A custom game in /etc/gvm/games cannot get past `gvm apply`: no approval command, and apply has no override flag

**High** · manifest-author · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 10 · Plan: 20 → **46** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/spec/load.go:242-252 gates only OriginOverride. ARCHITECTURE.md:492 forbids editing /usr/share/gvm/games. (orig: game.go:80-85 has only two authorizers: DenyOverrides, or RootCLIAuthorizer when --unapproved-override is passed. `gvm create --unapproved-override` loads the override and pins its sha (create.go:401). The first `gvm apply` then has no manifest.lock.yaml yet, and pinnedManifest falls back to `h.catalog().Load(in.Spec.Game, overrideAuth(false))` (instance.go:532). The override is skipped and apply has no --unapproved-override flag (apply.go:48-50). The result is "... has no pinned manifest and mygame could not be loaded from the catalog either: no usable manifest: \"mygame\": every candidate was skipped", and the reason ("not approved") is dropped with the diags. The only hint, "`gvm game approve <id>` after reviewing it" (spec/load.go:249), names a command that does not exist. A manifest author also has nothing to start from: `gvm game which generic-steam` prints "WINNER embedded games/generic-steam.yaml", and no subcommand prints an embedded manifest. `gvm game validate --json` -> "flag provided but not defined: -json". Validation also reports in stages: my test manifest showed 1 error, and 7 more appeared after that one was fixed.)

**Impact.** A manifest author can validate and create, then hits a confusing apply failure with no supported way forward. Adding a new game by writing YAML is the whole promise to this persona.

**Suggestion.** 1) Immediate fix: in pinnedManifest's first-apply fallback, accept an /etc/gvm/games override when its sha256 equals the instance's recorded spec.manifest pin. Root authorised exactly those bytes at create with --unapproved-override. Give apply --unapproved-override for parity, and surface the skip reason from diags in the error. 2) Add `gvm game publish <id>` (with `approve` as an alias) as root. It shows the sha256 and a summary of argv, env, exec and destroy globs, and requires the game id to be typed. It records the approval in a root:root 0600 file that is not a *.yaml, for example /etc/gvm/games/.approvals/<id>.sha256, so the loader never reads it as a manifest and gvm-web (group gvm) can neither write nor forge it. Mirror the approval to Postgres best-effort. The template guard and password rules still run at load (rule 10). 3) Add `gvm game show <id> [--layer embedded]` to print a manifest's bytes, so authors can copy generic-steam. 4) Add `game validate --json`, and report all stages' diagnostics in one pass where earlier stages leave a parseable document.

### cli-ergonomics-6 — `gvm doctor` reports false problems on every host and recommends commands that do not exist

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 2, 7 · Plan: **24** → 40 · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/doctor/checks_host.go:13 promises a graceful skip, which contradicts HOST-001's Problem severity. checks_fw.go:48-49 reports backend:none as a Note. (orig: doctor.go:55-61 hard-codes `var liveRules firewall.RuleSet; liveActive := false; liveTrusted := false`. doctor.go:150-151: `doctorLoadInstances() []spec.Instance { return nil }`, so no instance check ever runs. Ran `gvm doctor`: "✗ FW-001 firewall backend ufw is not active / Fix: gvm firewall enable" and "✗ FW-003 ufw status numbered could not be fully parsed; deletions suppressed (D-42)". With no Postgres: "✗ HOST-001 Postgres socket not found", "3 problem(s) found", "gvm doctor: doctor: 3 problem(s)" exit=1. The doctor.go:24-28 comment promises exit 2 for flag/config errors, but a bad flag exits 1. `gvm doctor --json` -> "flag provided but not defined: -json". Only `-q` exists, as a single-dash short flag.)

**Impact.** The health check fails on a perfectly healthy box, tells the operator to run a command that does not exist (and that would conflict with the 'never enable ufw implicitly' rule if it were added carelessly), and treats an absent Postgres as a problem although rule 1 makes it optional. Operators learn to ignore doctor, which defeats its purpose.

**Suggestion.** Read the live ruleset read-only through ufw.Parse on `ufw status numbered` (or the nft parser for that backend). Feed the parser's real 'fully parsed' flag into FW-003. If ufw is missing or the read fails, report FW-001 through FW-006 as Skip ('could not read the firewall: <reason>'), never as Problem. FW-001 Fix: when SSH ports were detected, print `ufw allow <port>/tcp` for every detected port, then `ufw enable`. When none were detected, print 'set spec.firewall.ssh.ports in /etc/gvm/gvm.yaml before enabling ufw'. FW-005/FW-006 Fix: name the offending rule and say whether it carries a gvm: tag. Suggest deletion only for gvm-tagged rules, and never when the parse is untrusted. Load instances through lifecycle.List and pinnedManifest, and skip an instance (with a note) when it does not load. Downgrade a missing Postgres to a Note ('dashboard and audit-database features unavailable'). Make it a Problem only when the host config says the dashboard is enabled or gvmd is installed. Add --json and the documented exit codes, coordinated with cli-ergonomics-13.

### cli-ergonomics-4 — Hints and Fix lines name commands that do not exist

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5, 7, 11 · Plan: **20** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm firewall` is registered at root.go:152 (notImplemented, exit 3). Also internal/update/pipeline.go:410 and provider/provider.go:280,290 (`gvm instance promote`). IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN Phase 15 planned internal/cli/manifest.go, which does not exist. (orig: None of these commands is registered (root.go:98-155, and the content/game/backup subcommand tables). `gvm manifest upgrade <name> --dry` is the Hint `gvm status` shows for a pin mismatch (lifecycle/derive.go:168; also lifecycle/verbs.go:206 and content/lock.go:245). `gvm instance promote` appears in update.go:148, provider/steamcmd/steamcmd.go:333 and 389, provider/minecraft/provider.go:93, provider/adopted/adopted.go:72, spec/capability.go:490 and update/repair.go:129. `gvm game approve` is in spec/load.go:249. `gvm game diff` is in restore.go:244; running `gvm game diff valheim` gives "gvm game: unknown subcommand \"diff\"". `gvm content resolve-blocked` is in content_blocked.go:127 ("Put them in /var/lib/gvm/downloads/mods, then: gvm content resolve-blocked X"), curseforge/blocked.go:128 and ladder.go:217. `gvm content promote` is in content.go:1653, content/lock.go:509 and content/source.go:502. `gvm content import` is in content/source.go:442 and steamworkshop/source.go:497. `gvm firewall enable|apply` is in doctor/checks_fw.go:56, 82, 147 and 176. `gvm disable` is in lifecycle/derive.go:533. `gvm secret rotate-key` is in secrets/filestore.go:394.)

**Impact.** The hint is shown exactly when the operator is stuck: a failed instance, an adopted server that needs promoting, a blocked CurseForge mod. The one suggested fix fails with "unknown command", and there is no fallback procedure.

**Suggestion.** Add the test exactly as proposed. It should scan non-comment string literals under internal/ for `gvm <verb> [<sub>]` and resolve them against the dispatch and subcommand tables, with an explicit allowlist of known-unbuilt targets that must appear together with the shared 'not available in this build' phrase. For each dangling hint, choose the replacement by safety: (a) Pin mismatch: show both sha256 values (instance YAML pin vs <name>.d/manifest.lock.yaml) and say 'if the YAML pin was edited by hand, restore it; upgrading a pin is not available in this build'. Never suggest re-creating. (b) `instance promote` and content promote/import: say 'not available in this build'. Do NOT print a YAML edit that flips managed_install or the content ownership, because that circumvents adoption and the content-ownership gates. (c) Firewall: print manual ufw lines only in the order `ufw allow <each detected ssh port>/tcp` then `ufw enable`, and only when SSH ports were detected. Otherwise say 'set spec.firewall.ssh.ports first'. (d) resolve-blocked: tell the operator to put the files in the drop-box and run `gvm content sync <inst> --dry-run` to see them picked up, if that path actually rescans; otherwise say 'not available'. (e) `gvm disable`: point to editing enabled: false and running `gvm apply`.

### cli-ergonomics-7 — destroy makes the operator find an archive path by hand and accepts any old archive without showing its age or class

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: 21 → **38** · Status: open

**Evidence.** backup.go:404-419 prints ARCHIVE, CLASS, SIZE, TAKEN (CreatedAt), VERIFIED and REASON. archiveRow.Dir is emitted in --json (backup.go:303). (orig: The root.go:126 summary says "remove an instance, archiving it first", but destroy.go:76-80 *requires* `--archive <path>` ("--archive is required ... `gvm backup <instance>` writes one") and archives nothing itself. verifiedArchive accepts any verified archive of the instance, whatever its age or class (destroy.go:264-299). The preflight prints `%s, verified %s` using ref.VerifiedAt, which is now (destroy.go:361-362), not CreatedAt or Class. `gvm backup list` prints only the bare file name in the ARCHIVE column (backup.go:419), so the operator has to know the backup directory to build a path. `gvm restore` additionally refuses relative paths (restore.go:209-212), while destroy accepts them (filepath.Abs, destroy.go:265). Archive-taking verbs also disagree on argument order: `gvm restore <archive> --instance <name>` versus `gvm destroy <instance> --archive <path>`.)

**Impact.** Destroying with last week's nightly archive silently drops a week of play. The preflight reads as freshly verified and the typed confirmation does not mention age. A non-preserve archive used to authorise a destroy may later be pruned. The "archive first" promise in the summary is not what happens.

**Suggestion.** `gvm destroy <inst>` with no --archive does the following: it checks that the instance is stopped (as today), checks free space for the archive, writes a fresh `preserve` archive through the archive engine WITHOUT running retention, verifies it (spot verify, as today), and then shows the preflight and asks for the typed name exactly as now. `--archive <path>` stays available. Accept `--archive latest` only together with a preflight line such as 'taken 2026-10-07 04:00Z (7 days ago), class nightly'. Show a WARNING, repeated just above the typed-name prompt, when CreatedAt is older than the newest mtime under the world paths. Non-preserve archives that authorised a destroy should be marked or reclassified as preserve so retention can never remove them. Print CreatedAt and Class in place of VerifiedAt. Show full paths or a DIR header in `gvm backup list`. For restore, resolve a relative path in the CLI (filepath.Abs in the operator's cwd) before handing it to the job, rather than refusing it. Defer the argument-order change until restore is built.

### cli-ergonomics-8 — User-facing text leaks design-doc IDs, Go identifiers, doubled sentinel prefixes and raw slog lines

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/db.go:19 sets dbConnTimeout = 15s, so retries print at 1s, 2s, 4s and 8s (4 lines). (orig: Help screens: update "(D-43)" and "(D-32)" (update.go:47,56); rebuild/restore --confirm "(section 16.4)" (job.go:147); job usage "(D-45)" (job.go:89); content diff/blocked "(section 12.9)" and "(section 12.8)" (content.go:59,68); restore --to "skips steps 2-7"; status --json "print lifecycle.Status as JSON" (status.go:38). Errors: `gvm status Valheim_1` -> "gvm status: instance: invalid name: \"Valheim_1\"; names are lowercase [a-z0-9-] ... (D-45)". Doubled prefixes: "gvm create: gvm: no transport...", "gvm doctor: doctor: 3 problem(s)", "gvm user: user: unknown subcommand", "gvm db: db status: store: datastore unavailable" (sentinels such as transport.go:93 and apply.go:236 carry their own "gvm:"/"pkg:" prefix, and Main adds another). Go plumbing in messages: jobengines.go:158 and 416 ("internal/update.Bind over steamcmd.New + jobs.NewSteamRunner"), restore.go:181 ("internal/archive.Restore"), execute.go:279. The non-root error carries a second paragraph of security rationale about admin.sock (transport.go:157-158). `gvm db status` with Postgres down prints five raw lines like `WARN store: pool ping failed, retrying err="failed to connect to `user=gvm_ops database=gvm`: ... dial error ..." delay=8s` over about 30s before failing. There are more than 40 other D-/section references in user-facing strings across internal/jobs, reconcile and spec (grep).)

**Impact.** An operator who has never seen ARCHITECTURE.md cannot act on "D-45" or "section 12.8". Doubled prefixes and Go names make every error look like a crash. A DB check that looks hung for 30 seconds and then dumps log lines looks broken.

**Suggestion.** Adopt one error shape: `gvm <verb>: <what happened>. <what to do next>`. Remove package prefixes from sentinels, or strip them in Main with a userError{Msg, Hint, Ref} type. Move D-/section references to a trailing `(more: gvm help <topic>)` or show them only under `GVM_DEBUG=1`/`--verbose`. Print the admin.sock rationale only in docs. For `gvm db *`, print one line ('waiting up to 15s for Postgres at /var/run/postgresql...') and silence the store's slog retries on an interactive terminal.

### cli-ergonomics-9 — Flag spellings, instance designation and --json coverage differ per command; help renders flags misleadingly

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** secret.go:46-47: --instance is the instance and --name is the secret. instance/name.go:61 already reserves 'verify'. (orig: Preview flag spelled three ways: `--dry` (destroy.go:63, rebuild.go:53), `--dry-run` (create.go:124, update.go:57, content sync), `--diff` (apply.go:48, update.go:51). Instance designation varies: positional (most verbs), `--instance` (restore.go:92, secret *, job list job.go:489), `--name` (adopt finalize adopt.go:66, secret set), or a slug (`gvm job run <instance>_<job>`). --json exists on status/list/start/backup/job/content but not on ports, logs, doctor, game validate/list/which, apply --diff, install, user/token/secret. Go's flag backtick rule renders booleans as if they take values: `-game gvm game list` (create.go:114), `-repair validate` (update.go:55), `-no-boot gvm adopt` (content sync). Help lists flags single-dashed (`-dry-run`) while usage lines say `--dry-run`. Job-plumbing flags appear on every operator verb (job.go:129-141): `-trigger ... timer | cli | web | api | guard | chained`, `-lease-for`, `-on-failure`. `--trigger timer` even changes the audited actor to timer (job.go:206-213). Admin commands use Go's default usage ("Usage of user list:"). `gvm backup <instance>` shares its namespace with subcommands, and 'list'/'prune' are not reserved names (name.go:50-67), so an instance named `list` cannot be backed up by name.)

**Impact.** Operators have to look up each command's spelling. Help output suggests `--repair` takes an argument. Internal knobs clutter every help screen and let a human mislabel an audit row. Scripting needs a different parse per command.

**Suggestion.** Write the conventions table and enforce it through shared helpers, as proposed. Use --dry-run everywhere, with --dry as a hidden alias. Keep `apply --diff` visible as the documented preview spelling, and accept --dry-run as its synonym. The instance is the first positional, with --instance/--name as hidden aliases. --json goes on every read verb. Use a custom flag printer that renders `--flag VALUE`, does no backtick extraction, and hides flags tagged internal. Move --trigger, --lease-for and --on-failure onto `gvm job run` only, and hide them there. On job run, derive the default trigger from the environment instead of hard-coding timer: TriggerTimer only when systemd's INVOCATION_ID is set and the unit is gvm-job@<slug>, otherwise TriggerCLI. Refuse an explicit `--trigger timer` from an interactive TTY so the audit actor cannot be mislabelled by hand. Add list and prune to the reserved instance names for new creates. Also accept `gvm backup run <instance>`, so an existing instance with such a name can still be backed up.

### cli-ergonomics-10 — `--budget` on stop/restart is accepted, ignored, and still written to the audit row; on start it is meaningless

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: **34** · Status: open

**Evidence.** job.go:127-128 (jobOptions.bind --budget) and job.go:443 (Shutdown: h.shutdown()) extend the ignored flag to job-driving verbs. (orig: start.go:43 registers `-budget duration  bound the shutdown ladder (default: the instance's shutdown.budget)` for start, stop and restart. stop.go:60-65: "The budget is NOT sent ... A caller who wants a different budget edits the spec and applies it. `_ = budget`". lifecycle/manager.go:256-258 still records `params["budget_sec"] = int(req.Budget.Seconds())` in the instance.stop audit row. The --reason help on start says "(for a stop) in the hold file", because the three verbs share one flag set.)

**Impact.** `gvm stop pz-main --budget 30s` still waits the spec's full 180s ladder, and the audit log claims a 30s budget was used. An operator in a hurry learns the flag does nothing only by watching the clock.

**Suggestion.** Remove --budget from start/stop/restart. Or, if it is passed, refuse with: "the shutdown budget comes from spec.shutdown.budget; edit /etc/gvm/instances/<name>.yaml and run gvm apply". Never audit a parameter that was not used. Give start its own usage text without stop-only wording.

### cli-ergonomics-11 — Next-step guidance stops after create, and there is no quickstart

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: 26 → **34** · Status: open

**Evidence.** create ends with `next: gvm apply <name> --diff` (create.go:517). `gvm apply --diff` ends with "nothing has been applied." (diff.go:65) and does not suggest `gvm apply <name>`. `gvm apply` ends with "N changed, M already correct." (diff.go:69-76) and does not suggest install or start. Install's preflight output gives no start hint. README.md has build instructions and design notes but no 'first server in 5 commands' walkthrough, and docs/ contains only the architecture spec, the plan, bug-sweep reports and research. Meanwhile lifecycle.Derive already computes a Hint per phase (status.go:186-188).

**Impact.** A first-time operator has to infer the create -> apply -> install -> secret -> start order from the architecture spec. Each step looks finished without saying what comes next.

**Suggestion.** End apply, install, start, update, restore and content sync with one `next:` line taken from the post-action lifecycle Hint. Filter that line through the same availability registry as cli-ergonomics-1: when the next step is unbuilt, print 'next step (<verb>) is not available in this build' instead of the command. Make `apply --diff` end with 'run `gvm apply <name>` to make these changes'. Insert the secret step (`gvm secret set <inst> join_password`) between apply and start whenever a `when: secret_set` guard would otherwise run the server without a password. Write docs/QUICKSTART.md covering create, apply, install, secret set, start, connect (cli-ergonomics-12), backup and logs, and add a test that extracts every `gvm ...` line from it and resolves it against the dispatch tables (the same checker as cli-ergonomics-4). Mark steps unavailable in the current build explicitly in the document, and point to it from the banner.

### cli-ergonomics-12 — No command tells you how to connect to a server (address, port, whether a password is set)

**Medium** · player · missing-feature · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · Plan: **33** · Status: open

**Evidence.** create.go:491-502 prints the ports at creation. ports.go:233-248 with occupancy.go:110 labels each endpoint gvm@<inst>:<portname>. (orig: The status Report (lifecycle/status.go:180-234) prints phase, unit, pid, ready time, players, lease and drift, but no ports. The list Line (status.go:136-146) prints name, phase, game and a short detail. `gvm ports <inst>` prints the whole host endpoint table and then 'its materialised allocation still holds' (ports.go:233-285). No manifest or host-config field marks the join port or the public address (grep for join/connect/advertise in spec, lifecycle and cli finds nothing). Valheim's game port is only discoverable by reading the instance YAML (spec.ports.game).)

**Impact.** To answer a friend's question 'what do I type to join?', the operator has to cat YAML and work out the VPS IP. They also cannot see whether the join password is actually set (see cli-ergonomics-2).

**Suggestion.** Add a declarative, control-flow-free manifest field marking the join port and an optional player note, for example `ports.game.join: true` and `connect.note: "Steam server browser: search by name"`. Add an optional `spec.public_address` to gvm.yaml. When it is unset, fall back to the first global-unicast address on local interfaces (no network calls), and label RFC1918/CGNAT addresses 'private: players outside this network need the VPS's public address'. In `gvm status <inst>`, print a connect block with the join endpoint first and then the other expose:public ports as addr:port/proto. Add 'firewall: admitted / NOT admitted / unknown' per port from the live ruleset when it is readable, and 'join password: set / NOT SET' via Store.Has, never reading or printing the value. Add `endpoints` to --json, add a JOIN column to `gvm list`, and make `gvm ports <inst>` filter to that instance.

### cli-ergonomics-13 — Exit codes are undocumented and inconsistent; 'problems found' usually exits 0

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4, 6 · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** root.go:194-243: no args or an unknown command -> 2, ErrNotImplemented -> 3, everything else -> 1. That includes usage errors (`gvm stop a b` -> exit 1), unbuilt features (ErrProviderUnbuilt etc. -> 1) and `gvm game diff` (unknown subcommand -> 1, while an unknown top-level command gives 2). The doctor.go:24-28 comment documents exit 2 for flag/config errors, but `gvm doctor --json` exits 1. `gvm list --problems` (list.go:81-88) and `gvm ports <inst>` with conflicts (ports.go:229) exit 0. `gvm db check-grants` prints "SKIP: unexpected error" and returns nil (db.go:185-186). The only exit-code table (ARCHITECTURE section 7.8) covers `gvm run`.

**Impact.** Cron jobs and monitoring scripts cannot reliably detect unhealthy instances or conflicts, and cannot tell a typo from a failure or an unbuilt feature.

**Suggestion.** Adopt the table for operator verbs only: 0 ok, 1 failed, 2 usage (all flag, argument and unknown-subcommand errors, including inside groups), 3 not available in this build, 4 checked and found problems (doctor, list --problems, ports with conflicts, adopt discover findings, db check-grants inconclusive), 5 refused or not confirmed (ErrNotConfirmed, lifecycle refusals). Apply it in Main only when routeOf(verb) != RouteRole. Role commands keep returning their own ExitError codes unchanged. Add a test asserting that `gvm run`'s 64/65/66/75/78 mapping is untouched and that gvm@.service's RestartPreventExitStatus still matches it. Document the table in `gvm help exit-codes` and the README. Note in release notes that `gvm doctor` and `list --problems` exit codes change, since cron scripts may depend on them.

### cli-ergonomics-14 — Small discoverability and terminal gaps: no completion, no typo suggestions, no NO_COLOR/TTY detection, logs cannot follow

**Low** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 2 · Plan: 22 → **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** console.go:91 already provides --no-color, so only automatic NO_COLOR/TTY detection is missing. (orig: `gvm stauts` prints "gvm: unknown command \"stauts\"" plus the full 50-line usage to stderr, with no suggestion (root.go:208-213). There is no completion subcommand anywhere (grep for completion in internal/cli finds nothing). console.go:100 sets ANSI=!noColor with no isatty or NO_COLOR check (grep finds no IsTerminal or NO_COLOR in the tree), so `gvm console x --no-follow > file` embeds escape codes. `gvm logs` has no -f, reads the whole file into memory (logs.go:100) and wraps the output in header and footer lines on stdout (logs.go:109,113), which spoils `| grep`. Job results print raw keys such as `stall_ms` and `settle_waited_ms` (job.go:295-303). `gvm content list` with no instance prints no usage, unlike the other verbs.)

**Impact.** This is everyday friction: retyping long subcommands, escape codes in saved logs, and no quick way to tail the console log without attaching.

**Suggestion.** Add `gvm completion bash|zsh|fish`. It is generated from the commands and subcommand tables and prints static script text (no shell is spawned, so rule 2 holds). Suggest the nearest command by edit distance instead of the full usage. Honour NO_COLOR and disable ANSI when stdout is not a TTY. Add `gvm logs -f` (tail the file), send logs decoration to stderr, and render job summary keys as human labels in non-JSON output.


---

<a id="day2-ops"></a>
## Day-2 operations and visibility (`day2-ops`)

For week-to-week running, gvm handles the low-level work carefully. It has one phase table with a reason and a hint, `gvm stop` reports which shutdown rung it ended on, the console makes detaching obvious, a running guard and a 5-minute guard timer protect the servers, and backup/verify/prune work with nothing but the filesystem. But several core day-2 journeys are not wired in this build. Update (including the read-only `--diff`), repair, rebuild, install and restore all refuse at run time, and scheduled `action: update` timers would fail every night while `gvm job list` shows them as runnable. Visibility is the second big gap. `gvm status` and `gvm list` show phase and pid, but not players (the poll is never wired), uptime in readable units, installed build, update available, last backup result, next scheduled job, disk usage or a join address. Last night's job results are only in journalctl, because `gvm audit tail` is a stub that says it "requires gvmd". Several hints and docs send the operator to commands that do not exist (`gvm enable`, `gvm disable`, `gvm manifest upgrade`, `gvm instance promote`, bare `gvm status`). The dashboard's start/stop/restart endpoints return 202 "accepted" and do nothing. Smaller rough edges: `gvm start` and `gvm restart` wait silently for up to an hour, `gvm stop` cannot stop a crash-looping unit, there is no warning to players before a restart, and `--help` runs the command after printing usage.

**Strengths to preserve**

- One phase table (lifecycle.Derive) feeds status, list and doctor, so they cannot tell different stories; every verdict separates Reason (what was observed) from Hint (what to run).
- `gvm status` prints what it could NOT establish ('?  source: detail') instead of letting an unreadable source look like 'fine' (status.go:91-99), and it says 'not checked on this host' when systemd did not answer instead of reporting the server as stopped.
- `gvm stop` reports the shutdown rung and a DEGRADED line when SIGKILL was needed (start.go:139-147); `gvm logs --shutdown` shows that history and survives a torn line (logs.go:163-190).
- Console UX is careful: the banner and exit notice both say Ctrl-D/:detach leaves the server running; there is a backlog, --no-follow and --timestamps, and a clear 'X is not running: there is no console socket' message (console.go:614-619).
- Job runs print a step-by-step report with skip reasons and a loud 'X IS STOPPED ... `gvm start X` is immediate' tail (job.go:294-309); the running guard and gvm-guard.timer turn a crashed nightly into at most 5 minutes of downtime.
- The hold semantics mean the guard never fights an operator's deliberate stop, and the held reason says who stopped it and why (derive.go:376-385, manager.go:302-312).
