- `gvm backup list` / `restore --list` work without Postgres (index plus a peek of each archive's first member), total the preserve archives and list UNREADABLE archives (backup.go:397-440); the backup refuses before freezing when the disk is short (backup/engine.go:477-504).
- Refusals offer --force only when forcing is safe (start.go:112-123); `gvm job clear` names the lease holder and refuses a LIVE lease unless --force is given (job.go:696-705); a stale lease yields 'failed' with a hint and is never auto-resumed.
- Every read verb has --json, with keys that match the database columns, so scripts and the dashboard share one vocabulary.

### day2-ops-1 — Update, repair, rebuild, install and content-sync jobs are not wired in production; even the read-only `gvm update --diff` and `gvm rebuild --dry` refuse

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 12, 2, 6 · Plan: 31 → 38 → 44 → **45** · Status: open

**Evidence.** jobengines.go:1-23 (header comment says outright that update/content_sync/rebuild are unbuilt); printJobRows header at job.go:621 has no runnable column, so Runnable=true is invisible as well as wrong. (orig: internal/cli/jobengines.go:152-160 (JobUpdate returns ErrProviderUnbuilt unless h.Jobs.Updates is set), :162-169 (content_sync → ErrContentApplyUnbuilt), :171-181 (rebuild), :411-419 (update --repair); internal/cli/install.go:109-111 refuses every install. Production never sets these seams: `grep -rn 'Jobs.Updates =|Jobs.Rebuilds =|Jobs.Repair ='` matches only job_test.go:339,392,749-752, and `grep -rln internal/provider` shows no importer in internal/cli or cmd (only content, hook, jobs, update), although internal/provider/{steamcmd,minecraft} and internal/update.Pipeline exist. job_test.go:761-762 skips these kinds as a 'tracked gap'. `gvm update <n> --diff` is documented as 'touches nothing' (update.go:51-53), but runUpdateDiff calls buildJobEnv (update.go:102) → h.attachEngines (job.go:391) → the same refusal. The same happens to `gvm rebuild --dry` (rebuild.go:53-54). The error tells the operator '`gvm job list` says which kinds this build can run' (jobengines.go:159), yet jobRows marks a kind runnable from jobs.Lookup alone (job.go:539-548; execute.go:259-268 registers updateRunner), so a nightly `action: update` job is listed as fine and its timer fails every night. Error text is written for developers: 'internal/update.Bind over steamcmd.New + jobs.NewSteamRunner'.)

**Impact.** The operator cannot update a Steam or Minecraft server, repair an install, or rebuild a world with gvm, and cannot even preview what an update would do. A scheduled update looks healthy in `gvm job list` and fails silently in the journal every night.

**Suggestion.** 1) Production HostEnv builds h.Jobs.Updates/Repair/Rebuilds/Contents from the existing pieces: update.Bind(steamcmd.New(steamcmd.WithRunner(jobs.NewSteamRunner(...))), ctx, progress) for Steam, and the minecraft providers with a provider.Ctx backed by internal/content/blobstore, so Apply only copies digest-verified blobs (rule 12). runInstall uses the same constructor. 2) Give each runner a static StepNames list that does not dereference the engine, so `update --diff` and `rebuild --dry` work even on a build without engines. 3) Add `gvm update <n> --check`: it runs only Preflight + check_build/check_content (Plan = network reads), takes neither the lease nor the lock, and prints installed build, remote build and update_available. 4) Until a kind is wired, compute jobRow.Runnable in internal/cli with a side-effect-free canAttach(kind) that shares code with attachEngines, and show 'cannot run in this build' in `gvm job list` and `gvm apply --diff`. 5) Rewrite the refusals for operators, for example: 'gvm cannot install game updates in this release; nothing was stopped or changed. Track: <doc link>'. Keep the package names in the --json/debug output only.

### day2-ops-2 — Restore is not built at all, yet `gvm restore --list` advertises it and the preflight describes steps that never run

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: **38** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/jobs/execute.go:278-280: `pending = {JobRestore: "internal/backup's restore path (section 13.2)"}`. internal/cli/restore.go:171 prints the full preflight ('step 2 the current world is archived as `preserve`...', :295-296) and then refuses. Even the inspection path `--to <dir>`, which touches no world, refuses (restore.go:176-184), although internal/archive.Restore exists (internal/archive/restore.go:107). Yet restore.go:331-332 prints 'gvm restore <archive> --instance <name> puts one back. The current world is archived first.' `--list` prints bare filenames (backup.go:419) while absArchive rejects anything that is not an absolute path (restore.go:205-213), so a pasted filename fails even once restore exists.

**Impact.** After a griefing, a corrupted world or a bad mod update, the operator's only recovery path is hand-extracting a tarball as root (stop, extract, chown), which is exactly the error-prone procedure gvm exists to replace. The tool points them at a command that cannot work.

**Suggestion.** S: Wire `gvm restore <archive> --to <dir>` to archive.Restore. Refuse a --to that is, contains, or sits inside any instance's install, data, backup or runtime path (resolve symlinks first). Require the directory to be absent or empty. Leave ownership as root unless --preserve-ownership is given. Change the `--list` footer and `gvm backup` help to say that restoring over a world is not in this release, and show the `--to` command. Make --list print absolute paths. Accept a bare filename only together with --instance, resolved strictly inside that instance's spec.backup.dir (reject anything containing a path separator or '..'). M: Build the restore runner from jobs and archive in this order: typed --confirm <name>, a verified `preserve` pre-archive, a stop through the ladder, the digest-checked extract, the chown, then start. Drop `--no-pre-archive`, or make it a hard refusal, so that a restore over a world always archives first (rule 5).

### day2-ops-3 — No way to answer 'did last night's backup or update work?' from gvm: no job history, `gvm audit` is a stub, and the guard's breadcrumb is never shown

**High** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 2, 6 · Plan: **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/instance.go:275-281 (h.Audit = productionAudit()), :306-321 (audit.OpenSpool(path.Join(instance.StateRoot, "spool"), bootID)); internal/jobs/execute.go:423-432 (AuditParams: kind, left_stopped, unit, run_as) written via span.Done at execute.go:831. (orig: Runner.finish (internal/jobs/execute.go:814-842) sends the run record only to the audit span and slog (journal); nothing is written under /var/lib/gvm. `gvm job list` columns are SLUG KIND STOPS ON FAILURE SCHEDULE (job.go:621), with no last run, result or next run, yet docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:6905-6906 tells the operator to run `sudo gvm job list --instance pz-main` and 'Expect state=skipped'. `gvm audit tail` → 'gvm audit: audit tail: requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running (`systemctl status gvmd`)' (auditcmd.go:33-46, user.go:141-142), while `gvm gvmd` → 'not implemented yet' (exit 3); its own doc comment says the spool works without Postgres. The observer reads /run/gvm/<n>/hold (observe.go:432-434) but never the `degraded` breadcrumb a failed job leaves (only jobs/sweep.go:234-248 reads it), so `gvm status` never says 'a job left this stopped at 05:03; the guard recovered it'. The only left_stopped check in doctor (checks_host_db.go:70) needs Postgres. gvm-job@.service has no OnFailure=.)

**Impact.** An operator running servers for friends finds out a backup has been failing for a week only when they need it. Checking means piecing together `journalctl -u gvm-job@...` across several units.

**Suggestion.** Write /var/lib/gvm/instances/<n>/jobs/<job>.json through hostops twice per run. At start: {state:running, started_at, unit, pid, boot_id}. In finish: state, ended_at, took, summary keys, error, left_stopped, degraded. Keep a bounded ring (for example the last 20 runs) in the same file, written atomically. A reader that finds 'running' from an earlier boot, or a dead PID, reports 'did not finish (killed or timed out); see journalctl -u <unit>'. Add LAST RUN / RESULT / NEXT to `gvm job list`. NEXT comes from the timer's NextElapseUSecRealtime via read-only D-Bus. Add `gvm job history <slug>`. Implement `gvm audit tail` (and search) by reading /var/lib/gvm/spool/*.ndjson directly, with no gvmd and no Postgres. Read the degraded breadcrumb into a display-only Status field that Derive never reads, and print it in `gvm status`. Leave OnFailure= notification (a Go `gvm notify` role, webhook URL operator-supplied in host config, payload without secrets) as a later, optional item.

### day2-ops-5 — Hints and docs send the operator to commands that do not exist

**High** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: 20 → **39** · Status: open

**Evidence.** More dead references: internal/jobs/runner_guard.go:30 and internal/lifecycle/verbs.go:16 describe `gvm disable` as the durable stop; spec/validate.go:350 WithFix names `gvm manifest upgrade`. (orig: lifecycle/derive.go:400 gives every disabled instance the hint 'gvm enable <n>'. derive.go:533 appends '; a reboot clears it, `gvm disable` does not' to every held instance. derive.go:168 and verbs.go:206 hint 'gvm manifest upgrade <n> --dry' for a pin mismatch (also content/lock.go:245). update.go:148, update/pipeline.go:410, update/repair.go:129 and the providers name '`gvm instance promote`'. phase.go:162 and verbs.go:73-75 describe `gvm start --wait`. Ran the binary: `gvm enable x` → 'gvm: unknown command "enable"' (same for disable, manifest, instance); `gvm start x --wait` → 'flag provided but not defined: -wait'. The Postgres-down page (web/handlers.go:67) and docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:26,77 recommend bare `sudo gvm status`, which gives 'gvm status: name an instance' (exit 1). docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:6912 tells the operator to run `sudo gvm instance promote pz-main`.)

**Impact.** The design promises that a failed or stopped phase always names the command that fixes it. Following that hint is the operator's main path out of trouble, so a dead hint leaves them stuck, often on a failed server.

**Suggestion.** Now: rewrite each dead hint to name a path that exists. Disabled: 'set enabled: true in /etc/gvm/instances/<n>.yaml, then: sudo gvm apply <n>'. Held: 'a reboot clears it; for a durable stop set enabled: false and gvm apply'. Pin mismatch, promote and --wait: name the real procedure, or say 'not available in this release'. Make bare `gvm status` run the `gvm list` view. Add a test that scans every Go string literal (not just Hint/Fix fields) for a backticked or leading `gvm <word> ...`, checks <word> (and its subcommand) against the dispatch table, and checks every --flag against that command's FlagSet. Next: implement `gvm enable|disable <n>` as a thin CLI over internal/reconcile's intent writer (edit the field, then apply), and point the hints back at them.

### day2-ops-6 — Dashboard start/stop/restart return 202 'accepted' and do nothing; the dashboard role is not runnable

**High** · dashboard-user · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 9, 1, 3 · Plan: 47 → **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/auth/auth.go:142-149 (cookie without bearer gets 401 on /api/v1); internal/web/handlers.go:293-298 (forms POST to /api/v1) and :318 (EventSource to /api/v1). The fix has to give the browser a non-/api/v1 action path through gvm-web's session flow (rule 9), not just wire the handlers. (orig: internal/cli/root.go:101-102 route `gvm web` and `gvm gvmd` to notImplemented. Ran: `gvm web` → 'gvm web: not implemented yet' (exit 3). No package imports internal/web (grep for the import path finds nothing). internal/web/handlers.go:224-251: handleAPIStart/Stop/Restart validate CSRF, then `writeJSON(w, 202, {"action":"stop","status":"accepted"})` without calling anything. The detail page posts plain HTML forms to those JSON endpoints (handlers.go:293-298), so a click shows raw JSON. There are no routes for logs, backup, update or job status (routes.go:49-58); InstanceSummary is only Name/Status/Game (server.go:31-35).)

**Impact.** A friend or moderator without SSH cannot start, stop, restart or check anything. Once the server is wired, the stub handlers would report success for actions that never happened, which is worse than an error.

**Suggestion.** Now: the three /api/v1 handlers return 501 with an operator sentence ('not available in this release; ask the operator to run sudo gvm restart <n>'), and the detail page hides the buttons. When wiring: add session-authenticated HTML routes on gvm-web, not under /api/v1 (POST /instances/{name}/{start|stop|restart|backup}, CSRF-checked, authz-checked per role). gvm-web forwards to gvmd, which runs lifecycle.Manager and returns 202 with an operation id at once. The page polls a status endpoint showing before/after phase, the ladder rung, degraded, and the refusal reason and hint. Offer --force only for refusals marked overridable and only to roles granted force. 'Backup now' starts gvm-job@<n>_<backupjob>.service through systemd, the same unit the timer uses, so the run survives gvmd and gvm-web dying (rule 1). /api/v1 stays bearer-only (rule 9). The CLI never dials admin.sock (rule 3).

### day2-ops-4 — `gvm status` and `gvm list` omit most at-a-glance facts: uptime, installed build, update available, last backup, next job, disk, join address

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6, 10 · Plan: **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm ports [instance]` already prints 'every endpoint this host owes and who owes it', which covers part of the join-address need. (orig: lifecycle/status.go:180-234 (Report) prints only phase, reason, hint, unit, pid, 'ready <RFC3339 UTC>', players, lease, drift and findings. The `gvm list` row (status.go:136-177) is name, phase, game, then 'N players, pid N' or the reason, with no header row. Uptime appears only as a raw Go duration in the reason, e.g. 'ready since 171h22m5s ago' (derive.go:469-470, 536-548). Every missing fact is already on disk or in systemd: the newest archive and its VerifiedAt are in the archive index (backup.go:343-360); buildid is in the steamcmd appmanifest (provider/steamcmd/acf.go); timers carry the next elapse; hostops already has VerbDiskUsage (hostops/verbs.go:232-233), which only destroy uses (destroy.go:134); the dashboard model even has Addr/Port (web/server.go:38-44). None is read by lifecycle.Observe (observe.go:160-216).)

**Impact.** The core weekly question ('is everything fine, when did it last back up, is it out of date, will the disk fill?') needs `gvm status`, `gvm backup list`, `systemctl list-timers`, `df` and journalctl. The operator also has no single line to paste to players.

**Suggestion.** Add a separate, display-only `lifecycle.Details` (or `cli.Facts`). It is gathered only by `gvm status`, `gvm list` and the dashboard model, never by Manager, the guard or Derive, and every field is best-effort: a read error becomes an 'unknown' marker and never changes the phase. Fields: up (from the status.json StartedAt, rendered '7d3h'); build (appmanifest buildid, or the provider's recorded version); last backup (newest index entry with time, zone, size and verified flag); next backup/next job (timer NextElapse via read-only D-Bus); disk free on the backup and data filesystems (statfs); join line built from expose:public ports plus an address from host config, falling back to the primary interface address, never an external lookup, with 'password set' or 'no password' and never the value. Add a header row and UPTIME / LAST BACKUP columns to `gvm list`, and use the same struct in --json and the web model.

### day2-ops-7 — `gvm stop` needs a live runner: on a crash-looping or already-stopped instance it writes the hold, errors, and never stops the unit

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: **28** · Status: open

**Evidence.** lifecycle/manager.go:228-245 writes the hold, then calls m.Shutdown; unit.stop (manager.go:263-267) is reached only if the ladder call succeeds. The production ladder (cli/stop.go:49-58) dials /run/gvm/<n>/console.sock and returns dialError ('pz-main is not running: there is no console socket at ...', console.go:617-618) when no runner is listening. Allowed never treats an already-stopped instance specially for stop (verbs.go:190-193). gvm@.service:54-55 has Restart=on-failure, RestartSec=20, so in a crash loop the runner is absent for 20 s of each cycle and `gvm stop` fails without cancelling the pending restart. No test covers stop-on-stopped (lifecycle/manager_test.go has none).

**Impact.** The moment an operator most needs `gvm stop` (a server crash-looping on a bad mod) it can fail, while leaving a hold behind. A second `gvm stop` on a stopped server exits 1. They end up running `systemctl stop` by hand.

**Suggestion.** In Manager.execute, for VerbStop, read ActiveState, SubState and MainPID before calling the ladder. If SubState is auto-restart, dead or failed and MainPID == 0, skip the console ladder and issue hostops unit.stop, which cancels a pending restart. Record rung=`not-running` in audit, re-write the hold, and print '<n> was not running; it is now held stopped (a reboot clears the hold)'. In every other state, including activating/start and active with a refusing socket, keep today's ladder path and refusal, and point at `gvm logs <n>` and `journalctl -u gvm@<n>`. A second `gvm stop` on a held, stopped instance exits 0 with 'already stopped'. Add manager tests for stop-on-dead, stop-on-auto-restart and stop-on-activating.

### day2-ops-8 — `gvm start` and `gvm restart` block silently until the game is ready (up to an hour), and restart never says whether the world saved

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: 34 → **41** · Status: open

**Evidence.** hostops/systemd_linux.go:59-96 waits for systemd's JobRemoved, and gvm@.service:20 is Type=notify, so the start job finishes only at READY=1. projectzomboid.yaml:65 sets hard_deadline 3600s (7DTD/ARK 1800s). runLifecycle prints nothing before mgr.Do returns (start.go:64-103). The ladder line is printed only when res.Shutdown is set, which happens only on stop (start.go:139-147, manager.go:242-246), so `gvm restart` output has no rung even though the runner wrote one to shutdown.jsonl. `--budget` appears on start, stop and restart help ('bound the shutdown ladder'), but consoleShutdown discards it (stop.go:60-65 `_ = budget`) while the audit row still records budget_sec (manager.go:256-258).

**Impact.** An operator restarting a modded PZ server watches a frozen terminal for many minutes and does not know whether Ctrl-C will kill the server; after a restart they cannot tell whether the world saved. The audit log records a stop budget that was never applied.

**Suggestion.** Print 'starting <n>; waiting for readiness (probe <kind>). Ctrl-C stops waiting; the server keeps starting' immediately. While waiting, re-read status.json every 2s and redraw one line with startingReason and the time since the last console output. Add `--no-wait` to start: it returns after the job is enqueued and points at `gvm status <n>`. After restart, print 'ladder ended on <rung> after <d>', plus DEGRADED, only from the first shutdown.jsonl record whose timestamp is at or after the moment the restart was issued; otherwise print 'no shutdown record for this restart; see gvm logs <n> --shutdown'. Remove `--budget` from start, stop and restart, and stop writing budget_sec. If someone passes it, say 'the ladder budget is shutdown.budget in the instance YAML; edit it and run gvm apply'.

### day2-ops-9 — The player count is never collected, so status and list never show players

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 10, 4 · Plan: **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm control verb <instance> players` exists (internal/cli/control.go); projectzomboid.yaml:112 min_poll belongs to the control block, :117 declares the players verb. (orig: runner.SetPlayers (internal/runner/status.go:118-120) has no production caller: `grep -rn SetPlayers` finds only status_test.go:64,80. Manifests declare the verb and a poll interval (projectzomboid.yaml:115-117 `min_poll: 60s`, `players: {control: "players"}`; minecraft-paper.yaml:100). Status.short and Report print players only when Status.Players is non-nil (lifecycle/status.go:153-160, 209-215), so a running row always reads 'pid 1234'.)

**Impact.** 'Is anyone on right now?' is the question asked before every manual restart or update, and gvm cannot answer it; the operator has to attach to the console and type the game's own command. The dashboard and any future 'restart only when empty' logic have no data.

**Suggestion.** Extend the manifest's `players` verb with a declarative parser, for example `players: {control: "players", count: '(?m)^Players connected \((\d+)\)'}` and optional `max:`. It is data, a regex with one capture group, and the template guard is unchanged (rule 10). In the runner, start a separate goroutine only when control.Ready is true and the verb has a control: form, never a console: form. Poll every max(min_poll, 60s) with a short per-call timeout. Stop polling as soon as a shutdown begins, so the ladder owns the channel. Call SetPlayers with the count, or with an Err ('unsupported' or 'last poll failed: …'). Show 'players 3/16 (40s ago)' in status, and a PLAYERS column in list where '-' means unknown and '0' means empty.

### day2-ops-10 — During a running job, the refusal to restart tells the operator to run `gvm job clear`, which itself refuses

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **28** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/start.go:114-116 formats the override with the invoked verb (gvm restart <n> --force). (orig: Every instance-scoped job, including the nightly backup, takes the lease (jobs/execute.go:655-661), and Allowed refuses start, stop and restart under any LIVE lease with hint 'wait for it, or: gvm job clear <n>' (lifecycle/verbs.go:185-188). start.go:114-116 then adds 'this refusal can be overridden: gvm stop <n> --force'. But `gvm job clear` refuses a live lease without --force ('the job is still running...', job.go:696-705). A backup lease doesn't change the phase (derive.go:192-199), so the `gvm list` row says running with no hint of the job (status.go:150-177).)

**Impact.** An operator who tries to restart during the backup window gets two contradictory suggestions, one of which leads to another refusal. They may reach for `gvm job clear --force`, which removes a running job's lease: the riskier of the two options.

**Suggestion.** For LeaseLive, set the hint to: 'a <kind> job is running (<lease.Unit or pid N>, started <ago>; <lease.Reason>). Wait for it: journalctl -fu <lease.Unit>. If it is stuck: gvm <verb> <n> --force'. Name `gvm job clear` only for LeaseStale or LeaseUnreadable. Drop the generic 'can be overridden' suffix from lifecycleError for this case so the override is not printed twice. In the `gvm list` row and the summary, append '(<kind> running <ago>)' whenever LeaseState == LeaseLive. Optionally add `--after-job`, which polls the lease file read-only until it is released and then performs the verb.

### day2-ops-11 — Stop, restart, update and the scheduled nightly restart give players no warning

**Medium** · player · missing-feature · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4, 10, 2 · Plan: **41** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Manifests declare a broadcast verb (projectzomboid.yaml:118 `broadcast: {control: "servermsg \"{{ .Msg }}\""}`) and `gvm control verb <n> broadcast Msg=...` exists. But `grep -rn -i 'announce|broadcast|countdown'` over internal/jobs, internal/lifecycle and internal/cli finds nothing that warns players before a ladder. 7daystodie.yaml:254-261 schedules a daily `restart` at 05:00 by default. The shutdown ladder's first step is the save (projectzomboid.yaml:89-93).

**Impact.** Players are disconnected mid-session by every manual restart, the nightly restart job, and (once wired) every update that has to stop. They cannot tell a planned restart from a crash.

**Suggestion.** Add `--warn <dur>` and `--message` to stop, restart and update, and `warn:` on spec.Job. Run the countdown before any state change: no hold, no stop, and for jobs inside the lease but before stopping steps. Ctrl-C or a context cancel during the countdown aborts cleanly ('nothing was stopped'). For update, warn only after the decision phases say Stop. Send the manifest's broadcast verb through the control channel (the runner's console.sock path, never admin.sock or a shell) at T-warn, T-1m and T-10s, then run the normal ladder. Skip the wait only when the player count is known to be 0. If the count is unknown, warn. With no broadcast verb or control not ready, print a note and continue without delay. Validate the message against the console sanitizer and also reject ' " \ $ ` and newlines. `gvm create` may set `warn: 5m` on the restart jobs it writes; the CLI default stays off.

### day2-ops-12 — Schedules are raw OnCalendar strings with no next-run, no timezone and no pause, and output mixes UTC and host time

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6, 1 · Plan: 37 → **39** · Status: open

**Evidence.** create.go:300-323 writes `*-*-* 04:00:00` with no zone (host time, usually UTC on a VPS); `gvm job list` prints it verbatim with no NEXT or zone column (job.go:608-631). The docs fall back to `systemctl list-timers 'gvm-job@pz-main*'` (ARCHITECTURE.md:6895). spec.Job has no field to pause or skip a job (spec/instance.go:255-266). Times are inconsistent: backup list prints UTC with no label (backup.go:412-418), `gvm status` prints RFC3339 Z (lifecycle/status.go:207), shutdown records use 'Z' (logs.go:146), and schedules are in host time.

**Impact.** An operator in another timezone cannot tell when the 04:00 backup or restart actually fires. To skip one night's restart for an event they must hand-edit YAML and re-apply or use systemctl, and they keep converting between UTC and local time.

**Suggestion.** Show 'next 2026-10-15 04:00 UTC (in 6h12m)' and 'last <time> <result>' in `gvm job list` and `gvm status`, read from the timer's NextElapseUSecRealtime and LastTriggerUSec (read-only). Format every printed timestamp the same way: host-local with an explicit zone, and keep RFC3339 in --json. Print the host zone beside zone-less OnCalendar values, and have `gvm create` state it. Add `paused: true` to spec.Job (apply renders a stopped, disabled timer; job list shows PAUSED). For one-off skips, add `gvm job skip <slug> --next`. It writes a dated marker under /var/lib/gvm/instances/<n>/jobs/ that the job checks at start. The run records `skipped (operator)` and the marker expires after that one firing, with no YAML edit. Add pause/resume commands only once internal/reconcile offers an intent writer that preserves comments, shared with enable/disable.

### day2-ops-13 — `--help` on status, list, logs, start, stop, restart and console prints usage and then runs the command; `gvm help <cmd>` prints one line

**Low** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm adopt --help` → 'gvm adopt: adopt: unknown subcommand "--help"'; `gvm update --help` exits 0 (not affected). (orig: flagErr maps flag.ErrHelp to nil (game.go:613-618), and parse*Args return (zero options, nil) on it (status.go:41-44; list.go:44-47; logs.go:60-63; start.go:47-50; console.go:94-97; install.go:53-56), so the run continues. Ran: `gvm logs --help` printed usage, then 'gvm logs: gvm: no transport for this command...'; `gvm stop --help` exits 1; as root, `gvm list --help` would print the whole listing after the help. `gvm help status` prints only 'gvm status — show instance state' with no flags (root.go:254-265).)

**Impact.** Asking for help produces an error and exit code 1, or unexpectedly runs a read, which makes the CLI feel broken and hides the flags operators are looking for.

**Suggestion.** Return a sentinel errHelpShown from every parse*Args and have Main exit 0 on it without running. Make `gvm help <cmd>` run `<cmd> --help`. Add a table test that every command's --help exits 0 and touches no HostEnv.

### day2-ops-14 — `gvm list --problems` on a healthy host says 'no instances', and the summary counts starting or updating servers as 'stopped'

**Low** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** list.go:81-83 filters to problems, then printList (list.go:105-106) prints 'no instances. `gvm create <name> --game <id>` defines one.' when the filtered list is empty. Summarise (lifecycle/status.go:258-272) puts every non-running, non-problem phase (starting, stopping, updating, installing, provisioning) in Stopped.

**Impact.** An operator checking for trouble is told their servers do not exist, and during a nightly update the summary reports a server as 'stopped'.

**Suggestion.** When --problems filters everything out, print 'nothing needs attention (5 instances, 5 running)'. Add a 'busy' count (starting, stopping, updating, installing) to Summary so the line reads 'N running, N busy, N stopped, N needing attention'.

### day2-ops-15 — `gvm logs` cannot follow, filter or reach rotated logs; the only live view is the interactive console

**Low** · operator · ergonomics · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 2 · Plan: **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** logs.go:45-58: the only flags are -n (default 30) and --shutdown. printConsoleTail reads the whole current console.log into memory (logs.go:98-111) and ignores rotated files (the runner rotates, internal/console/logfile.go:55-63). For a live view, `gvm console` (console.go:82-92) attaches with stdin forwarded to the game.

**Impact.** Watching a boot or tracking down a crash means either `tail -f /var/log/gvm/<n>/console.log` or attaching an interactive console where a stray keystroke goes to the game. 'What happened at 03:12' needs a manual grep across rotated .gz files.

**Suggestion.** Add `gvm logs <n> -f`: tail console.log from the end by seeking (not reading the whole file), and reopen on rename or truncate when the runner rotates. It never opens an input path. Change -n to seek from the end, so a 64 MB log is not loaded. Add `--all`, which walks the rotated generations oldest-first and decompresses .gz with compress/gzip. Skip --since unless a manifest declares a per-line timestamp regex. Skip --grep (pipes work). In the root CLI's human `gvm status` output only, for a stopped or failed instance, print the last 5 console lines with the hint `gvm logs <n> -n 100`. Keep them out of Status JSON and the dashboard model unless the viewer's role already has console-read permission.


---

<a id="content-mods"></a>
## Mods and workshop content (`content-mods`)

For the one game it really supports (Project Zomboid on the Steam Workshop), gvm's content tooling explains things very well: the three-way diff, `why`, the load-order checks with runnable fixes, the comment-preserving list edits and the cautious collection sync are all good. But it cannot finish the core journey. Every list edit, diff and dry-run ends with "apply: gvm content sync <inst>", and that command (and the content_sync job behind it) refuses because the apply adapter is not built. So no mod change ever reaches a server through gvm. Modrinth and CurseForge exist only as library code. The CLI never constructs either source, and all three shipped Minecraft content blocks fail content.Compile, even though `gvm game validate` reports them green. The CurseForge blocked-mod workflow is the least trustworthy part: the docs, the CLI and the ladder name three different drop-box paths and two commands that do not exist, API or key failures are reported as "author opted out", and a dropped JAR is accepted by filename without checking that it is the right build. Smaller frictions add up: `add` accepts URLs and mod names and fails only later, lists show bare numeric ids, setting up a collection or importing an existing server means editing YAML by hand, the first `add` wipes every comment in the instance file, and the dashboard has no content view at all.

**Strengths to preserve**

- The three-way `gvm content diff` (DECLARED vs LOCKED, LOCKED vs OBSERVED) names each finding with an id, the note, and the evidence for ambiguous items. It also separates a config the game has not written yet from an empty mod list (testdata/content/diff.golden).
- `gvm content why <activation-id>` answers the most common PZ support question in one lookup: which item the id came from, whether it was dropped as a lower-scoring build variant, its siblings, its evidence, and whether it is live in Mods= (why-dropped.golden, why-ambiguous.golden).
- List edits change intent only and say so every time: "Nothing was applied: the server still loads what ... lock.json and its config say." `remove` also warns that Mods= still enables the item until a sync rewrites it (content.go:712-718, 1195-1200).
- Load-order violations are reported, never fixed silently, and they come with a runnable fix: "! 3536052310 (ordinal 0) requires 3171167894 (ordinal 1)... gvm content move pz-main 3171167894 --before 3536052310". `add` refuses ambiguous placement such as `--first --after X`.
- Collection sync is careful. Known items keep their position and note, new items are appended under a dated head comment, `--dry-run` and `--no-fetch` are first-class modes, a private collection is refused unless `--allow-html-fallback` is given, and the raw-count cross-check fails closed (content_collection.go, steamworkshop/collection.go:500-573).
- The document editor keeps an operator's per-item and section comments across add, remove, move and collection sync when an `acquire:` sequence already exists (content_yaml.go). A removed item takes its own comment with it.
- The read-only verbs never touch the network and are safe with players online, so they keep working during a Steam outage (content.go:13-19).
- The fail-closed Unaccounted gate gives clear hints. A pasted URL is refused with "it is not a Steam Workshop publishedfileid, which is the numeric id out of the item's URL".
- `gvm content blocked` is honest that a Steam Workshop block cannot have blocked items. The CurseForge package has a build gate (nosynth_test.go) that stops gvm from ever building a CDN URL itself, and `--to` on a Workshop item is refused with the reason.

### content-mods-1 — No command can apply a mod change: `content sync` and the content_sync job both refuse, but every output points the operator at `gvm content sync`

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 3, 4, 5 · Plan: 20 → **44** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Extra pointer at internal/cli/content.go:1543 (end of runContentUpdate). The --apply refusal is at content.go:1124-1128. (orig: internal/cli/content.go:64 advertises sync as "resolve and apply the declared list". content.go:1338-1340 returns errContentSyncIsAJob whenever --dry-run is absent. content.go:1278-1286 says: "That job is not in this build". internal/cli/jobengines.go:53-54 and 162-169 give the content_sync job ErrContentApplyUnbuilt: "the content apply adapter (materialize, resolve, activate) is not built in this release". Yet afterListEdit (content.go:716-717) prints "gvm content sync pz-main      apply it" after every add, remove, move and collection sync, and content_diff.go:420 prints "apply:  gvm content sync pz-main". I ran this probe through `go test -overlay` with the existing fixture, without touching the repo. `gvm content sync pz-main` returns exit=1 with stderr "applying a content change stops the server ... That job is not in this build". `gvm content add pz-main 2392709985 --apply` gives the same refusal (content.go:1125-1129).)

**Impact.** An operator can declare, inspect and plan mods, but nothing ever reaches the server's WorkshopItems=/Mods= through gvm, whether from the CLI, a scheduled job or the dashboard. Every screen tells them to run a command that always fails. In practice they go back to hand-editing servertest.ini, which bypasses the lock, the stop ladder and the fail-closed gate.

**Suggestion.** Phase A (now): stop advertising apply. Reword the sync summary. Replace afterListEdit's "apply it" line and the diff's "apply:" line with "applying is not in this build yet; `gvm content sync <inst> --dry-run` shows what it would do". Remove --apply from add/remove and hide the inert --yes, --no-boot and --allow-ambiguous on sync. If --print-lines is added, print WorkshopItems= for every declared item but Mods= only from items already on disk, and list the ones that cannot be derived yet. Also say the edit must be made while the server is stopped, because PZ rewrites its ini on shutdown. Phase B: extend runner_content.go with a When(plan.Boot) branch inside the same job, lock and PolicyStartAnyway: stop (Super.Stop ladder), write the acquisition key with the owned_keys surgical editor, start with WaitReady bounded by acquisition.boot_timeout, stop (ladder), resolve from disk, write the activation key and the lock, start, then a post-start observe. The jobs.Contents adapter only writes files and resolves; it never calls the supervisor. `gvm content sync <inst>` builds jobs.Resolved directly, as `gvm backup` does, and runs runOneJob in-process, so it needs no gvmd and no admin.sock. When Plan.Impact() is rebuild, require --confirm <name> and take a preserve archive before the first stop.

### content-mods-2 — Minecraft mods (Modrinth) and CurseForge cannot be managed at all: all three shipped Minecraft content blocks fail to compile, and the CLI never builds those sources

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 12, 13 · Plan: 20 → **45** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/spec/validate.go:139-143 lists SourceModrinth as SupportImplemented, which is also misleading. (orig: games/minecraft-forge.yaml:111-121, minecraft-paper.yaml:146-156 and minecraft-neoforge.yaml:116-126 declare `acquisition: {mechanism: http}` and `activation: {}`. content.Compile (internal/content/schema.go:254-257, 276-279, 302-306) requires a write_key and a separator for both halves. I ran a probe through `go test -overlay` that calls content.Compile on each shipped manifest. It printed: `minecraft-forge block "mods" source modrinth: Compile err = content: invalid content schema: block "mods" declares no acquisition write_key ... declares no acquisition separator for , and the wrong one disables every mod with no error ... declares no activation write_key, so nothing would ever be enabled`. Paper and NeoForge fail the same way; projectzomboid compiles with err = <nil>. Even past Compile, content.go:513-525 `source()` builds only steamworkshop and says "Modrinth is v1.1 and CurseForge is v1.2". A grep for `modrinth.New(` and `curseforge.New(` finds no production caller. No shipped manifest has a `source: curseforge` block.)

**Impact.** On a Minecraft instance, every `gvm content` verb (list, add, diff and the rest) fails in openContent with four schema errors that blame a manifest the operator did not write. Phases 18 and 19 shipped Modrinth and CurseForge code that no user can reach, while the help text (`--pin ... Modrinth/CurseForge`) suggests it works.

**Suggestion.** Step 1 (cheap, now): add the test that compiles every content block of every shipped manifest. Add `activation: {mode: presence}`, keep refusing an empty `activation: {}`, and fix the three manifests. Until the sources are wired, have openContent print one sentence for modrinth and curseforge blocks ("Minecraft mod management is not in this build; jars under <root> load as-is") instead of four schema errors, and drop "Modrinth/CurseForge" from the --pin help. Step 2: before wiring modrinth.New, implement its Observe offline by hashing the jars in each root, so list, diff and why stay offline. Build its HTTP client from internal/content/fetch with the per-source host allowlist. Make Materialize fail on an entry with no URL instead of skipping it. Fill Schema.GameVersion and Loader from the rendered provider block. Presence deactivation may only unlink jars that the lock records with a matching digest; it never touches an operator-placed jar in mods/ or plugins/. Wire CurseForge only after content-mods-5 (local key path, rule 13) and content-mods-6 (ladder fixes).

### content-mods-3 — `gvm game validate` reports content blocks as fine when the runtime rejects them, and the resolvers and supported mechanisms cannot be discovered

**Medium** · manifest-author · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: 20 → **46** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm game hooks` lists pz.resolve_mod_ids under Hooks because it is also in hookNames (spec/validate.go:175-178). Resolver registry: spec/validate.go:190-193. Runtime resolver check: internal/content/resolver.go:212-214. (orig: `gvm.exe game validate games/minecraft-forge.yaml` printed "✓ 1 config file, 1 content source ... 0 errors, 0 warnings." with exit 0. The same block fails content.Compile (see content-mods-2). steamworkshop/source.go:93-102 refuses `mechanism: steamcmd_item` at runtime ("this build does not implement for the Workshop"), but Compile and validate both accept it. resolver.go:192 says "the name/doc table `gvm game resolvers` prints", yet `gvm.exe game resolvers` returns "unknown subcommand \"resolvers\"", and `gvm game hooks` lists hooks and validators but not content resolvers. The Compile error text also renders an empty key as "separator for , and...".)

**Impact.** The validator is a manifest author's only quick feedback. It gives a green result, and the first real error shows up on a Linux host after create and install, when `gvm content` refuses. An author writing an ARK or CS2 Workshop block with steamcmd_item, or a server_boot block without a resolver, will not find out until a sync. This already happened to all three shipped Minecraft manifests.

**Suggestion.** In `gvm game validate`, run content.Compile on each block with placeholder roots. Also check that the source and mechanism pair is implemented in this build, and that server_boot blocks name a resolver. Print one line per block, e.g. "✓ content mods (modrinth, http): jars in {{ .Data }}/mods, enabled by presence". Extend `gvm game hooks` with a "Content resolvers" section from content.ResolverDocs() and a table of supported source/mechanism pairs. When the key is empty, name the namespace in the error ("declares no acquisition separator (and no write_key)").

### content-mods-4 — The first `gvm content add` on an instance silently deletes every comment in the whole instance file

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **21** · Status: open

**Evidence.** content_yaml.go:40-43 and 59-62: when spec.content.<block>.acquire is missing, editAcquireSequence returns edited=false. content.go:653-657 then falls back to `spec.MarshalYAML(in)` and re-serialises the whole document. That is the normal state after `gvm create`, and also after following the collection-sync error's advice to add `collection: <id>` by hand (content_collection.go:75-77). Probe (go test -overlay, seedInstance, then I added `# operator note: keep max_players at 16` and `collection: "3374983339"   # our friends' collection`): `gvm content add pz-main 2392709985` exited 0, and afterwards the probe printed "operator comment survived: false" and "collection comment survived: false". The file's own header (content_yaml.go:24-32) says comments are where load-order reasoning lives and that losing them while reporting success was the bug this editor exists to fix.

**Impact.** An operator who documented their instance file loses all of it, including comments far away from the content block, on the first mod they add. The only message is a success line. Only one .bak is kept, so a second edit makes the loss permanent.

**Suggestion.** When the path does not exist, create the missing `content`, `<block>` and `acquire` nodes inside the parsed yaml.Node document and edit in place. Keep the full re-marshal only for an empty document. Add a test that a comment at the top of the file survives the first add and the first collection sync.

### content-mods-6 — The CurseForge ladder labels every API failure as "author opted out" and accepts drop-box JARs by filename without checking the build

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 12, 13 · Plan: **45** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/content/curseforge/blocked.go:32-36: the doc comment's rename example contradicts the stem matcher. (orig: curseforge/ladder.go:44-49 catches any API error (bad key, 404, rate limit, network) with "API error, treating as blocked". rungBlocked (ladder.go:201-219) then sets BanReason "mod author opted out of API distribution; drop the JAR into the blocked-mods drop-box". IsAvailable==false (a withdrawn file) is decoded at files.go:37 but never checked. IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:882 asks for "a 401 is handled as its own error class", and ARCHITECTURE 12.8 asks for three classifications. In rungManual (ladder.go:153-185), ScanDropBox can match on filename stem alone (blocked.go:65-68). The code then computes the digest from the dropped file itself (`dig := murmur2:<actualFP>`) and never compares it with the API's fileFingerprint or sha1, despite the comment "re-verify the fingerprint". Jars in the drop-box that match nothing are never reported.)

**Impact.** An expired or mistyped API key, or a CurseForge outage, would show every mod in a 250-mod pack as blocked, and the operator might spend hours downloading JARs by hand. A common mistake, such as downloading the Fabric build or the wrong MC version from the CurseForge page, is accepted silently if the filename matches, and the server crashes after the next sync. A browser rename like `foo (1).jar` is ignored with no message.

**Suggestion.** Use distinct resolutions with distinct messages. auth_failed (401/403): "CurseForge rejected the key in /etc/gvm/secrets/host/curseforge", and the plan refuses. not_found (404): "no such project or file id". withdrawn (isAvailable=false): terminal, pick another file. transient (network or 429): retry later, and the plan refuses rather than marking anything blocked. Only an empty downloadUrl is opted_out and goes into blocked-mods.json, with the project's websiteUrl copied verbatim from the API (rule 13). For drop-box matches, require murmur2 or sha1 to equal the API's values. On a mismatch, say "x.jar is a different build than <fileName> (fingerprint A != B)", and list drop-box jars that matched nothing.

### content-mods-7 — `gvm content add` accepts URLs and mod names and fails only later, and no command edits an existing item's note, requires or pin

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **43** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Probe (go test -overlay with the content fixture): `gvm content add pz-main https://steamcommunity.com/sharedfiles/filedetails/?id=2392709985` exits 0 and prints "+ https://steamcommunity.com/...?id=2392709985  ordinal 4". `gvm content add pz-main ThatDamnLibrary` (an activation id) also exits 0. The error only appears later in `gvm content diff`: "REFUSING: 1 of 5 declared item(s) could not be accounted for ... it is not a Steam Workshop publishedfileid". addRefs (content.go:1011-1040) does no per-source id check, and `--pin` is accepted for Workshop items even though `update --to` refuses it (content.go:1489-1498). Changing a note or `requires` is impossible from the CLI: re-adding is refused with "already declared at ordinal N; `gvm content move` changes its position" (content.go:1016-1019), and move changes only the position. order.go:77-79 prints the fix "gvm content add <inst> <id> --requires ''", which that same refusal blocks.

**Impact.** Operators naturally paste the Workshop URL or type the mod name they see in Mods=. gvm writes it, reports success, and the mistake shows up as REFUSING the next time they look. To fix a typo in `requires` or a note they have to edit YAML by hand, and one of gvm's own suggested fixes cannot be run.

**Suggestion.** Validate ids per source before writing, and exit non-zero without writing on failure. Workshop ids are numeric, CurseForge ids are projectID[:fileID], Modrinth ids are slug[:versionId]. Accept and normalise the common URLs: Workshop `?id=`, `modrinth.com/mod/<slug>` and CurseForge project URLs. When the argument is a known activation id, say "ThatDamnLibrary is a Mods= id from item 3171167894". Refuse `--pin` on a Workshop block at add time. Add `gvm content set <inst> <id> [--note T] [--requires IDS] [--pin V]`, which edits in place through the document editor, and change the requires-itself fix to use it.

### content-mods-9 — Getting an existing mod list into gvm means editing YAML by hand: no collection setter, no import from the live ini, and collection sync drops hand-added items without asking

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 11 · Plan: **43** · Status: open

**Evidence.** content_collection.go:116-134: the merge is printed and then written with no prompt. collection.go:546-549: vendored refs are kept. (orig: content_collection.go:75-77: "declares no collection for block %q; add `collection: <id>` under content.%s in %s". `gvm create --help` has no --collection flag, and api.Collection refuses a URL (collection.go:272-274: "is not a collection id"). ARCHITECTURE.md:4148 specifies `gvm content import <inst> (--from-ini | --from-dir PATH)`, but it is not among contentSubs (content.go:52-69). adopt/discover_pz.go:66-67 only counts WorkshopItems= and Mods= and writes no list. steamworkshop.Merge (collection.go:509 and 551-552) removes every declared item that is not in the collection, unless it is vendored. collection sync writes by default (content_collection.go:121-134) and reports "- <id>  no longer in the collection" only after writing.)

**Impact.** The two most common starting points, "I have a Steam collection" and "I have an existing modded PZ server", both begin with hand-editing /etc/gvm/instances/<n>.yaml, which also triggers the comment loss in content-mods-4. Retyping a 33-item WorkshopItems= list in load order is slow and error-prone. An operator who adds a few mods with `gvm content add` loses them, and their notes, on the next collection sync.

**Suggestion.** Add `gvm content collection set <inst> <id|URL>` and `gvm create --collection <id|URL>`, both writing through the document editor (after content-mods-4). Build `gvm content import <inst> --from-ini` as ARCHITECTURE 12.9 specifies. It does a read-only read of the live WorkshopItems= and refuses when the block already declares items, unless --replace is given. It writes only the declared list and sets activation_policy: preserve, so the existing Mods= line stays the operator's. Adopt discovery must never call it. For collection sync, always print the merge first. When it would drop items, require --yes on an interactive terminal. In unattended contexts (the future on_update mode), report the items it would drop and keep them. Add a `local: true` ref flag that Merge keeps like vendored while the item is still fetched, rather than overloading vendored.

### content-mods-10 — Mod lists show only numeric Workshop ids: `add` never looks up titles

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **43** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Every read verb and `add` build the source Offline (content.go:513-519). `gvm content add pz-main 2392709985` prints only "+ 2392709985  ordinal 4" and writes `- id: "2392709985"` with no note. Only collection sync stores the title (steamworkshop/collection.go:567-570). In list and diff, an item's only label is the note the operator typed (content.go:880 and content_diff.go:433-435). The diff note says "no Workshop metadata was fetched for 4 item(s): this source is offline". A wrong-game id (consumer_app_id != 108600) is caught only by collection sync's cross-check.

**Impact.** With 40 to 80 mods added one at a time, `gvm content list` and `diff` turn into a column of 10-digit numbers. The operator has to open each one in a browser to know what "! 3536052310 Mods does not enable Hilux88" refers to, and an id from another game is accepted silently.

**Suggestion.** Do the lookup best-effort with a short timeout, using the keyless Workshop client that collection sync uses (h.workshopAPI). If the lookup fails or times out, still write the item and say that titles were not fetched. Fill note with the sanitised title only when --note was not given. On a consumer_app_id mismatch, refuse before writing, name both appids, and offer --allow-other-app to override; nothing is removed, so this stays within the never-drop-on-API-evidence design. Provide --no-fetch. Keep list and diff offline, and fall back to the lock's Upstream.Title when note is empty. Update the file comments in content.go and content_collection.go that say collection sync is the only verb that fetches.

### content-mods-11 — `gvm content update` cannot say which mods were updated upstream

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6 · Plan: **43** · Status: open

**Evidence.** runContentUpdate (content.go:1471-1545) compares only the activation ids found on disk with the lock ("report items whose internal ids have moved since the lock"), always offline. The Workshop API's time_updated is decoded into Upstream.TimeUpdated (steamworkshop/api.go:497-501, content/content.go:184-185), but nothing compares it with anything. `--dry-run` is documented as "report only (which is all this command does)" and ignored (`_ = *dry`, content.go:1487). Probe output: "= every locked item still resolves to the same Mods ids" or "+ 3778511497  declared and not in the lock". Neither line says whether an item has a new version.

**Impact.** The everyday question after "why won't it load" is "which mods updated, and do I need to restart so players can join". PZ refuses clients whose workshop item versions do not match the server's. gvm records the data that would answer this but has no command that uses it.

**Suggestion.** Add `gvm content update <inst> --check` as a keyless, best-effort read of time_updated. Compare it first with steamapps/workshop/appworkshop_<appid>.acf (WorkshopItemsInstalled -> timeupdated per item), parsed with the existing internal/provider/steamcmd ACF parser. Fall back to the lock's Upstream.TimeUpdated, then to directory mtime labelled "approximate". Print titles and ages. Exit with a documented, distinct code for "updates available", and 0 when there are none (D-43). The check is report-only and never triggers or schedules a sync, so a timer or the dashboard can only flag it (rule 6). Remove the inert --dry-run and rename the summary as the reviewer suggests.

### content-mods-13 — The dashboard has no content view: moderators cannot see mods or blocked state, and players have no mod list

**Medium** · dashboard-user · missing-feature · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 9, 1 · Plan: **49** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/web/routes.go:22-58 serves only instance list and detail, console, healthz, status and start/stop/restart. A grep for content, mods or workshop in internal/web finds only HTTP Content-Type headers. authz/permission.go:22-25 defines content.view, content.edit and content.sync, and matrix.go:15 and 36-37 assign them, but no handler checks them. IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:795 is unchecked: "including the content two-list view with a visible `check` row". `gvm status` (internal/cli/status.go) says nothing about content beyond the AWAITING_MANUAL_FILES hint.

**Impact.** A moderator answering "why can't I join" or "is mod X installed" has to ask the operator to SSH in. Players on modded Minecraft need the exact mod list and versions to connect, and there is nothing to send them.

**Suggestion.** Serve GET /api/v1/instances/{name}/content from gvmd, gated on content.view, with no unauthenticated variant. Return only the contentListReport, blocked and diff-summary projections, never a raw config document. Read only the block's owned keys through the gamecfg parser, so the RCON password in the same ini can never be returned. Build the response from the declared list, the lock and a cached, presence-only observe rather than a full mod.info walk per request. Ship the Mods tab read-only first, with the AWAITING_MANUAL_FILES banner. Add a Sync action only after content-mods-1, as a request that starts the content_sync job unit (same lock, same shutdown ladder as the CLI) and requires content.sync. For players, add `gvm content list <inst> --format markdown|links`, built offline from the lock's titles.

### content-mods-5 — Blocked-mod instructions name three different drop-box paths and two commands that do not exist, and nothing writes or clears blocked-mods.json

**Low** · operator · docs · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 12, 13 · Plan: **45** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/secret.go:43-58: `secret set` is an unconditional stub for every secret, not something specific to curseforge or gvmd. ARCHITECTURE.md:510 and 4117 name /var/lib/gvm/downloads/mods. (orig: docs/curseforge-blocked-mods.md:20-21 and 41 say "/etc/gvm/curseforge/dropbox/" and tell the user to run "gvm content plan <instance>" (lines 35 and 45). internal/cli/content_blocked.go:126-127 prints "Put them in /var/lib/gvm/downloads/mods, then: gvm content resolve-blocked <name>". internal/content/curseforge/blocked.go:120-125 uses `/var/lib/gvm/instances/<name>/blocked-mods`, a directory next to the blocked-mods.json file, and ladder.go:216-217 and blocked.go:128 also point at `gvm content resolve-blocked`. Running `gvm.exe content resolve-blocked pz-main` gives "unknown subcommand \"resolve-blocked\"", and `gvm.exe content plan pz-main` gives "unknown subcommand \"plan\"". IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:881 lists resolve-blocked as unchecked. No production code writes or removes instance.Paths.BlockedMods (grep finds readers only). lifecycle/derive.go:215-223 ranks AWAITING_MANUAL_FILES above running. The CurseForge key also has no CLI route: `gvm.exe secret set --name curseforge` returns "requires gvmd".)

**Impact.** An operator following the doc puts JARs where nothing looks for them and runs commands that do not exist. Once blocked-mods.json exists, no command clears it, so `gvm status` would report AWAITING_MANUAL_FILES indefinitely. Supplying the API key requires gvmd, which contradicts the rule that the CLI works when everything else is down.

**Suggestion.** Use one constant for /var/lib/gvm/downloads/mods (ARCHITECTURE layout and 12.8) in the doc, the CLI and the ladder. Build `gvm content resolve-blocked <inst> [--from DIR]` so it reads the drop-box but never moves or deletes the operator's files. It copies a jar into the blob store only when its sha1 or murmur2 equals the API's value. It removes blocked-mods.json only when a fresh plan shows zero blocked items. Only the job's content.decide step writes blocked-mods.json, never `sync --dry-run`, and it copies websiteUrl verbatim from the API. For the key, make the existing `gvm secret set` work locally for every scope instead of adding --host; host scope is already "omit --instance". `gvm secret set --name curseforge --from-stdin` writes /etc/gvm/secrets/host/curseforge with mode 0600, atomically and audited through hostops. It never takes the value in argv and works with gvmd down. Rewrite docs/curseforge-blocked-mods.md so it uses only commands that exist.

### content-mods-8 — `content diff` and `content sync --dry-run` describe the same state differently, and a diff that is refusing still prints "apply:" and exits 0

**Low** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: partial · fit: sound · Plan: **43** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The plan's baseline is the live config (Change.Why: "not listed in the live WorkshopItems"), not the lock. content_diff.go:405-420 prints REFUSING and then apply:. Both commands exit 0, confirmed by the probe. (orig: Same fixture, same moment. `gvm content diff pz-main` shows "~ 3536052310  KI5 Hilux   ordinal 3 -> 2" and "- 3435796523  KI5 Chevrolet   locked, no longer declared". `gvm content sync pz-main --dry-run` summarises "4 declared, 2 change(s)" and shows "+ add 3536052310 ( -> ordinal 2): not listed in the live WorkshopItems", calling the moved item an add and never mentioning the Chevrolet removal. printContentPlan uses c.String() (content.go:1384), which produces the "+ add ... ( -> ordinal 2)" form. changeDetail (content.go:1413-1431) was written to avoid exactly that, but nothing calls it. The ladder says "budget 1h0m0s" (steamworkshop/source.go:711), while diff says "budget 60m". After a pasted URL, the diff prints "REFUSING: 1 of 5 declared item(s) could not be accounted for" and then "apply:  gvm content sync pz-main" (content_diff.go:405-420), and runContentDiff still returns exit 0.)

**Impact.** The operator cannot tell which of the two views is what will actually happen. A script or nightly check cannot detect a refusing list from the exit code, and the output suggests applying in the same breath as saying it refuses.

**Suggestion.** Give `sync --dry-run` the diff's DECLARED vs LOCKED rows, then one "will write" block showing the WorkshopItems= and Mods= lines before and after. Render changes with changeDetail and the boot budget with shortDuration. When Unaccounted is not empty, replace the "apply:" line with "fix first: gvm content remove <inst> <id>" and exit with a distinct non-zero code from both diff and dry-run.

### content-mods-12 — Modrinth and CurseForge dependency handling will confuse operators: required dependencies are never shown, any declared incompatibility aborts the plan, and CurseForge ignores the mod loader

**Low** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **45** · Status: open

**Evidence.** modrinth/deps.go:30-33 says "Required deps are noted in PlanEntry.Evidence, not auto-resolved", but resolveOne (modrinth/source.go:256-265) never sets Evidence. `--with-deps` from ARCHITECTURE.md:4135 does not exist (content.go:1110-1116). modrinth/source.go:212-216 and curseforge/ladder.go:57-60 return "has incompatible dependency ...; aborting plan" whenever a version declares any incompatibility, whether or not that project is installed. curseforge/client.go:53-61 getLatestFile filters LatestFilesIndexes by gameVersion only and ignores the decoded ModLoader field (files.go:83).

**Impact.** Once wired: adding a mod such as sodium without fabric-api gives a clean plan and a server that crashes on start. Popular mods that list an incompatibility with a project the operator does not use, OptiFine being the usual case, can never be added, and the message gives an opaque project id. A Forge server can be given the Fabric jar for the same MC version.

**Suggestion.** Record required dependencies on each plan entry. In diff and dry-run, show "! sodium requires fabric-api, which is not declared" followed by "gvm content add <inst> fabric-api --before sodium". Build `add --with-deps`, which does a breadth-first walk of required dependencies only and never downloads embedded ones. Abort on `incompatible` only when the incompatible project is also declared or resolved, naming both mods by title. Filter CurseForge files by the instance's loader.

### content-mods-14 — `gvm content why` with a Workshop id wrongly says "nothing on this box accounts for it"

**Low** · operator · ergonomics · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **43** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Probe: `gvm content why pz-main 3171167894` (the declared, locked and present library item) exited 1 and printed: "nothing on this box accounts for it: no locked item and no item under 1 workshop root contains a mod.info with that id ... this takes the ACTIVATION id (a mod.info id=), not the numeric workshop id". stderr: "3171167894 is not an activation id this instance knows" (content_diff.go:476-482 and 643-654).

**Impact.** The operator usually knows the Workshop id, not the mod.info id. The first line is false, the exit code reports failure, and they have to run list and then why again.

**Suggestion.** The two namespaces can be told apart (Workshop ids are numeric), so accept either. When the argument matches a declared or locked acquisition id, print that item's activation ids, dropped variants, evidence and live status, and exit 0. Keep the current refusal only for ids that match nothing in either namespace.

### content-mods-15 — Help text and flags: inert or mangled flags, ignored --json, internal jargon, help that assumes Project Zomboid, and a missing-root warning on every PZ command

**Low** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **43** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `--json` is ignored by add, remove, move, update, lock and collection sync. The probe ran `gvm content add pz-main 2392709985 --json` and `gvm content lock pz-main --json` and both printed text. `--remote` is offered on every subcommand but transport.go:173-175 always refuses it ("needs the API client, which lands with the web tier"). In `gvm content sync --help`, the flag appears as "-no-boot gvm adopt" because Go's flag package treats the backquoted text as the argument name. `gvm help content` prints only "gvm content — manage mods and workshop items". contentUsage (content.go:97-101) defines ids only as "a Steam publishedfileid" and "a mod.info id=". User-facing text cites "section 12.9", "section 12.8", "section 12.6", "unchanged from the bash suite" (content_diff.go:289), "Modrinth (v1.1) and CurseForge (v1.2)" (content.go:522 and 1497), and refers to `gvm content promote` (content.go:1653), which does not exist. projectzomboid.yaml:175-177 declares two roots, so list and diff always print "! content root .../install/steamapps/workshop/content/108600 is not there yet" even when every item is present (list.golden, diff.golden).

**Impact.** Script writers and dashboard builders get text where they asked for JSON. Operators try flags that never work, chase internal section numbers, and learn to ignore "!" lines because one of them appears on every run.

**Suggestion.** Support --json on every verb or remove the flag from that verb. Hide `--remote` and `--apply` until they work. Remove the backticks from the no-boot usage string. Make `gvm help content` print the subcommand table. In `gvm content --help`, give an id example for each source: Workshop `2392709985` or its URL, Modrinth `sodium` or `sodium:<versionId>`, CurseForge `238222` or `238222:4712345`. Replace section numbers, "bash suite" and version tags with plain sentences, and drop the reference to `promote` until it exists. Show the missing-root note only when no declared root holds any items, and otherwise only in `diff --against observed`.


---

<a id="game-catalog-config"></a>
## Choosing a game and configuring it (`game-catalog-config`)

For an operator, choosing a game and supplying values at create time works well. Settings are checked against the manifest, required variables are listed with copy-pasteable `--var` lines, `--dry-run` shows the exact YAML, and `gvm game validate` is an excellent tool for manifest authors. Past that point the path from settings to a running game is mostly not wired. Values that a manifest routes into game config files (max players, MOTD, PvP, RCON password, and the ports for 7 of the 16 games) are never written. No manifest hook runs in the shipped binary: `internal/hook` is not linked, even though `gvm game hooks` lists every hook as "ready". There is no command that can set a game password, so 12 of the 16 games fail the runner's credential preflight on first start. Changing a setting later means hand-editing `/etc/gvm/instances/<name>.yaml`, and `gvm apply` never re-validates it. Immutable world names can be renamed, out-of-range values pass, and raising the heap leaves the cgroup ceiling where it was. Discovery is also weak: no command shows which settings a game has, how players connect, or (for authors) how to approve, try or upgrade a manifest, even though docs, templates and status hints point to commands for this that do not exist.

**Strengths to preserve**

- `gvm game validate` reports every problem with file:line:col, the document path, the allowed values and a did-you-mean (verified on a broken manifest: wrong format, misspelled hook, misspelled var reference, wrong var type, missing apply, bad default all reported in one pass with exit 1).
- `gvm create` refuses undeclared and duplicated `--var` keys, lists missing required vars as ready-to-paste `--var name=…` lines with their help text, and validates the assembled instance (e.g. `max_players=500` -> 'spec.vars.max_players: 500 is above the declared maximum 64').
- `gvm create --dry-run` prints the full instance YAML and writes nothing; create never overwrites an existing definition and ends with 'nothing else on this host changed ... next: gvm apply <name> --diff'.
- `gvm apply --diff` is literally the plan apply runs, with masked unified diffs of the generated drop-in/timers and an honest '! the write-probe was NOT run' disclaimer.
- `gvm game list` / `gvm game which` work offline from the embedded manifests and show clearly which layer wins.
- Pinned manifests (manifest.lock.yaml) mean a package upgrade can never silently change a live server's argv.
- Manifests carry high-quality operator knowledge in `help:`, `note:` and `fail_markers[].hint` (PZ jvm_xmx native-memory warning, Valheim password rules, PZ 16261/16262 'visible but never joinable' note), and pin-port errors list the valid port names.
- The template guard (no control flow, closed function set), base62 generated passwords and secret taint/masking in diffs are solid safety foundations to build the missing pieces on.

### game-catalog-config-1 — Manifest config_files keys (settings AND config-file ports) are never written to the game's config files

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1 · Plan: **30** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Argv already carries some values: games/minecraft-paper.yaml:58 `--port={{ .Port.game }}`; games/palworld.yaml:47-49 `-port`, `-queryport`, `-players={{ .Var.max_players }}`. PZ (projectzomboid.yaml:31-41) and 7DTD (7daystodie.yaml:40-46) pass no port on argv, so the collision claim is exact for those two. (orig: `grep -rn "RenderFile(" internal --include=*.go | grep -v _test` -> only the definition at internal/spec/render.go:299; no production caller renders config_files[].keys or calls gamecfg Doc.Set for them (the only production Set calls are internal/hook/pz.go:326,488,967 for sandbox presets and mod lists, and internal/hook is not linked; see finding 3). Nothing in internal/runner, internal/reconcile, internal/jobs or internal/update touches m.Spec.Configs (grep). ARCHITECTURE.md:1950 promises 'gvm apply diffs desired vs observed', but observed.json is only a path constant (internal/instance/paths.go:193) that nothing writes. IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md has no phase that delivers this step (the only mention is the codec at line 137). Affected manifests: projectzomboid.yaml:139-150 (PublicName, MaxPlayers, Password, RCONPassword, PVP...) plus ports via config_key at :125/:127/:131 (DefaultPort/UDPPort/RCONPort); minecraft-paper/forge/neoforge (motd, max-players, difficulty, rcon.port, rcon.password, minecraft-paper.yaml:113-140); palworld.yaml:114-122; 7daystodie*.yaml:109-120. That is 7 of the 16 shipped games.)

**Impact.** For these games, `--var max_players=16`, `public_name`, `pvp`, `motd`, `difficulty` and so on are stored in the instance YAML and then do nothing. The RCON password is never written, so the control channel cannot authenticate. With several servers on one box, which is the core promise: a second PZ/Palworld/7DTD/Minecraft instance is allocated 16271, gets the firewall rule for it, and the game still reads 16261 from its own config. The result is 'Address already in use', or a server nobody can join.

**Suggestion.** In supervise(), after runner.Preflight has taken the job lock and before r.Spawn, render each config_files[].keys entry through hook.NewConfigAccess, with Active returning false because nothing is spawned yet. That path enforces deny_keys, the jail and the mode, and it writes atomically with a .bak. Record the values gvm last wrote, as hashes for secret-tainted keys, under /run/gvm/<n>/ where the runner can write. When a key's current value differs from the last-written one, the game or an admin changed it. Still apply the instance YAML's value, but write a console note: '-- gvm: MaxPlayers was 20 (changed outside gvm), set to 16 from <n>.yaml; keep it with sudo gvm set <n> max_players=20'. Port keys always take the allocated value. If a parse fails, refuse the start with ExitConfig and a STATUS= line rather than start with unknown ports. For v1 drop observed.json: `gvm apply --diff` runs as root and can parse the live file read-only while the unit is inactive, masking every secret-tainted key. For PZ, also pass the allocated ports on argv (-port/-udpport) so the first-boot generation cannot collide. Where the codec can create the file (java_properties), set create_if_missing:true. 7DTD and Palworld depend on their seed hooks (finding 3), so ship this together with or after that wiring. Keep the end-to-end test that pz-b's ini says DefaultPort=16271, and run it after first boot.

### game-catalog-config-2 — No command can set a game password/secret; create claims the server 'starts without them' but the runner refuses to start

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 3, 10 · Plan: **29** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Additional evidence: internal/cli/root.go:100 `{Name: "gvmd", ... Run: notImplemented}`. The daemon the error points to does not exist in this build. (orig: internal/cli/secret.go:43-58: `gvm secret set` parses --instance/--name/--generate and then unconditionally runs `return errRequiresDaemon("secret set")` ('requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running'); rotate, list and show-path do the same, and nothing dials gvmd. secrets.GeneratePassword has no non-test caller, although internal/secrets/generate.go:52 says 'DefaultPasswordLen is what `gvm create` and `gvm secret rotate` generate' and minecraft-paper.yaml:19-21 says the RCON password is 'generated at create time'. In the overlay harness, `gvm create pz-1 --game projectzomboid --var public_name=Friends` printed: 'secrets   admin_password, join_password, rcon_password — not generated yet; the unit starts without them and the game gets an empty value' (create.go:511-514). In fact runner/preflight.go:240-243 refuses the start: 'the unit passed no credentials, but this instance needs admin, join, rcon; add LoadCredential= lines and re-apply'. Re-applying never helps, because reconcile/units.go:519-547 only emits LoadCredential= for files that already exist (the drop-in says '# no LoadCredential= for admin, join, rcon: credentials not present under /etc/gvm/secrets yet.'). The validator fix text at spec/validate_instance.go:383 suggests `gvm secret set <inst> join_password`, a syntax that does not exist. 12 of the 16 manifests reference .Secret.* (grep). Even Valheim's optional join_password, guarded by when: secret_set, blocks the start because run.go:169-191 puts every referenced secret into CredIDs.)

**Impact.** The core journey create -> apply -> start fails for Valheim, PZ, Palworld, Rust, CS2, V Rising, Core Keeper, 7DTD, ARK and Minecraft Paper, and every hint leads in a circle. The only workaround is hand-writing /etc/gvm/secrets/instances/<name>/<id>, a layout documented only at ARCHITECTURE.md:77.

**Suggestion.** Implement the verbs locally for root: `sudo gvm secret set <inst> <name> (--generate | --stdin)`. Never accept the value on argv, where it would land in ps and shell history. Reject values containing ' " \ $ ` ; = CR LF NUL and run spec.ValidateInstance's secret validators against the new value before writing. Add `gvm secret list <inst>` (presence and mtime only), `sudo gvm secret show <inst> join_password` (root only, join password only), and `gvm secret rotate` (generate + write; with --restart, stop through the normal ladder). Every write ends with 'run sudo gvm apply <inst> so the unit loads it'. In `gvm create`, unless --dry-run, generate rcon and admin secrets. Generate a join password by default and print it once; `--open` opts out and creates no join secret. In run.go and BuildCredentials, leave secrets referenced only under `when: {secret_set: X}` out of CredIDs when the file is absent. Fix the hints in create.go:511, preflight.go:242 and validate_instance.go:383 to name the real syntax. The Postgres secret_refs row is mirrored best-effort only (logbook).

### game-catalog-config-3 — No manifest hook runs in the shipped binary, yet `gvm game hooks` says 'ready' and validate ticks them

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 2, 4 · Plan: 20 → 30 → 31 → **44** · Status: open

**Evidence.** rebuild.go:686-689 is internal/rebuild/rebuild.go, not internal/cli/rebuild.go. (orig: `go list -deps ./cmd/gvm | grep -c "internal/hook$"` -> 0, so the hook package is not linked. `grep -rn "NewConfigAccess("` finds only the definition (internal/hook/config.go:51). rebuild.go:686-689 skips hooks when cfg.Hooks is nil, and nothing sets it. `gvm game hooks` nevertheless prints 'pz.apply_sandbox ready', 'mc.write_properties ready', 'pal.settings_seed ready', 'sdtd.seed_config ready' and so on, and `gvm game validate valheim` prints '✓ 3 hooks resolve (steam.home_layout, steam.link_sdk, steam.appid_shim)'. Settings-relevant consequences: PZ's sandbox_preset/sandbox_overrides vars are applied only by pz.apply_sandbox; 7daystodie.yaml:41 passes -configfile={{ .Data }}/serverconfig.xml, which only sdtd.seed_config creates; Palworld's settings file is seeded only by pal.settings_seed; Minecraft's eula.txt is written only by mc.accept_eula, and ./gvm-jre (the exec path) only by mc.select_jdk. Even once wired, mc.write_properties (hook/mc.go:176-198) sets no keys, and because minecraft-paper.yaml:125 says create_if_missing:false it logs the misleading 'manifest declares no config file "server.properties"; mc.write_properties skipped'.)

**Impact.** Difficulty presets, settings seeds and the Minecraft EULA never happen. 7DTD and Minecraft cannot start from a fresh create. Manifest authors are told their hooks are 'ready' and resolve, so they look for the bug in their YAML.
