**Suggestion.** Ship now: `gvm game hooks` prints each hook's Doc() and phases with 'registered — not run by this build', and validate prints '! N hooks resolve but this build does not run them'. Then wire hook.Run into the install/update job (post_install, post_content) and into `gvm run` (pre_start, after Preflight and before Spawn), with Ctx.Config from NewConfigAccess over the instance jail. Before mc.accept_eula is wired, add `legal: {eula: {var: accept_eula, url: https://aka.ms/MinecraftEULA}}` to the three MC manifests. Make the hook record a Problem and write nothing unless that var is "true". `gvm create` should then require `--var accept_eula=true` and print the URL. Honour pz.first_boot's BootRequest through the runner (readiness marker, then the ladder's `quit`). Fix mc.write_properties to Set the manifest's server.properties keys into a codec.New() document and set create_if_missing:true for that file.

### game-catalog-config-4 — Changing settings means hand-editing the instance YAML, and `gvm apply` never re-validates it (immutable world names, ranges, memory ceiling)

**High** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 4, 5 · Plan: 21 → **39** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Additional evidence: internal/doctor/checks_inst.go:55-60 reads `inst.Spec.Vars["native_headroom"]`, which no manifest declares as a var (games/*.yaml put it under sizing.native_headroom), so INST-002 always skips. That contradicts the 'gvm doctor re-checks them' line at reconcile/units.go:646. (orig: create.go:397-398 tells the operator to 'Edit the file and run `gvm apply`'. spec.ValidateInstance has only two callers, create.go:425 and content.go:670. apply's loadInstance (cli/instance.go:482-503) only runs spec.ParseInstance, which is a non-strict yaml.Unmarshal (spec/load.go:373-387). VarSpec.Immutable (spec/manifest.go:664-666) is never enforced on an instance. Overlay harness (create + apply pz-1, then edit the YAML): max_players "8"->"500" (max 64), server_name servertest->"My Server" (immutable, and rejected by pz.server_name_is_world_dir), pvp->maybe, jvm_xmx 5g->7g. `gvm apply pz-1 --diff` exited 0 with '0 to add, 4 to change, 0 to remove, 20 already correct.', and `gvm apply pz-1` exited 0. The only trace was a drop-in comment, '# EDITED: the manifest's sizing computes MemoryHigh=9G MemoryMax=10G and this instance declares the values below', while MemoryMax=8G stayed (reconcile/units.go:640-647). The plan header also printed '(projectzomboid rev 7, , pinned)' with an empty origin. Nothing says that apply:restart vars need a restart.)

**Impact.** Renaming server_name/world_name/level_name silently abandons the world: the next start creates a new one, and the backup include globs, templated on server_name, stop covering the old save. Raising the heap without raising MemoryMax lets the kernel OOM killer SIGKILL the JVM mid-save, which is the outcome rule 4 exists to prevent. Invalid values reach the game unchecked.

**Suggestion.** Add `sudo gvm set <inst> KEY=VALUE... [--dry-run]`. It validates the assembled document with spec.ValidateInstance plus the game's validators, normalizes bools, and refuses immutable vars with 'renaming the world needs sudo gvm rebuild <n> --confirm <n>' (rebuild keeps its typed confirmation and archive-first, rule 5). It writes through hostops VerbFileWrite and prints 'takes effect on: <apply class> — run sudo gvm restart <n>'. When the heap var changes and spec.resources still equals what ComputeResources gave for the OLD vars, recompute it and show old -> new. If the resources were hand-edited, keep them and refuse when the new heap plus native_headroom exceeds MemoryMax, unless --resources=recompute or an explicit override flag is passed. Put the strict checks in a separate apply-only validation step, not in ParseInstance: unknown fields and ValidateInstance errors block apply. Immutables are compared against a last-applied snapshot in /etc/gvm/instances/<n>.d/applied.yaml. The 'EDITED' sizing note becomes a '!' line in the plan. `gvm run` keeps today's lenient load.

### game-catalog-config-5 — No way to see a game's settings, ports, requirements or description before creating it

**High** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **26** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The `gvm game` subcommands are only validate, list, which and hooks (internal/cli/game.go:31-41). `gvm create vh-1 --game valheim --var password=hunter22` -> 'valheim declares no variable "password"; `gvm game validate valheim` lists them' (create.go:261), but `gvm game validate valheim` only prints '✓ 4 vars, memory_model flat — MemoryHigh 2688M, MemoryMax 3G at the declared defaults' (game.go:356-366). No command prints metadata.doc (grep Metadata.Doc: no hits), and VarSpec help/apply/immutable/min/max/group/unit appear only in the missing-required-var error (create.go:290). `gvm game list` prints id, name, 'revision N' and the source path, with no header, no RAM or disk floor, no 'needs Proton' and no required vars. It also lists the template generic-steam and two nearly identical 7DTD entries without guidance; 7daystodie.yaml's doc still says telnet rungs fail 'until' v1.1. Valheim's required public_name has no help, so the error shows only '--var public_name=…'.

**Impact.** To learn which --var keys exist, their allowed values, which ones are permanent, and which ports and passwords a game uses, the operator has to find and read raw manifest YAML, which on a fresh box is embedded in the binary. Choosing between games is guesswork.

**Suggestion.** Add a read-only `gvm game show <id>` that works offline from the embedded floor. It should print the description (metadata.doc), RAM and disk floor, provider (Steam / Windows-under-Proton / Minecraft), ports with expose and notes, secrets (join/rcon/admin), and a vars table: name, type, default, required, values/min-max, apply badge (live/restart/rebuild), immutable, help. End it with a ready-to-paste `sudo gvm create <name> --game <id> --var ...` line. Point create's undeclared-var error at `gvm game show`. Give `gvm game list` a header plus RAM-floor and tags columns, and hide template-tagged manifests unless `--all` is passed. Add `help:` to every required var in the shipped manifests.

### game-catalog-config-6 — `sudo gvm create --help` panics with a Go stack trace; `gvm help <cmd>` shows no flags

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** In the overlay harness with a host env (the Linux-root path), running `create -h` prints the usage and then: 'panic: instance: instance: invalid name: a name is required; call ValidateName before deriving names from operator input' (stack: create.go:395 PathsFor -> instance.MustValidName). The cause: parseCreateArgs returns `createOptions{}, flagErr(err)` (create.go:127-130), and flagErr maps flag.ErrHelp to nil (game.go:613-618), so runCreate carries on with an empty name. On this Windows build, `gvm.exe create --help` prints the usage and then 'gvm create: gvm: no transport for this command...' with rc=1. `gvm apply --help` behaves the same way. `gvm help create` prints only 'gvm create — define a new instance' (root.go:256-265), although the top-level help ends with 'Run "gvm help <command>" for detail.' The flag help shows '-game gvm game list' because Go treats the backticked text in create.go:114 as the argument name.

**Impact.** The first discovery command a new operator types on the box crashes, and the advertised help path hides every create flag.

**Suggestion.** Return a sentinel such as errHelpShown from each parse*Args on flag.ErrHelp and have the run function exit 0 right away. Make `gvm help <cmd>` print the command's full usage (for example by invoking it with --help into stdout). Reword the -game usage to 'game id (see gvm game list) (required)'. Add a table test that `<cmd> --help` exits 0 and touches no HostEnv for every command.

### game-catalog-config-7 — Nothing tells players (or the operator) how to connect, or that a new server is public and passwordless

**Medium** · player · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: 29 → **33** → 49 · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm ports` already shows per-instance port occupancy (internal/cli/ports.go:233-246); it has no join semantics, address or password/public state. internal/cli/root.go:101: `web` Run: notImplemented. (orig: printCreated (create.go:483-518) lists raw port numbers but no address and no indication of which port players type. lifecycle/status.go:180-234 (`gvm status`) prints phase, unit, pid and players, with no ports or address. The dashboard detail page renders only Game and Status (internal/web/handlers.go:287), even though InstanceDetail has Addr and Port fields (internal/web/server.go:42-43). The manifest schema has no 'join port' concept: Valheim exposes game 2456 and query 2457, and PZ needs both 16261 and 16262 open. Defaults: valheim.yaml:115 `public: "true"` with an optional join password (valheim.yaml:41-42), and projectzomboid.yaml:259-262 public/open "true". create never mentions access.)

**Impact.** The operator has to work out and relay address:port and the password by hand, and players cannot check for themselves. An operator running a server for friends may only learn it is listed publicly with no password when strangers join.

**Suggestion.** Add a non-templated block, `connect: {port: game, also_open: [direct], public_var: public, password_secret: join_password, whitelist_var: ..., note: "..."}`, and exclude it from the runtime template scan. Compute the address at display time from host config (gvm.yaml public address, else the primary IPv4 read locally). Add a read-only `gvm connect <inst>` that needs no gvmd, a 'join' section in `gvm status`, and Addr/Port plus 'join password: set / not set' and 'listed publicly: yes/no' on the authenticated dashboard detail page. The value stays root-only through `sudo gvm secret show`. End `gvm create` with an Access line. With finding 2's default this line reads 'join password: generated (shown once above)', or 'none — anyone can join' when --open was passed. Do not add any unauthenticated player status page.

### game-catalog-config-8 — The dashboard cannot show or change any server setting

**Medium** · dashboard-user · missing-feature · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 5, 9 · Plan: 20 → **49** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/root.go:100-101: `gvmd` and `web` are Run: notImplemented. The binary run confirms 'gvm web: not implemented yet' (rc=3). (orig: internal/web/routes.go:22-58 registers only the instance list, detail, console, and start/stop/restart routes. There is no config or vars route, and the config_handlers.go listed for Phase 16 (IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:765-772) does not exist. ARCHITECTURE.md:6106 describes an 'Instance → Config' form generated from spec.vars with help tooltips, apply badges and read-only immutables. spec/capability.go:447-468 still derives config.edit as available, which `gvm control caps` would report as 'yes' although no verb exists.)

**Impact.** A moderator cannot raise max players, change the MOTD or difficulty, or rotate the join password without SSH. All the help, apply and immutable metadata written into the manifests never reaches the person editing.

**Suggestion.** Now: make spec.Capabilities report config.edit as unavailable ('this build has no settings editor; use sudo gvm set') until a verb exists. Later, once findings 1 and 4 have shipped: add a Settings tab generated from VarSpec (group, help, unit, apply badge), gated by a config.edit permission. Save posts to gvmd, which calls the same validated set service and writes the YAML through hostops. Send the YAML's sha256 as an If-Match token so a concurrent hand edit is refused instead of clobbered. Then show the `apply --diff` plan and a 'Restart to apply' button for restart-class vars; the restart goes through the ladder on an explicit click. Immutable and rebuild-class vars are read-only on the web, labelled 'change with sudo gvm rebuild over SSH'. Join-password rotation is a write-only gvmd action that never returns the value to gvm-web. The CLI path stays complete with the web tier down.

### game-catalog-config-9 — No way to move an instance to a newer manifest revision; status hints point at a command that does not exist

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5, 6 · Plan: 20 → **39** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm manifest upgrade pz-main --to 9 --dry` -> 'gvm: unknown command "manifest"'. Yet lifecycle/derive.go:168 and lifecycle/verbs.go:206 give 'gvm manifest upgrade <name> --dry' as the hint for a pin mismatch, as do content/lock.go:245 and ARCHITECTURE.md:137/2108. pinnedManifest (cli/instance.go:510-521) reads the catalog only when no pin exists, so an existing instance never sees a newer revision. spec.Diff with classified changes and RequiresRestart (spec/diff.go) is implemented but no CLI command uses it.

**Impact.** When gvm ships a fixed manifest (a corrected readiness marker or shutdown ladder), existing servers stay on the old revision forever unless destroyed and recreated. An instance that fails on a pin mismatch tells the operator to run a command that does not exist.

**Suggestion.** `sudo gvm manifest upgrade <inst> [--to N] [--dry]`. --dry prints spec.Diff classified into argv / destroy_globs / ports / shutdown / cosmetic, plus a ValidateInstance result against the new revision: new required vars with no default, removed vars, port changes. A real run needs `--confirm <inst>` when argv, destroy_globs, world_paths or ports change. It takes a verified `preserve` archive first and never prunes it. It writes the new lock and the new instance pin through hostops as one step and keeps the old lock as manifest.lock.rev<N>.yaml for rollback. It never starts, stops or restarts; it prints 'takes effect on next start: sudo gvm apply <n> && sudo gvm restart <n>'. `gvm list` and `game which` only report 'N instances pinned to an older revision' (read-only, so rule 6 holds). Until the command ships, change the three hints to describe the manual path or to say 'not available in this build'.

### game-catalog-config-10 — Adding a game: the documented approve/try loop does not exist, and an override in /etc/gvm/games is silently skipped

**Medium** · manifest-author · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1 · Plan: 20 → 32 → **46** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Additional evidence: internal/cli/instance.go:531 `h.catalog().Load(in.Spec.Game, overrideAuth(false))`. internal/cli/apply.go has no unapproved-override flag. (orig: generic-steam.yaml:9-22 says 'Copy this file to /etc/gvm/games/<yourgame>.yaml' and run `gvm game try <id> --scratch`. ARCHITECTURE.md:3142-3160 shows `gvm game publish`, `gvm game try` and `gvm instance create`. Binary: `gvm game try generic-steam --scratch` -> 'gvm game: unknown subcommand "try"'; `gvm game approve x` and `gvm game publish x.yaml` -> unknown subcommand. spec/load.go:243-252 skips every unapproved /etc/gvm/games override with the fix text '`gvm game approve <id>` after reviewing it, or pass --unapproved-override', naming a command that does not exist. The CLI only knows DenyOverrides and RootCLIAuthorizer (game.go:80-85). createExec drops the warnings and checks only diags.HasErrors() (create.go:401-407), so a new id fails with 'no usable manifest: "<id>": every candidate was skipped' and no hint. An override of a shipped id silently falls back to the embedded manifest; the only clue is '(embedded)' in the create output. `gvm game list` reports such a file as 'unreadable:' (game.go:483-489).)

**Impact.** Following the template's own instructions either fails with no hint or quietly creates a server from the stock manifest instead of the author's edit. The only way to test readiness markers and shutdown ladders is a real instance.

**Suggestion.** Add `sudo gvm game approve <id>`: show the sha256, the origin, and a classified spec.Diff against the layer it shadows (or 'new game id'), and require typing the id. Record `<sha256>` in /etc/gvm/games/.approved/<id> (directory 0700 root:root, file 0600) so that any later edit invalidates the approval automatically; the store may mirror it as a log entry only. Have create and apply print catalog warnings, and append the approve / --unapproved-override hint to 'every candidate was skipped'. Report the override as 'not approved (sha …)' in `gvm game list` instead of 'unreadable'. Remove `gvm game try`/`publish` from generic-steam.yaml and the docs until they exist; schedule `try --scratch` as its own phase.

### game-catalog-config-11 — Shipped manifests teach a port_stride shape the schema silently ignores; the template validates clean and is listed as runnable

**Low** · manifest-author · ergonomics · effort S · fact: partial · fit: adjust · Plan: 26 → **46** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Argv carries every port: rust.yaml:41/43/55, cs2.yaml:54/56, vrising.yaml:50/52/55, satisfactory.yaml:48-50, ark-se.yaml:46/48/49. `gvm game validate rust` reports 1 warning (websocket_rcon not implemented), not 0. (orig: games/ark-se.yaml:92, cs2.yaml:99, enshrouded.yaml:71, rust.yaml:105, satisfactory.yaml:75 and vrising.yaml:97 use `port_stride: {base: N, count: M}`. The schema is a group->stride map (ARCHITECTURE.md:1506, `PortStride map[string]int`). None of these manifests sets `group:` on a port, so spec.PortRequirements turns each port into its own group (spec/ports.go:66-84). validate.go:568-575 only checks groups that ports name and never flags unused port_stride keys, so `gvm game validate rust` prints '✓ 3 ports, no groups' with 0 warnings. `gvm game validate generic-steam` -> '0 errors, 0 warnings.' even though it contains REPLACE-ME placeholders and install_appid 999999999, and it appears in `gvm game list` beside real games.)

**Impact.** An author who copies rust.yaml learns a block that does nothing, and ports that must stay together (query 'usually game+1') are not grouped. A forgotten REPLACE-ME passes validation. An operator can `gvm create` the template.

**Suggestion.** Key off the existing `template` tag: validate warns on any REPLACE-ME token in a template-tagged manifest and errors on one that is not template-tagged; `gvm create` refuses a template-tagged id with 'copy it to /etc/gvm/games/<id>.yaml and change metadata.id first'; `gvm game list` hides it unless --all is passed. Make validate warn on port_stride keys that no port's group names (with the 'set group: on the ports that move together' fix text), and make the shipped-manifest test treat that warning as a failure. Fix the six manifests with real `group:` entries and a revision bump each.

### game-catalog-config-12 — Proton-based games need an absolute proton_path that nothing explains or checks

**Low** · operator · ergonomics · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: 26 → 32 → **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** ark-se, enshrouded, satisfactory and vrising declare a required `proton_path` var with no pattern ('Absolute path to the Proton binary'; satisfactory.yaml:103 gives an example path). resolveVars (create.go:258-298) and checkVarValue (spec/validate.go:1046-1091) only type-check it, and no step checks that the file exists. `gvm game list` does not mark these as Windows-under-Proton. enshrouded.yaml also exposes no name, password or slot settings, and its runtime.args pass only -savedir, so allocated ports never reach the game.

**Impact.** Someone picking Satisfactory or Enshrouded has to find and install a Proton build on their own (gvm rightly never installs packages, D-41) and type a path. A typo surfaces only when the server fails to start.

**Suggestion.** Add `pattern: ^/.+/proton$`. In create, apply and `gvm doctor`, do a read-only lstat/stat: the file exists, is a regular executable, every parent directory is searchable by others, and the path is not under /home, /root or /run/user (otherwise warn: 'ProtectHome=yes hides this from gvm@<n>; install Proton under /opt or /usr/share/steam/compatibilitytools.d'). List candidates only from system locations (/usr/share/steam/compatibilitytools.d, /opt/*/proton). Label these games 'Windows server under Proton — install Proton yourself' in `game list` and `game show`. Never install anything.

### game-catalog-config-13 — Manifest-author reference material is buried: hooks listed without descriptions, no schema/format listing, control-flow error hides the rest

**Low** · manifest-author · docs · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 10 · Plan: **46** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm game hooks` prints only names plus 'ready' (for example 'steam.home_layout  ready'), although every hook implements Doc() (internal/hook/*.go) and validators already print their docs. The codec formats, probe kinds and when-guard fields appear only inside error messages ("ini" is not a config format (want java_properties|json_path|...)). Validating a manifest with one {{ if }} stopped at '1 error, 0 warnings'; six more errors appeared only after it was removed. Its fix text ('...a config key is a plain string, so render it unconditionally...') talks about config keys although the field was spec.runtime.args[2]. The schema lives only in ARCHITECTURE.md §8 (lines 1444-2234 of a 7148-line spec), and the README has no 'add a game' pointer.

**Impact.** Authors have to read Go source or a 7,000-line architecture document to learn what a hook does or which formats exist, and fixing errors takes extra edit/validate rounds.

**Suggestion.** Have `gvm game hooks` print each hook's Doc() and phases. Add `gvm game schema [ports|vars|config_files|readiness|when]`, or a docs/manifests.md field reference with codecs, probe kinds, when-guards and a worked example, linked from the README. For control flow inside spec.runtime.args, show the concrete rewrite `{value: "-pvp", when: {var_true: pvp}}`. Keep running the other checks after a template-guard failure.


---

<a id="web-dashboard"></a>
## Web dashboard and API (`web-dashboard`)

For anyone without root SSH, the dashboard and API do not exist yet. `gvm web` and `gvm gvmd` are hidden and exit 3 with "not implemented yet". No gvmd.service or gvm-web.service unit is shipped, and internal/web, internal/auth, internal/authz and internal/identity are not linked into the gvm binary at all. The Phase 16 code is a skeleton, and wiring it up as written would be harmful. Pages are served to anonymous "guest" visitors. Start/Stop/Restart return 202 "accepted" and do nothing, for any role and any instance name. The browser's own buttons hit a 401 because the API wants a bearer token, while a bearer-only client (GVM_TOKEN) also gets a 401 because the middleware only reads cookies. The implemented role matrix also lets an "operator" rebuild, restore and prune. On the CLI side, the remote path (`--remote`, GVM_URL/GVM_TOKEN, `gvm token create`) and the whole Administration group (user/token/secret/audit) are advertised in help and errors but end in errors that send the operator looking for a gvmd daemon that does not exist. So today a friend or moderator can only be helped by sharing root, and gvm then cannot record who acted. Docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §15-16 is a strong, user-centred blueprint. The plan should be: make every unbuilt path say so honestly now, then ship a small, correctly authenticated dashboard v0 (login, status, start/stop/restart through gvmd, console with backlog and send) built on lifecycle.Status, which the CLI already computes.

**Strengths to preserve**

- The CLI transport ladder fails loudly and never quietly downgrades. parseRemote refuses plain http to a non-loopback host, and the bearer token is held in a secrets.Value so it cannot leak through fmt or error strings (internal/cli/transport.go:85-90, 196-216).
- The CLI never dials /run/gvm/admin.sock, and a test keeps it that way (transport.go:29-32). Root-local verbs run in-process, so every CLI command keeps working with gvmd, the web tier and Postgres dead.
- The datastore-down 503 page tells the reader that the game servers are NOT affected and gives the exact sudo commands to use instead (internal/auth/auth.go:172-191). This is the right tone for a non-developer at 2am.
- The console socket's peer-credential tiers separate the data plane (tail/send/status/caps) from lifecycle ops (verb/stop is root only). PeerInfo is derived from the same Allows() table, so a dashboard can never show an enabled Stop button that the socket would refuse (internal/socket/auth.go:84-115).
- Ring/Hub backpressure never blocks the game: a slow browser loses lines and is told so, it does not stall the runner (internal/console/ring.go:44-49). The SSE handler sends keepalives and X-Accel-Buffering: no, and the console page inserts lines with textContent, so a mod printing HTML cannot inject script (handlers.go:315-326).
- lifecycle.Status already carries phase, reason, a '->' hint, players online/max, drift, the lease (job in progress) and ready time, and serialises through `gvm status --json`. It is ready to be the dashboard's data model (internal/lifecycle/status.go:22-73).
- `gvm firewall` honestly exits 3 with 'not implemented yet', which is the right pattern to copy for every other unbuilt verb.
- Spec §16.4/§16.6/§16.7 already designs the user-facing behaviours that matter: a two-step plan plus typed-name confirmation for destructive actions, a console input that refuses quit/stop with a pointer to the Stop button, a 'starting - 14m elapsed' readiness display, and copy-able recovery commands on the backups page.

### web-dashboard-1 — There is no dashboard or API to run: no non-SSH path exists for friends or moderators

**High** · dashboard-user · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 3, 4, 6, 8, 9 · Plan: **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/root.go:101-102 registers `gvmd` and `web` as Hidden with Run: notImplemented. Command `gvm.exe web` printed "gvm web: not implemented yet" exit=3, and `gvm.exe gvmd` gave the same. packaging/systemd/ contains only gvm@.service, gvm-job@*, the timers and slices, with no gvmd.service or gvm-web.service (the Phase 12 plan lists both). `go list -deps ./cmd/gvm` includes internal/console and internal/socket but NOT internal/web, internal/auth, internal/authz or internal/identity, and no package in the repo imports them. Nothing implements web.InstanceQuerier or auth.SessionQuerier: grep for ListInstances/GetInstance/LookupSession outside internal/web and internal/auth finds nothing. console.Hub.Attach is never called. README.md:28-33 still lists 'D | Dashboard, RBAC, audit, API, Caddy, firewall' under 'What works'.

**Impact.** The README and help text promise a dashboard, but there is nothing to install or start. An operator who wants a moderator to restart the server or watch the console has one option: hand out SSH and root.

**Suggestion.** Add a 'Milestone D0: Dashboard v0' section to IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md with these prerequisites and scope.
(a) Root-CLI `sudo gvm user create` with TOTP enrolment, per §14.9. This is in-process and never touches admin.sock.
(b) The §15.2 owner/admin/operator/viewer matrix with instance_grants.
(c) gvmd.service (root) with a closed verb enum: status, start, stop, restart and auth.verify_totp. The verbs call the same hostops functions as the root CLI, so stop always goes through the ladder. gvmd's startup must not call reconcile actuation or auto-resume a lease. It must not run migrations: it calls AssertCompatible and refuses auth verbs when the schema is behind.
(d) gvm-web.service (User=gvm, SupplementaryGroups=gvm-console, no LoadCredential) on /run/gvm/web/dashboard.sock.
(e) /login, /login/totp, the empty-users page, the instance list and detail pages, start/stop/restart, and console read and send.
(f) The single-vhost Caddyfile.
Add golden-units.sh assertions that gvm@.service and gvm-job@.service have no Requires=, Wants= or After= on gvmd, gvm-web or postgresql. Change the README table to 'Milestone | Status | What it gives you', with D marked 'not yet built'.

### web-dashboard-2 — Start/Stop/Restart endpoints report success but do nothing, for any role and any instance name

**Medium** · dashboard-user · safety-ux · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4, 6 · Plan: **47** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/web/handlers.go:224-251: handleAPIStart/Stop/Restart only validate CSRF and then `writeJSON(w, http.StatusAccepted, {"status":"accepted"})`. They make no gvmd call, no authz.Check and no instance lookup. I ran a go test overlay probe from scratch (the repo was not modified) with a session whose Role is 'viewer'. `POST /api/v1/instances/pz-main/stop` returned `202 {"action":"stop","name":"pz-main","status":"accepted"}`. `POST /api/v1/instances/does-not-exist/restart` returned `202 {"action":"restart","name":"does-not-exist","status":"accepted"}`.

**Impact.** Once the server is wired up, a moderator who clicks Restart on a crashed server is told it was accepted. Nothing happens, players stay offline, and nobody knows why. Read-only users get the same fake success, and a mistyped instance name looks like it worked.

**Suggestion.** For now: have every mutation route return 501 with the envelope {code:'not_implemented', message:'Restart from the dashboard is not in this release; on the host run: sudo gvm restart <name>'}.
When wired, the steps are: resolve the instance through the authz ActorScope (404 when the actor cannot view it); require() instance:start/stop/restart; dispatch the verb to gvmd. Return 202 only after gvmd acknowledges the dispatch, carrying {request_id, operation, phase_before}. Return 409 conflict with the lease line and its hint when a job lease is held; never queue or auto-resume. The browser twin answers 303 to /i/{name} with a flash such as 'Stop requested: saving the world (ladder: save, quit, wait up to 180s)'. Live progress comes over the status SSE event. Add TestNoMutationReturns2xxWithoutDispatch, which uses a recording fake gvmd client.

### web-dashboard-3 — Dashboard pages are served to anonymous visitors, and the /login page the middleware redirects to does not exist

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: **47** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Probe: GET /instances with Cookie gvm_sess=stale returned 200 (page rendered, cookie not cleared), not a redirect. auth.go:132's redirect is effectively dead code because every wrapped route is under /api/v1, where line 127 returns a 401 instead. (orig: internal/web/routes.go:31-37 registers GET /instances, /instances/{name} and /instances/{name}/console without s.mw.Wrap, even though the comment at routes.go:10 says 'The auth middleware wraps all routes'. handlers.go:330-338 renders 'guest' when there is no session. In the probe, an unauthenticated `GET /instances` returned 200 with `<span>guest</span>` and the pz-main row. `GET /instances/pz-main` returned 200 with start/stop/restart forms and CSRF tokens. `GET /instances/no-such-server/console` returned 200 with a console page for a server that does not exist. auth.go:132 redirects expired sessions to /login, but the probe's `GET /login` returned `404 404 page not found`. This contradicts rule 9 and docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:59 ('Postgres up, no session | redirect to login').)

**Impact.** If this code is deployed behind Caddy as written, anyone on the internet can list the servers and open console pages. That is the unauthenticated read-only mode the design forbids. A real user whose session expires lands on a 404 instead of a login form.

**Suggestion.** Put every browser route behind a requireAuth middleware that redirects to `/login?next=<path>` (htmx requests get 401 plus HX-Redirect). Leave only /healthz and /static public. Implement GET/POST /login and /login/totp, with TOTP verified in gvmd, not gvm-web. When the users table is empty, serve only the page carrying the exact `sudo gvm user create --email ... --role owner` line (§14.9). Add TestBrowserRoutesRequireSession, which walks the mux and asserts that every non-public route returns 303 to /login without a cookie. Also have the console page 404 on an unknown instance.

### web-dashboard-4 — The implemented role matrix lets 'operator' (the natural moderator role) rebuild worlds, restore over them and prune backups

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: partial · fit: sound · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: **47** · Status: open

**Evidence.** grep 'authz\.' across the repo excluding internal/authz returns zero hits, so Check() and the matrix are never consulted. Spec §15.2 (ARCHITECTURE.md:5814-5830) puts ConsoleRead in viewerPerms; matrix.go puts PermInstanceConsole under RoleOperator (line 30). (orig: internal/authz/matrix.go:26-48 gives RoleOperator PermInstanceRebuild (line 35), PermBackupRestore (39), PermBackupPrune (40), PermAdoptCutover (43), PermAdoptFinalize (45), PermSecretRotate (46), PermInstanceApply (32) and PermContentEdit (36). RoleViewer gets PermSecretView (24), PermAuditView (22) and PermDoctorRun (19). There is no owner role (role.go:12-16 has viewer, operator and admin only) and no per-instance grant in Check(). The spec says otherwise: §15.1 (ARCHITECTURE.md:5741) says operators 'Cannot read secrets, cannot rebuild, cannot delete, cannot change what is declared' on granted instances only, and viewers see secrets as '••••'. §14.9's bootstrap is `gvm user create --role owner`. Running `gvm.exe user create --help` prints `-role string  global role: viewer|operator|admin`.)

**Impact.** An operator who reads the role names and makes a friend 'operator' so they can restart the server has also, unknowingly, let them restore an old archive over the live world, prune backups or finalize an adoption. A 'viewer' given to a player could reveal secrets. There is also no way to limit a moderator to just their own server.

**Suggestion.** Replace matrix.go with the §15.2 cumulative matrix: owner > admin > operator > viewer. Operator gets only start/stop/restart/update/console:write/content:sync/backup:create/job:run. Restore, rebuild, prune, apply, adopt and secrets go to admin or owner. Add per-instance grants (`sudo gvm user grant alice@x pz-main operator`). In the UI and in `gvm user create --help`, show what each role can do, e.g. 'operator: start/stop/restart/update and use the console on granted servers; cannot restore, rebuild or delete'. Destructive routes keep the typed-name plus archive-first contract on top of RBAC.

### web-dashboard-5 — The auth wiring rejects both the browser's own buttons and the CLI's GVM_TOKEN, yet accepts any bearer string when a cookie is present

**Medium** · dashboard-user · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: **47** · Status: open

**Evidence.** handlers.go:293-298 makes the detail page's buttons plain HTML forms posting to /api/v1/instances/{name}/{action}. auth.go:143-151 returns 401 on /api/v1 when a cookie session has no `Authorization: Bearer` prefix, and it only checks the prefix; the token is never verified. auth.go:95-104 returns 401 on /api/v1 when there is no cookie, so a bearer-only request fails too. handlers.go:225 requires a session-bound CSRF token even for bearer callers. Probe results: a cookie-only form POST to `/api/v1/instances/pz-main/stop` gave `401 {"error":"bearer token required"}`. A bearer-only `GET /api/v1/instances` with `Bearer gvmt_realtoken` gave `401 {"error":"authentication required"}`. A cookie plus `Authorization: Bearer anything-at-all` gave `200` with the instance list. internal/web and internal/auth have no tests (no routes_test/api_cookie_test/csrf_test, which Phase 16's definition of done requires).

**Impact.** A dashboard user who clicks Stop gets a raw JSON 401 page. An operator who sets GVM_URL/GVM_TOKEN is always refused. Meanwhile the bearer check is cosmetic. Neither intended client can work as designed.

**Suggestion.** Follow §16.2 and D-40 literally, with two separate muxes. The browser mux uses form POSTs at /i/{name}/start|stop|restart, cookie session plus http.NewCrossOriginProtection, and answers with 303 back to the instance page and a flash message ('Stop requested: saving...'). The /api/v1 mux ignores cookies, verifies the bearer by SHA-256 lookup in api_tokens (identity.HashToken), clamps to the token's role ceiling, and needs no CSRF. Add TestAPIIgnoresCookies (cookie with no bearer gives 401), TestBearerIsVerified (an unknown token gives 401), and a test that every browser form action points into the browser mux.

### web-dashboard-6 — The Administration commands (user/token/secret/audit) are listed in help but all send the operator after a gvmd daemon that does not exist

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 3 · Plan: 20 → **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** README.md:35-36 reads 'exit non-zero with `not implemented`'; it does not specify exit code 3. There are also 4 call sites outside user.go plus 9 in user.go, matching the 17 total. (orig: `gvm.exe help` lists 'Administration: user, token, secret, audit'. `gvm.exe user create --email a@b.c --role operator` printed "gvm user: user create: requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running (`systemctl status gvmd`)" exit=1. `token create --name laptop`, `audit tail` and `secret show-path --name rcon --instance pz-main` print the same sentence. The source is errRequiresDaemon in internal/cli/user.go:138-143, used 17 times across user.go, token.go, secret.go and auditcmd.go. gvmd has no unit file, and `gvm gvmd` gives 'not implemented yet'. token.go:41 and user.go:62 parse --role and then discard it (`_ = role`). auditcmd.go:10-14 says 'Postgres connectivity is NOT required -- the spool is the durable offline copy', yet line 46 demands gvmd. README.md:35-36 promises unbuilt commands 'exit non-zero with `not implemented`', but these exit 1, not 3. `gvm.exe user --help` prints 'user: unknown subcommand "--help"'.)

**Impact.** The first step to enabling the dashboard is creating a user, and it cannot be done. The error sends the operator to `systemctl status gvmd`, which will report 'Unit gvmd.service could not be found', and they lose time debugging a service that was never shipped. It also breaks the 'every CLI command works over SSH as root' promise for audit and secrets.

**Suggestion.** Now: make errRequiresDaemon wrap ErrNotImplemented (exit 3) with the text '<verb>: not available in this release; dashboard users arrive with Milestone D0 (docs/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md)'. Mark user, token, secret and audit '(not yet available)' in the usage text. Fix the 'every command below works...' sentence so it excludes them. Make `gvm <group> --help` and `-h` print the subcommand list with exit 0.
Then, in the D0 milestone: implement `sudo gvm user create|grant|reset-totp` in-process over peer-auth Postgres. Read the password with term.ReadPassword. Seal the TOTP secret with master.key, which root can read. Refuse with 'Postgres is unreachable; users are stored there. Your game servers are not affected' when the database is down.
Implement `gvm audit tail` from audit_log when Postgres is up. When it is down, fall back to the spool with the banner 'Postgres down: showing only records not yet replayed'. Implement `gvm secret show-path` from the filestore (D-28 keeps game secrets as files).
Leave `token create` as not_implemented until the API client ships.

### web-dashboard-9 — The web console is a read-only pane with one line of history, no server state, and no path from the runner

**Medium** · dashboard-user · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The subscribe call is at internal/console/hub.go:48, not :47. (orig: handlers.go:302-328: the console page has a <div> and an EventSource only, with no send box. hub.go:47 subscribes at `ring.Head()`, and Ring.Subscribe (ring.go:303-325) delivers backlog from `from` onward, so a new viewer gets only the newest line. When no ring is attached, hub.go:37-44 returns a closed channel, the handler emits `event: close {"reason":"ring closed"}` (handlers.go:205-207; probe output confirmed), and the page's close listener shows nothing. src.onerror appends '[connection lost]' on every reconnect, and Last-Event-ID/?from= are ignored. Hub.Attach takes an in-process *Ring, but rings live in the `gvm run` process and no console-socket dialer feeds the hub. Compare `gvm.exe console --help`: '-backlog int lines of history ... (default 200)', '-timestamps', '-actor'.)

**Impact.** A moderator opening the console of a quiet or stopped server sees an empty black box, with no hint that the server is stopped. They cannot type a command such as 'save' or a broadcast, so they still need SSH for the one thing they most often need to do.

**Suggestion.** Implement §16.6 as written: hello{state, cursor, oldest}, a default 200-line backlog through the socket's tail op, Last-Event-ID taking precedence over ?from=, and lagged, truncated and resync rendered as '— N lines skipped —'. Serve SSE under /stream/i/{name}/console.
For a stopped server: do not read console.log from gvm-web. Show a banner built from lifecycle.Status (phase, reason, hint) and the last shutdown record ('stopped cleanly 2h ago by alice'). If a backlog is really wanted, add a bounded read-only gvmd verb, console.log_tail with at most 200 lines and RBAC console:read, so file permissions stay unchanged.
Send box: POST /i/{name}/console, requiring console:write, writing the audit row first, single line of at most 1024 bytes. Refuse quit, stop, shutdown, exit and every command word in the manifest's own shutdown ladder, with the message 'Use the Stop button: it saves the world first'.

### web-dashboard-11 — The edge config cannot reach the dashboard: route names, upstream and listener disagree

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: 47 → **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/firewall/desired.go:32-44 does consume Edge.Mode (it opens 80/443). There is a double prefix: edge.go:71 `upstream := "unix/" + ... + "/web.sock"` plus render.go:65/71 `reverse_proxy unix/{{ .Upstream }}`. (orig: internal/caddyconf/render.go:55-71 exempts only `/stream/*` from `encode gzip zstd` and gives only that path flush_interval -1, but the web's SSE route is `/api/v1/instances/{name}/console/events` (routes.go:53). Under the template's own comment, that stream would be compressed and buffered. internal/edge/edge.go:62-73 proxies every enabled instance to `<home>/web.sock` on `<instance>.<site>`, not to the dashboard socket /run/gvm/web/dashboard.sock declared in internal/spec/host.go:143. internal/web/server.go:99 listens with `net.Listen("tcp", s.cfg.Addr)`, not on the unix socket. edge and caddyconf are imported only by each other and their tests, so `edge.mode: caddy_managed` in /etc/gvm/gvm.yaml currently has no effect.)

**Impact.** An operator who follows §17 and sets a site name gets no dashboard vhost. Once the pieces are wired, the live console would look frozen behind Caddy, the exact failure the comments warn about, and would be hard to diagnose.

**Suggestion.** Make edge.Desired emit a single vhost for edge.site that reverse-proxies to unix//run/gvm/web/dashboard.sock. Fix the double 'unix/' prefix and delete the per-instance <home>/web.sock routes.
Move every SSE route under /stream/ and /api/v1/stream/, give both prefixes `handle` blocks with flush_interval -1, and add a test that walks both muxes and asserts every text/event-stream route matches a Caddyfile stream matcher.
gvm-web listens only on the unix socket. A loopback TCP listener exists only behind `gvm web --dev`, which logs a warning; there is no edge.listen in production config.
`gvm apply` renders /etc/gvm/caddy/Caddyfile but never installs Caddy. Until `gvm caddy install` exists, the doctor hint is 'edge.site is set but caddy is not installed — install Caddy >= 2.11.1 from the official apt repo (see docs/network-edge.md)'.

### web-dashboard-12 — The docs and doctor describe gvmd and dashboard behaviour that has not been built

**Medium** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6, 8 · Plan: 20 → **24** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Also docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:30 ('reconciles systemd/ufw/Caddy'), which contradicts the unwired internal/edge and internal/caddyconf. (orig: docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:42-49 states 'gvmd starts and listens on its unix socket regardless of Postgres state ... Verbs that do not need the database (start, stop, console) work normally'. Lines 55-59 say 'Postgres up, no session | redirect to login', which the probe contradicts (an anonymous GET /instances returns 200 and /login is a 404). The Phase 17 definition of done says this doc records 'measured' behaviour. internal/doctor/checks_host_db.go:270 tells the operator to 'restart gvmd — it calls ReapStaleRuns when the pool becomes ready', but there is no gvmd unit and nothing calls store.ReapStaleRuns in production (grep shows only its definition). README.md:33 lists the Dashboard under 'What works'.)

**Impact.** An operator who trusts the docs plans around a dashboard and gvmd that are absent. They then follow a doctor fix ('restart gvmd') that fails with 'Unit gvmd.service could not be found', which undermines trust in doctor's other hints.

**Suggestion.** Mark the gvmd and gvm-web rows of docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md 'Planned (Milestone D0) — not in this release'. Change the README status table to show done or not-done per milestone.
HOST-011: count only `state='running' AND boot_id <> current`. Its fix becomes 'sudo gvm db replay-spool (also marks previous-boot runs failed)', and replay-spool calls store.ReapStaleRuns in-process.
Add TestDoctorFixesNameRealThings. It parses every doctor Fix string; any `gvm <verb> [<sub>]` must resolve to a registered command that does not return ErrNotImplemented, and any `systemctl ... <unit>` must name a unit in packaging/systemd or a known OS unit (postgresql, caddy, ufw).

### web-dashboard-7 — The non-root error and the --remote flag on 15 commands advertise a remote path that dead-ends after three tries

**Low** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 3 · Plan: **20** · Status: open

**Evidence.** There are 14 flag registrations, not 15. internal/cli/transport.go:47 is the ModeRemote constant, not a flag. (orig: `gvm.exe start pz-main` (non-root) prints: "`gvm start` is a host operation. Run it as root (sudo gvm start), or point it at a dashboard with --remote / GVM_URL plus GVM_TOKEN (`gvm token create` mints one)." and then a second paragraph: "gvm deliberately does not dial /run/gvm/admin.sock: that socket is 0660 root:gvm, so a CLI that used it would hand the whole root verb surface to every member of group gvm" (transport.go:154-159). Following the advice: `GVM_URL=https://gvm.example.com gvm start pz-main` gives "no GVM_TOKEN is set; mint one with `gvm token create` on that host". `gvm token create` gives 'requires gvmd'. Setting both variables finally gives "the remote transport is not built in this release" (transport.go:174-175). 15 flag registrations (apply, backup, content, create, destroy, install, job x3, list, logs, ports, start, status) advertise `-remote string  dashboard URL to run this against instead of the local host`.)

**Impact.** An operator who simply forgot sudo gets a lecture about socket permissions. One who tries the remote route spends three round trips and an attempt at a token before learning it cannot work in this build.

**Suggestion.** While ErrRemoteUnbuilt holds: (a) rule-4 errors say only 'gvm start needs root on the host: sudo gvm start pz-main'; (b) when GVM_URL or --remote is present, fail immediately with 'this gvm build has no API client yet; unset GVM_URL and run it as root on the host'; (c) hide --remote from flag help, or suffix it '(not available in this release)'. Move the admin.sock rationale into a `gvm help transport` topic or the docs. When the API client lands, restore the GVM_URL/GVM_TOKEN hint, and have `gvm whoami --remote` check URL plus token in one step.

### web-dashboard-8 — The only interim way to delegate a restart is root SSH, and the audit trail cannot say which person did it

**Low** · operator · safety-ux · effort S · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 2, 4, 5 · Plan: **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/start.go:41 has --reason, 'why, recorded in the audit row and (for a stop) in the hold file'. sudo's own syslog/journal entries name the invoking user and the exact gvm command. (orig: With no dashboard (web-dashboard-1), every lifecycle verb needs euid 0 (transport.go:136-143). audit.RootCLI() records `Actor{Kind: root_cli, Via: cli}` with no human identity (internal/audit/audit.go:119). pgActorKind maps root_cli to 'system' (pgsink.go:152). grep for SUDO_USER/SUDO_UID in internal/ finds nothing, and grep -i sudoers in docs/README finds nothing.)

**Impact.** Operators running servers for friends will share the root password or add friends to sudo ALL. After an unexpected stop, `gvm logs` and the audit can only say 'root_cli'/'system', so 'who stopped the server?' cannot be answered.

**Suggestion.** Ship /usr/share/doc/gvm/examples/sudoers.d/gvm-moderators, commented out by default. It uses absolute paths, exact argv and no wildcards, and lists only `gvm status <name>`, `gvm start <name>`, `gvm stop <name>`, `gvm restart <name>` and `gvm logs <name>`. A header says to install it with `visudo -f /etc/sudoers.d/gvm-moderators` (which validates the file) and warns never to add console, control, destroy, rebuild, restore or wildcards.
Add docs/delegating-before-the-dashboard.md, which shows `journalctl _COMM=sudo | grep gvm` for 'who did it'.
In the root CLI: when euid==0 and SUDO_UID parses as an integer, add params.sudo_user and params.sudo_uid, allowlisted for lifecycle actions and marked 'claimed'. Render them in `gvm logs` shutdown records as 'stopped via sudo by alice (claimed)'. Never add a gvm command that edits sudoers.

### web-dashboard-10 — The dashboard model throws away the status the CLI already computes, and shows players no way to connect

**Low** · player · missing-feature · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 9, 10 · Plan: 48 → **49** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm ports [instance]` (internal/cli/ports.go:175-179) already prints every endpoint this host owes, per instance. Neither lifecycle.Status nor `gvm status` includes ports, and there is no public-address setting in spec. (orig: internal/web/server.go:31-44: InstanceSummary is {Name, Status, Game} and InstanceDetail adds only Addr and Port. handlers.go:287-288 renders only Game and Status. In the probe, GetInstance returned Addr '203.0.113.5' and Port 16261, and the rendered body was `<dl><dt>Game</dt><dd>projectzomboid</dd><dt>Status</dt><dd>running</dd></dl>` with no address or port. lifecycle.Status (internal/lifecycle/status.go:22-73) already has Reason, Hint, Players/PlayersMax, Drift, Lease, ReadyAt and Findings, and its comment at lines 17-21 says it is meant to share keys with the dashboard. A single Port int cannot express multi-port games (PZ 16261/16262, whose manifest port note §16.7 wants shown inline).)

**Impact.** A moderator sees 'running' or 'failed' with no reason, no '->' fix hint, no player count and no 'update job in progress'. A player has nothing to tell them which address and ports to join, so the operator keeps answering 'what's the IP?' by hand.

**Suggestion.** Have gvm-web obtain lifecycle.Status through the gvmd status verb, not by reading root-owned state files. Render the phase pill, reason, '→ hint', players x/y, the lease line and the drift badge.
Add a 'How to connect' card for authenticated roles: host:port for each player-facing manifest port, with its note inline and a copy button. The join password shows '••••' unless the effective role holds secret:read.
For players, add `sudo gvm status <name> --connect`. It prints one paste-ready line, 'pz-main (Project Zomboid) — 203.0.113.5:16261 (UDP, also 16262) — up, 3/16 players'. It omits the password unless --with-password is given, which prints a warning first.
Add optional spec.public_address to /etc/gvm/gvm.yaml, with the fallback chain above. Do not create player dashboard accounts and do not add a public status page.

### web-dashboard-13 — Dashboard and API errors are bare strings with no code or request id, and the 503 page hard-codes 'pz-main'

**Low** · dashboard-user · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: **47** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The two Go 503 constants are textually equivalent apart from the em-dash encoding. The divergence is between them and docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:66-79. (orig: handlers.go:27-30 writeJSONError emits `{"error": msg}`, and auth.go:102/130/148 write ad-hoc strings, unlike the §16.3 envelope (ARCHITECTURE.md:5969-5978) with code, message, detail and request_id. handlers.go:97 and auth.go:135 return plain-text 'internal error'. An unknown instance page returns Go's default '404 page not found'. The 503 page exists twice and the copies have already drifted (auth.go:172-191 and handlers.go:51-70; the docs version adds a WAL hint). Both hard-code `sudo gvm console pz-main` / `sudo gvm stop pz-main` for every operator.)

**Impact.** When something fails, a moderator has nothing to quote to the operator, and the CLI's future API client cannot branch on error kinds. An operator who has no 'pz-main' is shown commands for a server that does not exist.

**Suggestion.** Add internal/webapi/envelope.go with codes unauthenticated, forbidden, not_found, conflict, confirmation_required, plan_expired, precondition_failed, rate_limited, degraded and internal. Every handler and both middlewares use it. request_id equals the X-Request-Id header and audit_log.request_id. Unknown instances get the envelope's not_found (JSON) or an HTML 404 page that shows the request id.
Keep one 503 template in internal/web/errors.go. It uses generic commands only: 'sudo gvm list', 'sudo gvm status <name>', 'sudo gvm console <name>', 'sudo gvm stop <name>' and 'sudo gvm doctor'. It never shows real instance names or any host data to an unauthenticated visitor. Add a test asserting the 503 body contains no name from a fixture /etc/gvm/instances.


---

<a id="admin-security"></a>
## Users, tokens, secrets and audit (`admin-security`)

For an operator, the admin side of gvm is mostly a well-designed plan that has not been built yet, and the CLI doesn't say so. All four admin command groups (user, token, secret, audit) are flag-parsing stubs. Each one blames a "gvmd" daemon that is itself `not implemented`, has no unit file, and that the CLI is forbidden to dial anyway (rule 3). The core journeys therefore have no working path today: invite a friend, enrol TOTP, reset a password, mint a token, set an RCON or join password, read the audit trail. Some of them dead-end in loops. `gvm create` says the game will get an empty password, while the runner refuses to start and tells you to hand-edit LoadCredential lines. The role model in code has also drifted from the documented one: the role you would give a "moderator" friend can rebuild worlds, restore backups and rotate secrets. On the positive side, the primitives underneath are careful and well explained: secret redaction, base62 password generation, atomic 0600 secret files, allowlisted and spooled audit records, and AAD-bound TOTP sealing. The improvement plan is mostly about wiring these in-process for root, telling the truth about what is unbuilt, and making the role model and the error messages match the docs.

**Strengths to preserve**

- secrets.Value cannot leak into logs: String/GoString/LogValue/MarshalJSON all render a mask, and plaintext only leaves through a grep-able Reveal() (internal/secrets/value.go). Error messages describe a bad secret by byte offset, never by content (filestore.go ValidateValue).
- Generated passwords are base62 with rejection sampling, and the comment explains exactly why (PZ's ini uses ; and = with no escaping). This satisfies rule 10 by construction (internal/secrets/generate.go).
- The secret file store is safe for hand edits: atomic 0600 writes, no .bak left behind after a rotation, one trailing newline trimmed so `echo pw > file` works, and empty or multiline files refused with distinct, explained errors (filestore.go:113-174).
- The audit design suits an outage: per-action param allowlists (never denylists), UUIDv7 spool ids so journal, spool and DB rows are the same event, idempotent replay with ON CONFLICT, and a failed audit sink never blocks a stop (internal/audit, cli/instance.go productionAudit).
- The transport refusal explains why the CLI will not use /run/gvm/admin.sock (rule 3) and refuses to send a bearer token over plain http to a non-loopback host (cli/transport.go).
- Secret reference validation names the exact clause a bad name broke and why (path segment, systemd credential id and DB key at once), and blocks path traversal structurally (secrets/refs.go ValidateName, dirRel).
- The Postgres-down 503 page tells the user their game servers are NOT affected and gives exact recovery commands (auth.go downPage503).
- `gvm db status` gives a clear, actionable verdict ('needs migration — run `gvm db migrate`', 'schema is AHEAD of binary'), and `gvm db check-grants` checks the D-27 TOTP-column isolation.
- TOTP seeds are sealed with AES-GCM using an HKDF per-purpose subkey and bound to the user id by AAD, and gvm_web has no SELECT on the ciphertext column (identity/box.go, migrations/0010_grants.sql).

### admin-security-1 — Every user/token/secret/audit command is a stub that blames a daemon that does not exist

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 3 · Plan: 20 → 29 → 37 → **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The auditcmd.go comment is at lines 10-14 and the in-function note at 131-133. The README promise is at README.md:35-36. (orig: internal/cli/user.go:42-136, token.go:30-64, secret.go:32-88 and auditcmd.go:33-62 only parse flags, then return errRequiresDaemon (user.go:141-143). `gvm user create --email a@b.c --role moderator` -> "gvm user: user create: requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running (`systemctl status gvmd`)" exit=1. But `gvm gvmd` -> "gvm gvmd: not implemented yet" exit=3 (root.go:101). packaging/systemd/ has no gvmd.service, so the suggested `systemctl status gvmd` cannot work. ARCHITECTURE §3.3 / transport.go:1-17 say the CLI never dials admin.sock, so even a running gvmd could never serve these commands. `gvm help` (usage.go:16) promises "every command below works over SSH as root with nothing else running" and lists all four under Administration. The README promises unbuilt commands exit with `not implemented`; these exit 1 with a different message. The comment at auditcmd.go:12-14 says audit tail works offline from the spool, but the code returns the gvmd error.)

**Impact.** An operator trying to add a user, mint a token, set a password or read the audit log is sent to debug a daemon that doesn't exist. Fixing that is impossible, so they lose time and trust. Scripts cannot tell 'unbuilt' from 'daemon down'.

**Suggestion.** Now: delete errRequiresDaemon. Every unbuilt subcommand returns ErrNotImplemented (exit 3) with one honest line and a workaround where one exists. Derive the '(planned)' tags in `gvm help` from the dispatch table so they cannot drift. Implement `gvm secret` (files only, works with Postgres down) and `gvm audit tail` (spool, works with everything down) first, as in-process root verbs. Build `gvm user` and `gvm token` with the web tier, also in-process as gvm_ops over peer auth, never through gvmd or admin.sock. When Postgres is down they fail fast with a typed 'datastore unavailable' message (see admin-security-15). Qualify the help banner: 'game-server commands work with nothing else running; user/token need Postgres'.

### admin-security-2 — No way to set a game/RCON password, and the messages around it contradict each other and loop

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 10, 11 · Plan: **29** → 50 · Status: open

**Evidence.** Correction: the 'optional' games fail too. gatherCreds sets Want=req.Secrets=ld.CredIDs (run.go:167-195, preflight.go:391-396), which includes the conditional join_password from valheim.yaml:41-42, corekeeper.yaml:43 and generic-steam.yaml:63. checkCreds returns the 'passed no credentials' failure when Dir is empty. The path mapping is documented in the refs.go:192-199 comment, with FromTemplate at refs.go:205. (orig: 1) create.go:511-514 prints "secrets rcon_password, ... — not generated yet; the unit starts without them and the game gets an empty value". 2) The runner refuses instead: preflight.go:242 "the unit passed no credentials, but this instance needs rcon...; add LoadCredential= lines and re-apply" (exit 65, no restart). 3) Re-applying never adds those lines, because reconcile/units.go:540-546 (BuildCredentials) only emits LoadCredential for files that already exist. 4) Nothing in the CLI writes a secret: `grep GeneratePassword` hits only generate.go and its tests, and `gvm secret set --instance pz-main --name rcon` -> "requires gvmd". 5) Names don't match across messages: create prints template spellings (rcon_password), preflight prints store ids (rcon), and the file is /etc/gvm/secrets/instances/<n>/rcon (refs.go:205). 6) The Valheim validator hint (validate_instance.go:383) says "rotate it with `gvm secret set valheim-1 join_password`"; that syntax fails: `gvm secret set valheim-1 join_password` -> "--name is required". 7) cs2.yaml:44 needs `.Secret.gslt`, an operator-supplied Steam token, and nothing tells the operator where to get it.)

**Impact.** Every shipped game except corekeeper, generic-steam and valheim (whose passwords are optional) fails to start after create+apply until the operator works out the undocumented path and hand-writes files as root. The error points them at hand-editing generated unit files instead.

**Suggestion.** Add a declarative per-secret block to manifests. It is plain data, so it stays within rule 10: e.g. `secrets: {rcon_password: {source: generate}, admin_password: {source: generate}, join_password: {source: optional}, gslt: {source: operator, help: 'get one at steamcommunity.com/dev/managegameservers'}}`. `gvm create` generates the `generate` secrets with GeneratePassword(20) and audits secret.write with source=generated. `gvm apply` generates only *missing* `generate` secrets, shown in --diff as '+ secret instances/pz-main/rcon (generated)'. It never generates `optional` or `operator` secrets, and never touches an adopted instance before finalize (adopt should import the existing values with source=adopted). Preflight should require only generate- and operator-source secrets, so an unset optional join password starts an open server. Add `sudo gvm secret set <instance> <name> [--generate|--from-file F|-]`: TTY no-echo by default, never argv. It enforces rule 10 plus the codec separators. On a running instance it prints 'takes effect at next restart: sudo gvm restart <n>' and does not restart anything itself. create/apply print 'required before start: sudo gvm secret set cs2-1 gslt' with the manifest's help line. Rewrite the preflight message, and fix the Valheim WithFix hint to the real syntax. Use store ids everywhere, with the template name in parentheses.

### admin-security-4 — Inviting a friend, TOTP enrollment and password/TOTP reset have no flow at all

**High** · dashboard-user · missing-feature · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The subcommand list at user.go:15 includes reset-totp. It is a stub returning errRequiresDaemon (user.go:126-136). (orig: `gvm user invite --email f@x.y --role operator` -> "unknown subcommand \"invite\"", although §14.9 documents `gvm user invite --email … --role admin --ttl 24h`. `gvm user reset-password f@x.y` -> unknown subcommand, although §14.9 lists reset-password, unlock and `session revoke --all` as break-glass. `user create` (user.go:51-64) takes only --email and --role: no password prompt, no TOTP. internal/identity has NewTOTPSecret and GenerateRecoveryCodes but no otpauth:// URL builder, no QR and no caller (`grep otpauth|QR` finds nothing). internal/web/routes.go:16-60 has no /login, /login/totp or /account route, yet auth.go:132 redirects expired sessions to /login, which falls into the GET / handler and 404s (routes.go:22-25). `gvm web` itself is notImplemented (root.go:102).)

**Impact.** A moderator cannot be onboarded, cannot enrol a second factor, and cannot recover from a lost password or phone. If create worked as designed today, root would have to choose and send the friend's password.

**Suggestion.** Build one guided flow. `sudo gvm user invite friend@example.com --instance pz-main --role operator --ttl 24h` prints a single-use enrolment URL with a viewer ceiling, accepted only by the enrolment route (§14.9). The enrolment page sets the password, shows a QR code plus the otpauth:// URL and base32 secret, requires one valid code (verified in gvmd per rule 9), then shows the recovery codes once. Add `sudo gvm user reset <email> [--totp] [--password]` as the audited root_cli break-glass: it revokes the user's sessions and tokens, clears the factor(s) and prints a fresh invite link, so root never handles the friend's password. Add `gvm user sessions <email>` and `gvm user revoke-sessions <email>`. Make `user create` read the password from the TTY only (term.ReadPassword) and force TOTP for owner/admin.

### admin-security-5 — API tokens are never checked, and dashboard buttons can't authenticate

**High** · dashboard-user · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: **47** · Status: open

**Evidence.** An additional gap: internal/auth and internal/web contain no *_test.go files, so the D-40 and api_cookie tests promised in Phase 16 do not exist. (orig: internal/auth/auth.go:95-106: a /api/v1 request with no session cookie gets 401 "authentication required" even with a valid `Authorization: Bearer` header, so a bearer-only client (the documented model, and what cli/transport.go sends) can never succeed. auth.go:143-150: with a cookie, the only check is that the header STARTS WITH "Bearer "; the token is never hashed or looked up (`grep LookupToken|HashToken|token_sha256` outside internal/identity: none; dbgen/webq has no api_tokens query). handlers.go:293-298 renders start/stop/restart as browser forms POSTing to /api/v1/... with only _csrf, which the middleware answers with 401 "bearer token required". routes.go:31-37 registers /instances, /instances/{name} and /instances/{name}/console WITHOUT s.mw.Wrap, and the handlers render for a 'guest' (handlers.go:330-338): an unauthenticated read-only view, against rule 9 and docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:61. Latent today only because `gvm web` is notImplemented (root.go:102).)

**Impact.** When the web tier is switched on, a moderator's Stop button always fails with a JSON 401, scripts holding a minted token are always refused, and anyone who can reach the dashboard sees the instance list without logging in.

**Suggestion.** Before wiring `gvm web`, build two separate muxes as §16.2/D-40 describe. Browser mux: session middleware, then requireAuth (redirect to /login), then CrossOriginProtection; mutations go to browser routes (/i/{name}/stop) that call the same app layer. /api/v1 mux: bearer only, cookies never read. Look up identity.HashToken(token) in api_tokens, reject revoked or expired tokens, clamp to role_ceiling, record last_used_at/ip, and audit as actor api_token. Add tests: bearer-only succeeds, cookie-only gets 401, an arbitrary 'Bearer x' gets 401, and an unauthenticated GET /instances redirects to /login.

### admin-security-3 — The 'moderator' role is far more powerful than the docs say, and per-instance grants can't be set

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **47** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The role constants are at role.go:9-13. `grep authz.Check|AllGrants|EffectiveRole` finds no production callers, so the issue is latent. (orig: internal/authz/matrix.go:26-49: operator holds PermInstanceRebuild, PermBackupRestore, PermBackupPrune, PermAdoptCutover/Finalize, PermInstanceApply, PermContentEdit and PermSecretRotate. ARCHITECTURE §15.1 says the opposite: operators "cannot rebuild, cannot delete, cannot change what is declared", and "operators execute intent, admins change it". Viewer (matrix.go:11-25) holds PermSecretView, PermAuditView, PermDoctorRun and PermFirewallView, but the live console (PermInstanceConsole, one permission for read and write) is operator-only. role.go:12-16 has three roles; there is no owner, yet the documented bootstrap (§14.9) is `gvm user create --role owner` with mandatory TOTP. migrations/0003_instances.sql:16-23 has instance_grants, but `gvm user grant ...` -> "unknown subcommand \"grant\"". `user create`/`set-role` only set global roles. opsq.go:22-49 EffectiveRole returns the instance grant INSTEAD of the global role (COALESCE), while its own comment and §15.1 say highest (GREATEST).)

**Impact.** An operator inviting a friend to restart the server will pick 'operator'. That friend can then restore an old backup over the world, rebuild it, finalize an adoption or rotate RCON on every instance on the box. Typed confirmation still applies, but the blast radius is not what the docs promise, and nothing lets you scope it to one server.

**Suggestion.** Rewrite matrix.go to §15.2's cumulative lists. operator gets start/stop/restart/update, console:write, content:sync, backup:create, job:run. admin adds apply, rebuild, restore, prune/delete, adopt, content:edit, secret read/write, firewall, audit:read. Split console into console:read (viewer) and console:write (operator). viewer sees secrets only as set/unset and gets no audit:read. Make EffectiveRole `GREATEST(u.global_role, COALESCE(grant,'viewer'))`: role_t enum order already ranks the roles. Document 'grants only add'. For v1, keep three web roles and make user/grant management, secret rotation, manifest publish and host config root-CLI-only. Update §14.9/§15 to say root is the owner, instead of adding an owner enum value. Add `sudo gvm user grant <email> <instance> <role>`, `revoke-grant`, `gvm user roles` (plain-English table) and `user show` (effective role per instance, with the reason). Add a matrix test asserting operator lacks rebuild/restore/prune/apply/adopt/secret/content.edit.

### admin-security-7 — There is no way to read the audit trail, and the documented spool replay fails on its default path

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 2 · Plan: 22 → **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The audit tail default is at auditcmd.go:37 (spool-dir /var/lib/gvm/spool). replay-spool dials Postgres first (dbWaitPool) and only then fails on the missing directory. (orig: `gvm audit tail -n 5` -> "requires gvmd" (auditcmd.go:41-46, despite its comment saying the spool makes this work offline). `gvm db replay-spool --help` shows the default "/var/lib/gvm/audit-spool" (db.go:127). Records are actually written to /var/lib/gvm/spool (instance/paths.go:88, cli/instance.go:316, packaging/tmpfiles/gvm.conf:54), and docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:96 says `sudo gvm db replay-spool  # replays /var/lib/gvm/spool/`. With the default, ListSpools fails with "list spool directory /var/lib/gvm/audit-spool: no such file". `audit tail` defaults to /var/lib/gvm/spool (auditcmd.go:37), so the two commands disagree. Root CLI audit records go to slog.Default() (cli/instance.go:306-316), and cmd/gvm/main.go installs no handler, so they print to the operator's terminal stderr in log format and never reach journald. slogsink.go:11-13 advertises `journalctl -u gvmd MESSAGE=audit`, but no gvmd unit exists.)

**Impact.** 'Who stopped pz-main last night?' has no answer short of reading root-only NDJSON files or querying Postgres by hand. The recovery step in the Postgres-down doc fails, and interactive commands spray raw audit lines into the terminal.

**Suggestion.** Implement `gvm audit tail [-n 50] [--follow] [--since 24h] [--instance] [--action] [--actor] [--json]`. It reads the spool via ListSpools + ReplayReader and, when Postgres answers, merges audit_log rows deduplicated on spool_id. Root-only, since the spool is 0600. Output is one human line per record. In the CLI, send the audit sink to journald over the native socket (/run/systemd/journal/socket) in pure Go with SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER=gvm-audit. No cgo and no spawned logger (rule 2). Fall back to spool-only when there is no journald, and never write to the operator's stderr. Update the journalctl hint to `journalctl -t gvm-audit`. Change the replay-spool default to instance.SpoolDir and print the directory it read.

### admin-security-8 — The audit trail mislabels or skips the operations people most need to trace

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5, 8 · Plan: **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Rebuild is partly traced: backup/engine.go:855-875 records backup.create for the pre-rebuild archive, and jobs/execute.go:618-621 records job.run with kind. Restore is unbuilt (execute.go:278-280). (orig: Only five actions are ever recorded in production code: BackupCreate, InstanceApply, InstanceStart, InstanceStop and JobRun (grep of `audit.<Action>` outside internal/audit). InstanceRebuild, BackupRestore, ConsoleSend, ContentSync, Secret* and User* are defined in actions.go:29-56 but never emitted, and there is no instance.destroy action at all. hostops/verbs.go:205-209 audits VerbUnitRestart as audit.InstanceStart. `gvm destroy` deletes data through archive.Destroy (cli/destroy.go:160) with no audit row of its own; its unit and file steps are recorded as generic 'instance.apply' rows (destroy.go:208-228, verbs.go:210-252), so the archive path is never in the trail. Root CLI actions are stored as actor_kind 'system' (pgsink.go:143-155, opsq.go:82-95), because actor_t lacks root_cli (migrations/0001_types.sql:5).)

**Impact.** After a world disappears, the trail shows a few 'instance.apply' rows by 'system' and no 'destroyed, archived to X'. A restart reads as a start. This is exactly the evidence §14.8 promised for archive-before-destroy (rule 5).

**Suggestion.** Keep the verb-to-action mapping fixed in hostops, but correct it: add instance.restart and map VerbUnitRestart to it. Have the destroy, rebuild, restore, update and prune commands each emit their own parent rows: instance.destroy (Required: archive_path, archive_sha256, archive_bytes; a started row before archive.Destroy), instance.rebuild, backup.restore, instance.update, backup.prune. The hostops step rows correlate with them by request_id/parent_id. Emit console.send and content.sync from their commands. Add 'root_cli' to actor_t in a new migration applied only by `gvm db migrate`, and map ActorRootCLI straight through in mapActorKind and pgsink.

### admin-security-9 — Hand-written secrets (today's only route) bypass the password character rules, and doctor never checks them

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 10 · Plan: **29** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The runner-side read is at internal/cli/run.go:184-187 and bypasses the secrets.Store entirely. (orig: secrets/filestore.go:97-112 ValidateValue rejects only empty, newline, CR and NUL; nothing rejects ' " \ $ ` (rule 10) or the ini separators ; and =, which generate.go:9-22 says silently corrupt PZ's ini ("the server starts anyway, with a different password than the one gvm recorded"). filestore.go:141-146 (Read) is written for `echo hunter2 > join`, which is effectively the only way to set a secret today (see admin-security-2). Implemented doctor IDs are EDGE-001/003/004/005/008/009, FW-001/002/003/005/006, HOST-001..005/009..012 and INST-001/002/003/005/008/009/010. None checks credentials. checks_host.go:14 says HOST-006..008 (master.key mode, web tier has no LoadCredential) "are added in Phase 15", but they aren't, although filestore.go:25 points operators at HOST-006.)

**Impact.** An operator who hand-writes a PZ admin password containing ';' or '$' gets a server whose real password differs from the stored one, with no diagnostic. A master.key with loose permissions goes unreported.

**Suggestion.** Have `gvm secret set` enforce rule 10 plus the codec-specific unsafe characters, and explain why ("';' is Project Zomboid's list separator; this password would be cut at that character"). Add a doctor check (e.g. INST-011 credentials) that, per instance, lists missing credential ids with the exact `sudo gvm secret set` fix and flags files with forbidden characters, wrong mode/owner or CRLF, without printing values. Implement HOST-006..008 as documented.

### admin-security-10 — No supported way to get the join password to players

**Medium** · player · missing-feature · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: **29** → 33 · Status: open

**Evidence.** secret.go:13 offers list, set, rotate and show-path; `gvm secret show --instance pz-main --name join` -> "unknown subcommand \"show\"". internal/cli/status.go contains no connection info (grep join|connect|password: no matches). The password files are 0600 root under /etc/gvm/secrets/instances/<n>/ (filestore.go:20-21), so the only route is `sudo cat` of a path the operator has to work out (see admin-security-2 for the naming mismatch).

**Impact.** Telling friends 'connect to 203.0.113.5:16261, password X' means digging through /etc by hand. Players have nothing to confirm they have the current password after a rotation.

**Suggestion.** Add `sudo gvm secret show <instance> join`: root only, prints to a TTY, audited as secret.read with scope and name and never the value. Restrict it to player-facing secrets (join) unless --admin-secrets is given. Add `gvm status <instance> --connect`, printing address:port per declared player port plus 'password: set (sudo gvm secret show pz-main join)'. Later, show the same block on the dashboard to roles with an explicit permission, keeping rcon/admin always masked.

### admin-security-11 — Admin commands have one-line help, accept invalid input, and treat --help as an error

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm help user` prints only "gvm user — manage dashboard users and roles" (root.go:254-265), with no subcommands, flags or roles. The same holds for token, secret and audit. `gvm user create --help` prints "Usage of user create: ..." then "gvm user: flag: help requested" exit=1.
