Invalid input passes validation and only then hits the gvmd error: `--role moderator`, `gvm user set-role a@b.c superuser`, `gvm token create --name x --role owner`, `gvm secret set --instance "../../etc" --name "Bad Name!"` all end in "requires gvmd" (user.go:62 `_ = role`; secret.go:55). Prefixes are doubled: "gvm user: user create: --email is required". `gvm user invite` gives "unknown subcommand" without listing the valid ones.

**Impact.** Operators can't discover what the admin commands do or which roles exist without reading Go source. Typos aren't caught until a later layer, and `--help` looks like a failure to scripts.

**Suggestion.** Make `gvm help user` (and `gvm user -h`) print subcommands, flags, an example and the role table, and exit 0 on -h. Validate before anything else with authz.ParseRole, instance.ValidateName, secrets.ValidateName and an email check, naming the valid values ('role must be viewer, operator or admin'). On an unknown subcommand, list the valid ones and suggest the nearest. Drop the doubled prefix.

### admin-security-12 — The non-root error leads to a fix that can't work, and drops the operator's arguments

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 3 · Plan: **20** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The message is built at transport.go:148-160, not 150-156. The console variant is at 150-152. (orig: As non-root, `gvm stop pz-main` -> "gvm stop: gvm: no transport for this command: `gvm stop` is a host operation. Run it as root (sudo gvm stop), or point it at a dashboard with --remote / GVM_URL plus GVM_TOKEN (`gvm token create` mints one).\ngvm deliberately does not dial /run/gvm/admin.sock: that socket is 0660 root:gvm, ..." (transport.go:150-156). 'sudo gvm stop' loses 'pz-main'. `gvm token create` is a stub. After setting both variables: `GVM_URL=https://gvm.example.com GVM_TOKEN=gvmt_abc gvm stop pz-main` -> "the remote transport is not built in this release" (transport.go:96, 174-176).)

**Impact.** Someone who only forgot sudo gets an architecture lecture plus a remote-access path that cannot work in this build. They find that out only after creating a token and configuring the environment.

**Suggestion.** Lead with the exact retry: "run as root: sudo gvm stop pz-main" (rebuild it from env.Args). While ErrRemoteUnbuilt holds, don't suggest GVM_URL/GVM_TOKEN/`gvm token create` at all. Once remote exists, mention it in one line. Move the admin.sock rationale to `gvm help transport` or the docs, keeping one short clause ('the CLI never uses gvmd's socket, by design').

### admin-security-13 — The admin docs show commands and checks that don't exist

**Medium** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: 20 → **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Also missing: docs/THREAT-MODEL.md, which §14.9:5727 references for break-glass. The doctor sample Fix line is at ARCHITECTURE.md:7033. (orig: ARCHITECTURE §14.9 (lines 5700-5727) gives the bootstrap as `sudo gvm user create --email ... --role owner` with a password prompt, an inline TOTP QR and recovery codes; none of these exist, and there is no owner role. It also documents `gvm user invite`, reset-password, unlock and `session revoke --all`. §14.7:5635 documents `gvm secret rotate <inst> --all --restart` and `gvm secret rotate-key`. §14.8:5694 documents `gvm audit export --before <date>` (`gvm audit export` -> unknown subcommand). The doctor sample in §20 (~6962-6966) shows HOST-004 as a TOTP grant check and HOST-012 as 'spool empty', but implemented HOST-004 is 'gvm_migrate role exists' and HOST-012 is 'no left_stopped job_runs' (checks_host_db.go:48-70). It also shows 'Fix: sudo gvm secret rotate pz-main --all --restart'. doctor HOST-011's fix says "restart gvmd" (checks_host_db.go:270). IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN Phase 15 lists docs/bootstrap.md and internal/identity/bootstrap.go; neither exists, though the git log says Phases 0-19 are committed.)

**Impact.** An operator following the official runbook for first-admin setup or credential rotation fails at every step, and has no way to tell a planned feature from a broken one.

**Suggestion.** Add docs/admin-guide.md covering only working commands, with planned ones in a 'Not yet available' box. Add a status banner to ARCHITECTURE.md ('target design; see admin-guide for what works today') and mark the unbuilt commands in §14.7-14.9. Add a CI test that pulls every `gvm <cmd> [<sub>]` from Go string literals used as hints (WithFix, doctor Fix, error text) and from fenced blocks in user-facing docs (README, admin-guide, what-happens-if-postgres-is-down, runbooks). It checks them against the dispatch table and the subcommand lists, and fails on any command that is unknown or returns ErrNotImplemented. Make the §20 doctor sample match the implemented IDs, not the other way round.

### admin-security-6 — Token lifecycle is incomplete, and the token 'prefix' barely identifies anything

**Low** · operator · ergonomics · effort S · fact: partial · fit: adjust · Plan: 48 → **49** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The `gvm token create` mentions are at transport.go:130, 152 and 156, not 130, 150 and 153. (orig: token.go:13 offers only create, list and revoke; `gvm token rotate x` -> unknown subcommand. `token create` takes --name and --role only (token.go:33-34). There is no --user, although api_tokens.user_id is NOT NULL (0002_identity.sql:33-36) and the root CLI has no user identity. There is no --expires, although the expires_at column exists. --role accepts anything (`--role owner` is accepted, then fails on gvmd). identity/tokens.go:30-36 sets the prefix to the first 8 characters of "gvmt_"+base64, so every prefix is 'gvmt_' plus 3 random characters. `token revoke <token-prefix>` (token.go:61) would be ambiguous and `token list` would show nearly identical prefixes. Every transport error tells users to "mint one with `gvm token create`" (transport.go:130,150,153).)

**Impact.** Once built, operators can't tell tokens apart, can't safely revoke one by prefix, can't make a CI token expire, and can't rotate a leaked token without a gap. When root mints a token, it is unclear whose rights it carries.

**Suggestion.** Ship with the web tier. `sudo gvm token create --user <email> --name backup-bot [--role operator] [--instance pz-main] [--expires 90d|never]`. Validate with authz.ParseRole first. Refuse a ceiling above the user's effective role. Default to a 90-day expiry; 'never' must be explicit. Print the token once in a 'shown once' box and suggest `read -rs GVM_TOKEN` or a 0600 file instead of an export line. Prefix: 'gvmt_' plus 8 random characters. `token list [--user]` shows name, prefix, ceiling, expiry, last used and revoked. `token revoke <name|prefix>` refuses an ambiguous match and lists the candidates. `token rotate <name> [--grace 1h]` mints the replacement and sets the old token's expires_at to now+grace; no background revoker needed.

### admin-security-14 — Password limits and recovery codes are unfriendly

**Low** · dashboard-user · ergonomics · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** identity/password.go:10-24 uses bcrypt cost 12 with no minimum length (only empty is refused) and rejects anything over 72 bytes with "identity: password exceeds 72 bytes". ARCHITECTURE §14.9:5707 specifies argon2id. identity/tokens.go:12,56-63 makes recovery codes 22-character base64url (mixed case plus - and _), while §14.9:5717 shows them as '4f2a-91cc-08de'.

**Impact.** Password-manager users with long passphrases get an internal-sounding error, one-character passwords are accepted, and recovery codes are easy to mis-transcribe from paper. That leads to a lockout followed by a root break-glass.

**Suggestion.** Switch to argon2id as documented, which removes the 72-byte cap. Enforce a stated minimum (e.g. 12) and give the rule in the prompt ('12 or more characters'). Generate recovery codes as grouped lowercase hex or Crockford base32 ('4f2a-91cc-08de-77b1-...', 80 bits or more) and accept them case-insensitively, with or without dashes.

### admin-security-15 — The db commands hang for 15 s on raw driver warnings, with no hint about peer auth

**Low** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 8 · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The retry loop is in internal/store/open.go:46-80, and the timeout is dbConnTimeout=15s at db.go:19. (orig: `gvm db status` printed four lines of the form "WARN store: pool ping failed, retrying err=\"failed to connect to `user=gvm_ops database=gvm`: ... dial error ...\" delay=1s/2s/4s/8s" and then "gvm db: db status: store: datastore unavailable: timed out waiting for postgres" exit=1 (dbWaitPool, db.go:189-206, 15 s timeout). The message never mentions that peer auth maps only OS user root to gvm_ops/gvm_migrate (§14.2 pg_ident.conf), which is the usual cause when run without sudo or before the pg_hba lines exist. `db check-grants` reports an inconclusive result as "SKIP: ..." with exit 0 (db.go:184-186).)

**Impact.** User management depends on Postgres, so this is the first wall an operator hits when setting up the admin side: a 15-second wait, driver noise, and no next step.

**Suggestion.** Try once quietly and fail fast with a diagnosis: socket missing -> 'is postgresql running? systemctl status postgresql'; peer auth failure -> 'run as root; pg_hba.conf needs the gvm lines above `local all all peer` (see gvm doctor HOST-002)'. Keep retries behind --wait. Make check-grants exit non-zero on an inconclusive result.


---

<a id="network-firewall"></a>
## Ports, firewall, edge and connectivity (`network-firewall`)

For an operator, gvm's networking is mostly design with little delivery. The pure pieces are careful and well tested: the port allocator (per-group strides, pins, ledger reservations, an honest ephemeral-range report), tagged rule ownership, a reconcile guard with no bypass flag, and the SSH detection ladder. But nothing connects them to the commands people run. `gvm firewall` exits "not implemented yet", and `gvm create/apply/start` never stage, print or even mention a firewall rule, even though the README says gvm keeps the firewall rules. No command or dashboard page shows a player's connect address. The dashboard cannot be put behind TLS or a domain, because `gvm web` and the Caddy apply path are unbuilt, and the edge code renders routes for the wrong upstream. `gvm doctor` makes this worse by feeding its FW checks hard-coded values: every host shows two false firewall problems, and the fixes it suggests are commands that do not exist. Before anything is wired, the SSH-lockout detection and both firewall backends have defects that would break their own safety promises. These should be fixed first, inside the existing design rules.

**Strengths to preserve**

- firewall.Desired is pure and file-derived (internal/firewall/desired.go:12-92). It orders SSH rules first and gives a disabled instance zero rules, so 'enabled: false' really closes the door. This keeps the firewall rebuildable with gvmd and Postgres down (rule 1).
- The ownership and safety model is enforced in code: a strict gvm:<instance>:<label> tag grammar (tag.go:17), Guard.Check running before any diffing with no bypass flag, adds before deletes, SSH rules never deleted, all deletions suppressed when the ufw parse is untrusted (D-42), and foreign rules counted but never acted on (reconcile.go:36-143). A golden corpus backs the ufw status parser, including a corrupt-untrusted fixture.
- The port allocator is genuinely good. Each group shifts by its own stride, which keeps contiguity (allocate.go:88-210). --pin-port fixes a group for adoption. The ledger reservation stops two concurrent `gvm create` runs taking the same port. Disabled instances and instances whose manifest will not load still claim their ports. When ip_local_port_range cannot be read, the output says ASSUMED instead of presenting a guess as a measurement (cli/ports.go:47-60, 249-256).
- Allocation failures are specific: ExhaustedError and Rejection name the shift, the port, the reason and the blocking claim (allocate.go:69-84, 223-283).
- Manifests model exposure as three levels (public/admin/none). Instances may only narrow it, with none->admin as the one allowed widening, and validation rejects expose:public on a loopback-bound port (validate.go:587-593, validate_instance.go:331-356). Shipped manifests carry real operational knowledge in port notes, such as Project Zomboid's two-port join requirement and the ssh -L route to RCON.
- Readiness probes always dial loopback (cli/run.go:628-651), so a firewall or NAT problem is never misread as a failed boot.
- The edge design keeps the Caddy admin API on a unix socket (render.go:101-112, EDGE-004). gvm never writes /etc/caddy/Caddyfile (D-37, EDGE-003). The remote CLI transport refuses plain-http URLs except to loopback (transport.go:190-212).
- `gvm create` prints what it did NOT change ('nothing else on this host changed: no account, no directories, no unit') and the next command to run (create.go:516-517). That is the right tone for every networking command.

### network-firewall-1 — Firewall management is not wired: no command opens, stages or even lists the ports a server needs

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: 33 → **40** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Additional: internal/firewall/desired.go:111-116 sshPorts() returns spec.firewall.ssh.ports or a hard-coded [22] and never uses sshport.Detect. So even a wired `firewall plan` would only open 22 under autodetect. README.md:24,33,35-36 already hedge the claim. (orig: Ran `gvm.exe firewall` and `gvm.exe firewall apply`: both print "gvm firewall: not implemented yet" with exit 3. `gvm help firewall` prints only "gvm firewall — manage host firewall rules". The command is routed to notImplemented (internal/cli/root.go:152). A grep over the whole tree finds no non-test caller of firewall.Desired, firewall.Reconcile or any Backend.Apply; the only non-test use of the package is the Guard literal in doctor/checks_fw.go:76. The reconcile plan has no firewall step kind (internal/reconcile/plan.go:24-41). printPlan for `gvm apply --diff` has no firewall section (internal/cli/diff.go:27-66). IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:578-579 lists internal/reconcile/{firewall,caddy}.go and internal/cli/{firewall,caddy,ca,web}.go; none of them exist. README.md:5 and :14-15 still promise 'a firewall derived from what is actually running' and that every server 'keeps its firewall rules'.)

**Impact.** Typical journey: the operator runs `gvm create` and `gvm apply`, the server reports ready, and players cannot connect because ufw (or the default-deny the operator already had) blocks the game port. Nothing in gvm's output says the firewall was left alone or what to open. For Project Zomboid the operator also has to know that both 16261/udp and 16262/udp are needed, a fact that sits only in a manifest note gvm never prints.

**Suggestion.** Stage 0, now: mark firewall, web and gvmd as '(not built yet)' in `gvm help` and fix the README claims. Stage 1: add a read-only `gvm firewall plan [instance]`. It must work with backend none and as non-root; without root it shows Desired only and says 'live rules not read'. Render it in the section 17.6 shape and end with copy-paste ufw commands, SSH first, in correct syntax: `ufw allow proto udp to any port 16261 comment gvm:pz1:game`. Also remind the operator that the cloud provider's firewall must allow these ports. Stage 2 comes only after findings 2 and 9 are fixed and the Phase-11 Linux CI round-trip passes. Then add a '~ firewall' block to `apply --diff` and the report. `gvm apply <name>` adds and deletes only gvm:<name>:* rules; host rules (ssh/http/https) change only through `gvm firewall apply`. Compute Desired over every instance file; if one fails to load, suppress deletions for its tag, mirroring the 'its ports are still claimed' rule in `gvm ports`. Apply never runs StepAdopt: print 'adoptable' and point to `gvm firewall adopt-rules`, which needs confirmation. When ufw is inactive, stage adds only (ufw accepts rule changes while disabled), make no deletes, and print 'staged; run `gvm firewall enable`'. Never enable ufw, never install a package. Stage 3: `gvm firewall enable` with the section 17.5 guards.

### network-firewall-2 — SSH-port detection can miss the operator's live session, so the lockout guard has nothing to protect

**High** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: **33** · Status: open

**Evidence.** (c) correction: with Accept=no socket activation, the sshd process holds the listening inode, so rung 3 still detects it. Additional: desired.go:111-116 ignores Detection entirely. With autodetect on a 2222 host, Desired opens only 22 and the guard refuses: safe, but unusable. Guard also only compares the rule set against detected ports. With configured=[2222], Desired=[2222] and SSHPorts=[2222], so Check is tautologically nil (guard.go:43-95). (orig: (a) internal/sshport/detect.go:89-97: when spec.firewall.ssh.ports is set, Detect returns before reading SSH_CONNECTION. A scratch test run with `go test -overlay` (no repo files changed) printed, for configured=[2222] with SSH_CONNECTION on port 22: "Ports=[2222] LiveSessionPort=0 Confident=true" and "Guard.Check(desired) = <nil> (desired: [port 2222 proto tcp])". So nothing refuses a rule set that would cut off the session being typed in. This contradicts ARCHITECTURE.md:6359-6360 ('LiveSessionPort gets the strongest guarantee') and the sample refusal at :6413-6419 ('You are connected on 2222'). (b) The port is read from the process environment (detect.go:206), but every refusal tells the operator to run `sudo gvm ...` (transport.go:153-154), and sudo's default env_reset does not pass SSH_CONNECTION through. (c) The listener rung matches only processes named 'sshd' (detect.go:111, env_linux.go:23,70-79). With socket activation (the Ubuntu 22.10+/24.04 default) the listening socket belongs to systemd, PID 1. The socket-unit rung reads only ssh.socket.d/override.conf and the vendor unit (env_linux.go:157-161), so a drop-in named listen.conf, or the generator's /run/systemd/generator/ssh.socket.d/, is ignored and detection falls back to sshd_config with Confident=true. (d) Guard.Confident and Guard.AllowEnable are never read, and ErrSSHUnknown is never returned (grep: no non-test use).)

**Impact.** When `gvm firewall enable` or apply is wired on top of this, an operator with a typo in ssh.ports (or a non-standard ssh.socket drop-in) gets a firewall that admits the wrong port. With a deny-by-default policy that ends SSH access to the VPS. This is the exact failure hard rule 7 forbids, and it would look safe because the guard reported no problem.

**Suggestion.** Always run rungs 2-5. Find live sessions from sockets, not environment variables. Scan the processes whose comm is 'sshd' or 'sshd-session' (the OpenSSH 9.8+ name), map their fds to socket inodes, and take the local port of every ESTABLISHED row in /proc/net/tcp{,6}. Treat all of those ports as live-session ports. This also protects the second session the operator is told to keep open. Fallback: walk PPid up to the first ancestor whose environ contains SSH_CONNECTION. Everything is pure /proc: no exec, no shell. If ssh.ports is empty, build Desired's SSH rules from Detection.Ports, so autodetect just works and the guard stays a backstop. If ssh.ports is set and any observed port is missing from it, refuse with the section 17.5 message naming each port's source. Read *.conf from /etc, /run and /usr/lib under systemd/system/ssh.socket.d/, and keep the union, which is the conservative choice. The enable path checks Confident and returns ErrSSHUnknown. Add a read-only `gvm firewall ssh` that lists each port and its source. Add no bypass flag.

### network-firewall-3 — `gvm doctor` reports firewall problems that are hard-coded, not measured, and suggests commands that do not exist

**High** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: 24 → 33 → **40** · Status: open

**Evidence.** EDGE-009 also has a third 'gvm caddy render' Fix at checks_edge.go:194. checks_edge.go:51-68 never consults edge.mode. (orig: internal/cli/doctor.go:56-60 passes `liveActive := false`, `liveTrusted := false`, nil rules, nil instances and nil manifests into FirewallChecks, and doctorLoadInstances and doctorLoadManifest are stubs returning nil (doctor.go:150-151). Ran `gvm.exe doctor`; output included "✗ FW-001 firewall backend ufw is not active / Fix: gvm firewall enable", "- FW-002 firewall not active", "✗ FW-003 ufw status numbered could not be fully parsed; deletions suppressed (D-42) / Fix: inspect output of: sudo ufw status numbered", "✓ FW-005 no ufw rule for any expose:none port", "✓ FW-006 all gvm-managed ports carry a gvm: tag", and ended "3 problem(s) found". EDGE-008 and EDGE-009 suggest 'run: gvm caddy render' (checks_edge.go:163,186), and `gvm.exe caddy render` prints 'unknown command "caddy"'. EDGE-001 suggests 'apt install caddy' even when edge.mode is none (checks_edge.go:62-67). IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:621 requires that 'a clean host reports 0 problems — so a permanently non-zero count never trains anyone to ignore it'.)

**Impact.** Every host, including one with a correctly working ufw, gets two red firewall problems on every run. The SSH-admitted check (FW-002), the one that matters most, is always skipped. FW-005 and FW-006 give false reassurance about instances they never looked at. The operator either chases a ufw parse fault that does not exist, runs a 'Fix' that exits 3 or is unknown, or learns to ignore doctor.

**Suggestion.** Now: return Skip from FW-001/002/003/005/006 with 'not checked: firewall management is not built in this release'. Skip EDGE-001 and EDGE-003..009 when edge.mode is none. Add a test that every Fix naming a `gvm ...` command resolves to a built, non-hidden verb. Once wired: load real instances and manifests with lifecycle.List and loadInstance, and read `ufw status numbered` through ufw.Parse, root only, otherwise Skip. Set severities: ufw not installed → Note ('apt install ufw, or set backend: none if your provider firewall covers this'). Inactive with manage:true → Warning saying 'N rules staged; `gvm firewall enable` when ready'. Reserve Problem for a real FW-002 lockout risk, a real untrusted parse, and expose:none ports that have a live allow rule.

### network-firewall-5 — The dashboard cannot be put behind TLS or a domain, and the unwired edge code renders the wrong Caddyfile

**High** · dashboard-user · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: 47 → **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Additional: no production importer of internal/web (grep); packaging/systemd contains only gvm@, gvm-job@*, gvm-guard.timer and the slices, with no gvm-web or gvmd unit; cmd/ has only cmd/gvm. The tmpfiles group comes from IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:625. (orig: `web` is Hidden and routed to notImplemented (internal/cli/root.go:102). There is no caddy, ca or edge verb: `gvm.exe caddy render` prints 'unknown command "caddy"'. edge.ComputePlan creates one vhost per instance, `<instance>.<site>`, pointed at `<home>/web.sock` (internal/edge/edge.go:62-85), instead of the single dashboard site that ARCHITECTURE.md:6255-6280 describes, which reverse-proxies to /run/gvm/web/dashboard.sock. Scratch render with mode caddy_managed and site gvm.example.com produced `pz1.gvm.example.com {`, an empty `tls ` directive (render.go:43-44 when tls.email is unset), a `redir http://pz1.gvm.example.com{uri} https://...` line, and `reverse_proxy unix/unix//srv/gvm/pz1/web.sock` (a doubled unix/ prefix from edge.go:71 plus render.go:65,71). With zero instances the rendered Caddyfile has no site block at all, so there is no dashboard route. web.Server only listens on TCP Config.Addr (internal/web/server.go:98-99), never on edge.socket.path. tls.mode values acme_http and acme_dns silently fall through to auto (edge.go:90-101). EdgeSpec.Listen (spec/host.go:39), the documented SSH-tunnel path, is never read. tmpfiles hard-codes group caddy for /run/gvm/web and /etc/gvm/caddy (packaging/tmpfiles/gvm.conf:43,61), even though edge.socket.group may be www-data or traefik and Caddy may not be installed. docs/network-edge.md and docs/tls-cases.md are listed in IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:613-614 but do not exist.)

**Impact.** A moderator who should manage servers without SSH has no supported way to reach the dashboard: there is no TLS, no domain support and no tunnel recipe. If the current renderer were wired as it is, it would produce a Caddyfile that should fail `caddy validate`, or, worse, route hostnames to sockets nothing listens on.

**Suggestion.** 1) Do the pure work now: rewrite edge.ComputePlan and Render to emit the single dashboard site from section 17.2, reverse-proxying to edge.socket.path. Add golden tests for acme-http, acme-dns, internal-bareip and snippet-import. Validate the edge.mode and tls.mode enums and their required fields instead of falling through to auto. 2) Build gvmd plus `gvm web` serving edge.socket.path. Accept an optional edge.listen only on a loopback address; refuse a non-loopback plain-HTTP listen. Document the `ssh -L` path for the operator. 3) Add a read-only `gvm edge plan` showing the TLS mode, ports and Caddyfile diff. Show any DNS-versus-host-address mismatch as a Note, because NAT'd hosts legitimately differ. 4) `gvm edge apply`: validate, swap, reload over the admin unix socket, verify, roll back. Never install Caddy implicitly. 5) `gvm ca export` for the bare-IP moderator path. 6) Instead of hard-coding the group in the packaged tmpfiles file, write a /etc/tmpfiles.d drop-in from edge apply using edge.socket.group, and default to gvm:gvm when edge.mode is none so the line never fails on a missing group. Defer `web snippet --proxy nginx`.

### network-firewall-4 — Nothing tells an operator, a moderator or a player how to connect: no address:port, protocol or exposure anywhere

**Medium** · player · missing-feature · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: **33** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm ports` already shows proto/port/name per instance (internal/ports/occupancy.go:107-112, cli/ports.go:247-255). Note is carried into PortRequirement (spec/ports.go:91-95) but never rendered. Actual validate line: '✓ 3 ports, group "pz" stride 10, group "admin" stride 1, no expose/bind contradiction'. (orig: Status.Report and Status.Line print phase, unit, pid, players and drift, but no ports or address (internal/lifecycle/status.go:136-233). printCreated prints port names and numbers only, with no protocol, exposure or public address (internal/cli/create.go:491-502). The dashboard's InstanceDetail has a single Addr/Port pair (internal/web/server.go:37-44), and a grep finds no code that populates or renders it. PortSpec.Note is documented as 'UI tooltip + ufw comment' (internal/spec/manifest.go:451), but no CLI code ever reads a port's Note. That hides Project Zomboid's 'opening only one gives a server people can see but never join' and 'reach it with ssh -L 27015:127.0.0.1:27015' (games/projectzomboid.yaml:126-132). Manifests have no field marking which port a player types in. ARCHITECTURE.md contains no concept of a public or connect address (grep for public_address, 'connect address', 'how to connect' finds nothing). `gvm game validate projectzomboid` only says "✓ 3 ports, group \"pz\" stride 10, group \"admin\" stride 1".)

**Impact.** To tell friends where to connect, the operator has to read /etc/gvm/instances/<name>.yaml, work out which port is the join port, and find the VPS's public IP somewhere else. A moderator using the dashboard cannot see it at all. With several instances whose ports were shifted by the allocator (16271 instead of 16261), wrong connect info is easy to hand out.

**Suggestion.** Add an optional spec.public_address. Auto-detect it locally only, from /proc/net/route plus net.Interfaces, with no network call, and use the result only if it is globally routable (not RFC1918, CGNAT 100.64/10, link-local or ULA). Otherwise print 'connect <your-public-ip>:16271/udp (this host looks NATed; set spec.public_address)'. Add one manifest port field, `role: join|query`, defaulting to join for the first public port. rcon and admin are already covered by expose. Show a connect line in `gvm status <name>` listing every other public port that must also be open. Until the firewall is wired, add 'firewall not managed by gvm: open these ports in ufw and at your provider'. Add a CONNECT column to `gvm list`, print notes in `gvm ports <name>`, and add a port table to `gvm game validate`. Add `gvm status --share` without secrets, pointing to `gvm secret` for the password. Put the same line on the authenticated dashboard page once finding 5 lands; no public status page (rule 9).

### network-firewall-6 — `gvm ports` hides which port is in conflict and with whom, never checks host-wide overlaps, and labels each server's own socket 'foreign'

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **33** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Conflict.String() at internal/ports/conflicts.go:61-86 already contains the endpoint, instance:name and each claim. Ports reach the apply diff only via reconcile/units.go:494-499. (orig: printPortConflicts prints only `c.Kind` and `c.Detail` (internal/cli/ports.go:280-281), and Check sets Detail to 'also held elsewhere', keeping the endpoint, port name and holders in separate fields (internal/ports/conflicts.go:241-249). A scratch run of printPortConflicts with a pz1/pz2 overlap on udp/16261 printed "pz1: 1 finding(s)\n    ! overlap  also held elsewhere", with no port, no name and no holder. The no-argument form only prints occupancy; ports.Conflicts is never called anywhere (the only caller of the conflict logic is CheckSpec at ports.go:225, used when an instance is named), even though the command summary is 'show port occupancy and conflicts' (root.go:125). AttributeListeners is never called (grep: no non-test caller), so a scratch printOccupancy run showed "udp/16261  instance:pz1 (gvm@pz1:game), listener (a foreign listener (pid 4242 ProjectZomboid6))" for pz1's own running server. The footer advises '`gvm apply pz1 --diff` shows what the file says today', but apply --diff has no port section.)

**Impact.** In a conflict, the operator learns that something is wrong but not which port, which instance, or who holds it, and every healthy running server looks as if a foreign process were squatting its port. Resolving a conflict means hand-correlating `ss -lunp`, the YAML files and the manifest, which is exactly what the command exists to avoid.

**Suggestion.** Attribute each listener by reading /proc/<pid>/cgroup and matching gvm@<name>.service. This is pure /proc and covers grandchildren. Print Conflict.String() with one fix line per kind. Mention `gvm ports move` only once finding 7 ships, enforced by the same Fix-must-resolve test as finding 3. Run ports.Conflicts in the no-argument form and exit 1 on an overlap. Remove the misleading 'apply --diff shows what the file says' footer. Widen the table to ENDPOINT, NAME, OWNER, EXPOSE and LISTENING, adding FIREWALL once finding 1 lands.

### network-firewall-7 — No way to move or re-allocate a port after create, and apply/start never re-check a hand-edited port

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: **39** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/spec/validate_instance.go:326-327 gives the Fix 'run `gvm apply`' for a missing allocation, but no apply path allocates (grep: Allocate( appears only at cli/create.go:187). The runtime error at cli/run.go:638-639 repeats the same 'run `gvm apply` first' advice. (orig: Ports are materialised literally (internal/spec/instance.go:151-153). Only `gvm create --pin-port` chooses numbers (create.go:116). The internal/ports package is imported only by internal/cli/create.go, instance.go and ports.go, so `gvm apply` and `gvm start` never consult occupancy, the ephemeral range or other instances' declared ports. ValidateInstance checks only that each declared port has an allocation (validate_instance.go:324-329). The only route to a different port is editing /etc/gvm/instances/<name>.yaml by hand, with nothing to say which numbers are free or which ports are in the same group.)

**Impact.** Moving a server off a port (provider block, a collision with a non-gvm service, a friend's client limitation) is a manual edit with no guard. A typo that collides with another instance or lands in the ephemeral range is applied silently. It only appears later, as a failed boot or as players who can see the server but cannot join.

**Suggestion.** Add only `gvm ports move <instance> NAME=PORT [--dry-run]`, and have it move the whole group by its stride. Refuse while the instance is running ('gvm stop pz1 first'). Optionally offer an explicit `--restart` that goes through the normal save-and-stop ladder; a reconciler never restarts anything. Run Allocate with the pin, reserve in the ledger, and write the YAML through hostops with literal values. Then print the delta for config keys and the firewall, plus a warning that players' saved addresses change, and tell the operator to run `gvm apply`. Have `apply --diff` run ports.CheckSpec as Warn steps. Make apply refuse when a port overlaps another instance's declared port or the reserved list (not an unattributed listener), naming the move command as the fix.

### network-firewall-8 — A partial or mistyped /etc/gvm/gvm.yaml silently turns off safe defaults and drops keys

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: **24** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Today's consumers: cli/ports.go:144 (reserved_ports only) and cli/doctor.go:38-60 (edge and FW checks). The zero-value admin trips checks_edge.go:116-122. (orig: hostConfig unmarshals into a zero spec.HostConfig instead of starting from the defaults (internal/cli/ports.go:165-169). doctorLoadHostConfig does the same with KnownFields(false) (internal/cli/doctor.go:141-145). Scratch run: a gvm.yaml containing only `spec: firewall: reserved_ports: [8080]` gave backend="" manage=false rate_limit=false fail2ban=false http3=false edge.mode="" caddy.admin="". These are exactly the regressions the DefaultHostConfig comment warns against (spec/host.go:127-131). Misspelled keys (`reserved_port:`, `admin_cidr:`) gave err=<nil> reserved=[] admin=[]. No HostConfig validator exists (grep finds none). The field comment at host.go:87 still says 'nft parses and returns ErrUnsupported', although an nft backend now exists.)

**Impact.** An operator who adds one line to gvm.yaml loses SSH rate limiting, fail2ban, the managed firewall and the safe Caddy admin socket, without any message. A misspelled reserved_ports entry lets the allocator hand out the port the operator meant to protect. With admin_cidrs misspelled, admin-only ports get no rule at all. In every case gvm reports success.

**Suggestion.** Use one spec.LoadHost for every reader: start from DefaultHostConfig(), decode on top, and run a second strict (KnownFields) pass to collect unknown keys with 'did you mean' hints. Treat an explicitly null section (`firewall:` with no value) as defaults. Add spec.ValidateHost: backend in ufw|none (nft rejected until finding 9 is reworked), parseable CIDRs, ports 1-65535, and the edge/tls enums. Policy: read-only commands (ports, status, list) warn once on stderr. Doctor reports a HOST-0xx Problem. Commands whose output depends on the file (create's allocation via reserved_ports, firewall and edge writes) refuse until it validates. Fix the host.go:87 comment.

### network-firewall-9 — Both firewall backends have defects that would break the ownership and anti-lockout model once wired

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: **40** · Status: open

**Evidence.** ufw delete also uses UFWSpec, quoted comment included (ufw_linux.go:47). nft ruleToNFT sets the comment to r.UFWSpec(), which already contains "comment '...'" (nft.go:154). (orig: ufw: Rule.UFWSpec wraps the comment in literal single quotes (internal/firewall/rule.go:110-112). Backend.Apply splits it with strings.Fields and execs ufw without a shell (internal/firewall/ufw/ufw_linux.go:36-41). The scratch run shows the argv "comment" "'gvm:pz1:game'", so ufw would store the quotes. tagRe requires ^gvm: (tag.go:17), so gvm would treat its own rules as foreign: closed ports never deleted, and an 'adopt' step re-issued on every apply. The golden corpus only contains unquoted comments (ufw/testdata/status/active-v4v6.txt), so tests never round-trip a stored comment. Also, a rule with no `from` renders as `allow port N proto P`, while ufw(8)'s grammar accepts PORT only after `from ADDRESS` or `to ADDRESS`; this needs checking in the Phase-11 Linux CI round-trip, which is still unchecked (IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:592). nft: buildScript and Enable begin with `flush table inet gvm` (nft.go:65,93), which fails on a host where that table does not exist yet. Rules carry `# gvm:` script comments (nft.go:109-110), which nft does not keep, so Status/parseRuleComments (nft.go:191-208) cannot read them back. VerbLimit becomes a plain accept (nft.go:145-151). The table's input chain sets `policy drop` (nft.go:97), which would also drop every port the operator opened in ufw outside gvm, such as WireGuard or node_exporter.)

**Impact.** Once the firewall verbs exist, ufw users would build up stale open ports that gvm refuses to remove, which undermines the 'closing an instance closes its door' promise. Switching to backend: nft would either fail on first use or, once its table exists, override the operator's own firewall and block unrelated services. Neither is visible from the plan output.

**Suggestion.** ufw: pass `comment` and `gvm:<inst>:<label>` as two bare argv elements. Emit `allow proto P to any port N`, with `from X` before `to` when set. Delete by spec without the comment. Never execute StepAdopt in Apply; that belongs to `gvm firewall adopt-rules` with confirmation. Add a parse-after-apply golden fixture and the Linux CI round-trip, including 'apply twice = zero steps'. nft: for this release, make Available() return false with 'nft backend not supported yet; use ufw or none', as IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:570 specified for v1, and have ValidateHost reject backend: nft. When it is reworked, declare `table inet gvm {}` before flushing and render from the full desired set, never from Steps. Use the native `comment "gvm:..."` attribute and `limit rate`. Use `ip saddr` or `ip6 saddr` by family and `meta l4proto {tcp,udp} th dport`. Use no `policy drop` unless `gvm firewall enable` explicitly made gvm the sole firewall, and refuse while ufw or firewalld is active.

### network-firewall-10 — 'expose: admin' can quietly produce no rule, or a rule for a port that is not reachable

**Low** · manifest-author · ergonomics · effort S · fact: partial · fit: adjust · Plan: **33** · Status: open

**Evidence.** bind semantics: internal/cli/run.go:630-650 (dial address). ARCHITECTURE.md ~6993-6997 (the FW-004 example recommends expose.rcon: admin). (orig: With empty admin_cidrs, Desired emits nothing for an admin port (internal/firewall/desired.go:79-86; spec/host.go:95-97 'Empty means an admin port gets no rule at all'), and nothing warns about it. Instance validation explicitly allows the none->admin widening (validate_instance.go:347-349) but never looks at the manifest's bind, while the loopback check exists only for expose:public (validate.go:590). Project Zomboid's rcon is `expose: none, bind: 127.0.0.1` (games/projectzomboid.yaml:130-132). Setting `firewall.expose: {rcon: admin}`, the case D-20 names, therefore validates cleanly and would open a rule for a socket bound to loopback.)

**Impact.** An operator follows the documented 'open RCON to my IP' pattern and either gets no rule (forgot admin_cidrs) or gets a rule that still cannot connect (loopback bind). In both cases gvm says nothing and the operator debugs the network instead of the config.

**Suggestion.** In ValidateInstance, make expose:admin on a loopback-bound manifest port an error. Use the manifest's note as the Fix, for example 'use ssh -L 27015:127.0.0.1:27015', following the validate.go public+loopback rule. Make empty admin_cidrs (host and instance) on an admin-exposed port a Warn in validate, `gvm firewall plan` and `apply --diff`: 'rcon: expose admin but no admin_cidrs: no rule will be created'. Print each port's effective exposure in `gvm create` and `gvm status`.

### network-firewall-11 — There is no operator-facing networking guide, and the README and help overstate what exists

**Low** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: partial · fit: sound · Plan: 33 → **40** · Status: open

**Evidence.** README.md:22-36 already hedges: 'Early', the milestone table, and the 'not implemented' sentence. (orig: docs/ contains ARCHITECTURE.md (7,148 lines, where networking is section 17), the implementation plan, bug sweeps, curseforge-blocked-mods.md and what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md. The Phase-12 docs network-edge.md, tls-cases.md and THREAT-MODEL.md (IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:613-614) do not exist. README.md:5 and :14-15 promise a derived firewall that 'keeps its firewall rules'. The top-level usage lists `firewall  manage host firewall rules` with no 'not built' marker, while the command exits 3.)

**Impact.** An operator has nowhere short to learn which ports to open (at ufw and at the cloud provider's security group, which gvm cannot see), how to avoid SSH lockout, how to fix a port conflict, or how to reach the dashboard. The README makes them believe gvm already handles the firewall.

**Suggestion.** Write docs/networking.md as task recipes: 'open a new server's ports today' (the output of `gvm firewall plan`, or the exact ufw commands), 'check your provider firewall too', 'SSH safety checklist' (keep a second session open, `gvm firewall ssh`), 'resolve a port conflict' (`gvm ports`, `gvm ports move`), 'reach the dashboard' (SSH tunnel first, then a domain with Caddy). Link it from the README, change the README status table to say the firewall and edge are not built yet, and mark unbuilt verbs in `gvm help`.


---

<a id="migration-adopt"></a>
## Adopting an existing server (`migration-adopt`)

You can't adopt the live Project Zomboid server with gvm today. Only `discover` and `finalize` are wired. `cutover` and `rollback` print stub messages, and the full runbook exists only in ARCHITECTURE.md §19, which uses flags and commands that are not built (--from-bash-suite, --write-plan, --rotate-credentials, `gvm instance promote`, `gvm content promote`). `discover` reads config.env from the install dir, but the bash suite keeps it next to its scripts (/root/pz-server), so it would fail on the reference server; its report also leaves out most of the lines the runbook tells the operator to check. The two wired-up pieces have real safety gaps. `finalize` skips its 7-day guard when --adopted-from is missing or uses the documented "bash-suite:..." format. The library code that will back `cutover` would cause harm if wired as written: it never disables the legacy nightly timer and never writes the Conflicts= guard, so the old suite can start a second server on the same world. Its rollback script extracts the archive into the wrong directory after restarting the server, and a failure before the commit point leaves the server stopped. The parts to keep are the read-only discovery design, ports read from the live ini, config.env parsed and never sourced, and the commit-point/auto-rollback structure. The plan should wire these pieces up behind an on-disk adoption record that the operator can inspect.

**Strengths to preserve**

- config.env is parsed, never sourced: ParseConfigEnv (internal/adopt/configenv.go:16) returns $(...) / backticks / ${VAR} as literal strings, backed by the fixture's DANGEROUS_KEY=$(rm -rf /) line
- Discovery is read-only by construction: DiscoverEnv (internal/adopt/env.go:8-17) exposes only ReadFile/ReadDir/Stat/UserProcesses, and TestDiscoverIsReadOnly asserts zero mutating calls. That makes 'safe with players online' credible
- Ports come from the live <server>.ini, never from manifest defaults (discover_pz.go:59-69, TestDiscoverPortsFromINI), so players keep the same address:port after adoption
- Ambiguous workshop items are named with their candidate folders (ADOPT-002), and the screen(1) wrapper is called out (ADOPT-004). These are the two-list pitfalls the legacy README warns about
- Cutover is split at a commit point, and a test injects a failure at each post-commit step and asserts automatic rollback (cutover_test.go:112-175)
- The rollback script is meant to depend only on systemctl and tar, and ValidateRollbackScript enforces that. 'Works even if gvm is broken' is the right property to keep
- managed_install:false is enforced consistently, with plain-language messages: install refuses (cli/install.go:96-99), and the nightly reports 'binary update X -> Y available; pz-main is adopted' without stopping the server (jobs/runner_update.go:103-111, TestAnAdoptedInstanceIsNeverStoppedForABinaryUpdateItMayNotPerform)
- `gvm create --user <existing>` points the operator to `gvm adopt` instead of half-building an instance (cli/create.go:151-155)
- `gvm restore --list --from <dir>` reads legacy bash-suite tarballs, so the old /var/backups/pzserver archives stay usable
- ARCHITECTURE.md §19.3/§19.6 is an excellent target runbook: Day 0 / Day 1 / Day 7 structure, 'what adoption never does', and 'Join the server from your own game client. Nothing else counts.' The plan should build to it
- The Conflicts= guard templates already exist and validate their inputs (reconcile/units.go:724-746, templates/adopt-guard.tmpl)

### migration-adopt-1 — `gvm adopt cutover` and `gvm adopt rollback` are stubs, so the core adoption journey cannot be completed, and the stub messages mislead

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 2 · Plan: 20 → 50 → **51** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The ErrNotImplemented exit-3 mapping is at internal/cli/root.go:230-232, not 224-227. Additional pointer to the unusable flow: internal/cli/create.go:153-154 ('an adoption (managed_install: false); use `gvm adopt`'). (orig: internal/cli/adopt.go:23-28: cutover prints "adopt cutover: not yet implemented in this phase (requires Linux)" and rollback prints "adopt rollback: run the rollback script printed by cutover". Ran `gvm.exe adopt cutover pz-main` -> "adopt cutover: not yet implemented in this phase (requires Linux)\ngvm adopt: adopt cutover: not yet implemented" exit=1, and `gvm.exe adopt rollback pz-main` -> "adopt rollback: run the rollback script printed by cutover" exit=1. CutoverPZ/OsCutoverEnv (internal/adopt/cutover.go:56,179) have no non-test caller (grep: only cutover_test.go). These are plain fmt.Errorf values, not ErrNotImplemented, so they exit 1 instead of the 'unbuilt' exit 3 (cli/root.go:224-227). IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:26 calls this Milestone C, 'the point of the project'.)

**Impact.** The operator can't move the live server under gvm at all. The '(requires Linux)' wording is hardcoded and also prints on Linux, which suggests a platform problem rather than an unbuilt feature. The rollback hint points at a script that no cutover has ever written.

**Suggestion.** Wire `gvm adopt cutover <instance>` and `gvm adopt rollback <instance>` to the library only after the safety fixes in findings 4-7 land. Until then, return ErrNotImplemented with: "gvm adopt cutover is not built in gvm <version>. Nothing on this host was changed; keep running zomboid.service as before." Have `rollback` say the same, without referring to a cutover script that cannot exist.

### migration-adopt-2 — `gvm adopt finalize` skips its 7-day guard unless the operator hand-types a specially formatted --adopted-from, then deletes whatever --unit-path names

**High** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 11, 1 · Plan: 21 → **50** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Also, --unit-path is not restricted to /etc/systemd/system/*.service, so any root-readable file can be copied and deleted (finalize.go:51-69). (orig: internal/adopt/finalize.go:33-43 applies the guard only `if !adoptedAt.IsZero()`. adoptedAtTime (internal/adopt/rollback.go:114-126) parses only "<unit>@<RFC3339>". cli/adopt.go:68 defaults --adopted-from to "". The spec's actual adopted_from is "bash-suite:/root/pz-server" (spec/instance_test.go:22, ARCHITECTURE.md §19.5), which contains no '@', so it parses as zero time and the guard is skipped. finalize.go:46-67 then copies and os.Remove()s any path given as --unit-path, with no `systemctl disable`, daemon-reload, timer removal, guard drop-in removal, or the §19.4 '24h active + 3 backups' checks. finalize.go:56-58 returns nil when the file is missing, and cli/adopt.go:85 still prints "finalize: <name> legacy unit archived and removed". `gvm.exe adopt finalize --help` lists four flags, two of them required, and none positional. Side effect for contributors: the tests call the real FinalizePZ, which MkdirAll's /var/lib/gvm/... before anything else; this machine has C:\var\lib\gvm\adopt\servertest-legacy-units dated 2026-10-13 23:35 (rollback_test.go:62,75).)

**Impact.** Breaks hard rule 11 in practice. `sudo gvm adopt finalize --name pz-main --unit-path /etc/systemd/system/zomboid.service` deletes the legacy unit immediately, even on a box that was never adopted. That leaves a dangling multi-user.target.wants symlink and a live server that won't come back after reboot. The one-week safety net that rollback depends on disappears without warning.

**Suggestion.** `gvm adopt finalize <instance> [--force]`. Split the preconditions into two classes. (a) STATE, never bypassable even with --force: the instance YAML exists with managed_install:false/adopted_from set; the adoption record (/var/lib/gvm/adopt/<name>.json) says state=cutover-complete; the legacy service and timer are disabled AND inactive; gvm@<name> is enabled and active. If any fails, refuse and name the failing check. If there is no record but the YAML says adopted, refuse with 'cannot tell when <name> was adopted or whether cutover completed', and say what to check. (b) TIME and EVIDENCE, which --force may bypass: 7 days since the record's cutover-completed timestamp; gvm@ active for 24h or more (systemd ActiveEnterTimestamp); at least 3 verified archives found by reading the on-disk archive index in /var/backups/gvm/<name> (internal/archive), with no Postgres read. Print the checklist, then: copy both legacy unit files and the timer's .service into <name>-legacy-units/, `systemctl disable` them, remove the files and both guard drop-ins, daemon-reload, and update the record. Print 'nothing to do' when there is nothing to do. Optionally also write adopted_at into the instance YAML at cutover, so the 7-day clock survives loss of /var/lib/gvm. Use a FinalizeEnv seam so tests stop creating C:\var\lib\gvm.

### migration-adopt-3 — Discover can't find config.env on the reference server, and the documented command shape is silently ignored

**High** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 11 · Plan: **25** · Status: open

**Evidence.** discover_pz.go:43 reads filepath.Join(r.InstallDir, "config.env") = /opt/pzserver/config.env. The suite keeps config.env next to its scripts: legacy lib/common.sh:17 says "config.env not found next to the scripts", the README:27-32 install is `scp -r pz-server root@vps:/root/` then `cd /root/pz-server`, and install.sh never copies it to INSTALL_DIR. The test fixture hides this by placing config.env at testdata/live-pz/rootfs/opt/pzserver/config.env. The runbook command is `sudo gvm adopt discover pz-main --from-bash-suite /root/pz-server [--write-plan]` (ARCHITECTURE.md:6597). Ran `gvm.exe adopt discover --write-plan` -> "flag provided but not defined: -write-plan". Ran `gvm.exe adopt discover pz-main --write-plan --totally-bogus` and got no flag error: Go's flag parsing stops at the positional and fs.Args() is never checked (cli/adopt.go:37-42). There is no instance-name argument, so cutover names everything after PZ's SERVER_NAME (cutover.go:100,121,127: /etc/gvm/instances/servertest.yaml, gvm@servertest.service), and nothing calls instance.ValidateName. runAdopt never calls Resolve(), so a non-root run gets a raw permission error rather than the usual 'Run it as root (sudo gvm adopt)' hint.

**Impact.** Step 2 of the runbook fails on the real box with "read config.env at /opt/pzserver/config.env: no such file or directory". An operator who copies the documented command gets a run that silently ignored their flags. The instance ends up called 'servertest', PZ's default name, which collides on a second PZ server and can fail D-45 when SERVER_NAME has upper case.

**Suggestion.** `gvm adopt discover <instance> [--from-bash-suite DIR] [--unit zomboid] [--write-plan]`, rejecting extra arguments and unknown flags anywhere on the line. Treat the RUNNING unit as authoritative for world identity: take DataDir from ExecStart's -cachedir= and ServerName from -servername (both are in the fixture unit). Locate the suite directory in this order: the --from-bash-suite flag; the $SCRIPT_DIR in zomboid-restart.service's `ExecStart=/usr/bin/env bash <dir>/update.sh`; /root/pz-server; otherwise ask for the flag. Print which source was used. Use config.env only for things the unit doesn't carry (BACKUP_DIR, RESTART_*, UPDATE_ON_RESTART, passwords), and make any mismatch between config.env and the unit (INSTALL_DIR vs WorkingDirectory, DATA_DIR vs -cachedir, SERVER_NAME vs -servername) a blocker. --write-plan may write only /var/lib/gvm/adopt/<instance>.plan.json and nothing else; without it, discover writes nothing. Add a fixture with config.env outside INSTALL_DIR and one with a mismatch.

### migration-adopt-4 — Cutover leaves the legacy nightly timer active and never writes the Conflicts= guard, so two servers can run on one world

**High** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6 · Plan: **50** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Guard plumbing also exists unused in internal/hostops/fs.go:83-88 and :183-193 (FileAdoptGuard/FileLegacyGuard); the gap is a missing caller, not missing infrastructure. (orig: cutover.go:166-172 disables only disc.UnitName (zomboid.service). zomboid-restart.timer is never read by discover or touched by cutover. That timer's service runs `ExecStart=/usr/bin/env bash $SCRIPT_DIR/update.sh` or `ExecStart=/bin/systemctl restart zomboid.service` (legacy nightly.sh:46,56), and update.sh:35 runs `systemctl start zomboid.service`; start and restart work on a disabled unit. generateDropin (cutover.go:220-226) writes only a '# Adopted from ...' comment. RenderAdoptGuard/RenderLegacyGuard (reconcile/units.go:724,738), which render Conflicts= in both directions, have no non-test caller. The generated rollback script runs `systemctl stop gvm@servertest.service` but never disables it. The doctor checks that §20 promises for this (INST-012 'zomboid.service disabled; Conflicts= on both', INST-013 'never rotated', ARCHITECTURE.md:7029-7030) do not exist; the implemented doctor ids are INST-001,002,003,005,008,009,010.)

**Impact.** At 05:00 on the first night after a 'successful' cutover, the old timer starts zomboid.service against the world gvm@ is serving. A rollback by script followed by a reboot starts both units. This is the 'never two servers on one world' catastrophe (§11.7), and nothing on the host would report it.

**Suggestion.** Discover reads /etc/systemd/system/zomboid*.{service,timer}, records whether the timer was enabled, and shows its schedule and whether it runs update.sh or a plain restart. At the commit point, run `systemctl disable --now zomboid.service zomboid-restart.timer`. Write gvm@<name>.service.d/20-adopt.conf and zomboid.service.d/20-gvm-adopt.conf from RenderAdoptGuard/RenderLegacyGuard, and daemon-reload, before starting gvm@. Rollback (library and generated script) disables --now gvm@<name> AND the instance's gvm-job@<name>_*.timer units, listed explicitly by name since the script is generated with the job names known. It re-enables the legacy timer only if the record says it was enabled, and leaves both guards in place. The cutover success block warns: 'do not run /root/pz-server/update.sh or rebuild.sh; starting zomboid.service will stop gvm@<name>'. Implement doctor INST-012 as a hard error, report only and never actuate (rule 6), when: both units are enabled, either guard is missing while the record says cutover, or the legacy unit is active while the record says cutover. Implement INST-013 for unrotated adopted credentials.

### migration-adopt-5 — The generated rollback script restores the archive to the wrong directory, after restarting the server, and discards all post-cutover play

**High** · operator · safety-ux · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 2, 4, 5 · Plan: **50** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The archive is `tar -czf dst -C /home/pzserver/Zomboid Saves` (cutover.go:90 + rollback.go:81-83), so its members are Saves/... The script extracts with `-C filepath.Dir(dataDir)` (rollback.go:38,48). Rendering GenerateRollbackScript with the fixture values gives, in order: `systemctl enable zomboid.service` / `systemctl start zomboid.service` / `tar -xzf /var/backups/pzserver/adopt-servertest-20261014T120000Z.tar.gz -C /home/pzserver` / `echo "=== Rollback complete. Legacy server started. ==="`. The files land in /home/pzserver/Saves, not /home/pzserver/Zomboid/Saves. The extract happens after the start, and it runs on every rollback whether the world is damaged or not. The §19.2 design script only echoes the restore commands, with stop -> extract -> chown -> start.

**Impact.** An operator who rolls back on day 3 because of a gvm bug gets 'Rollback complete' while the world is never actually restored. If the path were right, the extract would overwrite a running server's save (corruption) and silently throw away three days of player progress. The tool's emergency exit is the most dangerous command in the flow.

**Suggestion.** The generated script runs only systemctl: disable --now gvm@<name> and its gvm-job timers, then enable --now the legacy unit, plus the timer if it was enabled. It then ECHOES, and does not run, a restore sequence in the correct order: `systemctl stop zomboid.service`; move the current world aside (e.g. `mv <DataDir>/Saves/Multiplayer/<srv> <...>.after-cutover-<ts>`, and likewise for _player and db/); `tar -xzf <archive> -C <DataDir> <prefix-strip matching the internal/archive member layout>`; `chown -R <user>:<group> <DataDir>`; `systemctl start zomboid.service`. Mention `gvm restore` only after the restore engine is built AND refuses while the adoption record says rolled-back or the legacy unit is active. gvm never executes the script (rule 2). Add a test that builds the real pre-cutover archive, applies the printed -C and strip, and asserts the files land under DataDir.

### migration-adopt-6 — Cutover failures can leave the server stopped, claim a rollback that didn't happen, and lose the recovery paths

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7, 1 · Plan: **50** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The hold/enabled:false requirement is ARCHITECTURE.md:6768-6769 (automatic rollback item 5) and IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md Phase 13 DoD ('instance is marked degraded and held'), not §19.2 step 5. (orig: cutover.go:69-105 stops the legacy unit at step 2. A failure at step 3 (fingerprint), step 4 (mkdir/tar, e.g. disk full) or step 5 (rollback script) returns an error with the server still stopped; §19.2 says 'Timeout or failure -> systemctl start zomboid.service, abort'. The rollback closure (cutover.go:109-114) discards every error (`_ = env.SystemctlStop/Enable/Start`) but always returns ErrCutoverRolledBack, whose text is "cutover step %d failed (%v); rollback executed" (cutover.go:45). The closure returns a nil *CutoverResult, so BackupPath and RollbackScriptPath never reach the caller. The rollback script is written at step 5, after the stop and archive; the design says step 2, 'BEFORE anything reversible'. No enabled:false or hold is written, and the instance YAML written at step 6 stays in /etc/gvm/instances.)

**Impact.** The most likely real-world failure, running out of disk while archiving a multi-GB world, leaves players offline with only "cutover step 4: backup: ..." on screen and no instruction to restart. When the automatic rollback itself fails (for example, zomboid.service won't start), the operator is still told 'rollback executed' while the server is down. They also no longer know where the archive or rollback script is.

**Suggestion.** Write the rollback script, the adoption record, and the chosen archive path before touching anything, and print them. Record whether the legacy unit and timer were active and enabled at the start. On any failure before the commit point, return the legacy unit and timer to exactly that state, rather than always starting them, and print 'Legacy server restored to its previous state; nothing else was changed'. Post-commit rollback reports each action with ✓/✗ plus the error, returns ErrCutoverRolledBack only if every step succeeded, and otherwise returns a distinct 'rollback incomplete' error that prints the manual sequence in red. Return a populated CutoverResult (archive and script paths) on every path, success or failure. To keep gvm from fighting the rollback, use the following instead of enabled:false: disable gvm@<name> and its gvm-job timers, write the /run hold, and set state=rolled-back in the adoption record. Then make firewall desired-state (and the reconciler) keep the instance's port rules while the record says rolled-back, or have rollback put the pre-adoption rules back untagged. Either way, the ports never close on the legacy server.

### migration-adopt-7 — The pre-cutover archive leaves out server config and player accounts, isn't a verified preserve archive, and the 'same world' check can't catch a fresh world

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: **50** · Status: open

**Evidence.** cutover.go:84-93 runs a raw `tar` of DataDir/Saves only, into /var/backups/pzserver (the legacy BACKUP_DIR, which the suite chowns to pzserver; ARCHITECTURE.md:6821 says gvm archives go to root-only /var/backups/gvm/<name>). The §19.2 step 5 archive is class=preserve via internal/archive, contains Server/, Saves/Multiplayer/<name>{,_player}/, db/, config.env, mods.txt and sandbox.txt, and is followed by verify --spot 24; none of that is done. The post-start check (cutover.go:146-164) is superset-only. It has no 'newest mtime advanced' check (§19.2 11b), no UDP probe and no RCON handshake. A wrong -cachedir makes PZ generate a fresh world elsewhere, while the old files stay put and pass the superset check.

**Impact.** If an adoption goes wrong, the only gvm-made archive can't bring back the server ini, sandbox settings or player accounts (db/). `gvm restore` treats it as a legacy, reduced-verification tarball. The operator can get a success message while players spawn in a brand-new world, the exact catastrophe D-34 exists to catch.

**Suggestion.** Before stopping anything, estimate the archive size and compare it with statvfs on /var/backups/gvm; refuse while the server is still up if free space is below estimate x1.2, so 'disk full mid-archive' cannot happen inside the downtime window. Then use archive.CreateAndVerify: class=preserve, reason=adopt-pre-cutover, Dir=/var/backups/gvm/<name> (root-only), Sources = DataDir {Server/, Saves/Multiplayer/<srv>{,_player}/, db/} plus the suite's config.env/mods.txt/sandbox.txt under a separate prefix, SecretsIncluded=true. Verify with spot 24. On failure, restore the legacy unit to its prior state and stop. For world verification after start, don't depend on PZ choosing to write: after SERVER STARTED, send an RCON `save`, then require (a) every pre-cutover path present, (b) newest mtime under the world dir greater than the fingerprint time, within a budget, (c) the UDP probe, and (d) RCON auth. Print the 'N/N files present, newest write Xs ago -> same world' line.

### migration-adopt-8 — Discover's finding severities and exit status don't line up with what blocks cutover, and no finding says how to fix it

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 11 · Plan: **25** · Status: open

**Evidence.** discover.go:44-48: DiscoverFinding has Code/Severity/Message but no Fix (§19.1 specifies Finding{Severity, Code, Message, Fix}). discover_pz.go:169-175 makes ADOPT-001 (Mods= count != WorkshopItems= count) an 'error', although §19.1 treats that diff as expected (dual-build items) and preserved verbatim. ADOPT-003 'SERVER_PASSWORD is set in cleartext' is an 'error' whose remedy (`--rotate-credentials`) doesn't exist. cli/adopt.go:53-57 exits non-zero with "adopt discover: 2 finding(s) require attention before cutover" (golden: '2 errors · 1 warning · 1 note'). CutoverPZ never looks at Findings, so 'error' blocks nothing, while the §19.1 blockers (instance exists, gvm@ already enabled, data dir not writable, low disk, world dir missing, two appmanifests, JVM pid not found) are not checked at all.

**Impact.** A normal B41/B42 server always 'fails' discovery with two errors the operator can't clear, and isn't told what attention is needed. Meanwhile the conditions that really should stop a cutover go unreported. Operators learn to ignore red output on the step that matters most.

**Suggestion.** Add Fix to DiscoverFinding and use the tiers blocker/warning/note. ADOPT-001 becomes a note ('Mods= preserved verbatim; review after cutover with gvm content diff <name>'). ADOPT-003 becomes a warning whose fix names only commands that exist in this build. End with 'Cutover would proceed' or 'Cutover would REFUSE: <codes>', and exit non-zero (a distinct code) only on blockers. Implement the §19.1 blockers using reads only: instance YAML exists; gvm@<name> enabled (wants symlink); world dir missing; two appmanifest_380870.acf; JVM pid not found while the unit is active (/proc); free disk below estimate x1.2 (statvfs). For data-dir writability, check owner, group and mode bits with stat against PZ_USER's uid/gids, and never write a probe file during discover. The real write-probe belongs in cutover/apply (D-15). Cutover re-runs the same discovery as step 1 and refuses on any blocker.

### migration-adopt-9 — No adoption record and no `gvm adopt status`: during the week between cutover and finalize the operator has to remember everything

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 1 · Plan: 50 → **51** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The adoption fact does surface in `gvm update` (internal/cli/update.go:144-149) and in the nightly job's skip reason (internal/jobs/runner_update.go:103-111), just not in status or the web. (orig: Nothing in internal/adopt writes a state file. --write-plan (/var/lib/gvm/adopt/<name>.plan.json, §19.1) doesn't exist, and finalize requires --name, --unit-path and --adopted-from by hand (cli/adopt.go:65-73). `gvm status` prints lifecycle.Status.Report() (cli/status.go:79-81), which has no adoption fields, although lifecycle/observe.go:169-170 already collects ManagedInstall and AdoptedFrom. internal/web has no adopt or managed_install reference, although §16.7 promises an Adopt wizard page and an 'adopted — gvm will not run app_update … validate' badge on the overview. The runbook spreads the post-cutover work over Day 0, Day 1 and Day 7 (ARCHITECTURE.md:6843-6936).)

**Impact.** A week after cutover the operator has to rediscover which legacy unit, which archive and which rollback script belong to the adoption, and when the 7-day window ends. Moderators on the dashboard can't see that the server is in its adoption week or that binary updates are only being reported.

**Suggestion.** Write /var/lib/gvm/adopt/<name>.json (filesystem control plane, rule 1) at discover --write-plan and at each cutover step: state, times, legacy unit and timer, archive, rollback script, credentials rotated. Add `gvm adopt status <name>`: "cutover 2026-09-04 (day 3 of 7) · gvm@pz-main active 2d 22h · backups 3/3 ok · rollback: sudo gvm adopt rollback pz-main · finalize available 2026-09-11". Add one line to `gvm status`, "adopted from zomboid.service (day 3/7); binary updates reported, not installed", and surface the same text in the dashboard overview.

### migration-adopt-10 — `gvm instance promote`, the only documented way to let gvm update an adopted server, doesn't exist, and five messages point to it

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: 20 → **51** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm instance promote` is also referenced in internal/provider/adopted/adopted.go:72, internal/provider/minecraft/provider.go:93, internal/provider/provider.go:280 and :290, and internal/provider/steamcmd/steamcmd.go:333 and :389, besides the five cited. `gvm content promote` is referenced in user-facing messages at internal/cli/content.go:1653, internal/content/lock.go:509 and internal/content/source.go:502. (orig: Ran `gvm.exe instance promote pz-main` -> "gvm: unknown command \"instance\"" exit=2, and `gvm.exe help instance` -> "gvm help: unknown command \"instance\"". Referenced in cli/update.go:147-149, update/pipeline.go:410, update/repair.go:128-130, jobs/runner_update.go:109-110 (the nightly's own 'update available' reason) and spec/capability.go:490, plus runbook step 13 (ARCHITECTURE.md:6912). Runbook step 9 also uses `gvm content promote pz-main --dry`; ran it -> "gvm content: unknown subcommand \"promote\"".)

**Impact.** Every night the adopted server logs 'Allow gvm to update it with: gvm instance promote pz-main', and the operator who follows that advice hits 'unknown command'. The only real path is hand-editing managed_install in the YAML, which nothing documents.

**Suggestion.** Now: change every message that names `gvm instance promote` to 'taking over binary updates for adopted instances is not available in gvm <version>; updates are reported and never installed', and remove `gvm content promote` from the runbook. With or after cutover: implement `gvm instance promote <name> --confirm <name>`, which takes and verifies a class=preserve archive, flips managed_install via the content_yaml-style Node edit, runs `gvm apply`, and records it in the adoption record. Before acting, it prints the plan: 'the nightly will now stop, update via SteamCMD as <user>, post-verify the appmanifest and start; it never runs validate'. For Minecraft, list which files the provider will now overwrite.

### migration-adopt-11 — Discover's report leaves out most of what the runbook tells the operator to check, and never says it changed nothing

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 11 · Plan: **25** · Status: open

**Evidence.** report.go:13-49 prints only service/install/data/user/pid, three bare port numbers and two content counts (golden testdata/golden/discover-pz-main.txt). Runbook step 3 (ARCHITECTURE.md:6853-6856) says 'is the world path the one with your save in it? is the free-disk line green?', but neither line exists. The §19.1 sources table (timers, ufw rules, appmanifest buildid, backups dir, credentials file existence, statvfs headroom, world size and mtime) is not read; DiscoverEnv has no statvfs. The pid line names only the first match, and detectPIDType (discover_pz.go:151-161) returns the screen shell before the game process, so 'running (screen, pid N)' looks like the game pid. Ports have no protocol and no exposure note (16262/udp is required; RCON is 127.0.0.1-only). The report doesn't end with 'Nothing has been changed' or a next command.

**Impact.** Before the one risky step, the operator can't confirm the facts that decide whether the world survives: the world path, disk space for the archive, the nightly schedule and the firewall. Running an unfamiliar tool against a live server with players on, they get no explicit reassurance that discovery was read-only.

**Suggestion.** First tier, required before cutover ships: WORLD (path, size, file count, newest mtime), DISK (free space vs estimated archive x1.2, which feeds the blocker), SCHEDULE (legacy timer enabled, OnCalendar, update.sh vs restart), shell and JVM pids each with starttime, and ports shown with protocol and exposure ('16261/udp game · 16262/udp data (both must be open) · 27015/tcp rcon 127.0.0.1 only'). Second tier: BACKUPS (legacy dir, count) and CREDENTIALS (existence of cleartext copies, contents never read). Build FIREWALL from the same read-only parser D-42 uses; if parsing is not confident, print 'firewall: could not be parsed; nothing will be changed', never a guess. Take the player address only from local evidence (the host spec's public address, or the server-side address in SSH_CONNECTION), and never call an external IP-echo service. Always end with 'Nothing has been changed.' The next-step line must name only commands that exist in the running build: until cutover is wired, print 'cutover is not available in gvm <version>'.

### migration-adopt-12 — The config.env parser keeps literal quote characters when a quoted value has an inline comment, which gives wrong values and false findings

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 10 · Plan: **25** · Status: open

**Evidence.** configenv.go:41-52 checks for surrounding quotes before stripping a trailing '  #' comment. I ran a scratch copy of ParseConfigEnv over legacy/projectzomboid-bash/config.env.example and got: ADMIN_PASSWORD => "\"\"", SSH_PORT => "\"\"", ADMIN_IP => "\"\"", SERVER_PASSWORD => "\"REDACTED-see-the-live-server-config.env\"". The suite's template writes exactly this shape (`ADMIN_PASSWORD=""                    # empty = generated ...`, config.env.example:41).

**Impact.** An empty value written with a comment parses as a two-character string, so ADOPT-003 fires as an 'error' for a server with no join password. Any credential imported from config.env would contain `"`, which rule 10 forbids for game passwords, so the adopted secret would be wrong or rejected.

**Suggestion.** Parse quote-aware, still without evaluating anything. If the value starts with ' or ", take everything up to the matching closing quote; after it allow only whitespace and an optional '#...' comment, and flag anything else as an unparseable line. For a bare value, the comment starts at the first '#' preceded by whitespace. Then apply rule 10 at import: if an imported game password contains ' " \ $ or `, raise a blocker ('password for <role> contains characters gvm cannot carry; rotate it') rather than importing it altered. Add the vendored config.env.example as a fixture asserting ADMIN_PASSWORD=="", SSH_PORT=="" and SERVER_PASSWORD unquoted, plus a quoted value containing ' #'.

### migration-adopt-13 — The cutover design has no progress reporting for the downtime window

**Low** · operator · missing-feature · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: **51** · Status: open

**Evidence.** CutoverPZ (cutover.go:56) takes no progress writer or callback, and CutoverResult (cutover.go:30-35) carries only paths and two timestamps. The long steps are silent: the legacy stop can take ExecStop save + `sleep 10` + quit up to TimeoutStopSec=180 (zomboid.service fixture:19-25), the tar of a multi-GB world runs via exec with CombinedOutput buffered (proc_linux.go:13-14), and a PZ workshop boot can take up to hard_deadline 3600s (§19.2 step 10). The §19.2 design shows only the final summary.

**Impact.** During the one window when players are offline, the operator watches a silent terminal for minutes. They can't tell a slow save from a hang, and are tempted to Ctrl-C mid-archive or kill the process, which the save-and-stop ladder exists to prevent.

**Suggestion.** Give CutoverPZ a step reporter, and also append each step to the adoption record and a log under /var/log/gvm/<name>/, so `gvm adopt status <name>` shows progress from a new SSH session. Get archive progress from internal/archive's byte counter (finding 7) rather than exec tar. Handle signals: ignore SIGHUP for the whole run. On SIGINT before the commit point, finish the current step, restore the legacy unit's prior state and exit. After the commit point, SIGINT means 'roll back at the next safe point', never die mid-step. Print the commit marker, the downtime counter and the final §19.2 block. For the stop step, implement §19.2 step 4: save via the console, wait for save evidence, quit, poll the pinned JVM pid+starttime within its budget, and on timeout abort without escalating to SIGKILL, reporting '[2/12] save confirmed, JVM exited in 41s'.

### migration-adopt-14 — The adopt runbook is buried in the 7,000-line design doc, describes commands that don't exist, and `gvm help adopt` is one line

**Low** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: partial · fit: sound · Plan: 25 → **51** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm help <cmd>` is one line for every command (e.g. `gvm help status` -> 'gvm status — show instance state'). Bare `gvm adopt` prints the subcommand list (internal/cli/adopt.go:14-15). README.md:43 links docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §19. (orig: docs/adopt-projectzomboid.md is planned (IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:650, '§19.6 standalone') but absent; docs/ contains no such file. The runbook starts at ARCHITECTURE.md:6590 and uses --from-bash-suite, --write-plan, --rotate-credentials, --promote, --yes, `gvm instance promote` and `gvm content promote`, none of which exist. Ran `gvm.exe help adopt` -> "gvm adopt — import an existing server install" (no subcommands, no safety note). `gvm.exe adopt --help` -> "gvm adopt: adopt: unknown subcommand \"--help\"" exit=1. DiscoverMC (discover_mc.go:14) is unreachable from the CLI, and the report title hardcodes 'Project Zomboid' (report.go:13), so a Minecraft operator gets no signal about whether adoption applies to them.)

**Impact.** A non-developer operator has to find §19 inside an architecture document, then run into flag errors on the first documented command. That undermines trust in a procedure whose whole value is being exact.

**Suggestion.** Ship docs/adopt-projectzomboid.md generated from what is actually built, with a 'not yet available' callout for anything unbuilt, and link it from README. Make `gvm help adopt` and `gvm adopt --help` list discover/cutover/rollback/finalize, each with a one-line safety statement ("discover — reads only; safe with players online", "cutover — one supervised downtime window; archives first; rolls back by itself", "finalize — refuses within 7 days without --force"). Say explicitly that only Project Zomboid bash-suite installs can be adopted today.


---

<a id="failure-recovery"></a>
## When things go wrong (`failure-recovery`)
