The parts of gvm that record a failure are well built: the ordered phase table with separate reason and hint, the shutdown ladder report (rung plus DEGRADED), the job engine's running guard and loud "X IS STOPPED" line, SteamCMD error classification, and backup's free-space refusal. The paths that lead an operator from "something is wrong" to "here is the fix" are mostly broken or unwired. `gvm doctor` never looks at an instance (doctorLoadInstances returns nil). It reports firewall problems on every host because live ufw state is stubbed, and its fixes name commands that are not built. The phase-table hints also send operators to commands that do not exist (`gvm manifest upgrade`, `gvm enable`, `gvm disable`), and a pin mismatch, which cannot be overridden, has no way forward at all. Crash loops show up as "starting" and port conflicts as "an orphaned game process". `gvm stop` cannot stop an instance whose runner is not up. Nightly job outcomes (failed update, needs_repair, server left stopped) never reach `gvm status`, `gvm job list` or any on-disk record. The Postgres-down recovery commands in the doc fail as written (wrong spool directory; `gvm audit tail` needs a gvmd that is not shipped). Most fixes are wiring or message changes over code that already exists, and none of them needs to weaken the safety model.

**Strengths to preserve**

- The phase table (internal/lifecycle/derive.go) is ordered, pure and names its rule (`phase_rule` in --json). It separates Reason (what was observed) from Hint (what to run), and prints 'not checked on this host' instead of guessing 'stopped' when systemd did not answer (status.go:190-197). Keep this vocabulary; most suggestions below feed more facts into it.
- Job failures that leave a server down are loud and specific: Run.Line appends '-- THE SERVER IS STOPPED' and printJobTail prints 'X IS STOPPED. gvm-guard.timer runs within 5 minutes; `gvm start X` is immediate.' (job.go:305-308, execute.go:448-449).
- Shutdown outcomes are recorded and readable: shutdown.jsonl keeps the final rung and a DEGRADED flag, `gvm logs --shutdown` still shows every other record when one line is torn (logs.go:163-190), and `gvm stop` prints 'ladder ended on <rung> after <t>' with an explicit 'world may not have saved' warning (start.go:139-147).
- Refusals tell the operator whether --force is allowed and say so once ('this refusal can be overridden: gvm start X --force'). Pin mismatches are deliberately not forceable (verbs.go:123-136, start.go:112-123).
- `gvm job clear` refuses a LIVE lease, names its unit and says exactly what --force would do (job.go:696-706).
- SteamCMD output is classified with plain-language explanations of undocumented codes ('0x202 out of disk space or over quota'), a 3-attempt backoff, and a 'start on existing binaries + run `gvm update X --repair`' fallback (classify.go:227-237, runner_update.go:326-337).
- Backups refuse before the freeze when free space is short, so a full disk never leaves a truncated tarball and autosave switched off (backup/engine.go:477-504).
- status, list, logs, stop, start and doctor are all file- or systemd-derived and work with Postgres down. Doctor says 'POSTGRES not reachable; database checks skipped' at the top and records a panicking check as a problem instead of crashing.
- The unit file encodes the right supervision policy: RestartPreventExitStatus for failures a restart cannot fix, LogRateLimit 0 so crash lines are not dropped, and the console log kept out of journald (packaging/systemd/gvm@.service:54-75).

### failure-recovery-1 — `gvm doctor` never checks any instance, though every failure path sends the operator there

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 1, 6 · Plan: **24** · Status: open

**Evidence.** One extra detail: INST-001's own Fix text, 'run: gvm apply <n>' (checks_inst.go:25,33), would also fail on a real pin mismatch. cli/instance.go:510-517 pinnedManifest verifies the spec pin against the lock and errors out. (orig: internal/cli/doctor.go:150-151 `func doctorLoadInstances() []spec.Instance { return nil }` and `func doctorLoadManifest(...) *spec.Manifest { return nil }`, so the INST loop at doctor.go:65-70 never runs. The instance checks that do exist are mostly stubs: INST-003 always Skips with 'requires systemd introspection (Phase 14)' (checks_inst.go:98-102), INST-005 'requires Postgres mirror (Phase 14)' (:111-113), INST-009 'workshop tree check requires resolved data path' (:136). No lifecycle Observe/Findings (stale lease, failed unit, drift, degraded breadcrumb) and no `gvm ports` check is called. doctor also ignores a positional name: `gvm doctor x` prints the same host-only report. Places that send operators to doctor anyway: derive.go:432 (exit 65 hint `gvm doctor <name>`), every shipped manifest's fail_marker hint, e.g. games/7daystodie.yaml:65-66 'another server holds this port; check `gvm doctor` and `ss -lntup`', and runner_update.go:357 '`gvm doctor` will suggest clearing the item's directory'. ports.go:10 calls `gvm ports` 'gvm doctor's port half', but doctor never calls it. ARCHITECTURE.md:7009-7039 specifies an 'INSTANCE pz-main' section; the real output has none.)

**Impact.** When a server fails preflight (exit 65), hits a port conflict or stalls on workshop content, gvm and the manifests tell the operator to run `gvm doctor`. It returns a host-level list with nothing about the broken instance, so the most-advertised diagnostic is a dead end in exactly the cases it is suggested for.

**Suggestion.** Implement doctorLoadInstances with lifecycle.List and load each manifest from /etc/gvm/instances/<n>.d/manifest.lock.yaml. For each instance, also emit INST-level findings from lifecycle.Observe + lifecycle.Findings (the same pure function `gvm status` uses, so the two cannot disagree) and from ports.Check (the `gvm ports <n>` logic). Accept `gvm doctor [<instance>]` to scope the run. Group output under 'INSTANCE <name>' headers as in ARCHITECTURE §20. All of this is read-only and DB-free, so rules 1 and 6 hold.

### failure-recovery-2 — Doctor reports problems on every healthy host, and its fixes name commands that do not exist

**High** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: **24** → 40 · Status: open

**Evidence.** ufw.Backend.Status (ufw_linux.go:26-33) drops the parser's completeness flag. ParseResult.Trusted already exists (ufw/parse.go:18,52), so wiring FW-003 means returning Trusted from Status, not just calling it. (orig: doctor.go:56-62: 'Firewall state: stub until ufw backend CLI wiring is in place.' with `liveActive := false`, `liveTrusted := false`, an empty RuleSet and nil instances. FW-001 and FW-003 are therefore always CheckProblem (checks_fw.go:51-57, 98-105), even though internal/firewall/ufw/ufw_linux.go:26 already has a real `Status(ctx)`. Running `gvm doctor` printed: '✗ FW-001     firewall backend ufw is not active\n     Fix: gvm firewall enable' and '✗ FW-003     ufw status numbered could not be fully parsed; deletions suppressed (D-42)'. But `gvm firewall enable` prints 'gvm firewall: not implemented yet' with exit=3 (root.go:152 `Run: notImplemented`). FW-002 and FW-006 also mention `gvm firewall apply`. HOST-011's fix is 'restart gvmd — it calls ReapStaleRuns' (checks_host_db.go:270), but packaging/systemd ships no gvmd.service and `gvmd` is a hidden notImplemented role (root.go:101). HOST-010 reads the binary's own embedded unit copies (checks_host.go:39-50, packaging.FS), not /usr/lib/systemd/system, so it can never fail on a real host. IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:621 sets the bar as 'a clean host reports 0 problems — so a permanently non-zero count never trains anyone to ignore it'.)

**Impact.** Every `gvm doctor` run ends with '3 problem(s) found' and exit 1, two of them false. Operators learn to ignore the count and the red crosses, and the fixes on offer cannot be run. An operator who tries `ufw enable` by hand to clear FW-001 bypasses the SSH-lockout guard gvm built for exactly that step.

**Suggestion.** 1) Extend firewall.Backend.Status to return the parser's Trusted flag, or add StatusDetailed; ufw.Parse already computes it. Wire the configured backend into runDoctor (exec ufw directly, no shell). 2) FW-001: Problem only when the backend really reports inactive. Use Warning (not Skip) when the configured backend is not installed or its status cannot be read, and Skip only when backend is none. 3) FW-001's Fix must never say `ufw enable`. Until `gvm firewall enable` ships, it should say 'gvm will not enable the firewall for you (D-41); see docs/troubleshooting.md#firewall', and that section must spell out the SSH-admit-first sequence. 4) HOST-010: for each gvm@<n>.service and the job template, read the merged dependency properties (Requires, Wants, After, BindsTo, PartOf, Requisite) over read-only D-Bus. That covers every drop-in location. 5) HOST-011: drop 'restart gvmd'. Make it a Note saying the rows are harmless and are reaped when gvmd runs. 6) In the cli package, add a test that iterates every doctor Fix and lifecycle Hint and asserts that each leading `gvm <verb>` resolves through lookup() to a Run other than notImplemented.

### failure-recovery-4 — Crash loops show as 'starting', and port conflicts as 'an orphaned game process'

**High** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6 · Plan: **28** · Status: open

**Evidence.** derive.go:447-452 comment: 'the runner never emits it'. Manifests without the 'address already in use' marker: games/corekeeper.yaml, games/projectzomboid.yaml, games/valheim.yaml. (orig: Between crashes, systemd holds a Restart=on-failure unit in activating (SubState auto-restart) for RestartSec=20 (gvm@.service:54-55). derive.go:284-288 maps every 'activating' to PhaseStarting with reason 'the unit is starting'. phase.go:151-153 makes Starting SevNotice, so `gvm list --problems` (list.go:82, onlyProblems at :94) hides it, and Summarise (status.go:262-268) counts it as 'stopped'. platform.UnitState (platform.go:46-59) has no NRestarts. Port conflicts: every shipped manifest declares fail_marker 'address already in use' as fatal. That readiness failure exits with the game's status or 75 (run.go:485-488 `reportExit(rep, runner.ExitTempFail)`). exitMeaning(75) says 'an orphaned game process from a previous run is still alive' and exitHint(75) is 'gvm status <n>' (derive.go:440-442, 517-518), which points back to the command already being read. Exit 66 ('port unavailable at bind time', ARCHITECTURE.md:1437; derive.go:433) is emitted by nothing: grep of internal/runner and cli/run.go finds no producer.)

**Impact.** A server crashing every 30 seconds reads as 'starting', is left out of `gvm list --problems`, and counts as 'stopped' in the summary. After five crashes it becomes failed with a message blaming an orphan process. Operators go looking for a phantom process instead of the port clash or mod crash actually causing it.

**Suggestion.** Read NRestarts and SubState over the Service properties call you already make (systemd_linux.go:152). Add RuleCrashLooping when ActiveState=activating and SubState=auto-restart, which is unambiguous, or when NRestarts>0. Use PhaseDegraded with reason 'restarted N times; last exit <code> (<meaning>)', hint 'gvm logs <n> --shutdown', and count it as needing attention. Give readiness failures a distinct code, documented in the §7.8 table and not in RestartPreventExitStatus. Fix exitMeaning(75). Produce 66 deterministically in one of two ways: (a) the manifest enum from finding 14, or (b) a pre-spawn bind probe in the runner of the instance's declared ports. That is a plain bind/close as the instance user, with no shell, and it exits 66 before the game starts. Either way systemd stops the loop and the existing `gvm ports <n>` hint fires.

### failure-recovery-5 — `gvm stop` cannot stop an instance whose runner is not up (crash loop, failed preflight, already stopped)

**High** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: **28** · Status: open

**Evidence.** manager.go:242-245: the stop runs m.Shutdown first and returns on error, so the systemd stop step at :263-268 is never reached. stop.go:49-54 consoleShutdown dials /run/gvm/<n>/console.sock; with no runner this returns dialError, 'X is not running: there is no console socket at …' (console.go:617-618), or a raw connection-refused for a stale socket file. Meanwhile systemd keeps restarting the unit every 20 s. On an already-stopped instance the hold file is written first (manager.go:233-241), then the command still exits with that error, so the operator is told it failed when the hold was in fact set. `gvm stop --budget` is documented as 'bound the shutdown ladder' (start.go:43) but stop.go:60-65 does `_ = budget`. The rung-by-rung progress callback exists in socket/client.go:261-278 but stop.go:68 passes nil (`c.Stop("", "gvm stop", nil, nil)`), so a 5-minute ladder prints nothing until it ends. The user's --reason is not passed to the runner either; it sends the fixed string 'gvm stop'.

**Impact.** The command an operator reaches for first in a crash loop fails and calls a restarting server 'not running'. They must already know `systemctl stop gvm@<n>`, which gvm never mentions. During a slow or blocked save, `gvm stop` looks hung, which invites Ctrl-C and reaching for kill.

**Suggestion.** In Manager.execute, when the unit is activating/auto-restart, failed or inactive and the socket is absent or refuses connections, skip the ladder and issue hostops VerbUnitStop. That is safe under rule 4: either no game process exists, or systemd's SIGTERM reaches the runner, which still walks the ladder (KillMode=mixed). Record rung 'no-runner' in the audit row and print 'no runner was up; asked systemd to stop the unit (nothing to save)'. For an already-stopped instance print 'already stopped; hold set so the guard leaves it down' and exit 0. Pass a step callback that prints each rung ('save sent… waiting for save marker (40s/300s)'). Pass --reason through. Either drop --budget from `gvm stop` or say in its help that the budget comes from the spec.

### failure-recovery-6 — Results of nightly jobs never reach status, job list or disk: a failed update, a needed repair, a server left stopped

**High** · operator · missing-feature · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1 · Plan: **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** No INSERT INTO job_runs in the repo (grep finds only UPDATEs in internal/store/reap.go:11 and internal/store/dbgen/opsq/opsq.go:55). The job's audit span (execute.go:618-622, span.Done with jr.AuditParams) is written to the on-disk spool by cli/instance.go:316. (orig: A job's Run record goes only to stdout/slog (the journal of gvm-job@<slug>), to the audit span with params kind/left_stopped only (execute.go:421-432), and to Postgres job_runs when it is up. Nothing writes it to disk: instance.Paths.JobLog (paths.go:275) has no caller, though hostops creates /var/log/gvm/<n>/jobs (fs.go:349-352). `gvm job` has only run/list/clear (job.go:56-58). `gvm job list` columns are 'SLUG KIND STOPS ON FAILURE SCHEDULE', with no last run or result (job.go:621). The Observation struct (observe.go:160-215) does not read /run/gvm/<n>/degraded, which only the guard sweep reads (sweep.go:228-247), and does not read the needs_repair flag set at runner_update.go:330. HOST-012 'left_stopped in the last 24 h' needs Postgres (checks_host_db.go:274-306). `gvm audit tail` cannot help either (see failure-recovery-9).)

**Impact.** The questions every operator asks in the morning, such as 'did last night's backup work?', 'why was pz-main down at 05:10?' and 'does it need a repair?', have no gvm answer. With Postgres down the only source is reading `journalctl -u gvm-job@pz-main_nightly`, which gvm never names. A backup failing every night stays invisible until a restore is needed.

**Suggestion.** Have Execute's finish step write the Run JSON to /var/lib/gvm/instances/<n>/jobs/<started>-<job>.json, and host jobs to /var/lib/gvm/jobs/. Both are root-owned 0750 state directories, like lease.json. Write with an atomic rename and O_NOFOLLOW, through the existing hostops file-kind table if possible. Keep the last N records per job so the directory stays bounded. Then add `gvm job last [<n>]`, `gvm job history <n> [-n N]`, and LAST RUN / RESULT / NEXT columns to `gvm job list`. Read NEXT from the timer's NextElapseUSecRealtime over read-only D-Bus. Teach Observe to read the newest record, needs_repair and the degraded breadcrumb, so `gvm status` prints 'last job' and 'last backup' lines. Add the doctor check that flags a routine archive older than 2x the schedule (INST-016). Name `journalctl -u gvm-job@<slug>` in each failed-job line.

### failure-recovery-3 — Status and refusal hints name commands that do not exist; a manifest pin mismatch has no way forward

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5 (archive-first spirit), D-17 · Plan: 20 → **39** · Status: open

**Evidence.** pin.go:72-75 (LoadPinned is used instead of the catalog, D-17); observe.go:344-368 (the pin check is spec pin vs lock bytes); cli/instance.go:510-517 (apply also refuses on a mismatch). (orig: derive.go:168 and verbs.go:205-206: a pin-mismatch hint and refusal of 'gvm manifest upgrade <n> --dry', with Force:false. Running it gives 'gvm: unknown command "manifest"' (exit 2). derive.go:400: the disabled hint is 'gvm enable <n>', which gives 'gvm: unknown command "enable"'. derive.go:533: the hold reason says '`gvm disable` does not', which gives 'unknown command "disable"'. derive.go:432: the exit-65 hint 'gvm doctor <n>' silently ignores the name (see failure-recovery-1). content/lock.go:245 also cites `gvm manifest upgrade --dry`. The verbs.go:15-21 comment treats `gvm disable` as a design term, but root.go:98-155 registers none of these commands.)

**Impact.** After an `apt upgrade gvm` ships a new manifest revision, an instance shows 'failed' and `gvm start` refuses without allowing --force. The only next step offered is a command the CLI rejects, so the operator is stuck or starts hand-editing the pin. The disabled and held cases send people to commands that do not exist either.

**Suggestion.** Interim, now: change the RulePinMismatch hint and refusal to a sequence that works. Print both sides, for example 'the YAML pins rev X sha Y and the lock holds rev A sha B; restore spec.manifest in <spec> to rev A sha B, then gvm apply --diff <n>'. Point the enable/disable hints at 'set enabled: true|false in <spec>, then gvm apply <n>'. Then implement `gvm manifest upgrade <n> [--to N] --dry` as read-only, rendering spec.Diff. The non-dry form should go through internal/reconcile, the single intent write path, rewriting the YAML pin and the lock together. It should take a preserve archive first, as ARCHITECTURE §4303 lists for manifest upgrade, and require typed name confirmation when the diff contains argv or destroy_globs changes. Add the hint-resolves-to-a-real-command test from finding 2.

### failure-recovery-7 — `gvm status` leaves out why the server last stopped, though the reason is already on disk or in systemd

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1 · Plan: **28** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The preflight failure sentence (e.g. 'credential rcon is missing from …', '<dir> is not writable; check ReadWritePaths=…', preflight.go:194-283) goes only to sd_notify STATUS= and the journal. UnitState has no StatusText (platform.go:46-59; systemd_linux.go:142-156 reads only LoadState/ActiveState/SubState/Result/MainPID/ExecMainStatus). The readiness or fail-marker reason, with the manifest's hint, is written to shutdown.jsonl as 'readiness failed: …' (run.go:485), but only `gvm logs --shutdown` reads that file (logs.go:123-156). Observe never does. The hint for an unclassified exit is 'gvm logs <n>' (derive.go:444), which prints the console tail and not the shutdown record. `gvm status` also prints each observation gap twice: once as a FindingObservation 'note' in Report (reconcile.go:327-329, status.go:222-232), then again via printObservationGaps (status.go:81, 91-99).

**Impact.** To learn why a server will not start, the operator has to run status, then logs, then logs --shutdown, then journalctl or systemctl status. The answer was a one-line sentence gvm had already recorded.

**Suggestion.** Read StatusText in the same Service-property call and show it as 'last runner message: …' while the unit is failed or activating. Have the runner append a kind:'preflight' record to shutdown.jsonl. In Observe, read only the last record: open with O_NOFOLLOW, require a regular file, cap the read by seeking to the last 64 KiB, and parse only the typed fields (time, rung, degraded, reason, exit). Strip control and ANSI characters before printing, and mark the line as runner-reported. Change the unclassified-exit hint to `gvm logs <n> --shutdown`, and remove printObservationGaps (Report already lists the notes).

### failure-recovery-8 — A failed `gvm start` prints only the raw systemd error, and after five crashes gvm has no way to start again

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6 · Plan: **28** · Status: open

**Evidence.** manager.go:187-189: when execute fails, Do returns before re-observing (:191-195). start.go:95-97 drops res and prints only the error, so the operator sees 'systemd job for gvm@X.service ended "failed"' (hostops/systemd_linux.go:85), with no phase, reason or hint. gvm@.service:16-17 sets StartLimitIntervalSec=600 / StartLimitBurst=5. Once systemd's start limit is hit, it also refuses manual starts until the interval passes or `systemctl reset-failed` is run. grep for 'reset-failed|ResetFailed|start-limit' across internal/ finds only a comment in jobs/jobs.go:382, and no hostops verb or hint covers it. derive.go:410-417 then reports 'failed with result=start-limit-hit (exit N: …)' and the hint is chosen by exit code only.

**Impact.** After fixing the cause of a crash loop, the operator runs `gvm start` and it fails for up to 10 minutes with an opaque systemd message and no next step.

**Suggestion.** On an execute error, populate res.After by re-observing and print phase, reason, hint and StatusText under the systemd error. Detect Result=start-limit-hit and explain it in plain words. Add hostops VerbUnitResetFailed, scoped by the hostops table to gvm@<n>.service only. Issue it only on an interactive operator start when Result=start-limit-hit, either automatically with a printed note or behind a dedicated --reset-failed flag, never through --force. Add a test asserting that the guard and job actors can never issue it.

### failure-recovery-9 — The documented Postgres-recovery steps fail as written

**Medium** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 1, 8 · Plan: 22 → **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:37,96 say audit rows spool to /var/lib/gvm/spool/ and that `sudo gvm db replay-spool` replays it. The CLI does write there (cli/instance.go:316 `audit.OpenSpool(path.Join(instance.StateRoot, "spool"), …)`; tmpfiles gvm.conf:54 creates it). But db.go:127 defaults `--spool-dir` to '/var/lib/gvm/audit-spool', which nothing creates, so ListSpools errors 'list spool directory … no such file or directory' (audit/spool.go:486-490). `gvm audit tail` claims 'Postgres connectivity is NOT required' (auditcmd.go:12-14) yet always returns errRequiresDaemon. Running it printed: 'gvm audit: audit tail: requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running (`systemctl status gvmd`)' (exit 1), and no gvmd.service is shipped. The doc's gvmd and web rows (lines 35, 42-79) describe components the CLI marks notImplemented (root.go:101-102). With Postgres down, `gvm db status` printed five 'WARN store: pool ping failed, retrying err="failed to connect …"' lines over 15 s, then 'db status: store: datastore unavailable: timed out waiting for postgres', with no pointer to `systemctl status postgresql` or the doc.

**Impact.** After an outage the operator follows the doc, `replay-spool` fails, and the audit history stays out of the database. `gvm audit tail` cannot show 'who stopped my server' even though the spool on disk holds exactly that.

**Suggestion.** Default `gvm db replay-spool --spool-dir` to instance.SpoolDir (/var/lib/gvm/spool) and add a test that the CLI writer, tmpfiles and replayer agree on it. Implement `gvm audit tail/search` offline with audit.ListSpools + audit.Replay into a printing sink, as its own comment describes. Make dbWaitPool fail fast when the socket file is absent, with 'Postgres is not running (no socket at …): systemctl status postgresql; see docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md'. Mark the doc's gvmd and dashboard rows 'not yet shipped' until they are.

### failure-recovery-10 — The guard timer restarts failed units every 5 minutes with no backoff, undoing RestartPreventExitStatus

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6 · Plan: **28** · Status: open

**Evidence.** gvm@.service:56-59: '64 … 65 … 66 … 78 … Restarting cannot fix any of these and a restart loop hides the error. RestartPreventExitStatus=64 65 66 78'. But reconcile.go:248-252 `DesiredRunningAndDown` is true for ActiveState 'failed', whatever ExecMainStatus is. jobs/sweep.go:100-118 turns that into a Recovery, and runner_guard.go:128-148 calls Recover for every candidate on every run (OnUnitActiveSec=5min) with no backoff, attempt counter or exclusion. lifecycle.Allowed refuses only the pin cases, so exit 65 and 78 are restarted. A start-limit-hit crash loop is re-entered every time the 10-minute window lapses. `gvm status` never says the guard is retrying.

**Impact.** A server that cannot pass preflight, or keeps crashing on a bad mod, is restarted all day. The journal and job history fill with guard runs, and the operator sees 'failed' with no hint that gvm keeps starting it. That is exactly the loop the unit file was written to prevent.

**Suggestion.** In internal/lifecycle, add Observation.NeedsOperator(): failed with ExecMainStatus in {64,65,66,78}, or Result=start-limit-hit. Take the code set from one exported constant, and add a test that compares it with gvm@.service's RestartPreventExitStatus. Make DesiredRunningAndDown false in that case. Have Findings emit FindingNeedsOperator, with text like 'the guard will not restart this: exit 65 preflight failed' and the exit hint. Status, doctor and the guard then agree automatically, and the guard reports these instances as 'not recovered: needs an operator'. For other failures, keep a simple attempt cap rather than exponential backoff, e.g. 3 recoveries per boot recorded in /var/lib/gvm/instances/<n>/guard.json with the boot id, written only by the guard. Show it in status as 'guard: 3 recovery attempts this boot; giving up'.

### failure-recovery-11 — On a stale lease, status says the operator must decide while the guard clears it within 5 minutes; `gvm job clear` gives no next step

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6 · Plan: **28** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Sweep.Recover is at internal/jobs/sweep.go:251-284; deadHolder at :149-154. (orig: derive.go:185-191 reports 'failed … a update job took this instance <age> ago and did not release it; the holder is gone' with hint 'gvm job clear <n>'. verbs.go:196-198 refuses start: 'a job died holding this instance and the install tree may be half-written'. derive.go:118-122 says the fix 'is a person'. Yet jobs/sweep.go:105-135 (deadHolder) and :293-317 (Recover) remove that same lease and start the server on the next guard tick. `gvm job clear` prints only 'cleared the lease on X (/var/lib/gvm/instances/X/lease.json)' (job.go:714-715): not what the lease was, not that the update never finished, and no next command. The derive.go:120-121 comment says job clear 'says what the lease was, how old it is and what it was doing'. The reason string reads 'a update job' (derive.go:188), and ages print as Go durations like '26h14m3s ago' (derive.go:547).)

**Impact.** The operator is told to investigate a possibly half-written install, and the guard then starts the server anyway before they finish. After clearing by hand they have no prompt to verify the install or to read the dead job's journal.

**Suggestion.** In deriveLease, choose the hint from the same predicate deadHolder uses (o.DesiredRunningAndDown()). When it holds: 'gvm-guard.timer will clear this lease and start <n> within 5 min; to inspect first: gvm stop <n> (sets a hold); dead job's log: journalctl -u <lease.Unit>'. Otherwise: 'gvm job clear <n>' plus the journalctl pointer. Have `gvm job clear` print the cleared lease's Describe() line and a next step for each kind, e.g. update → 'gvm update <n> --repair --dry-run, then gvm start <n>'. Use the correct article ('an update job'). Render ages as coarse human units ('26h ago'). Correct the derive.go comment to say that the guard restarts the server but never resumes the job.

### failure-recovery-12 — No early warning for a filling disk, and the backup's out-of-space refusal is in raw bytes with no remedy

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: **36** · Status: open

**Evidence.** None of HostChecks/EdgeChecks/FirewallChecks (checks_host.go:17-34) check free space. grep for statfs/Bavail finds only backup, destroy and snapshot code. backup/engine.go:497-503 refuses with '…: %s holds %d bytes free and this archive needs about %d (%d source bytes plus %.0f%% headroom); %d bytes short…', all raw byte counts, and suggests no next action. The Postgres WAL warning ('df -h /var/lib/postgresql # an unrotated WAL filling the disk is the usual cause') appears only on the web 503 page (web/handlers.go:60-68), not in doctor.

**Impact.** The usual sequence is: the disk fills, the nightly backup fails (only in the journal, see failure-recovery-6), then SteamCMD fails with 0x202, then the game cannot save. Nothing in gvm warns until those failures happen, and when the backup does refuse, the operator has to convert 12-digit numbers and work out how to free space without touching preserve archives.

**Suggestion.** Add HOST-013 (disk): statfs on /var/backups/gvm, /var/lib/gvm, /var/log/gvm, each instance's install and data paths, and /var/lib/postgresql, deduplicated by filesystem id. Warning below 15% free or below 2x the largest archive; Problem below 5%. Print GiB, with a split of routine vs preserve archive bytes taken from the archive index (no du over install trees). Reword ErrNoRoom in GiB, followed by: 'routine archives: X GiB (lower backup.keep in <spec>, gvm apply <n>, then gvm backup prune <n>); preserve archives: Y GiB, never pruned by gvm, so review them with gvm backup list <n> and remove by hand'. Have `gvm status` add a note when the instance's volume crosses the warning threshold. Move the Postgres WAL hint from the 503 page into this check.

### failure-recovery-14 — Hints written in manifest fail_markers reach only the journal; `gvm status` shows a generic code

**Medium** · manifest-author · not-wired · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 10 · Plan: **28** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/logs.go:148-150 prints r.Reason (which includes the marker hint) for each shutdown record; internal/cli/run.go:485. (orig: readiness.go:408-419 says the hint 'is the whole product difference between "java.lang.OutOfMemoryError" and "…raise memory_max or lower jvm_xmx" — one of which an operator can act on at 2am'. But run.go:484-488 only logs it (log.Error) and puts it in the shutdown reason, then exits with the game's code or 75. `gvm status` shows the exit-code table entry instead ('exit 75: an orphaned game process…', derive.go:517-518; see failure-recovery-4). The manifest schema has no way to say which exit class a marker means. Every shipped manifest's bind-failure hint points to `gvm doctor`, which checks no ports (games/7daystodie.yaml:65-66, games/cs2.yaml:75-76, games/minecraft-forge.yaml:62-63).)

**Impact.** A manifest author writes a precise, actionable hint and operators never see it in the command they actually run. Authors have no way to stop a known-fatal condition (port in use, missing files) from looping under Restart=on-failure.

**Suggestion.** Add an optional `exit:` enum to FailMarker, limited to `port_unavailable` (→66) and `data_missing` (→65). Validate it in spec. Keep 78 for the sandbox meaning; if config errors need a class, add a new documented code. Have the runner write the fired marker's pattern, its manifest hint and the sanitised matched line, truncated to one line, as structured fields of the shutdown record. Show them in `gvm status` through finding 7's reader as 'boot aborted: <line> — <hint>'. Update the shipped bind-failure hints to name `gvm ports <name>` and bump each manifest's revision. Release this together with `gvm manifest upgrade` (finding 3), so existing instances can adopt the new markers.

### failure-recovery-13 — The dashboard shows a bare status word, with no reason, next step or last failure

**Low** · dashboard-user · missing-feature · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: 48 → **49** · Status: open

**Evidence.** root.go:102 `{Name: "web", … Run: notImplemented}`; nothing in the repo imports github.com/S95F/gameVPSmanager/internal/web. (orig: web/server.go:37-43 `InstanceDetail{Name, Status, Game, Addr, Port}`. handlers.go:279-299 renders only '<dt>Status</dt><dd>%s</dd>', a console link and start/stop/restart buttons. The CLI's lifecycle.Status already carries phase_reason, hint, lease, drift and findings with JSON keys chosen to match the mirror (lifecycle/status.go:22-73), but none of it reaches the page. The CLI hints are shell commands (`gvm job clear …`) that a dashboard-user cannot run.)

**Impact.** A moderator sees 'failed' or 'degraded' and can only press Restart. Restart will be refused for a stale lease or pin mismatch, or will re-enter a crash loop. They cannot tell whether to wait, retry or call the operator, so routine incidents turn into SSH requests.

**Suggestion.** When InstanceQuerier is implemented, return lifecycle.Status itself (phase, phase_reason, rule, lease, held), plus the last job record and the sanitised last shutdown record, all delivered via gvmd or the mirror behind the existing session middleware. Keep one table mapping each Rule to dashboard-user text, with a test that every Rule has an entry. Never show raw shell hints to that role: map them to 'needs the server operator' or to a dashboard action. Render refused actions as disabled buttons that show the Allowed refusal reason, and keep the API-side refusal unchanged.

### failure-recovery-15 — Little steers operators toward doctor or a troubleshooting guide, and `gvm list --problems` says 'no instances' on a healthy host

**Low** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: 24 → **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** README.md (75 lines) does not mention doctor, status, logs or troubleshooting. The only operator runbook in docs/ is what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md. IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:613-614 lists further operator docs (backups-and-restore.md and others) that do not exist. `gvm help doctor` printed only 'gvm doctor — check the host for problems', with no mention of -q (root.go:263). `gvm doctor --help` printed 'Usage of doctor: -q …' then 'gvm doctor: flag: help requested' with exit=1. Doctor's last line is the doubled 'gvm doctor: doctor: 3 problem(s)'. The output is one flat list with no section headers and no 'N problems, N warnings, N notes' total, unlike ARCHITECTURE.md:6956-7039. `gvm list` ends with '… N needing attention' and does not point to `gvm status <name>` or `gvm doctor`. list.go:82 then :106: with --problems and no problem rows it prints '\nno instances. `gvm create <name> --game <id>` defines one.'

**Impact.** Operators who have never needed doctor rarely learn it exists until something breaks. A healthy `gvm list --problems` claims the box has no instances at all, which is alarming in the middle of an incident.

**Suggestion.** Add docs/troubleshooting.md keyed by symptom: 'stuck starting', 'failed after update', 'disk full', 'Postgres down', 'stop hangs'. For each, give the exact command and how to read its output, and link it from README and from `gvm doctor` output. Make `gvm help <cmd>` print the command's flag usage. Make `--help` exit 0 for doctor. Print the doctor problem count once, with warnings and notes totals and section headers. In printList, say 'no instances need attention (N healthy)' when --problems filtered everything out. When the count is nonzero, append '-> gvm status <name> for details; gvm doctor for host checks'.


---

<a id="documentation"></a>
## Documentation for users (`documentation`)

From a user's point of view, gvm's documentation is a strong design spec plus very little else. The only reference is docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (7,148 lines of design rationale and Go structs). There is no install guide, quickstart, per-game guide, command reference, troubleshooting/FAQ, firewall guide, manifest-authoring guide or anything written for dashboard users or players. The README status section still says "Early" and never says which milestones work, yet much of the documented surface is not wired in this build. That includes `gvm install` and the update/repair jobs ("the provider seam is not built in this release"), the dashboard (`gvm web` and `gvmd` are notImplemented), `gvm firewall`, `adopt cutover/rollback`, Modrinth/CurseForge content, `--remote`, and `gvm secret/user/token`, which all depend on the unbuilt gvmd. About 20 user-facing error and fix hints point to commands that do not exist (`gvm instance promote`, `gvm manifest upgrade`, `gvm firewall enable`, `gvm content resolve-blocked`, `gvm game approve`), so the recovery advice ends nowhere. The in-CLI `--help` for the core lifecycle, backup, restore and update commands is good and explains the safety model well, but `gvm help <cmd>` prints only one line, `--help` behaves differently from one command to the next (one call panics), and the output cites spec decision numbers (D-nn, "section 16.4") that an operator on a VPS cannot look up.

**Strengths to preserve**

- Top-level `gvm` usage is well grouped (Instances / Content / Operations / Migration / Administration / Host / Manifests), and its banner states the core promise in plain words.
- Per-command `--help` for create, apply, start/stop/restart, destroy, backup, restore, update, rebuild, content, job, control, logs and console is rich. It explains the safety model in operator terms, e.g. backup: "Retention only ever runs after a new archive has been written AND verified"; update: "On a quiet night nothing is touched"; destroy: "The archive is not optional".
- `gvm game list / which / validate / hooks` work locally as any user with no root and no host. They give clear check/warning output (e.g. `gvm game validate valheim` reports templates, ports, hooks, readiness and the shutdown ladder with '0 errors, 0 warnings').
- `gvm create` output lists the pinned manifest, uid, user and materialised ports, says plainly that nothing else changed, and prints the next step (`next: gvm apply <name> --diff`). Missing required vars are listed with their help text (create.go:285-295).
- The console UX is excellent and well documented in its own help: Ctrl-D or :detach leaves the console, the 'detached ... STILL RUNNING' banner is clear, and `:stop` is redirected to `gvm stop` (console.go:438-452, 513-519).
- The no-transport error names the most common fix first ('Run it as root (sudo gvm create)').
- Status verdicts carry a `->` hint that maps exit codes 64/65/66/75/78 to a next command (lifecycle/derive.go:424-445), which is the right foundation for a troubleshooting guide.
- Every shipped manifest has a `metadata.doc` block, and many vars have `help:` strings. games/generic-steam.yaml is an outstanding commented template for manifest authors (REPLACE-ME markers, explanations of sentinel, appid shim and process_group).
- docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md has the right format for operator docs (a verb-by-verb table, then exact commands to run) and is a good model for the missing guides.
- docs/research/steamcmd.md section 8 has a per-game matrix (app ids, ports, RCON, RAM floors) that can seed per-game guides.

### documentation-1 — README never says what works in this build; core journeys it advertises refuse with 'not built in this release'

**High** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **20** · Status: open

**Evidence.** version: internal/version Version="dev" is overridden by Makefile LDFLAGS; `git tag` is empty, so `make build` produces the version 'b69119b'. README.md:34-35 partly mitigates (a general 'not implemented' warning). (orig: README.md:22-36: 'Status: Early ...' plus a milestone table (A-D) with no done/not-done column; README.md:5 promises 'a web dashboard, a firewall derived from what is actually running'. IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md has 181 '- [ ]' boxes and 0 '- [x]', although git log shows phases 0-19 committed. In the binary: root.go:101-102 `gvmd`/`web` Run: notImplemented ('gvm web' -> 'gvm web: not implemented yet', EXIT=3); root.go:152 `firewall` notImplemented; install.go:109-111 `gvm install` always ends 'gvm: the provider seam is not built in this release: ... whose driver lands with the blob store' (production HostEnv never sets JobSeams; only job_test.go:170 does); jobengines.go:157-160 and 416-419: update job and `update --repair` refuse the same way; content.go:520-523 `gvm content` on a Modrinth/CurseForge block: 'this build implements the content phases for steam_workshop only; Modrinth is v1.1 and CurseForge is v1.2'; adopt.go:23-28 cutover/rollback 'not yet implemented'; transport.go:174 remote 'needs the API client, which lands with the web tier'. `gvm version` -> 'gvm dev (commit unknown, go1.26.2, windows/amd64)'.)

**Impact.** An operator who reads the README and the 16-game `gvm game list` reasonably expects to create, install and run a server, use a dashboard, and have the firewall managed. They find out command by command, after setup work, that install, update, dashboard, firewall and modded-Minecraft content are not usable. There is no single place that says what this build can do.

**Suggestion.** Add a 'What works in this build' table to the README, one row per user journey, with three states: works / partial (with the reason, e.g. install runs preflight and then stops at the provider) / not built. Put a matching status field on cli.Command and print it in the usage listing (e.g. 'firewall  (not in this build)'). A Go test should run every command or row against the fake HostEnv and fail when a 'works' row returns ErrNotImplemented, ErrProviderUnbuilt or ErrRemoteUnbuilt, and also when a 'not built' row starts working. Tag a release (git tag v0.x) so `make build` stamps a real version, and say in the README to build with make. Tick an IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN box only when its own definition of done has been checked. Add a CHANGELOG.md.

### documentation-2 — No install guide, prerequisites list or quickstart; the only install instruction names a .deb that nothing builds

**High** · operator · docs · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: **23** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The undocumented system accounts are referenced at packaging/tmpfiles/gvm.conf (e.g. 'd /var/lib/gvm/blobs 0755 gvm-fetch gvm-content', 'd /run/gvm/web 2750 gvm caddy'). runprepare_linux.go:27 shows the failure text 'create it with `groupadd --system ...`'. (orig: README.md:47-57 covers only `make build` / `make check`. The only install line in the repo is ARCHITECTURE.md:6846 `sudo dpkg -i gvm_0.4.1_amd64.deb`, but the Makefile has no deb/install target and packaging/ has no nfpm.yaml/postinstall.sh (planned at IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:608). packaging/packaging.go:24-27: where units and tmpfiles go 'is the installer's business', and no installer exists. Required accounts/groups (gvm, gvm-console, gvm-content, gvm-fetch, caddy) and paths (/etc/gvm, /srv/gvm, /var/lib/gvm, /opt/gvm/steamcmd) appear only inside ARCHITECTURE.md sections 5-6. Prerequisites gvm will not install (D-41) surface only as failure text, e.g. steamcmd.go:968-971 'host needs `dpkg --add-architecture i386 && apt-get install lib32gcc-s1`'. /etc/gvm/gvm.yaml (host config) is undocumented and no sample ships: `gvm doctor` -> 'could not load host config (open /etc/gvm/gvm.yaml ...); using defaults'.)

**Impact.** An operator who is not a Go developer cannot get from a fresh VPS to a first server from the docs. They have to reverse-engineer where the binary, gvm@.service, gvm-job@.service, slices and tmpfiles go, which accounts must exist, and which apt packages each game needs. Each gap is found by a failure partway through setup.

**Suggestion.** Write docs/install.md with: (1) prerequisites per game family as apt commands the operator runs themselves; (2) account creation, preferably by shipping packaging/sysusers.d/gvm.conf and running `systemd-sysusers` before `systemd-tmpfiles --create`; (3) binary to /usr/local/bin/gvm, the path the units hard-code, plus a test asserting the doc path equals ProbeBinary; units to /usr/lib/systemd/system; tmpfiles to /usr/lib/tmpfiles.d; then daemon-reload and `gvm doctor`; (4) a commented sample /etc/gvm/gvm.yaml generated from spec.DefaultHostConfig. Add `make install DESTDIR=` that the doc mirrors exactly. Hold the quickstart back until the provider seam is wired, or write it with an explicit, clearly marked manual steamcmd step that runs as the instance account into the instance's install dir. Have CI replay only the steps that work.

### documentation-3 — Firewall: README promises a derived firewall, but apply never touches ufw, `gvm firewall` is unbuilt and doctor sends operators to a missing command

**High** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: 24 → 33 → **40** · Status: open

**Evidence.** FW-003 cannot be silenced: internal/doctor/checks_fw.go:97-105 takes no host config. FW-001 has a 'backend none' escape at checks_fw.go:47-49. doctor_test.go:113 enforces the 'gvm firewall enable' string. (orig: README.md:5 'a firewall derived from what is actually running'; README.md:14-15 'keeps its firewall rules'; what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:30 '`sudo gvm apply` ... reconciles systemd/ufw/Caddy'. Reality: internal/reconcile has no firewall action; the firewall package is used only by doctor (cli/doctor.go:54-62, which hardcodes `liveActive := false`, `liveTrusted := false`, 'stub until ufw backend CLI wiring is in place'). So every host's `gvm doctor` reports '✗ FW-001 firewall backend ufw is not active  Fix: gvm firewall enable' and '✗ FW-003 ... deletions suppressed (D-42)', ending '3 problem(s) found' (EXIT=1, run on this machine). `gvm firewall enable` -> 'gvm firewall: not implemented yet' (EXIT=3). checks_fw.go:56,82,147,176 hard-code 'gvm firewall enable' / 'gvm firewall apply'. No doc explains enabling ufw safely or which ports to open.)

**Impact.** If ufw is already active (common on hardened VPS images), game ports are never opened and players cannot connect, and nothing tells the operator why. If ufw is off, doctor stays red forever and points at a missing command. An operator following that advice is likely to run `ufw enable` by hand, which is exactly the SSH-lockout risk gvm's guard exists to prevent (e.g. the Ubuntu 24.04 ssh.socket non-22 port trap described in ARCHITECTURE section 17.5).

**Suggestion.** (a) Write docs/firewall.md with SSH-first steps: `ss -tlnp` / `systemctl cat ssh.socket`, `ufw allow <ssh>/tcp` before `ufw enable`, test a second session, and explain that gvm never deletes untagged rules. (b) Replace the notImplemented `gvm firewall` with a read-only `gvm firewall plan` (RouteLocal, prints only, never executes). It renders firewall.Desired(host, instances, manifests) as `ufw <Rule.UFWSpec()>` lines with the gvm:<instance>:<label> comment, SSH rules first, and diffs against ufw.Backend.Status when the ruleset parses with confidence. (c) Wire doctor to ufw.Backend.Status. When the backend is unavailable or unreadable, FW-001 and FW-003 become Skip/Note pointing at docs/firewall.md, not Problem. When ufw is active and an enabled instance's expose:public port has no allow rule, report a Warning with Fix `gvm firewall plan`. Remove 'gvm firewall enable/apply' from the Fix strings until those verbs exist. Never enable ufw from any of this.

### documentation-4 — About 20 user-facing hints and fixes name commands that do not exist

**High** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **20** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Additional instance: internal/content/lock.go:245 ('gvm manifest upgrade --dry'). Unreachable in this build: internal/content/curseforge/* (no importers), plus provider/update paths gated behind ErrProviderUnbuilt. (orig: Strings in shipped code: lifecycle/derive.go:168 (status hint on manifest pin mismatch) 'gvm manifest upgrade <name> --dry', and lifecycle/verbs.go:206 the same; cli/update.go:148, jobs/runner_update.go:111, provider/steamcmd/steamcmd.go:333,389, provider/minecraft/provider.go:93, provider/provider.go:280,290, spec/capability.go:490, update/pipeline.go:410, update/repair.go:129 all say 'gvm instance promote'; content/lock.go:509, content/source.go:502, cli/content.go:1653 'gvm content promote'; cli/content_blocked.go:127, curseforge/ladder.go:217, curseforge/blocked.go:128 'gvm content resolve-blocked'; spec/load.go:249 'Fix: `gvm game approve <id>`'; doctor/checks_fw.go:56 'gvm firewall enable'; lifecycle/derive.go:533 '`gvm disable`'; secrets/filestore.go:394 '`gvm secret rotate-key`'. Running them: `gvm manifest upgrade pz-main --dry` -> 'gvm: unknown command "manifest"'; `gvm instance promote x` -> unknown command (EXIT=2); `gvm game approve foo` -> 'unknown subcommand "approve"'; `gvm content resolve-blocked x` -> 'unknown subcommand'. Also derive.go:432 hints 'gvm doctor <name>', but doctor ignores positional args and its instance checks load nothing (cli/doctor.go:150 `doctorLoadInstances() []spec.Instance { return nil }`).)

**Impact.** The moments where an operator most needs guidance (a failed unit after a gvm upgrade changed a manifest, an adopted server that will not update, a blocked mod, an unapproved override) end in 'unknown command'. Operators then improvise, for example by hand-editing the instance pin or the manifest lock, which the pin exists to prevent.

**Suggestion.** Add the AST-based test: extract every `gvm <verb> [<sub>]` from non-test string literals and run each through Main with `--help` against a fake env, asserting it is not 'unknown command/subcommand'. This depends on documentation-9's table-driven dispatchers and the exit-0 help path. Rewording: for pin mismatch, use '/etc/gvm/instances/<name>.d/manifest.lock.yaml or the manifest pin in <name>.yaml was changed outside gvm; restore both from your backup or version control, then `gvm status <name>` (no upgrade command exists in this build)'. For the adopted-instance hints, use 'not available in this build'. Change holdReason's '`gvm disable` does not' to 'a reboot clears it'. For exit 65, either make `gvm doctor` load instances (doctorLoadInstances returns nil today) and accept a name, or hint `gvm logs <name>`.

### documentation-5 — No documented way to set a game or join password; `gvm secret set` requires a gvmd that does not exist

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 10 · Plan: **29** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The format rule for game secrets is filestore.go:139-180 (one EOL trimmed; multiline and empty values refused) and run.go:186; filestore.go:394 is the master key only. The secret path is instance/paths.go:41-44, 190, 272. (orig: `gvm secret set --name join_password --instance vh1` -> 'gvm secret: secret set: requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running (`systemctl status gvmd`)' (user.go:141-143; secret.go:43-57 discards --instance/--generate and always returns that). gvmd is notImplemented (root.go:101) and no gvmd.service ships. `gvm create` prints 'secrets   join_password — not generated yet; the unit starts without them and the game gets an empty value' with no next step (create.go:511-514). games/minecraft-paper.yaml:19-21 claims 'The password is a random secret generated at create time', which contradicts create's output. The real location, /etc/gvm/secrets/instances/<name>/<key> (instance/paths.go:41-44,110,190), and the format rule (raw bytes, no trailing newline, filestore.go:394) appear in no operator doc. `gvm create ... --var join_password=x` -> '... declares no variable "join_password"'. The usage banner says 'every command below works over SSH as root with nothing else running', but secret/user/token all require gvmd.)

**Impact.** Running a private, passworded server, the most common setup for 'a few servers for friends', is impossible through the CLI and undocumented by hand. A Valheim or Palworld server either starts open to anyone or the operator has to guess the file path and format. The hard rule that game passwords may not contain ' " \ $ ` is not mentioned anywhere an operator would see it before choosing one.

**Suggestion.** Implement `gvm secret set|generate|list|show-path <instance> <name>` as RouteHostOp, in process, never touching admin.sock. Read the value from a no-echo TTY (golang.org/x/term, pure Go) or stdin, never from argv. --generate uses secrets.GeneratePassword (base62). Reject ' " \ $ ` plus newline, ';' and '=' with a message naming the character. Run the manifest's validators with the candidate value (ValidatorInput.Secrets) before writing. Write with the existing store at 0600 root:root, no trailing newline. Treat the secret_refs fingerprint update as best-effort with a warning. Print 'next: sudo gvm apply <name> && sudo gvm restart <name>' and do not restart automatically. The PZ rotate path keeps its documented stop, write, ini-rewrite, start order. Have `gvm create` print the exact set or generate command for each referenced secret. Fix minecraft-paper.yaml:19-21. Until this lands, docs/secrets.md documents the path, the 0600/0700 modes, the `printf '%s'` recipe, the forbidden characters, and the re-apply plus restart step.

### documentation-6 — The Project Zomboid adopt runbook, the README's headline document, does not match the CLI

**Medium** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 11 · Plan: 21 → 25 → **51** · Status: open

**Evidence.** adopt.go:39-43: fs.Parse stops at the first non-flag ('pz-main'), so --from-bash-suite and --write-plan are silently dropped, with no 'flag provided but not defined' error. Observed: `gvm adopt discover pz-main --from-bash-suite /root/pz-server` -> 'read unit /etc/systemd/system/zomboid.service: ...'. (orig: README.md:43 lists 'ARCHITECTURE.md §19 — The Project Zomboid adopt runbook'. ARCHITECTURE.md:6846 `sudo dpkg -i gvm_0.4.1_amd64.deb` (no such package); :6851/:6858 `sudo gvm adopt discover pz-main --from-bash-suite /root/pz-server [--write-plan]`, but adopt.go:39 defines only `-unit` (default "zomboid"), so the runbook's flags are 'flag provided but not defined'; :6871 `gvm adopt cutover pz-main --rotate-credentials` vs adopt.go:23-25 'adopt cutover: not yet implemented in this phase (requires Linux)', which is printed on Linux too; :6942 `gvm adopt rollback pz-main` vs adopt.go:26-28 'not yet implemented'; :6922 `gvm adopt finalize pz-main` vs adopt.go:66-73 '--name and --unit-path are required'; :6889-6890 `gvm content promote` and :6912 `gvm instance promote` do not exist. internal/adopt/cutover.go:56 `CutoverPZ` exists but is not wired. The planned standalone docs/adopt-projectzomboid.md (IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:613,650) does not exist.)

**Impact.** Adopting the live Project Zomboid server is described as 'the point of the project'. An operator following the runbook verbatim fails at step 1 (no .deb) and step 2 (unknown flags), and discovers only at the downtime step that cutover is not built. That is confusion on the riskiest journey, even though nothing destructive happens.

**Suggestion.** First fix finalize. Read metadata.adopted_from from /etc/gvm/instances/<name>.yaml itself, drop the --adopted-from flag, and fail closed (refuse without --force) when the timestamp is missing or unparseable. Then extract section 19.6 into docs/adopt-projectzomboid.md, written against the real CLI (`gvm adopt discover --unit zomboid`, `gvm adopt finalize --name pz-main --unit-path ...`). Mark cutover, rollback, content promote and instance promote as 'not available in this build', and keep the 7-day rule prominent. Fix adopt.go:24 so the refusal no longer claims 'requires Linux'. The runbook test should only flag-parse each line (a parse-only seam), never execute it.

### documentation-7 — No per-game guide, and no command shows a game's variables, defaults, secrets, ports or doc text

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: partial · fit: sound · Plan: **26** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Partial var discovery: create.go:285-295 (missing required vars printed with help) and `gvm create --dry-run` (create.go:440-442), which prints every declared var with its default. (orig: `gvm game` subcommands are only validate/list/which/hooks (game.go:37-40). For an unknown var, create says '`gvm game validate <id>` lists them' (create.go:261), but `gvm game validate valheim` prints only '✓ 4 vars, memory_model flat — MemoryHigh 2688M, MemoryMax 3G at the declared defaults', with no names. All 16 manifests have a `metadata.doc` block, and vars carry `help:` (e.g. valheim.yaml:107-116, minecraft-paper.yaml vars with type/default/min/max/help), but none of it is printed anywhere. The manifests ship embedded in the binary (`gvm game which valheim` -> 'embedded games/valheim.yaml'), so an operator on the VPS cannot even read the YAML. There is no docs/games/ directory.)

**Impact.** To create anything beyond the defaults (difficulty, max_players, crossplay, heap_size, world name, which is immutable), the operator has to guess var names or clone the repo. They also learn too late that some vars are immutable or force a rebuild (world_name, level_name, mc_version are 'immutable: true, apply: rebuild'), when a wrong value can only be fixed by rebuilding the world.

**Suggestion.** Add `gvm game show <id> [--yaml]`, a RouteLocal command that prints metadata.doc, a vars table (name, type, default, required, min/max/values, immutable, apply: restart/rebuild, help), secrets referenced, ports with proto/default/expose, recommended jobs and sizing floors; `--yaml` emits the manifest bytes. Point create's unknown-var error at `gvm game show`. Generate docs/games/<id>.md from the same code (`make docs-games`, checked by a CI diff) and add a 'Supported games' table to README linking them.

### documentation-8 — Nothing tells the operator what to give players: no connect address, port or join instructions in docs or output

**Medium** · player · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 10 · Plan: **33** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Mitigation: create.go:491-502 prints port names and numbers. Constraint for the suggestion: run.go:160-164 (.Host.* is apply-time only). (orig: lifecycle/status.go:180-233 (`Status.Report`) prints phase, unit, pid, ready time, players, lease, drift and findings, but no address or port. `gvm ports` prints raw 'proto/port  owner' lines (ports.go:245) with no 'this is the one players type' marker and no public IP. Manifests know which ports are player-facing (`expose: public`, e.g. valheim.yaml:84-85 game 2456/query 2457) but carry no join instructions. No doc covers 'how players connect' for any game. The per-game port knowledge lives only in docs/research/steamcmd.md section 8 (line 244).)

**Impact.** After a successful start, the operator still has to work out from game wikis which port players enter (Valheim's game vs query port, Satisfactory's single port, PZ 16261 plus direct). Players get wrong instructions, and 'server is up but nobody can join' becomes a support thread with no doc to point at.

**Suggestion.** Add an optional manifest `connect:` string rendered through the existing guard, restricted to .Port.<name>, .Var.<name> and a new .Host.Public leaf. Validation rejects any template carrying TaintSecret. Add an operator-set `spec.public_address` to /etc/gvm/gvm.yaml (no network lookup). When it is unset, fall back to .Host.IP4 and label it 'detected, may be private'. `gvm status` and `gvm game show` print 'join: <rendered>' and 'password: set|none' (never the value). Mark the player-facing port in `gvm ports` using expose: public. Add a 'Tell your players' section to each generated docs/games/<id>.md.

### documentation-9 — In-CLI help is inconsistent: `gvm help <cmd>` gives one line, `--help` keeps executing, several commands reject `--help`, and one panics

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** root.go:254-265 runHelp prints only `gvm <name> — <summary>`, e.g. `gvm help create` -> 'gvm create — define a new instance', although the banner says 'Run "gvm help <command>" for detail.' `gvm create --help` prints full usage, then continues because flagErr turns ErrHelp into nil (game.go:613-618, start.go:47-50), and ends with 'gvm create: gvm: no transport for this command ...' EXIT=1. `gvm adopt|user|token|secret|db|audit --help` -> 'unknown subcommand "--help"' EXIT=1. `gvm doctor --help` -> Go-default 'Usage of doctor:' then 'gvm doctor: flag: help requested' EXIT=1. `gvm firewall --help` -> 'not implemented yet' EXIT=3. `gvm control verb --help` -> 'panic: runtime error: index out of range [0] with length 0' at control.go:269 with a full goroutine trace. There are no man pages and no shell completion.

**Impact.** The documented way to get detail returns nothing useful, and the way that works (`--help`) looks like a failure: a non-zero exit plus an unrelated error, or a crash. A non-developer operator cannot trust the CLI as a reference, and the docs folder has no command reference to fall back on.

**Suggestion.** Make `gvm help <cmd> [<sub>]` call the command's own usage printer. Give flagErr's callers an explicit 'help shown, exit 0, do nothing else' path (a sentinel ErrHelpShown handled in Main), and add a table test that runs `<cmd> --help` and `help <cmd>` for every command and subcommand and asserts exit 0, non-empty stdout, and no host access or panic. Convert the adopt/user/token/secret/db/audit dispatchers to the same subcommand-table pattern game.go/content.go use. Generate docs/cli-reference.md (and optional man pages) from these usage printers.

### documentation-10 — No manifest-authoring guide; the generic-steam template's 'start here' workflow cannot be followed with this build

**Medium** · manifest-author · docs · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 10 · Plan: **46** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/game.go:82-84: the only authorizers are RootCLIAuthorizer (with --unapproved-override) and DenyOverrides; no approval store is wired. (orig: games/generic-steam.yaml:9-10 'Copy this file to /etc/gvm/games/<yourgame>.yaml', but the file is embedded in the binary and no command exports it. :22 '`gvm game try <id> --scratch` runs all three' -> `gvm game try valheim` -> 'unknown subcommand "try"'. Overrides in /etc/gvm/games are refused as unapproved with 'Fix: `gvm game approve <id>`' (spec/load.go:249), which does not exist; ARCHITECTURE.md:3142-3164 (section 9.5 'Onboarding a game') instead uses `gvm game publish`, `gvm game try`, `gvm instance create`, none of which exist. The only schema reference is Go structs (ARCHITECTURE.md:1469-1839, section 8.2), and the template grammar, `when:` predicates, probe kinds, codec names and the closed hook list are spread across sections 8.3-8.8.)

**Impact.** Someone who wants to add a game by writing YAML (the advertised 'zero lines of Go' path) has to read Go struct tags in a 7,000-line spec. They cannot get the template onto the box without the repo, cannot test it with the documented tool, and are told to approve it with a command that does not exist. The only working path, `--unapproved-override`, is undocumented beyond a flag description.

**Suggestion.** Write docs/manifest-authoring.md with a YAML-first field reference generated from the spec structs' yaml tags and doc comments (CI diff), the template leaves the guard allows (from render_guard.go's own lists, including .Host.*), when: predicates, probe kinds, shutdown steps and codecs, and a pointer to `gvm game hooks`. Document the working loop: `gvm game show generic-steam --yaml > /etc/gvm/games/<newid>.yaml` (use a new id, not a shadowing one), `gvm game validate <path>`, then create a throwaway scratch instance with `--unapproved-override`, explaining what that flag vouches for. State plainly that each manifest change means destroying and recreating the scratch instance (destroy archives first), and never editing manifest.lock.yaml. Remove `game try/publish/approve` from generic-steam.yaml, load.go:249 and ARCHITECTURE 9.5's shipped guidance, or mark them 'not in this build'.

### documentation-12 — Minecraft manifests accept Mojang's EULA on the operator's behalf, contradicting the documented legal gate

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort S · fact: partial · fit: sound · Plan: **30** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Latent: internal/hook is imported by no production package, and install.go:109-111 refuses before hooks. The existing gate is run.go:773-774 (EULAAccepted from legal.eula.var) and capability.go:430. (orig: spec/manifest.go:562-564: 'LegalSpec gates a game behind an agreement the operator must accept themselves ... no hook accepts on their behalf without one.' ARCHITECTURE.md:2865-2869 (section 9.3 neoforge example) declares `legal: eula: {var: eula_accepted, url: https://aka.ms/MinecraftEULA, prompt: ...}`. The shipped games/minecraft-{paper,forge,neoforge}.yaml have no `legal:` block (grep count 0) and run `post_install: [mc.accept_eula, ...]` (minecraft-neoforge.yaml:129). hook/mc.go:36-53 writes 'eula=true' unconditionally. Neither the manifest doc text nor any operator doc mentions the EULA.)

**Impact.** Creating a Minecraft server silently agrees to a third-party licence for the operator, which the project's own spec forbids. The operator is never shown the URL, so the consent the licence relies on never happens.

**Suggestion.** Add the `legal.eula` block plus an `eula_accepted` bool var (default false) to the three MC manifests, as in the section 9.3 example. Gate mc.accept_eula on that var (or have `gvm create` refuse with the prompt text and URL until `--var eula_accepted=true` is passed). Document it in docs/games/minecraft-*.md and in the manifest's doc block.

### documentation-13 — ARCHITECTURE.md is the only reference, CLI output cites it by decision number, and there is no troubleshooting or FAQ

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6 · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** 108 non-test string literals in internal/ contain 'D-nn' or 'section N.N' (grep), e.g. `gvm update --help`: '...which is a success (D-43)' and '...takes a preserve archive first (D-32)'; restore/rebuild `-confirm`: '...as the destructive-action contract requires (section 16.4)'; `gvm content` usage: '(section 12.9)', '(section 12.8)'; console.go:464 '(D-09)'; job.go:89 '(D-45)'. None of these docs are installed on the host. ARCHITECTURE.md:6953 says doctor check IDs exist 'so it can be referenced from documentation and from a support thread', but no doc lists HOST-/EDGE-/FW-/INST- IDs, status phases, or exit codes 64/65/66/75/78 with remedies (they live in sections 7.8 and 20 of the spec). The docs planned for operators (docs/{backups-and-restore,network-edge,tls-cases,control-channel,content-model,THREAT-MODEL,bootstrap,http-api}.md, IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:613-614,735,781) do not exist.

**Impact.** When something goes wrong on a VPS over SSH, the operator sees references they cannot look up and has no 'my server won't start / stuck starting / failed exit 65 / stale lease / port conflict / pin mismatch' page. Diagnosis means reading a 7,148-line design spec written for implementers.

**Suggestion.** Write docs/troubleshooting.md and docs/faq.md, embed them in the binary (the repo already embeds games/ and packaging/), and expose them with `gvm help <topic>` (e.g. `gvm help exit-65`, `gvm help FW-001`, `gvm help stale-lease`) so every hint can cite a topic that works offline on the box. Generate the exit-code and doctor-ID sections from derive.go's exitHint table and the doctor check registry so they cannot drift. In user strings, replace '(D-43)' with a plain clause plus 'see: gvm help <topic>', keeping D-numbers in code comments. Also install the files to /usr/share/doc/gvm once `make install`/deb exist.

### documentation-14 — Dashboard users have no docs and no product, while every command advertises `--remote` and tells non-root users to mint a token

**Medium** · dashboard-user · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: 20 → **48** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Every host-op command's help lists '-remote string  dashboard URL to run this against instead of the local host'. The no-transport error (transport.go:154-159) says '...or point it at a dashboard with --remote / GVM_URL plus GVM_TOKEN (`gvm token create` mints one)'. Following it: `GVM_URL=https://gvm.example.com GVM_TOKEN=abc gvm status vh1` -> 'the remote transport is not built in this release'; `gvm token create --name t` -> 'requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running (`systemctl status gvmd`)'; `gvm web` / `gvm gvmd` -> 'not implemented yet' (EXIT=3). docs/what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:35,42-50,55-79 describes gvmd's socket behaviour and the dashboard's 503 page as current, although Phase 17's DoD (IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:824) requires that file to record *measured* behaviour. No doc explains roles (viewer/operator/admin/owner), login/TOTP, or what a moderator can do.

**Impact.** An operator who wants a friend to restart the server without SSH follows the CLI's own advice through three dead ends (remote, token, gvmd). A moderator has nothing to read. The Postgres-down doc gives false assurance about components that are not running.

**Suggestion.** Until Phase 16 is wired: hide the `--remote` flag and drop the GVM_URL/token clause from the no-transport error (keep 'Run it as root (sudo gvm ...)'). Have README and what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md mark the gvmd and dashboard rows 'not built in this build'. When the dashboard lands, write docs/dashboard.md for moderators: logging in and TOTP enrolment, what viewer and operator roles can do, how Stop/Restart walk the save-and-stop ladder (never kill -9), console etiquette (typing quit/stop is refused; use the Stop button), and the typed-name confirmation for destructive actions. Do not add any unauthenticated read-only view (rule 9).

### documentation-11 — CurseForge blocked-mods doc names commands that do not exist and one of three different drop-box paths, for a source the CLI cannot reach

**Low** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 12 · Plan: 20 → **45** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/content/curseforge has no non-test importers (grep for the import path finds none); blocked.go:123 dropBoxPath is unreferenced; grep -l curseforge games/*.yaml returns nothing. (orig: docs/curseforge-blocked-mods.md:21 says the drop-box is `/etc/gvm/curseforge/dropbox/`; cli/content_blocked.go:126 tells the operator 'Put them in /var/lib/gvm/downloads/mods'; curseforge/blocked.go:123-124 actually scans `/var/lib/gvm/instances/<name>/blocked-mods`. The doc's workflow (lines 35, 45) is `gvm content plan <instance>` -> 'gvm content: unknown subcommand "plan"'; the real report is `gvm content blocked`. Resolver messages say 'run `gvm content resolve-blocked`' (ladder.go:217, blocked.go:128, content_blocked.go:127), which is an unknown subcommand. Line 50 says the API key comes from 'the gvm credential store' without naming it (HostSecretsDir /etc/gvm/secrets/host per instance/paths.go:42-44, where `gvm secret set` needs gvmd). `curseforge.New` is never called outside tests, and content.go:520-523 rejects non-workshop blocks. No shipped manifest declares a curseforge source.)

**Impact.** An operator with a modpack containing blocked mods gets three contradictory places to put the JAR and two commands that do not exist. Even a correct drop does nothing, because the source is not wired. The doc is the only operator guidance for this feature, and every actionable line in it is wrong.

**Suggestion.** Standardise on /var/lib/gvm/downloads/mods as one exported constant, used by the resolver (replace blocked.go dropBoxPath), by `gvm content blocked` output and by the doc, with a test asserting they agree. Change the doc's commands to `gvm content blocked <instance>` and `gvm content sync <instance> --dry-run`, and remove `content plan`/`resolve-blocked` from all strings. Name the key file /etc/gvm/secrets/host/curseforge (0600 root:root). Add a top banner: 'CurseForge content is not wired into gvm content in this build.' Keep rule 13 stated verbatim.

### documentation-15 — The headline 'one rule' claims a CI job that kills the control plane and reboots on every PR; the actual job only greps unit files

**Low** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **20** · Status: open

**Evidence.** ci/control-plane-dead.sh:15 UNIT_DIR defaults to internal/cli/testdata (no *.service files); lines 47-58 scan internal/**/*.service (only the legacy zomboid.service fixture); packaging/systemd is never scanned; DISALLOWED_KEYWORDS covers only Requires= and BindsTo=. .github/workflows/ci.yml:73-74 runs it with no argument. (orig: README.md:20 'CI asserts this on every change.'; ARCHITECTURE.md:35 'There is a CI job (`ci/control-plane-dead.sh`) that does exactly this on every PR' (kill gvmd, gvm-web, Postgres, reboot); what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:18 'The CI gate `ci/control-plane-dead.sh` enforces this.' ci/control-plane-dead.sh:1-11, 25-62 only greps *.service files for Requires=/BindsTo= on postgresql/gvmd/gvm-web, and there is no gvmd or gvm-web to kill. The Phase 17 DoD (IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:819: bring up an instance, stop services, reboot, assert pid/starttime) is unchecked.)

**Impact.** Operators are asked to trust the resilience guarantee because it is 'asserted on every change'. The guarantee is probably true by construction (no unit dependency), but the docs overstate the evidence, which undermines trust in the rest of the documentation when someone checks.

**Suggestion.** Reword README.md:20, ARCHITECTURE.md:35 and the Postgres-down doc to what CI actually checks ('CI asserts no game or job unit depends on Postgres, gvmd or gvm-web'). Restore the stronger claim only once the nested-VM kill-and-reboot job from Phase 17 exists, and link that job's latest run from the README.


---

<a id="cli-walkthrough"></a>
## Hands-on CLI walkthrough (`cli-walkthrough`)

I ran every command's help three ways and walked the full new-server journey, both on the Windows binary and as root against the repo's own fake host (a scratch copy of the repo). The parts that are built are careful and often excellent. Manifest validation, create's variable checks, `apply --diff`, refusals that name their fix, and the destroy safety gates all work well. But the core journey dead-ends: `create` → `apply` works, then `start` says to run `gvm install`, and `install` refuses with "the provider seam is not built in this release". `update` (even `--diff`), `content sync`, `rebuild`, `firewall`, `secret`, `token`, `user`, `audit`, `gvmd` and `web` are also unbuilt or stubs, yet `gvm help` lists them as normal commands and other messages keep recommending them. Help itself is unreliable: `gvm help <cmd>` prints one line, and `<cmd> --help` falls through and runs the command, or errors with exit 1, or treats "help" as an instance name. There is also no CLI path to set a game's join, admin or RCON password, and nothing tells a player how to connect. Most of the friction can be fixed without touching the safety model: wire the existing packages, fix help dispatch, label unbuilt commands honestly, and add small read-only views (vars, connect info, `logs -f`).

**Strengths to preserve**

- `gvm game validate` errors are excellent for manifest authors. They give file:line:col plus a reason and a fix, e.g. `mygame2.yaml:52:10: spec.runtime.cwd: {{if}} is not allowed: a manifest template has no control flow` followed by how to rewrite it.
- `gvm create` stops typos before anything is written. Missing required vars are listed with their help text (`--var mc_version=…   Minecraft version, e.g. 1.21.4. Immutable after creation.`). Undeclared or duplicate `--var`s are refused, and an existing spec is never overwritten. `--dry-run` prints the exact YAML.
- create's success output says what did NOT happen and the next step: `nothing else on this host changed: no account, no directories, no unit.` / `next: gvm apply valheim-1 --diff`.
- `gvm apply --diff` is the same plan apply executes. It honestly flags what it did not verify (`! the write-probe was NOT run ...`, `! the job schedules were NOT checked against systemd`).
- Lifecycle refusals name the fix, and say when it can be overridden: `start refused: the provider's install sentinel is not on disk -> gvm install valheim-1` / `this refusal can be overridden: gvm start valheim-1 --force`.
- Destroy safety gates are sound (rule 5): it requires a verified archive, a typed name, and a stopped server, and it prints the space needed to restore. `--dry` measures without removing anything.
- Unbuilt paths fail non-zero with a sentence instead of silently succeeding. `--remote` refuses plain http with a clear reason.
- `gvm list` has a helpful empty state (`no instances. gvm create <name> --game <id> defines one.`), and a stray positional suggests `gvm status`.
- `gvm logs` points at journalctl for runner diagnostics. `logs --shutdown` shows the shutdown step it ended on (the 'rung') and flags DEGRADED (SIGKILLed) stops.
- `gvm content` help explains acquisition ids versus activation ids clearly. `gvm job list` answers 'what runs on this box at 05:00', including host-level jobs.
- `gvm ports` and `gvm doctor` distinguish 'assumed' and 'not checked' from 'measured' and 'fine'. Doctor runs with Postgres down and says so first.

### cli-walkthrough-1 — The core journey dead-ends at install: install, update (even --diff), update --repair, content sync and rebuild all refuse as 'not built'

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 12 · Plan: 31 → 38 → 44 → **45** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Extra detail: the update --diff failure goes through job.go:391 (buildJobEnv -> attachEngines).
