The start refusal is verbs.go:218-222 and can be overridden (true), but --force cannot help without an install. (orig: Journey run as root against the repo's fake host (scratch copy, journey.txt): `gvm install valheim-1` prints its preflight, then `gvm install: gvm: the provider seam is not built in this release: valheim declares provider "steamcmd", whose driver lands with the blob store. Everything above this line was checked and holds` (exit 1). `gvm start valheim-1` gives `start refused: the provider's install sentinel is not on disk -> gvm install valheim-1`, so the two commands send the user in a loop. `gvm update valheim-1 --diff` (read-only) gives `the provider seam is not built in this release: valheim-1_update needs a provider context to run SteamCMD through (internal/update.Bind over steamcmd.New + jobs.NewSteamRunner)`. Sources: install.go:109-111 always returns ErrProviderUnbuilt; jobengines.go:155-160 (update), :164-169 (content_sync job), :178-180 (rebuild), :414-418 (--repair, including --dry-run); content.go:1338-1339 (`content sync` without --dry-run gives 'That job is not in this build'). `grep -rln internal/provider internal/cli` finds nothing, although internal/provider/{steamcmd,minecraft} exist and Phases 8 and 18 are committed. `gvm job list` after applying PZ shows `pz-1_nightly  update  yes  start_anyway  *-*-* 05:00:00` with no warning, because job.go:541-547 sets Runnable from jobs.Lookup, not from engine wiring.)

**Impact.** An operator following the documented flow (create, apply, install, start) cannot get any game server running from this build. The nightly update timer that apply installs will fail every night, while `gvm job list` presents it as healthy.

**Suggestion.** 1) Do the real wiring. `gvm install`, the update job, `update --repair`, content_sync and rebuild should all build their engines from internal/provider (steamcmd, minecraft). SteamCMD runs through jobs.NewSteamRunner as the instance uid, as an argv exec with no sh -c. Every Apply goes through provider.Authorize, and only digest-verified blobs from the store are written (rule 12). `--diff` and `--repair --dry-run` call Plan/Inspect only. 2) Until that lands: add a pure predicate `engineWired(kind) (bool, why)` that attachEngines' switch also uses, so the two cannot drift. `gvm job list` uses it and prints `cannot run in this build: <why>` per row, without constructing any engine. `gvm apply` prints a warning when it installs a timer for a kind this build cannot run. `update --diff` gets a non-executing placeholder Updates seam so jobs.StepNames can render the table. start's refusal checks the same predicate and says 'game files are not installed, and this build cannot install them (gvm install is not available yet)' instead of pointing back at install.

### cli-walkthrough-2 — No CLI way to set a game's join, admin or RCON password: every `gvm secret` subcommand needs gvmd, and gvmd is not implemented

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 10 · Plan: **29** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Correct impact: runner/preflight.go:236-252 (checkCreds) together with cli/run.go:170-196 (CredIDs = every referenced secret) and packaging/systemd/gvm@.service:59 (RestartPreventExitStatus=64 65 66 78) mean the server fails preflight instead of booting open. reconcile/units.go:519-547 deliberately omits LoadCredential for absent files. create.go:511-514's note is the misleading output. The secret layout is documented in ARCHITECTURE.md:77 and 6674. Another dead-end hint: spec/validate_instance.go:383 says `gvm secret set <inst> join_password` (positional), which matches no real syntax. (orig: `gvm create pz-1 --game projectzomboid ...` prints `secrets   admin_password, join_password, rcon_password — not generated yet; the unit starts without them and the game gets an empty value` (create.go:511-514), with no command to fix it. `gvm secret set --instance pz-1 --name rcon_password` gives `secret set: requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running (systemctl status gvmd)`. secret.go:32-88: list, set, rotate and show-path all return errRequiresDaemon unconditionally (user.go:141-143). Meanwhile `gvm gvmd` gives `not implemented yet` (exit 3, root.go:101), and packaging/systemd has no gvmd.service. secrets/generate.go:51-52 says `DefaultPasswordLen is what gvm create and gvm secret rotate generate`, but GeneratePassword has no non-test callers. games/valheim.yaml:41-44 passes -password only `when: {secret_set: join_password}`, and `public` defaults to "true" (line 115).)

**Impact.** A Valheim server created with defaults boots with no join password while advertised as public; PZ boots with empty admin and RCON passwords. The operator's only recourse is to hand-craft files under /etc/gvm/secrets/instances/<name>/, which nothing documents. This also breaks hard rule 1, because a CLI verb depends on gvmd.

**Suggestion.** Replace the gvmd stubs with local-root, in-process verbs that never dial admin.sock:
- `gvm secret set <instance> <key> (--generate | --stdin)` accepts the template or store spelling. It rejects ' " \ $ ` plus ; = whitespace and control characters, naming the offending byte offset and never the value. It writes through secrets.Store.
- `gvm secret list <instance>` shows each referenced secret as set/NOT SET with its path, never the value.
- `gvm secret show-path <instance> <key>` prints the path. Revealing the value, if wanted at all, needs an explicit `--reveal`.
- `gvm apply` generates any MISSING referenced secret with GeneratePassword (base62, so it satisfies rule 10). It never rotates an existing secret, and it reports the path of each one it generated.
- After set, print `next: gvm apply <n> && gvm restart <n>`.
- Fix create's note: today it says the unit starts with empty values, but the unit refuses to start until the secrets exist.
Keep only the dashboard-account verbs (user/token/audit) behind gvmd.

### cli-walkthrough-4 — The firewall is not wired: `gvm firewall` is unimplemented, apply never opens game ports, and doctor's fixes point at the missing command

**High** · player · not-wired · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: 33 → **40** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm firewall list` gives `gvm firewall: not implemented yet` (EXIT=3; root.go:152), yet `gvm help` lists it under Host. `gvm doctor` prints `✗ FW-001 firewall backend ufw is not active / Fix: gvm firewall enable`, and checks_fw.go:82, :147 and :176 suggest `gvm firewall apply`. internal/firewall is imported only by internal/cli/doctor.go and internal/doctor/checks_fw.go; nothing in reconcile or apply touches it. The full `gvm apply valheim-1` plan in the journey has 22 rows and none is a firewall rule. Doctor's checks are FW-001/002/003/005/006 (checks_fw.go:15-43); none verifies that an expose:public port is actually admitted. FW-005 and FW-006 printed ✓ even while FW-003 said the ruleset could not be parsed.

**Impact.** On any VPS with ufw active, the server can be running and players still cannot connect. gvm says nothing about which ports to open, and every hint it gives leads to 'not implemented'. The README promises 'a firewall derived from what is actually running'.

**Suggestion.** Wire internal/firewall into `gvm firewall status|diff|apply` and make `gvm apply` stage tagged rules (gvm:<instance>:<port>), never enabling ufw (D-41). Delete only gvm-tagged rules, and nothing when the parse is untrusted (D-42); keep the SSH guard. Keep `gvm firewall enable` a separate, explicit, guarded step. Until then, have apply and doctor print the exact manual commands (e.g. `ufw allow 2456:2457/udp comment 'gvm:valheim-1:game'`). Add a doctor check, 'public port not admitted', and make FW-005/006 Skip rather than OK when the ruleset is untrusted or ufw is inactive.

### cli-walkthrough-3 — Help is unreliable: `gvm help <cmd>` prints one line, and `<cmd> --help` falls through into running the command

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The root usage says `Run "gvm help <command>" for detail.` (usage.go:39), but `gvm help create` prints only `gvm create — define a new instance` (root.go:263), with no flags, for all 29 commands. `gvm create --help` prints usage and then `gvm create: gvm: no transport for this command...` (EXIT=1). As root on the fake host, `gvm list --help` prints usage AND then the instance table (exit 0); `gvm stop --help` and `gvm apply --help` end with `instance: invalid name: a name is required` (exit 1). `gvm status help` (root) prints `help  absent / no /etc/gvm/instances/help.yaml` (exit 0), and `gvm update help` gives `no instance "help"`. `gvm adopt|user|token|secret|audit|db --help` give `unknown subcommand "--help"`. `gvm doctor --help` gives `Usage of doctor: ... gvm doctor: flag: help requested` (exit 1), and `gvm doctor help` runs the full doctor. `gvm version --help` prints the version. Root cause: flagErr (game.go:613-618) turns flag.ErrHelp into nil, so parse*Args return zero-valued options with a nil error (create.go:127-129, start.go:47-50, status.go:41-43, list.go:44-46, apply.go:52-54, destroy.go:66-68), and the command keeps going. Usage is also written to stderr (fs.SetOutput(env.Stderr)), so `gvm stop --help | less` shows nothing.

**Impact.** The first thing a new operator tries, `--help`, errors out, runs the command, or reports an instance called 'help'. `gvm help <cmd>` never shows a flag. Users learn not to trust help.

**Suggestion.** Return a sentinel (errHelpShown) from every parser that Main maps to exit 0 with no further output. Have `gvm help <cmd>` call the command's own usage printer (store it on Command). Treat `help`, `-h` and `--help` uniformly as the first argument for every subcommand group (adopt, user, token, secret, audit, db, doctor). Print requested help to stdout and keep stderr for usage errors. Add a table test that runs `<cmd> --help`, `<cmd> -h` and `gvm help <cmd>` for every command and asserts exit 0 with a `Usage:` line.

### cli-walkthrough-5 — The 'forgot sudo' error is jargon-heavy and recommends two fixes that do not exist in this build

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 3 · Plan: **20** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Running `gvm status valheim-1` as non-root prints: `gvm status: gvm: no transport for this command: \`gvm status\` is a host operation. Run it as root (sudo gvm status), or point it at a dashboard with --remote / GVM_URL plus GVM_TOKEN (\`gvm token create\` mints one).` followed by `gvm deliberately does not dial /run/gvm/admin.sock: that socket is 0660 root:gvm, so a CLI that used it would hand the whole root verb surface to every member of group gvm` (transport.go:153-160; root.go:241 adds the doubled 'gvm status: gvm:' prefix). Following that advice: `GVM_URL=https://... GVM_TOKEN=x gvm status valheim-1` gives `the remote transport is not built in this release` (transport.go:174-175), and `gvm token create` gives `requires gvmd`. The sudo hint also drops the subcommand and arguments: `gvm backup list` says `Run it as root (sudo gvm backup)`. Every host-op `--help` advertises `-remote string  dashboard URL to run this against instead of the local host`.

**Impact.** This is the error every operator hits most often, and it sends them down two dead ends plus a paragraph of internal security rationale. A copied sudo hint even runs a different command.

**Suggestion.** Replace it with one line that echoes the full argv: `gvm backup list needs root. Run: sudo gvm backup list`. Mention a dashboard and token only once the API client and token commands exist. Hide `--remote` from flag listings until `ErrRemoteUnbuilt` is gone. Move the admin.sock rationale to the docs; rule 3 stays enforced in code and tests.

### cli-walkthrough-6 — `gvm help` lists unbuilt and stub commands as if they work, and refusals use five phrasings and two exit codes

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **20** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Top-level usage lists firewall, user, token, secret, audit and adopt with ordinary summaries. Actual behavior: `firewall` gives 'not implemented yet' (exit 3); `user|token|audit|secret *` give 'requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running' (exit 1; user.go:48-135, token.go:42-63, auditcmd.go:46-61); `adopt cutover` gives 'adopt cutover: not yet implemented in this phase (requires Linux)' (exit 1); `adopt rollback` says 'run the rollback script printed by cutover', but cutover is unbuilt (adopt.go:22-28). Hidden `web` and `gvmd` give 'not implemented yet'. install and update say 'the provider seam is not built in this release' (exit 1); remote says 'the remote transport is not built in this release' (exit 1). README.md:35-36 promises unbuilt commands 'exit non-zero with `not implemented`'. Adopt is Project-Zomboid-only (adopt.go:41-45 calls DiscoverPZ with default `--unit zomboid`; finalize calls FinalizePZ), yet it is summarised as 'import an existing server install'.

**Impact.** Operators waste time on commands that cannot work and cannot tell 'needs a daemon I should start' from 'not in this build'. Scripts cannot key on a single exit code. Dashboard users have no path at all.

**Suggestion.** Add a parent sentinel ErrUnavailable and have ErrNotImplemented, ErrProviderUnbuilt, ErrContentApplyUnbuilt, ErrRemoteUnbuilt, the gvmd stubs and adopt cutover/rollback all wrap it (errors.Is). Main maps it to exit 3 with one prefix: 'not available in this build: <specific reason>'. Keep each command's own detail, including install's preflight. Add Command.Available (or Unavailable string) and render such commands under an 'Not available in this build' heading in `gvm help`. Rename adopt's summary to 'import an existing Project Zomboid server' but keep the --unit default. Add a README table of what works today and fix README:35-36.

### cli-walkthrough-7 — Messages point to commands that do not exist, including `gvm disable`, so there is no durable stop

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 4, 6 · Plan: 20 → **39** · Status: open

**Evidence.** More dead-end hints: lifecycle/derive.go:400 (`gvm enable`), lifecycle/derive.go:168 and lifecycle/verbs.go:206 (`gvm manifest upgrade <n> --dry`), spec/validate_instance.go:383 (`gvm secret set <inst> <key>`, positional, matching no implemented syntax), jobs/runner_update.go:111 (`gvm instance promote`). (orig: These return `gvm: unknown command` or `unknown subcommand` from the binary: `gvm instance promote` (update.go:148; also provider/steamcmd/steamcmd.go:333, provider/provider.go:280,290, update/pipeline.go:410, update/repair.go:129, provider/adopted/adopted.go:72, spec/capability.go:490); `gvm disable` (lifecycle/derive.go:533, shown on every held instance: `held: an operator stopped it; a reboot clears it, \`gvm disable\` does not`); `gvm game approve <id>` (spec/load.go:249, a manifest override's Fix line); `gvm manifest upgrade` (spec/validate.go:350, content/lock.go:245); `gvm game try <id> --scratch` (games/generic-steam.yaml:22). The hold lives in /run (instance/paths.go:211, tmpfs), and ARCHITECTURE.md:4398 says 'A durable stop is `gvm disable`, which sets enabled: false and applies'; it was never built. `gvm stop --help` does not mention that a reboot restarts the server.)

**Impact.** An operator who stops a server for maintenance and then reboots the VPS finds it running again. The only durable stop is hand-editing YAML and re-applying, which nothing explains. Manifest authors and adopters are told to run commands that do not exist.

**Suggestion.** Implement `gvm disable <instance>` and `gvm enable <instance>`: rewrite spec.enabled and run the same apply. This keeps the filesystem as the control plane, and disable still stops through the save-and-stop ladder (rule 4). Say in `gvm stop` output: 'held until reboot; `gvm disable` keeps it off'. Add a test that every backticked `gvm <verb> [<sub>]` in non-comment string literals across internal/ resolves in the dispatch tables, and rephrase or remove the rest (promote, approve, manifest upgrade, game try) until they exist.

### cli-walkthrough-8 — There is no way to see a game's variables or read the shipped manifests, though errors and the template tell users to

**Medium** · manifest-author · missing-feature · effort S · fact: partial · fit: sound · Plan: **26** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Mitigations already present: create.go:266-296 (the missing-required-var error lists each var with its help text) and the create --dry-run flag (create.go:124), which prints the YAML with every declared var. Both need root via RequireLocal (create.go:373-379). (orig: `gvm create valheim-1 --game valheim --var password=hunter22` gives `valheim declares no variable "password"; \`gvm game validate valheim\` lists them` (create.go:261-262). But `gvm game validate valheim` prints only `✓ 4 vars, memory_model flat — MemoryHigh 2688M, MemoryMax 3G at the declared defaults` (game.go:357); `-v`, `--verbose` and `--json` all give 'flag provided but not defined'. Manifests are embedded in the binary only (games/embed.go; no packaging copies them to disk), yet `gvm game list` shows `embedded games/valheim.yaml` as if it were a path. The template's doc says 'START HERE ... Copy this file to /etc/gvm/games/<yourgame>.yaml' (generic-steam.yaml:9-10), but `gvm game show` is an unknown subcommand and `gvm game which generic-steam` prints only the sha. Also, the unedited template validates with `0 errors, 0 warnings` despite its REPLACE-ME markers and fake appid 999999999.)

**Impact.** Operators guess var names by trial and error (each try needs root and a full create). Manifest authors cannot get the template or any working example off the box they are on.

**Suggestion.** Add `gvm game show <id>` (local, no root). It should print the doc block plus a vars table (name, type, default, required, immutable, apply mode, help), the ports (name, proto, default, expose), the recommended jobs and the provider. Add `--yaml` to emit the raw manifest, so `gvm game show generic-steam --yaml > /etc/gvm/games/mygame.yaml` is the documented authoring start. Add a 'required vars' column to `gvm game list`. Make validate warn when REPLACE-ME markers remain.

### cli-walkthrough-9 — Nothing tells a player, or the operator relaying to players, how to connect

**Medium** · player · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: **33** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm status` (lifecycle/status.go:180-234) prints phase, unit, pid, ready time, players, lease and drift, but no ports, address or password state; the `gvm list` row (status.go:136-146) is the same. The only port view is `gvm ports`, a host occupancy table: `udp/2456  instance:valheim-1 (gvm@valheim-1:game)` / `udp/2457 ... (gvm@valheim-1:query)` / `udp/2458 ... (gvm@valheim-1:spare)` plus `ephemeral  32768-60999`. It mixes in the expose:none `spare` port and gives no public IP, protocol hint or password status.

**Impact.** The question every friend asks ('what do I type to join, and is there a password?') needs the operator to read YAML, work out which port is the join port, and find the IP elsewhere.

**Suggestion.** Add a `connect` block to `gvm status <instance>` and its --json, built from manifest ports with expose: public. It shows `<address>:<port> (<proto>, <port name>)` and `join password: set | not set`, plus a warning line when the firewall does not admit that port (tie this to cli-walkthrough-4). The address comes from an operator-set host config `public_address`, falling back to the primary local interface address labelled '(detected; may be private behind NAT)', never from an external lookup service. `--share` prints a copy-paste block for players. `--share --show-password` may reveal ONLY the join secret, read as root through secrets.Store. JSON reports booleans only, never values.

### cli-walkthrough-10 — No getting-started path: the help banner is design philosophy, and apply does not say what to do next

**Medium** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: 26 → **34** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The `gvm help` banner (usage.go:9-17) is 'The rule this tool is built around: if gvmd, the dashboard and the database are all dead...' with no workflow. create ends with `next: gvm apply valheim-1 --diff` (good), but apply ends with `19 changed, 3 already correct, 4 file(s) read back and matched.` and no next step (apply.go:181-186), although install is required before start. README.md has no install instructions or first-server walkthrough, only Status, Building and Design notes; the only other docs are a 7,148-line ARCHITECTURE.md and the implementation plan.

**Impact.** A new operator must reverse-engineer the create → apply → install → start order (and, today, the secret and firewall steps) from refusals.

**Suggestion.** Add a 'Getting started' block to `gvm help`: `gvm game list` → `gvm create <name> --game <id> --var ...` → `gvm apply <name>` → `gvm install <name>` → `gvm start <name>` → `gvm status <name>`. End apply with a context-aware `next:` line (`gvm install X` when the sentinel is absent, `gvm start X` when installed and stopped). Add a docs/quickstart.md and link it from the README and `gvm help`.

### cli-walkthrough-11 — Internal design-doc ids and Go internals leak into help and output, and apply is very verbose

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Help-page counts across all top-level and subcommand --help output: D-32 x1, D-43 x1, D-45 x1, section 16.4 x2, 12.8 x1, 12.9 x1. The D-45 name error comes from internal/instance/name.go:121-124. (orig: The help pages captured in my scratch output contain `(D-32)` ×3, `(D-43)` ×3, `(D-45)` ×4, `(D-42)` ×2, `section 16.4` ×6, `section 12.8` ×4, `section 12.9` ×4 and 'skips steps 2-7'. Across internal/{cli,lifecycle,doctor,spec,jobs}, 76 string literals contain `(D-nn)` or `section N.N`. Examples: create's name error ends `(D-45)`; `status --json` help says `print lifecycle.Status as JSON` (status.go:38); an update error names `internal/update.Bind over steamcmd.New + jobs.NewSteamRunner` (jobengines.go:157-160); `restore --from` mentions 'legacy bash-suite tarballs'. Unexplained terms include ladder, rung, lease, hold file, materialised allocation and slug. A fresh `gvm apply` prints 22 two-line rows, 11 of them `created with the mode and owner section 5 gives it`. A converged re-apply still prints all 22 rows (`1 changed, 21 already correct`), although apply.go:8-12 says a converged apply 'reports nothing to do'.)

**Impact.** Operators who are not gvm developers cannot act on references to a 7,000-line spec. The long apply output buries the one row that changed, which is the opposite of what apply.go's own design comment intends.

**Suggestion.** Strip `D-nn` and `section N.N` from user-facing strings and keep them in code comments; add a lint test for it. Add `gvm help concepts` (ladder, hold, lease, preserve archive, drift, pin) and link to it. By default, have `gvm apply` print only rows that change, plus a one-line summary, with `--verbose` for the full table. Replace Go identifiers in errors with operator language ('gvm cannot download games in this build').

### cli-walkthrough-12 — Flag-parsing friction: errors printed twice around a full usage dump, single-dash rendering, garbled flags, and a `--user` flag that rejects everything

**Low** · operator · ergonomics · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm create valheim-1 --game valheim --var password` prints `invalid value "password" for flag -var: ...`, then 25 lines of usage, then the same error again. `gvm stop valheim-1 --resaon x` does the same. Flags are listed as `-reason` and `-dry-run`, while usage lines and messages say `--reason` and `--diff`. Backticks in the usage text garble the flag name column: `-game gvm game list` (create.go:114) and `-repair validate` (update.go:55), which makes boolean `--repair` look like it takes an argument. `--enabled false` gives `name one instance, not 2` (it needs `--enabled=false`). `--user` is advertised as 'unix account to run as (default: gvm-<instance>)', but any other value is refused (create.go:151-156). `gvm stauts` dumps the whole 50-line usage with no 'did you mean status?' (root.go:209-212).

**Impact.** Small mistakes produce walls of text where the actual error is easy to miss, and some advertised flags mislead.

**Suggestion.** On a parse error, print only the error plus `Run 'gvm create --help' for usage.` Use a custom PrintDefaults that renders `--flag <type>` and never treats backticks as an argument name. Remove `--user` from create (or hide it) since it only accepts its default. Make `--enabled` / `--no-enabled` work as a pair. Add Levenshtein 'did you mean' suggestions for unknown commands and subcommands.

### cli-walkthrough-13 — Destroy and restore make the operator hunt for archive paths, and restore puts its arguments in the opposite order

**Low** · operator · ergonomics · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: **38** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm backup list <instance>` already lists the archives (backup.go:246-313, printArchiveRows at 397-440). The weakness is that the ARCHIVE column is r.File, a bare filename, not a path usable with --archive. destroy.go:264 verifiedArchive takes wantInstance, so a different instance's archive is refused. (orig: `gvm destroy <instance> --archive <path>` refuses without a path: '--archive is required ... `gvm backup <instance>` writes one' (destroy.go:76-80). The operator must run `gvm backup`, find the `archive_path` key in the run record (job.go:295-304 prints raw keys like `archive_path`, `stall_ms`, `settle_waited_ms`), and paste it back. Restore is `gvm restore <archive> --instance <name>` (restore.go usage), archive first, unlike every other command, which takes `<instance>` first. `gvm restore help` gives `name the instance to restore onto: gvm restore help --instance <name>`. No 'latest' shortcut exists anywhere (grep for latest in restore.go, destroy.go and backup.go returns nothing).)

**Impact.** Destructive flows are slower and more error-prone than they need to be. Copy-pasting long tarball paths at 2am is exactly when the wrong archive gets used.

**Suggestion.** `gvm destroy <instance>` without --archive offers to take one: it runs the backup engine against the already-stopped instance with class preserve, verifies it through the same verifiedArchive path, and prints path, size and time. Only then come the space check and the typed instance name. No --latest on destroy. `gvm restore <instance> --latest | --archive <file>` is added; --latest selects the newest archive of that instance and excludes archives written by an earlier restore's pre-archive step (or labels them). Before the typed confirmation it prints timestamp, age, class and size. The pre-restore preserve archive is unchanged. The old `restore <archive> --instance <n>` form stays as an alias. Run output shows labelled values ('archive: <path> (1.2 GB, verified)') instead of raw keys.

### cli-walkthrough-14 — `gvm logs` cannot follow, so the only live view is the interactive console

**Low** · operator · missing-feature · effort S · fact: partial · fit: sound · Plan: **37** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm logs valheim-1 -f` and `--follow` give `flag provided but not defined`; logs.go:56-58 defines only -n and --shutdown. The console log is a plain file the runner writes (/var/log/gvm/<name>/console.log, logs.go:99), yet watching it live means `gvm console`, which attaches with input enabled ('Ctrl-D or :detach leaves the console'). `-n 0` prints the whole file (logs.go:204), but the help says only 'how many trailing lines to print (default 30)'.

**Impact.** Watching a server boot means either re-running `gvm logs` or attaching a console where a stray keystroke goes to the game.

**Suggestion.** Add `gvm logs <instance> -f`, a read-only tail of console.log that survives rotation. It needs no socket or gvmd, so it is fine under rule 1. Document `-n 0` as 'all lines'.

### cli-walkthrough-15 — `gvm db` commands spew raw retry logs for about 15 seconds when Postgres is down, and `db version` prints a bare number

**Low** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1 · Plan: **22** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm db status` prints 4-5 lines like `2026/10/14 16:02:20 WARN store: pool ping failed, retrying err="failed to connect to \`user=gvm_ops database=gvm\`: ... dial error ..." delay=1s` with backoff up to 16s, then `gvm db: db status: store: datastore unavailable: timed out waiting for postgres` (db.go:19, dbConnTimeout = 15s). migrate, replay-spool and check-grants behave the same way. `gvm db version` prints just `10`.

**Impact.** In the exact situation rule 1 is built for (Postgres down, operator on SSH), each db command stalls with log noise and never says the game servers are unaffected.

**Suggestion.** Use a short bounded default (one or two connection attempts, about 2s), with `--wait <dur>` to opt into the current retry loop. Show slog retry lines only with --debug. On failure print one sentence: 'Postgres is not reachable at <socket> (check: systemctl status postgresql). Game servers, backups and updates are unaffected.' Keep `gvm db version` printing the bare number on stdout and put the human wording in `gvm db status` instead (or in a `--verbose` form of version).


---

<a id="offsite-backup-dr"></a>
## Off-box backups and disaster recovery (losing the VPS, moving to a new one) (`offsite-backup-dr`)

For an operator who loses the VPS, the disaster-recovery path does not exist yet, and parts of the backup UI suggest it does. The local nightly is solid. Archives are self-describing (the manifest is the first member, roots are prefixed, every member has a sha256, and the instance YAML is embedded), retention is verified-before-prune, and preserve archives are never pruned. Off-box copies do not happen. The `spec.backup.remote` block is never wired into the engine, never validated, and the job report prints `ok backup.push` while nothing leaves the box. `spec.backup.dir`, which looks like a way to move archives to a separate volume, is ignored by the code that writes archives but honoured by the code that lists them. On a fresh box there is no supported way back: `gvm restore` (including the non-destructive `--to`) is unbuilt. The archive also lacks the content lock, the pinned manifest bytes and the secrets, `gvm secret set` is a stub, and there is no DR document. The plan should first be honest about this (warn, mark runs degraded, add doctor checks, write a runbook), then wire `--to` extraction, push, restore and a "create from archive" path, without weakening the no-TOFU, encrypt-only-to-a-public-key and archive-before-overwrite rules.

**Strengths to preserve**

- Archives are self-describing and gvm-independent. `.gvm/manifest.json` is the first member, roots map prefixes back to base directories, and every member has a sha256, so `tar -xzf` plus `jq` can recover a world on a box with no gvm (internal/archive/manifest.go:17-62, ARCHITECTURE.md:4202-4243).
- `gvm restore --list --from <dir>` and `gvm backup list --from` work with no database and no index: they peek each tarball's first member, including legacy bash-suite tarballs (internal/cli/backup.go:337-395). This is exactly what a new box with rsynced archives needs.
- Every archive embeds the instance YAML (jobengines.go:310), and ports are materialized in that YAML, so a rebuilt instance can keep the ports players already know.
- Retention safety is real and mirrored off-box. Prune runs only after a verified create (runner_backup.go:139-153), preserve archives are never deleted, and PlanRemotePrune applies the same rules remotely: the fresh upload is never a candidate, preserve is kept, and unmanaged or unknown-class files are left alone (remote.go:464-532).
- The remote design is safe as specified. The host key is pinned with no TOFU and no known_hosts, `encrypt_to` is a public recipient with a strict bech32 checksum, a configured recipient with no encrypter refuses rather than uploading plaintext, and the pipeline streams without a second copy on disk (remote.go:17-41, remote_age.go:258-273, remote_sftp.go:127-144).
- The free-space preflight refuses before the freeze. Under a direct snapshot, a disk that fills mid-archive would otherwise leave a truncated tarball and a world with saving switched off (engine.go:477-504).
- Snapshot choice comes with a sentence explaining why, including the 'btrfs but not subvolume roots' case, and it falls back to direct rather than refusing to back up (snapshot.go:164-205).
- The restore preflight is well designed: it shows which root goes where, refuses manifest drift unless a named flag is given, requires typed confirmation for `--no-pre-archive` (and `--yes` does not satisfy it), and refuses relative archive paths (restore.go:131-140, 222-246).
- Backups work with Postgres, gvmd and the dashboard down, and nightly verification runs from a host timer (what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:31-33, ARCHITECTURE.md:4307).

### offsite-backup-dr-1 — A configured spec.backup.remote is silently ignored, and the job report says `ok backup.push`

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **21** → 54 · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/jobengines.go:304-326 builds backup.Config with no Remote field. Grepping internal/ for `backup.Remote{`, `Spec.Backup.Remote` or `Remote:` finds no production construction; the only hits are spec/instance_test.go:224 and remote_test.go. With Remote nil, internal/backup/engine.go:1017-1020 (`if e.cfg.Remote == nil || !e.cfg.Remote.Enabled() { return nil }`) returns success, so internal/jobs/runner_backup.go:154-165 records the step as passed and Run.Report prints `  ok   backup.push` (execute.go:480-481). Help promises a push: `gvm backup pz-main --help` prints 'Runs the `backup` job: freeze, snapshot, verify, prune, push.' (backup.go:95), while `gvm backup --help` and the runner Summary (runner_backup.go:68) say 'freeze, snapshot, verify, then prune'. Even when wired, remote_sftp.go:217-223 and remote_age.go:147-156 refuse because no dialer or AEAD is wired. The stated reason (remote_age.go:32-37: 'ChaCha20-Poly1305 is in golang.org/x/crypto, which this project does not depend on') is stale: go.mod requires golang.org/x/crypto v0.38.0, imported at internal/identity/box.go:17 and internal/identity/password.go:8.

**Impact.** An operator who writes a remote block (host, user, key_file, pinned host_key, encrypt_to) gets exit 0 every night and an `ok backup.push` line. They believe they have an off-box copy. When the VPS dies, the only copy dies with it. remote_sftp.go:36-38 names this exact outcome as the one to prevent.

**Suggestion.** Step 1 (S, one PR together with dr-7): set `Remote: &backup.Remote{Target: in.Spec.Backup.Remote}` in backupEngine. Change Engine.Push so on_failure=warn RETURNS the error. backup.push is a non-fatal step, so it becomes `warn backup.push: OFF-BOX COPY NOT MADE: ...` and the run is `degraded` (execute.go:752-756, 885). Add the `gvm apply --diff` warning when remote.kind is set and this build has no transport. Make both help strings say 'push (when a supported remote is configured)'. Step 2 (L): put x/crypto/ssh + pkg/sftp behind Dialer and filippo.io/age behind AgeEncrypter.New, using only the sftp subsystem and never exec. HostKeyCallback compares against the pin only, with no known_hosts. Encryption stays mandatory when encrypt_to is set. If push becomes its own kind (gvm-job@<n>_push, started --no-block by the backup job after verify), it takes a dedicated per-instance push lock rather than the instance job lock, so it never blocks update/restart. It reads only the already-verified archive through an open fd, so a concurrent prune cannot truncate it. on_failure then decides the push job's verdict. Add max_rate and a timeout below TimeoutStartSec.

### offsite-backup-dr-2 — spec.backup.dir (and spec.paths.backups) is ignored by the code that writes archives but honoured by list/verify/prune

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: **21** · Status: open

**Evidence.** spec.paths.backups: spec/instance.go:180 declares it; no non-test code reads in.Spec.Paths.Backups. Only spec.backup.dir is honoured, and only by h.backupDir (jobengines.go:263-269). (orig: The backup engine's directory is `Paths: h.paths(jenv.Instance)` (jobengines.go:312). In production h.Paths is nil, so this is instance.PathsFor(name) (instance.go:190-195), whose Backups is always `path.Join(BackupRoot, name)` (instance/paths.go:218). Archive creation (engine.go:832), the free-space check (engine.go:483) and the in-job prune (engine.go:977) all use that value. `gvm backup list`, host prune and host verify go through h.backupDir, which DOES honour spec.backup.dir (jobengines.go:263-270, backup.go:323, jobengines.go:236). Host-level enumeration only walks directories under /var/backups/gvm (internal/jobs/archives.go:122-151). `gvm create` writes `backup.dir: /var/backups/gvm/<name>` into every YAML (create.go:242-245), which invites editing. An empty listing prints 'no archives' (backup.go:398-400).)

**Impact.** Without a remote, the most natural DR step is to point backup.dir at an attached block-storage volume that survives VPS deletion. If the operator does that, nightlies still land on the root disk next to the world, the free-space check measures the wrong disk, `gvm backup list pz-main` says 'no archives' while archives exist, and the host verify timer verifies nothing for that instance. The setting looks like it works and fails both ways.

**Suggestion.** Use one resolver, h.backupDir(name), for the engine's Paths.Backups and for ArchiveStore. ArchiveStore.Instances should union the /var/backups/gvm subdirectories with each instance YAML's spec.backup.dir. Validate spec.backup.dir at create/edit/apply: it must be absolute, used by no other instance, and not inside any instance's install/data/home/logs path. As defence in depth, make archive.Prune skip entries whose manifest instance differs from the instance being pruned (classify them as unmanaged, never routine). Warn, without refusing, when the dir is on the same device as the world paths ('no protection against disk loss'), or when it lies under /mnt or /media yet is on the root filesystem's device ('volume not mounted?'). Mark spec.paths.backups ignored, with a load-time warning when it is set. Print the effective dir, filesystem and device relationship in `gvm backup list`.

### offsite-backup-dr-4 — There is no supported way to get a world back: `gvm restore` is unbuilt, even the non-destructive `--to`, and the failure is jargon after a preflight that reads as if it will proceed

**High** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4, 5 · Plan: **38** · Status: open

**Evidence.** restore.go:244 points at `gvm game diff`, which is not a subcommand (verified with the binary, exit 1). (orig: internal/jobs/execute.go:278-280: `pending = {JobRestore: "internal/backup's restore path (section 13.2)"}`. restore.go:171 prints the full preflight, including 'step 2 the current world is archived as `preserve`, reason pre-restore, first', and then runKind fails with 'jobs: no runner for this job kind: "restore" is implemented by internal/backup's restore path (section 13.2), which this build does not have'. The `--to <dir>` inspection path also refuses: restore.go:176-184 says 'extracting into %s is internal/archive.Restore, which this build has; what it is missing is the path that drives it'. archive.Restore with RestoreOptions.To is built and stream-verifies (internal/archive/restore.go:43-47, 101-107). `gvm restore --help` (run above) says nothing about the command being unavailable. There is also no `gvm backup verify --file <archive>` for an archive copied onto a new box: verify only walks instance directories (backup.go:112-142).)

**Impact.** In the worst moment (a dead VPS, or a rolled-back world) the operator learns from a source-path error message that gvm cannot restore. No in-tool pointer and no doc explains the manual procedure, so they are left reverse-engineering prefixes, ownership and the stop/start order by hand.

**Suggestion.** Wire `gvm restore <archive> --to <dir>` to archive.Restore{To: dir}, run in-process as root. Refuse unless <dir> is absent or empty. Also refuse when <dir> or any <dir>/<prefix> equals or overlaps any instance's install/data/home/logs or backup dir; the --to path should never be able to touch a world. Add read-only `gvm backup verify --file <abs> [--deep]`. Before the preflight, print: 'Restoring over a live world is not built in this release. Safe manual path: gvm backup <name>; gvm restore <archive> --to /root/restore-x; gvm stop <name>; mv the current <prefix> dirs aside (do not delete); move the restored ones in; chown -R <user>:<user>; gvm start <name>. See docs/backups-and-restore.md'. Then build the §13.2 runner with typed confirmation and preserve-first.

### offsite-backup-dr-5 — An archive is not enough to rebuild an instance on a new host, and no command helps do it

**High** · operator · missing-feature · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1, 10 · Plan: **55** · Status: open

**Evidence.** engine.create (engine.go:830-842) never sets archive.Options.ContentLock or SecretsIncluded, although manifest.go:33-39 and ARCHITECTURE.md:4219/4233-4242 promise a 'content_lock' and 'secrets_included' so the archive is 'self-describing enough to rebuild the instance on a different box'. The pinned manifest bytes (<name>.d/manifest.lock.yaml, instance/paths.go:109) are not archived; only the sha256 and revision are. Copying spec_yaml onto a box running a newer gvm then fails in pinnedManifest: '...records no pinned manifest bytes and the catalog's <game> does not match the pin' (cli/instance.go:532-544). There is no re-pin command (`gvm game --help` lists only validate/list/which/hooks). Override manifests in /etc/gvm/games (the manifest-author's own YAML) are not archived either. Secrets cannot be re-entered: `gvm secret set --instance pz-main --name join_password` prints 'secret set: requires gvmd — ensure the daemon is running', exit 1 (secret.go:43-57 is a stub, and the CLI never dials admin.sock). Meanwhile the PZ archive does carry the rendered passwords in Server/servertest.ini (games/projectzomboid.yaml:146,150,211). There is no `gvm create --from-archive`.

**Impact.** Moving hosts means hand-copying the YAML, hand-editing the manifest pin, hand-placing secret files at the right modes, and re-resolving mods with no lock, while the provider install and content apply are themselves unbuilt. Players find the join and RCON passwords changed unless the operator digs the old values out of the archived ini. A manifest-author's custom game definition dies with the box.

**Suggestion.** Priority order. (c) Implement `gvm secret set/list/show-path` directly against /etc/gvm/secrets as root through the existing filestore: atomic write at 0600 in the 0700 dir. Read the value from a no-echo tty prompt or from stdin, never from argv. Enforce the rule-10 character ban, and keep --generate. (a) Fill the existing manifest fields: ContentLock (manifest.go:38-39), plus a new field for the pinned manifest bytes and, for origin=override, the override manifest. (b) `gvm create <name> --from-archive <file> [--dry-run]` parses spec_yaml and manifest bytes as untrusted input. It re-runs manifest validation including the template guard (rule 10) and ValidateInstance, and installs the manifest only through the override-approval path. Ports are kept only when the allocator's occupancy check passes, otherwise it refuses and names the conflict. An adopted spec is normalised to managed paths and managed_install. It never runs install, restore or apply itself; it prints the checklist. (d) Defer the encrypted secrets bundle until age is wired.

### offsite-backup-dr-3 — The remote block is never validated, so typos and a pasted private age key are accepted and then copied into every archive

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 1 · Plan: **21** · Status: open

**Evidence.** backup.Remote.Validate (remote.go:187-235) has good messages, including the no-TOFU text at remote.go:219-222 ('Paste the line from `ssh-keyscan -t ed25519 host` after checking it against the server itself'). ParseRecipient's warning at remote_age.go:100-102 ('If this is an AGE-SECRET-KEY, it is the PRIVATE half and must never be in an instance file') is also never reached. Neither is called outside the backup package. spec.ValidateInstance (validate_instance.go:177-266) checks egress, content and jobs but nothing under spec.backup. Instance YAML is decoded non-strictly (load.go:383 `yaml.Unmarshal(raw, &in)`), so `hostkey:` or `encrypt-to:` is silently dropped. FailsTheJob treats any on_failure typo as warn (remote.go:162-170). The raw YAML is embedded in every archive (`SpecYAML: string(raw)`, jobengines.go:310). /etc/gvm is readable by the web tier so it can render specs (secrets/filestore.go:16-19).

**Impact.** `gvm apply` raises no complaint about a broken remote, so the operator finds out on the day they need the copy. The likeliest mistake is pasting the identity instead of the recipient. That puts the only decryption key in a group-readable YAML and then into every local and off-box archive, which defeats the 'the VPS cannot decrypt its own backups' property.

**Suggestion.** Move ParseHostKey, ParseRecipient and the bech32 code into a leaf package that spec can import (or into spec itself), and have backup.Remote.Validate call it. Run remote validation from ValidateInstance, i.e. at create, edit, `content`, and apply/apply --diff, which today does not call ValidateInstance, so add it to buildApply. Report missing fields, a bad pin, a bad recipient, negative keep values and unknown kind/on_failure as errors. At those same validate points, report an `AGE-SECRET-KEY-1` value anywhere in the YAML as a hard error with the fix text. In the runtime path, never refuse: the backup job redacts that value from SpecYAML before embedding it and records a job warning, and doctor raises an error. Unknown keys get a positioned warning from a strict-decode pass at validate time only. The runtime decode stays lenient.

### offsite-backup-dr-7 — Even once wired, a failed off-box push stays invisible: `warn` reads as ok, `fail` cannot fail the job, and nothing records the last good off-box copy

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: 21 → 36 → **54** · Status: open

**Evidence.** With on_failure: warn, Engine.Push only calls e.note(...) and returns nil (engine.go:1028-1033), so the step is `ok` and the run is `ok`. Engine notes go only to slog Info (engine.go:277-287) and never become job warnings (execute.go:751-757, 885-887). With on_failure: fail, Push returns the error, but the backup.push step is not `Fatal` (runner_backup.go:154-165), so the run is at most `degraded`. remote.go:69-71 documents `fail` as how an operator says 'I treat the off-box copy as the backup'. PushResult (target, name, bytes, encrypted_to, removed) is stored in Engine.Result (engine.go:291-307), but no CLI code calls Result(), and the job summary keys are only archive_path/bytes/sha256/verified (runner_backup.go:110-112, 135).

**Impact.** An expired key or full NAS produces weeks of green nightlies. The operator who opted into `on_failure: fail` gets the same degraded state as a minor warning. Nothing answers 'when did a copy last leave this box?'

**Suggestion.** Map warn to jr.Warn (run `degraded`, shown as `warn backup.push: ...`) and fail to a Fatal step (run `failed` under the backup kind's `fail` policy). The local archive is already written and verified by then, so rule one still holds. Put push_target, push_name, push_bytes, encrypted_to and push_skipped_reason in the run summary and print them in printJobTail. Stamp the archive index entry with `pushed_at`/`pushed_to` so `gvm backup list` can show an OFF-BOX column and doctor can measure the age of the last good off-box copy.

### offsite-backup-dr-9 — Snapshot method, freeze stall and fallback are computed but never reported to the operator

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **36** · Status: open

**Evidence.** ZFS: snapshot.go:176-191 picks reflink before the ZFS->direct case, and snapshot_linux.go:126-134 probes FICLONE regardless of fs type; only MethodZFS itself is a stub. (orig: SnapshotResult{Method, Why, StallMs, Fallback} is filled in (snapshot.go:231-244, engine.go:377, 640, 702-704), but the backup runner only sets archive_path, archive_bytes, archive_sha256 and verified (runner_backup.go:110-112, 135). printJobTail's key list includes 'stall_ms' (cli/job.go:295-299), but grepping internal/ shows nothing ever sets that key. The only record is the audit params snapshot_method/freeze_ms (engine.go:859-863) and slog Info lines. Choose's comment says '`gvm doctor` renders what each instance would choose without taking a backup' (snapshot.go:157-158), but no doctor check does (doctor/checks_host.go:83-102). AllowMethods ('an operator ... can pin an instance to `direct`') has no spec field (grep AllowMethods: engine.go only). ZFS hosts are detected but always fall to direct (snapshot.go:185-192, snapshot_linux.go:324-333). The dashboard has no backup route (web/routes.go:31-58).)

**Impact.** On an 8 GB ext4 VPS the operator never learns that the nightly stops saving the world for over a minute, or that making world_paths btrfs subvolumes would cut that to milliseconds. §13.2 says they 'deserve to know'. Neither the job report, `--json`, the dashboard nor doctor says it.

**Suggestion.** Put snapshot_method, snapshot_why, stall_ms and snapshot_fallback in the backup run summary. Print one line, e.g. 'snapshot direct (ext4 ...) - world not saving for 74.2s', marked red above StallRed. Fold Engine notes into Run.Notes. For doctor: report the LAST backup run's recorded method and stall from the on-disk job record. Add a statfs/stat-only capability hint (btrfs-without-subvolume-roots, zfs-not-wired), and label reflink as 'untested by doctor' rather than calling trialClone. Add `spec.backup.snapshot: auto|direct` mapped to AllowMethods.

### offsite-backup-dr-10 — btrfs snapshot clones are read-only subvolumes that the cleanup and startup sweep cannot delete

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 2 · Plan: **21** · Status: open

**Evidence.** btrfsSnapshot creates the clone with BTRFS_SUBVOL_RDONLY (snapshot_linux.go:304-310). snapshot_btrfs.go:12-18 states that read-only btrfs subvolumes require BTRFS_IOC_SNAP_DESTROY_V2 and cannot be deleted with os.RemoveAll. Both removal paths still use RemoveAll: Engine.dropClone (engine.go:781-797 calls e.cfg.FS.RemoveAll, and osFS.RemoveAll is os.RemoveAll, fs.go:69) and SweepSnapDir (snapshot.go:329-330). btrfsListSnaps, the helper written for this case, is never called (grep: only its definition). The failure note promises 'the next run's startup sweep will take it' (engine.go:790-791), which it cannot. Not exercised on Linux in this review (PLAUSIBLE).

**Impact.** This hits exactly the operators who followed gvm's advice to make world paths subvolume roots. Each nightly leaves a read-only snapshot under /var/lib/gvm/instances/<n>/snap that pins old extents, so free space shrinks night by night until the free-space preflight refuses backups. The only trace is an Info line promising a cleanup that never happens.

**Suggestion.** Implement subvolume destroy (BTRFS_IOC_SNAP_DESTROY_V2 via ioctl, no shell, per rule 2) and use it in dropClone and SweepSnapDir when a leftover clone is a subvolume (use btrfsListSnaps). Add a doctor check counting leftover `*.snap` directories with their age and size. Remove the 'next run's startup sweep will take it' wording for clones the sweep cannot remove.

### offsite-backup-dr-11 — Backup health is invisible: no doctor check, no status line, no dashboard view, no failure alert

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 2, 6, 9 · Plan: 36 → 37 → 49 → **54** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The doctor host checks are HOST-009/010 plus DB checks, and the instance checks are pin, memory, timeouts, spec applied, workshop trees, order and single group (doctor/checks_host.go:17-34, 83-102). Grepping for backup/archive/preserve in internal/doctor finds nothing. ARCHITECTURE.md:4305 promises '`doctor` reports total `preserve` bytes so they notice before the disk fills'. internal/cli/status.go and list.go contain no 'backup' or 'archive'. The web tier has instance list/detail/console and start/stop/restart only (web/routes.go:31-58). packaging/systemd/gvm-job@.service has no OnFailure= hook. The backup kind's policy is `fail` (runner_backup.go:66), but a failed nightly only reaches the journal and job_runs.

**Impact.** Nobody is told when nightlies start failing, or that every archive sits on the same disk as the world with no remote. Operators find out on the day they need a backup. A moderator using the dashboard cannot tell whether a backup exists before they restart into a risky mod change.

**Suggestion.** Ship BACKUP-001 to 004 as read-only doctor checks: stale newest-verified archive, backup dir on the same device as the world with no remote, preserve bytes as a share of free space, and remote configured but unsupported or never succeeded. Add the `gvm status <name>` backup/off-box line, built from the archive index on disk. Add an authenticated, read-only backup panel served by gvmd from the same on-disk index and job records, falling back gracefully when Postgres is down. Make the opt-in OnFailure= target `ExecStart=/usr/local/bin/gvm notify job-failed %i`. It writes an attention marker that status, doctor and the dashboard show, and can optionally POST to an operator-configured webhook via net/http. It never execs an operator command or a shell. None of this actuates (rule 6).

### offsite-backup-dr-12 — Running out of backup space: the refusal gives raw byte counts and no next step, and the estimate is about 3x the real archive

**Medium** · operator · ergonomics · effort S · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: **36** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The 3x figure applies to compressible worlds such as PZ (engine.go:79-86 comment). The conservative estimate is an intentional pre-freeze safety check, not an oversight. (orig: ErrNoRoom's message is '%s holds %d bytes free and this archive needs about %d (%d source bytes plus %.0f%% headroom); %d bytes short...' (engine.go:497-503), with raw integers. The size check uses the UNCOMPRESSED source size times 1.1 (engine.go:79-86, 494), although the comment admits a PZ world 'compresses to roughly a third'. Archives default to /var/backups/gvm on the root disk (instance/paths.go:218). keep comes from the manifest (14) and keep_days stays 0 (create.go:242-245). Preserve archives from destroy, rebuild, restore and adopt accumulate with `rm` by a human as the only removal (backup.go:82-86, 432-433).)

**Impact.** On the small VPS gvm targets, nightlies are refused while there is actually room for the compressed archive. The operator gets a line like '1288490188 bytes free ... 1188489606 bytes short' with no guidance on which archives to remove, whether to lower keep, or how to move backups.

**Suggestion.** Format every size with spec.FormatBytes, and end the refusal with next steps: `gvm backup list <n>` (with the preserve total, removable only by hand), lower spec.backup.keep (current value), or point spec.backup.dir at another volume. Never auto-delete preserve archives. Relax the estimate only when the plan's method thaws before archiving (reflink/btrfs), or when the backup dir is on a different device from the world. Use max(previous compressed size × 1.5 × the world-growth ratio since then, a fixed reserve such as 512 MB). Keep the uncompressed × 1.1 check for direct-method, same-device runs. Optionally add a free-space watchdog during the write that aborts and removes the partial file if free space drops below the reserve.

### offsite-backup-dr-13 — There is no backup/restore or moving-host runbook; the planned docs/backups-and-restore.md was never written

**Medium** · operator · docs · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 7 · Plan: 38 → **55** · Status: open

**Evidence.** IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:614 lists docs/backups-and-restore.md as a deliverable. `ls docs` shows ARCHITECTURE.md, IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md, two bug sweeps, curseforge-blocked-mods.md and what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md, and no backup document. The only user-facing backup text is the Postgres-down table (what-happens-if-postgres-is-down.md:31-33). Nothing tells the operator what a host move loses outside instance archives: /etc/gvm/secrets (archive SecretsIncluded is always false, engine.go:830-842), /etc/gvm/master.key (TOTP key, secrets/filestore.go:30-34), dashboard users, roles and tokens in Postgres (`gvm db` has only migrate/status/version/replay-spool/check-grants), /etc/gvm/games overrides, and the fact that `gvm apply` never enables ufw on the fresh box (D-41).

**Impact.** Without a runbook, an operator in a crisis learns these gaps one failure at a time. Players also get no prepared message about the new address or whether passwords survived the move.

**Suggestion.** Write docs/backups-and-restore.md (linked from `gvm backup --help`, `gvm restore --help` and doctor). Before disaster: what each archive contains and lacks, how to copy archives off-box today (rsync or scp of /var/backups/gvm until push exists), how to escrow secrets and override manifests, how to keep the age identity and test-decrypt. Moving to a new VPS: an ordered command list (install the .deb, copy archives, `gvm restore --list --from`, recreate the instance, set secrets, install, restore, `gvm apply`, enable ufw by hand, open `gvm ports` in the provider firewall, re-create dashboard users). A player-facing note: same ports, new IP, passwords unchanged only if secrets were restored. State plainly which steps are manual in this release.

### offsite-backup-dr-6 — Restore destinations default to the OLD box's paths recorded in the archive, not the target instance's paths

**Low** · operator · safety-ux · effort S · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: **38** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Latent: jobs/execute.go:278-280 has no restore runner, so the Dest fallback is never reached from the CLI; the only current effect is the preflight line at restore.go:268-276. (orig: printRestorePreflight shows `dest := r.Base` (the base recorded in the archive) as the destination unless `--to` is given (restore.go:268-276). archive.RestoreOptions.Dest says 'A prefix absent from the map restores to the base the manifest records' (internal/archive/restore.go:38-41). runRestoreOnto never builds a Dest map from the target instance's spec (restore.go:148-186). The CLI has no `--map prefix=dir` flag. An adopted instance records bases such as /home/pzserver/Zomboid (ARCHITECTURE.md:4214, 4226), while a gvm-created instance on the new box lives at /srv/gvm/<name>/data (create.go:197).)

**Impact.** Restoring an archive from the old box onto the same-named instance on a new box (or onto a renamed or re-pathed instance) would, as the preflight shows and as the library defaults, write into the old box's directory layout instead of the instance's data dir. That could create a stray tree, or worse, land on a path another instance owns. The preflight currently shows the wrong destination.

**Suggestion.** With --instance, build Dest from the target spec, mapping each prefix label to in.Spec.Paths.<label>. Show 'data/ -> /srv/gvm/pz-main/data (archive taken from /home/pzserver/Zomboid)' in the preflight. Refuse a prefix with no matching target path unless `--map label=/abs` is given. Every --map destination must lie inside one of the target instance's own declared paths and must not overlap another instance's paths. destinations() should also refuse nested destinations, not only equal ones. Never fall back to the recorded base for an over-the-world restore; the recorded base is used only under --to and in the forensic note.

### offsite-backup-dr-8 — No tooling for setting up or using the off-box copy: key custody, host-key fetch, target test, remote list/pull, decrypt

**Low** · operator · missing-feature · effort L · fact: partial · fit: adjust · Plan: **54** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Remote config format and the cannot-decrypt property are documented at docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:4320-4337 (not user-facing, but not source-only). (orig: `gvm backup --help` lists only <instance>, verify, prune and list (output above). No subcommand helps generate or explain an age identity, show a server's host key for out-of-band checking, test the target, list or pull remote archives, or decrypt one. `encrypt_to` is a single string (spec/instance.go:285), although AgeEncrypter already takes `Recipients []Recipient` (remote_age.go:120-131), so there is no way to add a second break-glass recipient. On a new box, `gvm restore --list --from <dir>` silently skips `.tar.gz.age` files because peekRows only reads names ending in `.tar.gz` (backup.go:377). The design (remote_age.go:13-18) means the VPS cannot decrypt its own backups, yet no help text or doc says that losing the identity loses every off-box archive.)

**Impact.** Setting up a correct remote means reading Go source to learn the format. The custody consequence (no identity, no backups) is never stated. A folder of downloaded encrypted archives shows 'no archives'.

**Suggestion.** Add `gvm backup remote check <instance>`: validate, dial, and compare the host key inside HostKeyCallback. With no pin or a mismatch, abort the handshake BEFORE user authentication. Print the presented key as 'SHA256:...' with the instruction 'compare with `ssh-keygen -lf /etc/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key.pub` run ON the backup server', and print the YAML line to paste. Never write the YAML. With a valid pin, do put/list/remove of a probe file. Add `remote list` and `remote pull` (read/get only). Do not ship `gvm backup decrypt`. Instead document running `age -d` on the operator's workstation or the new box, followed by `gvm backup verify --file` and `gvm restore --to`. Accept `encrypt_to` as a string or a list, recommending a second offline recipient. List `.age` files in `--from` output as 'encrypted, needs identity'. Add the custody warning to help text.

### offsite-backup-dr-14 — `gvm backup list --remote` means 'dashboard URL', not the off-box backup target

**Low** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: 20 → **54** · Status: open

**Evidence.** backup.go:260: `fs.StringVar(&remote, "remote", "", "dashboard URL to run this against instead of the local host")`. The same word names the off-box target in YAML (spec.backup.remote, spec/instance.go:272). Running `gvm backup list pz-main --remote backup.example.net` prints 'gvm backup: gvm: no transport for this command: "backup.example.net" names no host; want https://gvm.example.com' (exit 1).

**Impact.** An operator looking for their off-box archives types the obvious flag and gets an error about a dashboard. This is especially likely once remote list/pull commands exist.

**Suggestion.** Keep `--remote` as the global dashboard flag for consistency, but reserve the off-box verbs under `gvm backup remote ...` (see offsite-backup-dr-8). When `--remote` gets a value with no scheme, add a hint: 'did you mean the off-box target? see `gvm backup remote list <instance>`'.

### offsite-backup-dr-15 — The pinned host-key 'fingerprint' in push logs is the same string for every ed25519 key

**Low** · operator · confusing-output · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **36** · Status: open

**Evidence.** HostKey.Fingerprint returns `h.Algo + " " + truncate(base64.RawStdEncoding(blob), 24)` (remote_sftp.go:149-155), and Describe puts it in every job-log target line (remote_sftp.go:190-194). The comment claims it is 'enough for an operator to compare against `ssh-keygen -lf`'. An ed25519 wire blob starts with the length-prefixed name 'ssh-ed25519' and a 4-byte key length, so the first 24 base64 characters are fixed. A check with three random keys printed 'AAAAC3NzaC1lZDI1NTE5AAAA...' each time.

**Impact.** The one log field meant to tell the operator which server identity is pinned identifies nothing, and it cannot be compared with `ssh-keygen -lf`, which prints 'SHA256:...'. That undermines the out-of-band verification the no-TOFU design relies on.

**Suggestion.** Print the OpenSSH-style fingerprint, 'SHA256:' + base64.RawStdEncoding(sha256(blob)), so it matches `ssh-keyscan host | ssh-keygen -lf -`. Use the same format in `gvm backup remote check` and in the host_key validation messages.


---

<a id="game-runtime-compat"></a>
## Will each shipped game actually boot under gvm's hardened unit? (Mono/Wine vs W^X, Proton, 32-bit, xvfb) (`game-runtime-compat`)

Of the 15 real games gvm ships, only Project Zomboid and the three Minecraft manifests have a launch and readiness block that the runner can actually execute under the generated unit. They are all JVM titles that already set jit:true. Every other title fails before, during or after boot, and `gvm game validate` gives each one "0 errors, 0 warnings".
- 10 manifests use an `a2s` readiness probe that the runner does not build.
- Core Keeper's `xvfb` wrapper is refused at start.
- The Unity/Mono titles (Valheim, Rust, 7 Days to Die, Core Keeper) render MemoryDenyWriteExecute=yes.
- The four Proton titles start Proton without STEAM_COMPAT_DATA_PATH, under W^X, with arch native.
- On a Proton stop the ladder signals only the Python launcher, so the Wine game ends up SIGKILLed.

When a boot fails, the operator and the dashboard see "starting" for 5-10 minutes per attempt. The restart loop then never trips StartLimitBurst, and exit 75 is labelled "an orphaned game process ... is still alive". Neither doctor nor preflight checks Proton, python3 or Xvfb. The tool the manifests point to for catching all of this (`gvm game try`) does not exist. Most fixes are manifest data (jit, arch, env, process_group, native depots) inside the existing hardening model. They need a real scratch boot to confirm, plus three runner changes: a2s wiring, detecting a game that exits during boot, and not collapsing ladder waits when only the launcher has died.

**Strengths to preserve**

- The hardening choices are per-manifest data (spec.runtime.jit, spec.runtime.arch, wrapper) and the generated drop-in explains each directive in comments (units.go:557-618, dropin.tmpl:75-84). Most compatibility fixes are therefore YAML edits, not Go.
- The Proton wrapper is exec'd directly with an absolute path, never via a shell or a PATH lookup. A misconfigured wrapper is refused rather than silently started without Proton (wrapper_proton.go:13-20, 73-78). BuildLaunch likewise refuses a wrapper kind it cannot honour instead of dropping it (config.go:217-223).
- The shutdown ladder's settled() needs both a dead leader and an empty cgroup, so a launcher that exits early never makes gvm declare the world free (shutdown.go:838-863).
- The game's stdout and stderr share one pipe into the ring and console.log (pipe_linux.go:22-24), so Proton's 'No compat data path?' and Mono/Unity crash banners do land in `gvm logs`.
- Secret argv elements are redacted in the '-- gvm: exec' annotation (config.go:151-191), and secret-bearing env is kept out of Environment= (units.go:386-396).
- `gvm create` lists every missing required var with its help text in one error (create.go:285-295). The steamcmd-not-found error names the exact apt commands for 32-bit libs (steamcmd.go:968-971), which is a good model for Proton/Xvfb errors.
- A backup whose sources match no files fails loudly rather than leaving an empty tarball (archive/create.go:347-350), so a mis-pointed world path surfaces as an error rather than as silent data loss.
- The exit-code table plus RestartPreventExitStatus=64 65 66 78 and STATUS= lines (gvm@.service:56-59, preflight.go:21-41) give deterministic, non-looping failures for config mistakes.

### game-runtime-compat-1 — The a2s readiness probe is not built by the runner, so 10 of 16 shipped manifests can never reach 'ready'

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4, 6 · Plan: **27** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Spawn is at internal/cli/run.go:427 and AwaitReady at internal/cli/run.go:473-488; the reviewer's 'run.go' is internal/cli/run.go, not internal/runner. reportExit is at internal/cli/run.go:574-579 (it uses rep.ExitCode when >0). (orig: internal/runner/readiness.go:459-466 (BuildProbes default branch): "a2s, mc_ping and control_auth are declared SupportImplemented in spec's registry and are not built here yet" -> returns "kind %q is not built in this release". internal/runner/readiness_test.go:326 asserts this refusal for a2s. internal/probe/a2s.go implements the probe, but nothing outside the probe package references probe.A2S.

The spec registry claims support: internal/spec/validate.go:162-169 ("a2s": SupportImplemented).

Manifests using it: ark-se.yaml:64, cs2.yaml:73, enshrouded.yaml:60, generic-steam.yaml:82, 7daystodie.yaml:63, 7daystodie-telnet.yaml:54, palworld.yaml:73, rust.yaml:74, satisfactory.yaml:64, vrising.yaml:71.

`gvm.exe game validate games/vrising.yaml` -> "✓ 1 readiness probe, hard_deadline 600s ... 0 errors, 0 warnings."

Runtime path: run.go:427 spawns the game. Then run.go:473-488 AwaitReady fails immediately and the ladder runs. The unit exits reportExit(rep, ExitTempFail)=75, which is not in RestartPreventExitStatus (gvm@.service:59), so it restarts every cycle.)

**Impact.** For Rust, CS2, Palworld, 7DTD, ARK, V Rising, Satisfactory and Enshrouded, every start spawns the game and stops it seconds later through the ladder, then systemd restarts it. The server is never usable. Validate shows green, and `gvm status` shows 'starting' or an exit 75 labelled 'an orphaned game process ... is still alive'. Players see a server that keeps appearing and vanishing.

**Suggestion.** (1) Add probe.KindA2S and probe.KindMCPing cases to runner.BuildProbes, resolving the port through the existing addr() resolver as the TCP/UDP cases do. (2) Mark control_auth SupportPlanned in spec/validate.go probeKinds until a runner case exists, so `gvm game validate` warns. (3) In cli/run.go, call runner.BuildProbes (or a new r.PrepareReadiness) right after runner.New and before Preflight/Spawn, mapping its error to Failure{Check: "readiness", Code: ExitUsage} with a STATUS= line naming the probe kind. An unbuildable probe then never spawns the game and never restart-loops. (4) Add TestEveryShippedManifestBuildsLaunchAndProbes over games.FS, plus a test asserting that every probe kind the registry calls SupportImplemented has a BuildProbes case.

### game-runtime-compat-2 — On a Proton stop the ladder signals only the Python launcher; its death collapses every remaining wait, and the Wine game is SIGKILLed

**High** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: 27 → **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** process_group is unused at runtime: grep shows ProcessGroup only at internal/spec/manifest.go:245-248; internal/runner/spawn_linux.go:57 sets SysProcAttr{Setsid: true} unconditionally. Ladder steps: vrising.yaml:79-80, satisfactory.yaml:72-73, enshrouded.yaml:68-69 (no save step at all), ark-se.yaml:72-74 (saveworld first). (orig: wrapper_proton.go:44-51 makes the Proton script argv[0], so the pinned leader is python3 (Proton's shebang is '#!/usr/bin/env python3'). Proton's run() launches wine via subprocess.call (ValveSoftware/Proton proton_9.0 `proton`).

All four Proton manifests set process_group: false and use a process-scoped SIGTERM: vrising.yaml:61,79-80; satisfactory.yaml:54,72-73; enshrouded.yaml:50,68-69; ark-se.yaml:54,72-74.

shutdown.go:774-776: finishWait returns as soon as `w.gone` closes. w.gone is the direct child being reaped (process_linux.go:178-185) and stays closed, so every later rung's wait is zero-length. shutdown.go:496-505 then runs the implicit rungs (SIGTERM group, SIGKILL group, SIGKILL cgroup). shutdown_test.go:653-683 asserts exactly that sequence, with no grace period, for 'an engine that outlived its leader'.

The same collapse hits any non-exec launcher on its signal rung: palworld.yaml:55-58 (PalServer.sh, 'execs nothing') and 7daystodie.yaml:18-19.)

**Impact.** Stopping, restarting or updating V Rising, Satisfactory, Enshrouded or ARK under gvm goes like this: wait_exit times out, SIGTERM kills the Python wrapper, and within milliseconds the actual Wine game process gets SIGTERM then SIGKILL. That breaks hard rule 4 in practice (kill -9 of a live server mid-save), and every stop is recorded 'DEGRADED: a SIGKILL was needed'. Operators learn to ignore the degraded flag.

**Suggestion.** Runner, do first: in finishWait, treat a closed w.gone as the end of the wait only when l.settled() is true. Otherwise keep polling settled() (Proc.Alive then Proc.Empty) every waitExitPoll until the rung's budget, marker or ctx ends. This covers declared wait_exit and signal rungs and the implicit SIGTERM rung. Update shutdown_test.go:653-683 so an engine that outlives its leader gets the full SIGTERM grace before any SIGKILL rung. Manifests: drop the leading bare wait_exit rung. For Proton titles, lead with a game-level save/quit over the control channel where one exists (for example ARK RCON saveworld then DoExit). Use a signal only after a scratch boot proves which pid receives it and that it saves, and never use scope: cgroup for SIGINT/SIGTERM under Wine. Shrink the Proton surface by moving ARK and Satisfactory to native binaries (finding 8).

### game-runtime-compat-3 — Proton titles cannot start: no STEAM_COMPAT_DATA_PATH, W^X enforced, arch native, and the example Proton path is read-only

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/reconcile/templates/dropin.tmpl:84 MemoryDenyWriteExecute={{ .MDWE }}; packaging/systemd/gvm@.service:88 ProtectSystem=full. (orig: vrising.yaml:58-63, satisfactory.yaml:51-56, enshrouded.yaml:47-52 and ark-se.yaml:51-56 all set env {HOME} only, plus arch: native and jit: false. wrapper_proton.go:58-96 adds no environment.

Proton (ValveSoftware/Proton proton_9.0, `proton`, __main__) does: `if "STEAM_COMPAT_DATA_PATH" not in os.environ: log("No compat data path?"); sys.exit(1)`. setup_prefix() reads `os.environ["STEAM_COMPAT_CLIENT_INSTALL_PATH"]` unguarded (KeyError). Proton.__init__ does `self.dist_lock = FileLock(self.path("dist.lock"))` and builds files/share/default_pfx, both inside Proton's own directory.

satisfactory.yaml:103 and ark-se.yaml:136 suggest /usr/share/steam/compatibilitytools.d/GE-Proton9-27/proton. That path is root-owned and read-only under ProtectSystem=full (gvm@.service:88).

units.go:596-601 renders MemoryDenyWriteExecute=yes for jit:false. Wine's PE loader mprotects image sections to PROT_EXEC and services VirtualAlloc(PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE), both of which MDWE refuses.

units.go:557-570: arch native emits SystemCallArchitectures=native. Per gvm's own note, that kills every i386 syscall with SIGSYS, and classic-WoW64 Proton 9 builds run 32-bit helpers during prefix init.)

**Impact.** An operator who follows the var help text and starts V Rising, Enshrouded, Satisfactory or ARK gets an immediate exit 1 with 'Proton: No compat data path?' in console.log. Past that, it fails on a read-only dist.lock, a W^X fault in Wine, or SIGSYS. Nothing in gvm explains any of these four layers.

**Suggestion.** For each windows-depot manifest: set env STEAM_COMPAT_DATA_PATH to a per-instance path outside the backup include set (for example "{{ .Home }}/compatdata"), unless that title's world lives inside the prefix, and STEAM_COMPAT_CLIENT_INSTALL_PATH to /opt/gvm/steamcmd. Set jit: true and arch: i386 (renders 'native x86'), each confirmed on a scratch boot, with the drop-in note saying Wine's PE loader needs W^X off. Change the proton_path help to require a per-instance Proton copy owned by gvm-<inst> (for example under {{ .Work }}/proton) and say plainly that a Proton tree shared between instances must not be writable by any instance user. Add `gvm game validate` warnings for wrapper.kind=proton combined with jit:false, with arch native, or with no STEAM_COMPAT_DATA_PATH in env.

### game-runtime-compat-4 — Unity/Mono servers (Valheim, Rust, 7DTD x2, Core Keeper) run under MemoryDenyWriteExecute=yes, which Mono's JIT cannot survive

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: 32 → **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** jit: false in valheim.yaml:53, rust.yaml:66, 7daystodie.yaml:54, 7daystodie-telnet.yaml:45 and corekeeper.yaml:52. units.go:596-601 maps that to MemoryDenyWriteExecute=yes. ARCHITECTURE.md:1249 shows Core Keeper rendering exactly that.

The jit knob is documented as JVM-only:
- manifest.go:260-262: "Every Java title needs W^X off".
- units.go:608-617 mdweNote: "the JVM JIT needs W^X off"; for false: "the engine compiles nothing at runtime."
- generic-steam.yaml:72: "jit: false # true for anything JVM-based".

All five servers ship a Mono runtime (*_Data/MonoBleedingEdge). Rust's Oxide/Carbon and 7DTD's Harmony mods JIT-patch at runtime. Mono's code manager allocates RWX chunks, which systemd's MDWE filter refuses.

**Impact.** These servers are expected to crash during boot (SIGSEGV/SIGABRT, or Mono 'mono_valloc' failures). The operator sees a Unity crash banner in console.log with nothing pointing at MemoryDenyWriteExecute, and the generated drop-in comment actively claims the engine 'compiles nothing at runtime'. Valheim, the most common friends-and-community game, is in this set.

**Suggestion.** Set jit: true in valheim, rust, 7daystodie and 7daystodie-telnet after a scratch-boot confirmation. Decide Core Keeper by whether its install tree has *_Data/MonoBleedingEdge (Mono) or il2cpp_data (no JIT). Rewrite the comments in manifest.go:260-262, units.go mdweNote and generic-steam.yaml:72 to 'true for any engine that generates code at runtime: JVM, Mono/Unity-Mono, .NET, Wine/Proton, LuaJIT'. The jit:false line should read 'the manifest declares the engine compiles nothing at runtime' rather than stating it as fact. Do not add a jit_reason field. Add one doctor check: an install tree containing *_Data/MonoBleedingEdge or *_Data/Managed/mscorlib.dll under a pinned manifest with jit:false warns with the one-line fix.

### game-runtime-compat-5 — A game that dies during boot shows as 'starting' for stall_timeout, then loops forever with a mislabelled exit code
