**High** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4, 6 · Plan: 27 → **28** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/run.go:473-488 (not internal/runner/run.go); internal/cli/run.go:574-579 reportExit; internal/runner/process_linux.go:201-202 (signalled -> Code -1); internal/runner/runner.go:612 (ring closed only at teardown). (orig: readiness.go:172-199: Await selects on Stop, fail markers, bound and probes. There is no case for the child exiting. r.Wait is only called after readiness (run.go:498).

After the stall (valheim.yaml:58 stall_timeout 300s; satisfactory/ark 600s), run.go:484-488 exits reportExit(rep, ExitTempFail). A signalled child has Code -1 (process_linux.go:201-202), which falls back to 75. derive.go:517-518 renders 75 as "an orphaned game process from a previous run is still alive" with hint `gvm status` (derive.go:439-442).

gvm@.service:16-17 sets StartLimitIntervalSec=600 / StartLimitBurst=5 and :55 sets RestartSec=20. A ~330 s cycle allows at most 2 starts per window, so the limit never trips.

During all of this, systemd reports ActiveState=activating, and derive.go:284-288 shows PhaseStarting with 'waiting for readiness probe ... last output Xm ago' and no hint.)

**Impact.** This is the typical failure mode of findings 3, 4 and 10 (a crash in the first seconds). The operator and the dashboard-user see 'starting' indefinitely. The game died minutes ago, and the restart loop runs for days. When a reason is shown it points at a non-existent orphan.

**Suggestion.** In the runner, start a watcher on the already-broadcast osProcess.Wait before AwaitReady and add a select case that returns ErrExitedDuringBoot{status}. Keep the existing path that calls r.Shutdown after a readiness failure, so a non-empty cgroup still gets the full ladder and an empty one reports RungNone. Exit with a new code (for example 69, 'the game exited before it was ready') that is NOT added to RestartPreventExitStatus, so StartLimitBurst ends the loop. Put the exit status and the last console lines in STATUS=, with hint `gvm logs <name>`. Add the SIGSYS note (suggest arch: i386) and the SIGSEGV/SIGABRT-within-60s-under-MDWE note (suggest jit: true), worded as 'suspected'. Change exitMeaning(75) to 'readiness failed (timeout/stall/marker), or an orphan from a previous run'. Have derive show 'crash-looping' when SubState is auto-restart and the last run ended before ready. Derive stays read-only.

### game-runtime-compat-6 — Core Keeper's xvfb wrapper is not implemented: every start exits 64, and the hint sends the operator to `gvm apply --diff`

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 2, 4, 7 · Plan: **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/runner/config.go:220-222; internal/runner/runner.go:209; internal/cli/run.go:617; internal/spec/capability.go:536-545 shows 'runs under xvfb' as Available. (orig: corekeeper.yaml:31 has `wrapper: {kind: xvfb, args: ["-a"]}`. config.go:221-222 returns "runner: spec.runtime.wrapper (kind %q) is not implemented in this build". run.go:348-349 and run.go:615 turn that into Check "config", Code ExitUsage (64), which RestartPreventExitStatus stops (gvm@.service:59). derive.go:426-428 gives hint "gvm apply --diff <name>", and derive.go:509-510 gives meaning "spec invalid, or manifest pin mismatch".

`gvm.exe game validate games/corekeeper.yaml` -> "0 errors, 0 warnings", although ARCHITECTURE.md:3137-3138 promises "! spec.runtime.wrapper.kind=xvfb requires package `xvfb`". spec/manifest.go:267-269 never validates Wrapper.Kind. `gvm game list` lists corekeeper like any other game.

The '-a' flag belongs to xvfb-run, a /bin/sh script (rule 2).)

**Impact.** The operator creates, installs and applies Core Keeper successfully, and the first start fails permanently. `gvm status` blames the spec or manifest pin, and `gvm apply --diff` shows nothing to change. The real sentence is only in `systemctl status`.

**Suggestion.** Short term: add a wrapperKinds registry in spec/validate.go (proton implemented; xvfb and wine planned) so `gvm game validate` and `gvm create --game corekeeper` warn "Core Keeper needs the xvfb wrapper, which this build does not run". Add a 'needs' column to `gvm game list`. Give the BuildLaunch wrapper refusal its own Failure Check ("wrapper") and its own derive hint rather than 64's pin message. First test whether current Core Keeper builds boot under -batchmode -nographics without a display. If a wrapper is still needed: spawn /usr/bin/Xvfb directly (no shell) with -displayfd so it picks a free display, plus -nolisten tcp, in its OWN process group inside the unit cgroup. Start it before the game, set DISPLAY, and stop it only after the ladder has finished. Add a doctor check naming `apt install xvfb` when it is missing.

### game-runtime-compat-8 — Satisfactory and ARK: SE are forced onto Proton though native Linux servers exist; Satisfactory's 1.0 port model is wrong

**High** · player · docs · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** satisfactory.yaml:43-50 args, :64 a2s on query, :75-82 ports; ark-se.yaml:43-50 split ?-args. (orig: satisfactory.yaml:9-11 and ark-se.yaml:9-11 claim "no native Linux dedicated server binary".
- satisfactory.wiki.gg/Dedicated_servers: 64-bit Linux binaries exist for app 1690800, launched with ./FactoryServer.sh. As of 1.0, "port numbers 15000 and 15777 are no longer used". Ports are 7777/TCP (server + HTTPS API), 7777/UDP (game + Lightweight Query) and 8888/TCP (reliable messaging), with flags -Port= and -ReliablePort=.
- ark.wiki.gg: "ARK: Survival Evolved supports both Windows and Linux natively", binary ShooterGame/Binaries/Linux/ShooterGameServer.

satisfactory.yaml:47-49 passes -BeaconPort/-ServerQueryPort and declares beacon 15000/udp and query 15777/udp, all public (75-82). The a2s probe targets the query port (64). The sentinel at :32 is Engine/Binaries/Win64/UnrealEditor-Cmd.exe, an editor binary name worth verifying against the depot.)

**Impact.** Players cannot add or join the Satisfactory server: the firewall opens two dead UDP ports and never opens 7777/TCP or 8888/TCP. Readiness waits on a port that never answers. Both titles carry all of Proton's failure modes and its extra RAM and disk cost for no reason.

**Suggestion.** Ship native manifests.

Satisfactory: platform: linux; exec ./Engine/Binaries/Linux/FactoryServer-Linux-Shipping, or FactoryServer.sh if it execs, with process_group set accordingly. Ports: game 7777 proto both, reliable 8888 tcp. Args: -Port={{ .Port.game }} -ReliablePort={{ .Port.reliable }}. Readiness: udp_probe or log_marker rather than a2s.

ARK: platform: linux; exec ShooterGame/Binaries/Linux/ShooterGameServer, same ?-args.

Keep the Proton variants only as explicitly named `-proton` manifests if anyone needs them. Correct the `doc:` blocks so the dashboard's game page does not repeat the wrong claim.

### game-runtime-compat-7 — For Satisfactory and Enshrouded the world is not where backups, restore and rebuild look

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: 32 → **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** satisfactory.yaml:86-91 and enshrouded.yaml:80-86 backup blocks; enshrouded.yaml:44-46 args contain only -savedir (no port args); recommended_jobs backup at satisfactory.yaml:112-113 and enshrouded.yaml:107-108; internal/archive/create.go:347-350. (orig: Satisfactory:
- satisfactory.yaml:17-19 says saves live under %LOCALAPPDATA%/FactoryGame/... inside the Proton prefix and "The data path should point at the Proton prefix".
- Its args (43-50) pass no save directory, and no STEAM_COMPAT_DATA_PATH is set.
- backup include/world_paths are "{{ .Data }}" (86-91).

Enshrouded:
- enshrouded.yaml:44-46 passes "-savedir {{ .Data }}", but the server's documented configuration is enshrouded_server.json in the install root (saveDirectory, gamePort, queryPort; see vendor guides). The manifest has no config_files at all; validate prints "0 config files".
- Backups target "{{ .Data }}" (80-86).

archive/create.go:347-350 fails a backup whose sources match no files.)

**Impact.** The recommended nightly backup job (created by `gvm create`) either fails every night with 'the sources matched no files', or archives the wrong tree, while the real world sits in the Wine prefix or the install directory. Restore and rebuild operate on the wrong path. The operator believes the world is protected until they need a restore.

**Suggestion.** Satisfactory: go native (finding 8). Keep saves inside {{ .Data }} by setting XDG_CONFIG_HOME="{{ .Data }}/config" if a scratch boot shows UE honours it. Otherwise point include and world_paths at the exact {{ .Home }}/.config/Epic/FactoryGame/Saved/SaveGames directory. Enshrouded: add a config_files json_path entry on {{ .Install }}/enshrouded_server.json owning saveDirectory="{{ .Data }}/savegame", gamePort and queryPort, drop -savedir, and set world_paths to that save directory. In general: when an archive fails for no files, `gvm backup` should name each empty world_path. `gvm doctor <inst>` should flag a world_path still empty 24 h after the first ready. `gvm destroy` should refuse, before asking for typed confirmation, when every world_path is empty but the instance has reached ready.

### game-runtime-compat-9 — No preflight, create or doctor check for Proton, python3 or Xvfb; a hidden Proton path yields 'install is incomplete' and a dead-end `gvm doctor` hint

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 7, 12 · Plan: 32 → **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/cli/run.go:413; internal/runner/preflight.go:196-201; internal/reconcile/templates/dropin.tmpl:23,48. (orig: vrising.yaml:131-135, satisfactory.yaml:99-103 and enshrouded.yaml:94-98 declare proton_path as type: string with only help text. create.go:258-297 accepts any value.

run.go:413 passes Exec: r.Launch().Path, which is the Proton path. preflight.go:199-201 reports "<path> does not exist; the install is incomplete or the manifest's exec is wrong" with exit 65. derive.go:429-432 hints "gvm doctor <name>".

The doctor check summaries (grep 'Summary:' internal/doctor) cover Postgres, the firewall, Caddy, the manifest pin, MemoryMax, TimeoutStopSec and workshop trees. Nothing covers proton, python3, Xvfb or lib32gcc.

units.go:146-166: the sandbox's ProtectHome decision considers only install/data/logs/work. A Proton installed in the usual guide locations (~/.steam/root/compatibilitytools.d under /home or /root) is invisible under ProtectHome=yes, so the operator `ls`-es a file that preflight says is missing.

No doc or command explains where to get GE-Proton or that it must be writable by the instance user (it writes dist.lock and default_pfx into its own directory).)

**Impact.** The Proton onboarding journey has no guide rails. Every mistake (wrong path, path under /home, root-owned /usr path, no python3) surfaces only at first start, as a message that blames the install, with a hint pointing at a doctor that has nothing to say.

**Suggestion.** Do now: (1) At `gvm create` and `gvm apply`, check proton_path is absolute, exists and is executable, and that its shebang interpreter exists. Extend the D-15 transient write-probe to stat the wrapper binary and its interpreter as gvm-<inst> under the real sandbox, and to test that the wrapper's directory is writable. Report by name 'hidden by ProtectHome=yes', 'not writable by gvm-<inst>: Proton writes dist.lock next to itself', or 'shared with another instance'. (2) Add doctor checks for proton_path (inside the sandbox), python3 (proton wrappers), Xvfb (xvfb wrappers) and lib32gcc-s1 (steamcmd), each naming the apt command and installing nothing. (3) Give preflight a 'wrapper' check whose message names spec.runtime.wrapper. Defer item 4. If built later, it must be a pinned, digest-verified provider landing in a per-instance tree, with its egress host declared. Write docs/proton.md now.

### game-runtime-compat-10 — 7 Days to Die's startserver.sh sends the Unity log to a file, so the console is empty and the 'StartGame done' marker never arrives

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 2 · Plan: **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** 7daystodie.yaml:38 and 7daystodie-telnet.yaml:33 set exec: ./startserver.sh. 7daystodie.yaml:18-19 says it "launches 7DaysToDieServer.x86_64 as a child without exec". The distributed startserver.sh ends with `./7DaysToDieServer.x86_64 -logfile $SERVERDIR/7DaysToDieServer_Data/output_log__<date>.txt -quit -batchmode -nographics -dedicated $PARAMS` (community copy found via web search; verify against the installed file).

Readiness relies on console output: progress markers at :60, log_marker "StartGame done" at :62, and stall_timeout 600s at :59 measured on console bytes (readiness.go:297-317).

No manifest passes a Unity -logFile option. The Windows-Unity title under Proton (V Rising) writes Player.log into the prefix's AppData/LocalLow rather than stdout, so its fail_markers (vrising.yaml:72-74) are never seen either.

**Impact.** With a2s fixed, 7DTD still fails readiness after 10 minutes of 'no console output' and restarts in a loop, while the server may actually be up. `gvm console`, the dashboard console and the fail markers show nothing. A new timestamped log file accumulates in the install tree on every boot.

**Suggestion.** Exec the engine directly and drop the launcher shell script:
- exec: ./7DaysToDieServer.x86_64
- args: -logfile - (Unity's 'log to stdout'), -quit, -batchmode, -nographics, -dedicated, then the existing -configfile and -UserDataFolder.
- env: LD_LIBRARY_PATH: "{{ .Install }}".
- process_group: false.

For V Rising add -logFile - so Wine forwards the Unity log to the pipe. In generic-steam.yaml, document 'make the engine log to stdout' as a requirement for log_marker/stall readiness.

### game-runtime-compat-11 — RCON, admin and join passwords go on argv, which every local account can read in /proc; the unit has no ProtectProc

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: 27 → **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Secrets rendered into argv:
- vrising.yaml:56-57 -rconPassword {{ .Secret.rcon_password }}
- ark-se.yaml:44 ?ServerAdminPassword=...
- rust.yaml:56-57 +rcon.password
- cs2.yaml:43-44 +sv_setsteamaccount (GSLT) and :57-58 +rcon_password
- valheim.yaml:41-42 and corekeeper.yaml:43-44 -password

gvm@.service:85-105 sets no ProtectProc=. units.go:390-395 keeps secrets out of Environment= because /proc/<pid>/environ is readable, but /proc/<pid>/cmdline is world-readable (0444, versus 0400 for environ). config.go:151-156 knows argv carries secrets and only redacts the console annotation.

The json_path codec (validate.go:157) and owned_lines exist for config-file alternatives.

**Impact.** On a box that runs several servers as separate gvm-<inst> users, one compromised mod or plugin in any game can `cat /proc/*/cmdline` and read every other server's RCON or admin password and the CS2 GSLT. That defeats the per-instance user separation the design is built on.

**Suggestion.** Add ProtectProc=invisible to the package-owned gvm@.service (systemd ≥247; Debian 11 and Ubuntu 22.04 qualify), so each game sees only its own uid's processes. Move secrets out of argv where the game allows it:
- V Rising: config_files json_path on ServerHostSettings.json Rcon.Password.
- Rust: owned_lines on server/<identity>/cfg/server.cfg rcon.password.
- CS2: owned_lines on game/csgo/cfg/server.cfg rcon_password.
- ARK: ServerAdminPassword in GameUserSettings.ini [ServerSettings].

Have `gvm game validate` warn: "spec.runtime.args[N] places .Secret.X on the command line, readable in /proc/<pid>/cmdline; prefer a config_files key".

### game-runtime-compat-12 — `gvm game try --scratch`, the advertised way to prove a manifest boots, does not exist, and validate checks nothing about runtime compatibility

**Medium** · manifest-author · missing-feature · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 5, 7 · Plan: **32** · Status: open

**Evidence.** The command is referenced in generic-steam.yaml:20-23 ("`gvm game try <id> --scratch` runs all three against a throwaway instance"), corekeeper.yaml:60 and 7daystodie.yaml:62 ("# VERIFY via `gvm game try`"), and ARCHITECTURE.md:3145-3169.

`gvm.exe game try corekeeper --scratch` -> "gvm game: unknown subcommand \"try\"" (rc=1). `gvm game` lists only validate, list, which and hooks.

Validate passed every manifest above (all '0 errors, 0 warnings' except Rust's websocket_rcon warning), despite an unbuildable wrapper, unbuildable probes and Proton without env. ladder_manifests_test.go:12-56 tests that every shipped LADDER renders, but nothing tests BuildLaunch or BuildProbes over games.FS.

**Impact.** A manifest author adding a Unity, Wine or 32-bit title has no way to learn, short of a real VPS and a failing unit, that jit, arch, env or the probe kind is wrong. The shipped catalogue shows the cost: most manifests were written, and validated green, without ever booting under the unit.

**Suggestion.** Phase 1: add a non-spawning launch check (`gvm run <inst> --check`, or `gvm doctor <inst> --launch`). It builds launch, probes and ladder, prints the redacted argv, env and rendered sandbox (MDWE, SystemCallArchitectures, ProtectHome), and stats exec, wrapper and interpreter as gvm-<inst> through the D-15 transient probe. Add the validate rules: wrapper kinds, probe kinds the runner builds, proton with jit:false or arch native, .Secret on argv. Add TestEveryShippedManifestBuildsLaunchAndProbes. Remove or mark the `gvm game try` references in the manifests and ARCHITECTURE.md as planned. Phase 2: `gvm game try <id> --scratch` creates an instance carrying a scratch marker and a reserved name prefix, with every port expose: none (no firewall rule), and runs it in a transient unit with the generated drop-in. It reports READY in Ns / exited before ready / SIGSYS or W^X suspected, then stops it through the ladder. Removing it either goes through the normal `gvm destroy` with typed confirmation, or uses a scratch-only removal that refuses any instance lacking the marker created by that same invocation.

### game-runtime-compat-13 — CS2 omits steam.link_sdk, which gvm's own shim code says leaves a server invisible to the browser

**Low** · player · not-wired · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · Plan: **42** · Status: open

**Evidence.** cs2.yaml has no `hooks:` block. valheim.yaml:94, corekeeper.yaml:78, palworld.yaml:152 and 7daystodie.yaml:151 all list steam.link_sdk. provider/steamcmd/shims.go:155-161 explains why: "SteamCMD does not place steamclient.so where SteamAPI_Init looks for it; without this the server prints [S_API FAIL] and is invisible to the server browser". cs2.yaml:14-16 relies on a GSLT login, which needs a working SteamAPI.

**Impact.** Players cannot find the CS2 server in the community browser, and the GSLT login (sv_setsteamaccount) cannot succeed. The server remains reachable only by direct IP, with a Steam error the operator has no context for.

**Suggestion.** Add `hooks: {post_install: [steam.home_layout, steam.link_sdk]}` to cs2.yaml and bump its revision. Add a doctor/validate rule: a steamcmd-provider manifest whose binary is not Rust-style self-contained (no steamclient.so in the install tree) and that lacks steam.link_sdk gets a warning naming the hook.


---

<a id="gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall"></a>
## Upgrading gvm itself, and removing it cleanly (`gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall`)

For an operator, upgrading gvm and removing it are both unbuilt and undocumented. There is no .deb, no install target, no postinst/prerm and no gvmd/gvm-web/gvm-db-migrate/gvm.target units. An upgrade today means copying a binary over the file every live runner is executing, then syncing eight unit files by hand. After a binary swap, every live world keeps running the old runner and the old drop-in, and nothing in status, list, doctor or the dashboard says so. The on-disk and wire formats are shared between the old runner and the new CLI and jobs, and they use exact-match version checks with no skew policy. The first release that bumps any of them would break `gvm stop`, silently degrade nightly backups, let an old runner thaw a new backup's freeze in the middle of a snapshot, or turn a live update's lease into a "failed" instance whose suggested fix deletes the lease. Removal has no command or runbook. `destroy` quietly leaves secrets, accounts and state behind, and there is no supported path to stop everything safely, remove units, timers, users, tmpfiles and Postgres roles, and keep the backups. The safety foundations are sound (manifest pinning, restart refusing during a live job lease, the tag-owned firewall reconciler, self-describing archives), so most of the fix is wiring and policy rather than redesign.

**Strengths to preserve**

- D-17 pinning holds across a binary swap: buildApply renders from the instance's PINNED manifest, never the catalog (internal/cli/apply.go:76-87), so a new gvm cannot silently change a live world's argv, env or destroy globs.
- Package-owned gvm@.service plus a generated drop-in (packaging/systemd/gvm@.service:3-7) means a template fix reaches every instance after daemon-reload, and D-14's missing PartOf=gvm.target means a package upgrade cannot restart worlds by accident.
- Converged applies are empty: SameDropIn strips the '# rendered <time> by gvm <ver>' line (internal/reconcile/actions.go:113-133, units.go:699-713), so `gvm apply --diff` after an upgrade would show only real rendering changes. That makes it a good base for a host-wide re-render check.
- `gvm restart` is a single systemctl restart (internal/lifecycle/verbs.go:90-93). It uses SIGTERM and the runner's own ladder, so it works across console-protocol skew, and Allowed() refuses it while any job lease is live (verbs.go:180-187). That makes it a safe primitive for a rolling restart onto a new runner.
- The D-31 freeze watchdog lives in the runner and has a fallback thaw built from the runner's own manifest (internal/cli/runfreeze.go:96-133), so a job SIGKILLed mid-freeze during an upgrade does not leave a world unsaved.
- Archives are self-describing: the manifest records gvm_version, gvm_commit, the redacted spec YAML and the content lock (internal/archive/manifest.go:23-39). That is exactly what is needed to rebuild after an uninstall and reinstall.
- The firewall reconciler already has the invariants an uninstall needs: SSH rules are never deleted even when gvm-tagged, deletions are suppressed when the live ruleset is not trusted (D-42), and ownership comes from the tag (internal/firewall/reconcile.go:28-35). The nft backend owns one table and has an idempotent Disable (internal/firewall/nft/nft.go:72).
- Instance accounts carry GECOS 'gvm instance <name>' (internal/hostops/user.go:220-229), so an uninstall can tell what gvm created apart from an adopted account like pzserver.
- The destroy chain is the right shape to reuse for removal: verified archive required, typed name, space check before the prompt, and timers and units disabled before their files are removed (internal/cli/destroy.go:142-232).
- Transport refusals are clear and name the fix (`sudo gvm stop` or --remote/GVM_TOKEN), and doctor runs and says so when Postgres is down.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-1 — No supported way to get a bug-fix release onto a box with live worlds

**High** · operator · missing-feature · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: 23 → 35 → **52** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Makefile:28-89 (targets include build-arm64, test-short, fmt-check, shellcheck); docs/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md has 181 '- [ ]' and 0 '- [x]'; internal/cli/root.go:101-102 gvmd/web Run: notImplemented; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:4675 gvm-db-migrate.service also hard-codes ExecStart=/usr/local/bin/gvm (orig: packaging/ contains only packaging.go, packaging_test.go, systemd/ (8 files) and tmpfiles/. The nfpm.yaml, postinstall.sh, prerm.sh, gvmd.service, gvm-web.service, gvm-db-migrate.service and gvm-spool.* promised by IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:608-611 and ARCHITECTURE.md:451-459 do not exist, and the Phase 12 checkbox for `make deb` (IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:624) is unchecked. Makefile targets: build, build-arm64, build-native, test, cover, vet, fmt, tidy, check, clean, help. There is no install, deb or release target. `git tag` prints nothing, so VERSION falls back to `git describe` = 'b69119b'. Every unit hard-codes the binary the runners are executing: gvm@.service:23-24 (ExecStartPre=+/usr/local/bin/gvm run-prepare %i, ExecStart=/usr/local/bin/gvm run %i), gvm-job@*.service:16/32, reconcile/probe.go:30 and runner/screencompat.go:39. packaging.FS is embedded, but the only reader is doctor's HOST-010 (internal/cli/doctor.go:47), so no command writes the units to disk. ARCHITECTURE.md:124 says packaging will `systemctl restart gvm.target` on upgrade, but no gvm.target unit exists anywhere (grep finds only comments).)

**Impact.** An operator with live worlds has no runbook. The obvious move (`cp gvm /usr/local/bin/gvm` or scp) fails with 'Text file busy' because every gvm@ runner is executing that file. If they get past that, they must find and copy eight unit files and the tmpfiles fragment by hand, then run daemon-reload. Forgetting either half leaves the binary and units mismatched, and nothing detects it (see finding 12). Debian policy also forbids a .deb from shipping /usr/local/bin, so the paths baked into the units conflict with the packaging plan.

**Suggestion.** Make the nfpm .deb the one supported install path: /usr/bin/gvm plus package-owned units in /usr/lib/systemd/system with ExecStart=/usr/bin/gvm. Ship no /usr/local/bin symlink. Instead, postinst and a doctor HOST check warn loudly when a real /usr/local/bin/gvm exists, because it shadows /usr/bin in PATH. Write the maintainer scripts by hand, without dh_installsystemd defaults. On upgrade they never stop, restart or try-restart gvm@*.service or gvm-job@*.service, because dpkg's rename-replace is safe under live runners. postinst steps: (1) systemd-tmpfiles --create /usr/lib/tmpfiles.d/gvm.conf; (2) daemon-reload; (3) enable gvm-guard.timer and the _verify timer on first install only, so an operator who disabled them is respected; (4) try-restart only gvmd.service and gvm-web.service, by name; (5) systemctl start --no-block gvm-db-migrate.service; (6) print the local, DB-free `gvm upgrade status` (no admin.sock, no Postgres) and exit 0 whatever it reports. Delete the 'systemctl restart gvm.target' sentence from D-14 rather than adding a target. For tarball installs, `gvm self-install` copies to /usr/bin/gvm.new and uses rename(2). It writes packaging.FS to the same /usr/lib paths, runs systemd-tmpfiles and daemon-reload through exec (never a shell), and touches no gvm@ unit. docs/upgrading.md documents exactly this.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-5 — A new gvm that renders different drop-ins or timers is invisible: drift compares only the spec sha

**High** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: **35** · Status: open

**Evidence.** Drift = DropIn.SpecSHA256 != SpecSHA256 (internal/lifecycle/status.go:103-108). The drop-in header records the generator ('rendered <ts> by gvm <ver>'), and ParseDropInHeader parses it into DropInHeader.Version (internal/lifecycle/observe.go:136-152, 562-571), but grep finds no reader of DropIn.Version. Doctor's drift check INST-005 always returns Skip 'requires Postgres mirror (Phase 14)' (internal/doctor/checks_inst.go:107-115), and doctor loads no instances anyway (`func doctorLoadInstances() []spec.Instance { return nil }`, internal/cli/doctor.go:150-151). apply takes exactly one instance (`gvm apply --all` errors with 'flag provided but not defined: -all'). printApplyReport (internal/cli/diff.go:68-85) never says the new drop-in only takes effect at the next restart. A real case in this repo: spec.egress has existed since Phase 1 (`git show 5c467d6:internal/spec/instance.go` line 159), but the drop-in only started rendering IPAddressDeny/IPAddressAllow in Phase 19 (`git show 23d73e5 -- internal/reconcile/templates/dropin.tmpl`; 0 egress mentions in units.go at 5c467d6).

**Impact.** An operator who declared `spec.egress.policy: allowlist` and then upgraded to the Phase 19 build has no allowlist in force. `gvm list` shows the server healthy with no drift, and it stays that way until they happen to run `gvm apply` and then restart. In general, any rendering fix shipped in a release is silently not applied.

**Suggestion.** Do the full in-memory re-render plus SameDropIn comparison only where the cost is acceptable, and only through the pure planning path (no ProbeWritable, no ports.lock write, no DB): `gvm status <n>`, `gvm apply --all --diff`, a real DB-free INST-005, and `gvm upgrade status`. For `gvm list`, use a cheap signal: the drop-in header's version differs from this build, or a RenderGeneration constant written into the header differs. Golden-unit tests force that constant to change whenever template output changes. Show 'apply suggested'. Classify the diff. When changed lines touch sandbox or egress directives (IPAddressDeny/Allow, Protect*, ReadWritePaths, RestrictAddressFamilies), raise a warning, e.g. 'declared egress allowlist is NOT in force'; otherwise a note. `gvm apply --all` runs each instance's normal plan serially and refuses destructive steps without that instance's typed name. It never restarts a game unit, and after each changed running instance it prints '-> takes effect at next restart: gvm restart pz-main (or gvm restart --pending)'.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-2 — Old runner + new CLI/jobs: console and freeze-lease formats are exact-match with no skew policy

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 4 · Plan: 20 → **35** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/runner/status.go:233-246 (ReadStatus exact-match, unused); internal/cli/jobengines.go:352-354 (dial failure takes the same 'no console' path); internal/socket/client.go:129 Build() has no callers (orig: internal/console/protocol.go:14-18 justifies Proto=1 because 'there is exactly one client and one server and they ship in the same binary'. After any binary swap that is false: the runner keeps the old binary in memory, while the CLI and timer-launched jobs (gvm-job@.service:16) run the new one. internal/socket/client.go:196-199 hard-fails Hello on any mismatch ('socket: runner speaks console protocol %d, this build speaks %d'), and dialError (internal/cli/console.go:614-626) adds no hint. Consequences in code: (1) `gvm stop` writes /run/gvm/<n>/hold BEFORE calling the console ladder (internal/lifecycle/manager.go:233-245; consoleShutdown internal/cli/stop.go:49-57). The stop fails, the server keeps running, and the hold is left behind. (2) Nightly backups: freezeChannels swallows the failed hello (internal/cli/jobengines.go:363-366), and the freeze rung records 'this game captures no console, so the rung could not be attempted' (internal/backup/freeze.go:390-393), a wrong reason, then archives a DEGRADED, unsaved world every night. (3) freeze.lease: backup.ParseLease requires Schema == LeaseSchema (internal/backup/lease.go:64, 258-261). The OLD runner treats a newer-schema lease as DecisionUnreadable and thaws immediately from its fallback (lease.go:661-675) within one 5 s poll (DefaultWatchInterval, lease.go:611). That sends save-on in the middle of the NEW job's snapshot. status.json, by contrast, is read with no proto check at all (internal/lifecycle/observe.go:409-428). That makes three different policies for one problem.)

**Impact.** The first release that bumps either format leaves the operator unable to stop a server with `gvm stop`, given a misleading error and a stray hold. Every un-restarted world silently gets degraded or torn nightly backups. The archive is labelled with the wrong cause, so the operator has no reason to suspect the upgrade.

**Suggestion.** Adopt the N-1 skew rule for console.Proto and freeze LeaseSchema, with a cross-version test, as suggested. Add lease_schema and gvm_version to the hello event; an absent field means 1. On a hello or proto mismatch, `gvm stop` falls back to hostops VerbUnitStop (systemctl stop gvm@<n>). That sends SIGTERM to the old runner, which under KillMode=mixed walks its own save-and-stop ladder, so this is not a kill. Read the rung back from shutdown.jsonl and print 'stopped via systemd: pz-main still ran gvm 0.3.1's runner (protocol 1); its ladder saved at 40s'. Keep the hold-before-ladder order, which is what keeps the guard away, but remove the hold if the stop fails before the unit stop is issued. Freeze rules: (1) never arm a lease in a schema the runner did not advertise; (2) if the runner advertises an older schema the writer still supports, write that one; (3) otherwise skip the freeze and archive DEGRADED with the true reason, e.g. 'pz-main's runner (gvm 0.3.1) cannot read this freeze lease; `gvm restart pz-main` re-enables saved backups'. The degraded note must carry the actual hello error, never 'captures no console'. Give status.json the same rule: a newer proto means 'fields may be missing', not fatal.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-3 — An upgrade mid-job turns a LIVE lease into 'failed' and `gvm job clear` deletes it without --force

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort S · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 6 · Plan: **35** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/lifecycle/verbs.go:197-199 ('a job died holding this instance' for LeaseUnreadable); internal/cli/job.go:655 usage text; internal/instance/paths.go:140-152 and 238-262 (runner lock is not the job lock) (orig: internal/lifecycle/lease.go:30-34 (LeaseProto=1, 'A document from a future gvm is refused') and :158-161 reject any other proto as ErrLease. The observer then sets LeaseUnreadable (observe.go:488-496). Derive maps that to phase 'failed', reason '... cannot tell whether a job is running', hint 'gvm job clear <n>' (internal/lifecycle/derive.go:176-182). In runJobClear (internal/cli/job.go:688-705) the only LIVE-lease refusal is `case o.LeaseState == lifecycle.LeaseLive && !force`, and an unreadable lease never classifies as Live. So `gvm job clear` removes it with no --force and no check that the holder unit or pid is alive. The lease's own doc comment (lease.go:21-25) says treating an unreadable lease as absent 'is how two updaters end up writing the same install tree'.)

**Impact.** Suppose an operator upgrades while the 05:00 update job (old binary) holds pz-main and a later gvm changed LeaseProto. `gvm list` reports pz-main as failed. The printed fix deletes the lease of a job that is still running, and `gvm start` then races SteamCMD writing the install tree. That is exactly the collision the lease exists to prevent, reached by following gvm's own advice.

**Suggestion.** Make liveness version-independent. Decode pid, boot_id, unit and started_at with a minimal struct before the proto check, and classify live or stale from those whatever the proto. Reserve 'unreadable' for files that are truly corrupt. In `gvm job clear`, refuse an unreadable lease unless the named unit is inactive and the pid is dead or on another boot, and otherwise require --force. Change the hint to 'lease.json was written by a different gvm version; its holder gvm-job@pz-main_nightly.service is active. Wait for it (`journalctl -u ...`).' Add the old-proto-lease and live-holder case to derive_test.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-4 — Nothing tells the operator which worlds still run the old runner, and there is no safe fleet restart

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 6 · Plan: **35** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/socket/client.go:129 (Build() unused); internal/cli/root.go:102 (web notImplemented) (orig: runner.json (RunnerFile, internal/runner/preflight.go:528-541) and status.json (internal/runner/status.go:31-49) record no gvm version or commit. The only build stamp is the hello frame's Build = version.Commit (internal/cli/run.go:340), which is 'unknown' on a plain build, and nothing compares it. lifecycle.Status (internal/lifecycle/status.go:22-72) has no runner-version or restart-pending field. D-14 promises 'Stop everything is `gvm stop --all`', but `gvm stop --all` prints 'flag provided but not defined: -all'. `gvm apply --all` gives the same error, and `gvm restart --help` shows no --all and no --wait. oneInstance (internal/cli/instance.go:553-565) accepts exactly one name.)

**Impact.** After an upgrade, the bug fix the operator installed (a ladder fix, a freeze fix) is not active on any running world, and gvm gives no indication. The operator must remember to restart each server, one command at a time, guessing which ones need it. A moderator on the dashboard cannot tell either.

**Suggestion.** Record gvm_version, gvm_commit, console_proto and lease_schema in runner.json and status.json as additive fields. On the root CLI, also use the '/proc/<MainPID>/exe (deleted)' or inode-mismatch check, which works even when the version stamps say 'dev'. The dashboard badge reads the status.json fields, because gvm-web cannot readlink another account's /proc entry. Surface 'restart pending: runner gvm X, installed Y' in `gvm list`, `gvm status`, and one local, DB-free `gvm upgrade status` that also absorbs finding 11's 'upgrade notes'. `gvm restart --pending` should work this way: (1) one instance at a time is the default, not a flag; (2) it prints the list and asks y/N (or takes --yes); (3) it skips held instances and any with a live or unreadable lease; (4) it uses the existing systemctl restart path; (5) it waits for phase running with a timeout and halts the sweep at the first instance that does not come back. Defer --when-empty, because player counts are not available for every game. Implement `gvm stop --all` as D-14 promises: same list and confirmation, the ladder per instance, and a hold written for each. Neither command is ever invoked by postinst, a timer, the guard or the reconciler; installing a package must never restart a world.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-6 — No command or runbook to take gvm off a box safely while keeping the backups

**Medium** · operator · missing-feature · effort L · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 2 · Plan: **53** · Status: open

**Evidence.** docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:494 and 513 and 6830; internal/hostops/verbs.go:62-91; internal/doctor FW-001 output 'Fix: gvm firewall enable' (orig: No uninstall, purge or prerm exists in the repo (grep -i uninstall finds nothing outside research notes; packaging/ has no prerm.sh; the legacy suite has none either). The things a removal must touch, and the ordering traps, are spread across the code. gvm-guard.timer restarts any enabled, un-held instance every 5 min (packaging/systemd/gvm-guard.timer:12-19), so it has to be stopped first. gvm@.service has no ConditionPathExists, so an enabled unit whose spec is gone fails at every boot (comment at internal/cli/destroy.go:166-172). tmpfiles/gvm.conf:43,50-51 names users gvm and gvm-fetch and group caddy, so deleting the users before the fragment produces systemd-tmpfiles errors at boot. `gvm firewall` exits 3 'not implemented yet', so gvm-tagged rules can only be removed by hand next to the SSH rule. Adopted instances run as a pre-existing account (pzserver), and their only copy of the legacy units is /var/lib/gvm/adopt/<n>-legacy-units/ (ARCHITECTURE.md:513, 6826), even though §5 labels /var/lib/gvm 'DERIVED. rebuildable. genuinely safe to lose' (ARCHITECTURE.md:494). Postgres roles gvm_migrate, gvm_ops and gvm_web plus pg_hba/pg_ident lines (§14.2) have no removal path. hostops has no user or group removal verb (internal/hostops/verbs.go:62-91).)

**Impact.** An operator who wants gvm gone must reverse-engineer about 15 locations. Wiping /var/lib/gvm loses an adopted server's way back to its legacy units. Deleting users in the wrong order breaks boot-time tmpfiles. The guard may restart a server mid-teardown. Firewall cleanup by hand risks deleting the SSH rule. The easiest mistakes (rm -rf /etc/gvm /var/lib/gvm /srv/gvm) destroy worlds and secrets with no archive.

**Suggestion.** Build it in two steps. Step 1: docs/uninstalling.md plus a read-only `gvm uninstall --dry` listing every location under REMOVE / KEEP / LEFT FOR YOU. This delivers most of the value at no risk. Step 2: `gvm uninstall` with typed-hostname confirmation and the suggested order, with these changes. (a) Refuse while any job or freeze lease is live or unreadable. Stop the guard and job timers first. For each instance, take a verified backup, then stop it through the ladder. (b) Implement hand-back in Go through hostops: restore the legacy units, remove 20-adopt.conf and the legacy guard, then daemon-reload. Never exec the stored rollback.sh. Move the legacy-units copy out of /var/lib/gvm into /etc/gvm/adopt or /var/backups/gvm/<n>. (c) Follow dpkg remove/purge semantics. By default, KEEP /etc/gvm (specs, secrets, master.key) and list it under LEFT FOR YOU. `--purge-config` removes it. Never write a cleartext secrets copy into /var/backups/gvm: age-encrypt it to the configured recipient, or put it 0600 under /root. Delete master.key only together with --drop-database. (d) Add the firewall step only once `gvm firewall` is wired, through the same reconciler: D-42 fail-closed, SSH never removed, never `ufw disable`; on nft, only gvm's own table. (e) Remove only pg_hba and pg_ident lines between gvm's own marker comments. (f) Remove accounts only when they carry gvm's GECOS. (g) Neither uninstall nor a .deb purge ever touches /var/backups/gvm. The prerm-on-remove refusal while specs exist is good as suggested.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-7 — `gvm destroy` silently leaves secrets, the account and per-instance state behind

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: 40 → **53** · Status: open

**Evidence.** After deleting install and data, runDestroy (internal/cli/destroy.go:157-232) removes only the timers and job descriptors (OrphanTimerAction, internal/reconcile/timers.go:383-391), disables the unit, and removes 10-gvm.conf, manifest.lock.yaml and the spec. It never removes any of these instance.PathsFor entries (internal/instance/paths.go:175-224): SecretsDir /etc/gvm/secrets/instances/<n> (cleartext rcon/admin/join; the jail marks it Protected, destroy.go:309-314), ContentLock <n>.lock.json, the empty <n>.d directory, State /var/lib/gvm/instances/<n>, SteamHome, the Logs dir, the 20-adopt.conf drop-in, or the gvm-<n> account and group (there is no hostops verb for that). Archives never include secrets, because nothing sets archive.CreateOptions.SecretsIncluded (grep shows only internal/archive/create.go:89,166). So keeping the secrets dir is correct for reversibility, but it is never explained. The output is only 'removed N file(s)...' and 'the archive is still at <path>' (destroy.go:162-163, 231). Once firewall wiring lands, destroy also has no step to remove gvm:<n>:<port> rules.

**Impact.** An operator who destroys a server reasonably believes it is gone. In fact its passwords stay on disk, a system account stays in /etc/passwd, and, once firewall management is wired, its game ports stay open to the internet. Repeated create and destroy cycles accumulate gvm-* accounts and directories, and a later create under the same name inherits stale state.

**Suggestion.** End destroy with an explicit 'LEFT IN PLACE' block listing each residue, its path and why it was kept (for example 'secrets kept: the archive does not contain them; remove with `gvm secret purge <n>` once you no longer need to restore'). Add `--purge-residue`, which removes state, logs and the Steam home, and removes the account only when it carries GECOS 'gvm instance <n>' and owns no remaining files. Add a hostops user.remove verb restricted to such accounts. When firewall management is wired, have destroy reconcile away gvm:<n>:* rules under the existing SSH and D-42 invariants. The same residue list feeds `gvm uninstall`.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-9 — Rolling back to the previous binary is undocumented and silently drops fields the newer binary understood

**Medium** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · Plan: 39 → **52** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/spec/load.go:337,383; internal/cli/doctor.go:143; gvm game validate output shows the 'driverABI 1 (this build implements 1)' gate for manifests (orig: Manifests and instance specs are decoded with plain yaml.Unmarshal (internal/spec/load.go:337 and 383), so unknown keys are ignored. Repro: copy games/valheim.yaml, add `futureField: true` under spec, and run `gvm game validate v.yaml`. Output: '0 errors, 0 warnings.' exit 0. Doctor even sets `dec.KnownFields(false)` explicitly for gvm.yaml (internal/cli/doctor.go:141-142). apiVersion stays 'gvm/v1' (internal/spec/instance.go:123) with no minimum-gvm marker. Units and binary must roll back together because units embed argv (gvm@.service:23-24), but nothing records which pair is installed. No document describes a rollback.)

**Impact.** An operator who rolls back after a bad release gets an older binary that quietly ignores newer spec fields at its next apply. Examples are a narrowed port exposure or an egress allowlist, so the box becomes less restricted with no warning. A rollback that swaps only the binary and keeps new units can leave instances failing at the next restart.

**Suggestion.** Report unknown keys as positioned diagnostics (file:line) from the existing NodeIndex walk, so all of them are listed, not only the first. Use the wording 'spec.egress.policy is not known to gvm 0.3.1; this file was probably written by a newer gvm'. Make them ERRORS in the operator-facing paths: `gvm game validate`, create, apply and its plan/diff, and doctor. Make them journal WARNINGS in `gvm run` and run-prepare, so a rollback never strands a world. Keep an `x-` prefix for manifest extensions. Do not write annotations into /etc/gvm/instances/*.yaml. Record the writer version in gvm-owned files instead: the drop-in header already has it, and manifest.lock.yaml should get it. Doctor warns when either file was written by a newer gvm than the one running. Write docs/upgrading.md#rollback as suggested.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-11 — `gvm version` says 'dev (commit unknown)' and there is no 'what changed for my instances' view

**Medium** · operator · confusing-output · effort M · fact: partial · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 9 · Plan: 23 → **35** → 49 · Status: open

**Evidence.** Makefile:9-20 (ldflags stamping); internal/version/version.go:3-5; internal/content/lock.go:245 and internal/spec/validate.go:350 (more references to the nonexistent `gvm manifest upgrade`) (orig: `gvm.exe version` printed 'gvm dev (commit unknown, go1.26.2, windows/amd64)'. `go version -m gvm.exe` on the same binary shows 'build vcs.revision=b69119ba37ab... vcs.modified=false'. String() ignores build info (internal/version/version.go:22-25), while Info() reads it (version.go:28-50) but has no caller; the dashboard does not use it either (no version reference in internal/web). The same 'dev'/'unknown' propagates into the runner hello (run.go:340), the archive manifest's gvm_commit (archive/create.go:150-151) and the drop-in's 'by gvm dev'. `gvm version --json` silently ignores the flag and prints the banner. There are no tags, no CHANGELOG, and no command that lists per-instance consequences of a release. The status hint for a pin mismatch names `gvm manifest upgrade <n> --dry` (derive.go:168, verbs.go:206), but `gvm manifest` returns 'gvm: unknown command "manifest"'. `gvm game list` shows only the shipped revision (e.g. 'projectzomboid ... revision 7'), not which instances are pinned to older ones.)

**Impact.** In a bug report or support thread, the operator cannot say which gvm they run, and every provenance stamp is useless. After an upgrade they cannot see what changed or what they need to do: re-apply, restart, migrate, or review a newer game manifest. A dashboard user cannot see the version at all.

**Suggestion.** Make String() fall back to vcs.revision, adding '-dirty' from vcs.modified. Implement `gvm version --json` with version, commit, store.SchemaVersion, console.Proto, LeaseProto, freeze LeaseSchema, archive SchemaVersion and driver ABI. Tag releases with semver, and embed a CHANGELOG.md whose required 'Operator actions' section per release covers re-apply, restart, migration and manifest revisions. Fold 'upgrade notes' into the single `gvm upgrade status [--since <ver>]`, computed locally from files and systemd (no Postgres, no admin.sock). It shows per-instance runner version, drop-in render version, and pinned versus shipped manifest revision. In the dashboard, show the version and restart-pending count only to authenticated users (rule 9; a version on the login page is a fingerprinting aid). Fix the dangling hints now (derive.go:168, verbs.go:206, content/lock.go:245): implement `gvm manifest upgrade --dry`, or point them at a command that exists.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-12 — Doctor checks the binary against itself, not against what is installed, and the unit-not-found hint loops

**Medium** · operator · not-wired · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: sound · rules at stake: 1 · Plan: **24** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/lifecycle/verbs.go:214-215 (start refusal repeats the `gvm apply` hint for RuleUnitNotFound) (orig: packaging/packaging.go:13-16 says doctor 'will need to compare what is installed on a host against what this build expects'. In practice HOST-010 reads only the embedded FS (internal/doctor/checks_host.go:39-80). On this Windows machine, with nothing installed, `gvm doctor` printed '✓ HOST-010   no game or job unit references postgresql.service'. No check compares /usr/lib/systemd/system/gvm@.service, the timers or /usr/lib/tmpfiles.d/gvm.conf with packaging.FS, confirms that the ExecStart path is this binary, or confirms gvm-guard.timer is enabled. Instance checks never run (doctorLoadInstances/doctorLoadManifest return nil, internal/cli/doctor.go:150-151). When the package template is missing, Derive returns RuleUnitNotFound with hint 'gvm apply <n>' (internal/lifecycle/derive.go:274-280). apply writes only the drop-in and cannot recreate a package-owned template, so following the hint does not clear the error.)

**Impact.** After a half-done manual upgrade (new binary, old units) or a partial removal, doctor reports green and `gvm status` sends the operator round in an apply loop that cannot fix anything. Doctor cannot serve as the 'did my upgrade land' check an operator expects.

**Suggestion.** Add HOST checks: each packaging.FS file is present at its install path with identical bytes (diff on request; flag both missing and locally edited files); the binary named by gvm@.service ExecStart has the same inode as /proc/self/exe; gvm-guard.timer and gvm-job@_verify.timer are enabled and have a future elapse; no stray /usr/local/bin/gvm alongside /usr/bin/gvm. Add INST checks for 'runner is gvm X, installed is Y' (finding 4) and 'drop-in would re-render' (finding 5), and load real instances so INST-* runs. Split RuleUnitNotFound: when the template itself is missing, the hint should be 'the gvm package's /usr/lib/systemd/system/gvm@.service is missing: reinstall gvm (`apt install --reinstall gvm`)' rather than gvm apply.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-8 — Database migrations have no upgrade unit, no lock, and a dead-end rollback story

**Low** · operator · missing-feature · effort M · fact: confirmed · fit: adjust · rules at stake: 8 · Plan: **52** · Status: open

**Evidence.** `gvm db status` run: 4 WARN lines, real 0m15.055s; internal/cli/root.go:101-102 gvmd/web notImplemented; internal/store/migrate.go:17-41 (sync.Mutex around goose globals) (orig: §14.3 (ARCHITECTURE.md:4664-4680) specifies /etc/systemd/system/gvm-db-migrate.service (Before=gvm-web.service). It is not in packaging/systemd. AssertCompatible is never called (grep shows only its definition, internal/store/migrate.go:82-91). Its message tells the operator to 'run `gvm db migrate`' even when the schema is AHEAD, the rollback case, where migrate cannot help. `gvm db status` prints 'schema is AHEAD of binary — downgrade not supported' (internal/cli/db.go:83-89), although every migration file carries a `-- +goose Down` section (0001-0010; 0010_grants.sql:38 is a no-op down). Doctor's HOST-003 'ahead' result is a Warning with no Fix (internal/doctor/checks_host_db.go:199-203). Migrate uses goose's package globals under an in-process mutex (migrate.go:29-41), not the Provider + session locker §14.3 requires for 'a postinstall racing a manual run' (ARCHITECTURE.md:4650). Run with Postgres down: `gvm db status` printed five 'WARN store: pool ping failed, retrying ... delay=1s/2s/4s/8s/16s' lines before 'db status: store: datastore unavailable: timed out waiting for postgres', after about 15 s.)

**Impact.** There is no defined moment in an upgrade when migrations run. If a postinst calls `gvm db migrate` while Postgres is down, it hangs about 15 s and fails the package configure step, which is the kind of DB coupling rule 1 forbids. Two concurrent migrates are not serialised. An operator who needs to roll back is told downgrade is impossible, and the old binary tells them to run a command that does nothing.

**Suggestion.** Ship gvm-db-migrate.service as a package-owned unit in /usr/lib/systemd/system: After=postgresql, Before=gvm-web only, ExecStart=/usr/bin/gvm db migrate. postinst starts it with --no-block and never fails the package; a failure blocks only gvm-web, and doctor HOST-003 reports it with a Fix. Switch to goose.NewProvider plus the Postgres session locker, as §14.3 requires. Wire AssertCompatible: fatal in gvm-web, warning in gvmd. Word the message by direction. Behind: 'run `gvm db migrate`'. Ahead: 'schema 11 was written by a newer gvm; the CLI and every game server keep working, only the dashboard waits; reinstall gvm >= X or see docs/upgrading.md#rollback'. Add --no-wait to db status. Make expand-only migrations within a release line the rollback policy, so a binary downgrade rarely needs a schema change. Treat `gvm db migrate --to N` as a later escape hatch. If it is added, it takes a pg_dump first, requires --confirm, writes an audit row, and refuses any Down not covered by an up/down/up CI test.

### gvm-self-upgrade-uninstall-10 — Archive and index readers accept only the exact current schema, so a format bump strands every existing backup

**Low** · operator · safety-ux · effort M · fact: partial · fit: sound · rules at stake: 5 · Plan: **52** · Status: open

**Evidence.** internal/archive/index.go:145-154 (Reindex rebuilds on LoadIndex error); internal/cli/backup.go:343-346 (archiveRows calls LoadIndex with no fallback), used at backup.go:288 and restore.go:321; internal/archive/verify.go:141, restore.go:120 (orig: internal/archive/manifest.go:160-162: `if m.Schema != SchemaVersion` -> 'schema %d, this build writes and reads %d'. internal/archive/index.go:106-108 rejects any other index schema outright instead of reindexing. destroy and restore both go through archive.Reindex and VerifyRef (internal/cli/destroy.go:252-284). Preserve archives are kept forever by design ('A `preserve` archive is deleted by no code path at all', `gvm backup` usage text).)

**Impact.** The first release that bumps the archive schema leaves the new binary unable to verify or restore any backup taken before the upgrade, including the never-pruned preserve archives. A rollback cannot restore backups the newer binary took. A reinstall after uninstall hits the same wall. It fails closed rather than corrupting, but the safety net is gone exactly when an upgrade has gone wrong.

**Suggestion.** Make archive readers backward-compatible by policy: accept every schema at or below the current one (with an in-code upgrade-on-read for each), and refuse only newer ones with 'archive written by gvm 0.5.0 (schema 3); install gvm >= 0.5.0 to restore it'. Rebuild the index from the archives on an index schema mismatch instead of erroring. Add a CI fixture that keeps one small archive per released schema and asserts `gvm backup verify` and `gvm restore --dry` pass on each.
